# Arco — gotchas and invariants

Coupling warnings and "don't regress" rules that aren't obvious from the code alone. Each entry exists because someone previously broke it, or because the load-bearing detail is surprising enough that a fresh agent would miss it. Read the relevant section before touching chat rendering, worker lifecycle, session rotation, or the protocol.

For the architecture map, see [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md). For chronological history, see [`../DEVLOG.md`](../DEVLOG.md). For terminology, see [`GLOSSARY.md`](GLOSSARY.md).

## Shadow-git checkpoints (A2)

- **Snapshots must never touch the user's real index / HEAD / working tree.** `server/src/gitSnapshots.ts` snapshots via a **unique per-operation `GIT_INDEX_FILE`** (`$TMPDIR/arco-snapshot-<cwd-hash>-<pid>-<uuid>.index`; the shadow repo uses an equally unique index inside its bare gitdir) and only ever writes `refs/arco/<wfid>/<seq>`. The unique name is load-bearing because checkpoint capture is fire-and-forget and multiple workflows can share one cwd; a fixed `snap.index` races on `snap.index.lock`. The repo scratch file must stay outside the worktree too, or one concurrent capture can stage another's scratch when `.arco` is not ignored. No `reset`/`checkout`/`stash`/`add` against the real index — the operator's `git status` must stay byte-identical. The smoke (`npm run smoke:checkpoints`) asserts isolation, cleanup, and concurrency; don't weaken it. All git calls use `execFile` arg arrays (no shell-string interpolation).
- **The pre-turn checkpoint hook lives in `turnEngine.handleUserMessage`, not `seedWorker.ts`.** `runPrimaryTurn` is shared by reviewers / handoff seeds / `exportContext` / Task children, which must **not** checkpoint the operator tree. Snapshot only on operator turns, skipped in `armPlanMode`, fire-and-forget so it never blocks/fails the turn. See `docs/CHECKPOINTS.md`.
- **Checkpoints are never exposed to the LLM** — no tool, no system-prompt mention. Pure operator infra.
- **The non-git fallback's size caps are load-bearing in BOTH directions — and size is not stable.** The shadow store (bare repo at `~/.arco/checkpoints/<cwd-hash>/git`) writes `<wfid>.<seq>.skipped.json` beside each ref. Revert excludes target-skipped paths from its seed even if they later shrink; if a currently over-cap path overlaps the target tree, it aborts before any write because the safety snapshot cannot preserve those bytes. A same-caps walk alone is insufficient: a large-at-capture file can shrink and be deleted, while a small-at-capture file can grow and be overwritten. Don't remove the sidecar/pruning/purge paths or weaken the abort. Don't reintroduce tar creation either: a tar per turn copies the entire cwd with zero sharing (a 318 GB ComfyUI cwd accrued 302 GB of timeout-truncated partial tars, twice — 2026-08-18/19); the legacy tar READER stays only for pre-shadow checkpoints. See `docs/CHECKPOINTS.md`.
- **Revert's seed must be `add -A` + `rm --cached -- .arco`, not an exclude pathspec.** `add -A -- ':!.arco' ':!.git'` makes git treat matched-but-ignored dirs as explicitly requested; with a global `**/.arco/` ignore rule (the operator's `~/.config/git/ignore`) the add REFUSES and every revert in a repo containing `.arco` fails (found 2026-08-19; pinned by `npm run smoke:checkpoint-revert`).

## A2 checkpoints vs I3 real git mutation (keep the code paths separate)

- **A2 checkpoints (`server/src/gitSnapshots.ts`) NEVER touch the real index/HEAD/working tree. I3 direct git ops (`server/src/gitOps.ts`) INTENTIONALLY DO — that's the whole point of I3.** These are deliberate opposites and must stay strictly separated: **`gitOps.ts` must NEVER import from `gitSnapshots.ts`, and `gitSnapshots.ts` must NEVER import from `gitOps.ts`.** A2 is the operator's safety net (shadow refs under `refs/arco/`, a separate `GIT_INDEX_FILE`); I3 is the operator's hands on the wheel (`git add`/`reset`/`commit`/`push`/`stash`/`branch`/`tag` against the real repo). Routing one through the other would either corrupt the operator's index (A2's invariant) or silently fail to mutate (I3's contract). If you ever need shared helpers, put them in a third module (e.g. the argv validators already live in `server/src/gitSafe.ts`, imported by both `gitOps.ts` and `gitWorking.ts`/`gitDiff.ts`) — never have one of the two import the other.
- **I3 is safe under the four-question test even though it mutates real git state.** Each op is an explicit operator click, bounded by deterministic argv with inputs validated by `gitSafe.ts` (`isSafePath`/`isSafeRef`/`isSafeRemote`), logged to `events.jsonl` as a `git_op_result` event, and reversible (git reflog / unstage / stash pop / branch recreate). The mutation is allowed *because* of those four properties, not in spite of them.
- **The security contract is non-negotiable.** Every git invocation is `execFile('git', argv, {cwd})` — never a shell string. Every operator value is validated before it reaches argv; `--` always precedes pathspecs. A push to a protected branch (`PROTECTED_BRANCH_RE`, shared from `shared/src/protocol/gitOps.ts`) or ANY force push requires explicit `confirm:true` — without it `push()` returns `needsConfirm` and git is NOT run; confirmed force uses `--force-with-lease`, never bare `--force`. Commit messages are committed verbatim — **no `Co-Authored-By` (or any) trailer is ever injected.** `npm run smoke:git-ops` locks all of this in (incl. adversarial flag-smuggling / metachar / no-confirm-push / empty-message assertions); don't weaken it.
- **I4 PR creation rides the REST `body`, never a `gh pr create` shell/argv string.** `github.createPullRequest` → the pure `buildCreatePrRequest` → `gh<T>(path, { method:'POST', body })`. Operator title/body/base/head go in the **JSON body** — never interpolated into a command, so a title beginning with `-` is harmless (it can't be read as an argv flag). This mirrors `mergePull`/`commentIssue`. PR creation is an outward action gated on an explicit form submit (no auto-create, no agent path) and — like the other GitHub write endpoints — does **not** broadcast to `events.jsonl`. `npm run smoke:github-pr` unit-tests `buildCreatePrRequest` (incl. the leading-dash-title-in-body and empty-field-rejection cases) with no live `gh`/network/PR; never create a real PR from a test.
- **GitHub's `files[].patch` is BARE HUNKS — reconstruct a git header before parsing.** A PR/commit file's `patch` starts straight at `@@ … @@` with no `diff --git`, `--- a/…`, or `+++ b/…` lines. `react-diff-view`'s `parseDiff` needs those to bind a filename + add/delete/rename type, so the raw patch yields a path-less file. `client/src/components/GitHubPanel/ghDiff.ts` (`ghFileToUnifiedDiff` / `ghFilesToUnifiedDiff`) prepends a synthetic `diff --git a/<old> b/<new>` / `--- a/<old>` / `+++ b/<new>` header per file, using `previous_filename ?? filename` for `<old>` and `/dev/null` for the add (`---`) / remove (`+++`) sides. Files with no `patch` (binary/oversized) are EXCLUDED from the renderer and shown as a notice row. The helper is pure (no React) and reused by both the PR files view and the commit-detail view; `npm run smoke:github-pr` asserts the header/`/dev/null`/rename/exclusion behavior. Don't feed GitHub's raw `patch` straight to `DiffView`.

## Worker safety (non-negotiable)

These exist because of the 2026-04-16 OOM incident that ballooned 63 GB of system RAM. Don't loosen without thinking through what the guardrail prevents:

- `MAX_WORKER_OUTPUT_BYTES = 5MB` per-worker (capped in per-provider accumulators).
- `RSS_KILL_BYTES = 32GB` tree-RSS auto-kill via `WorkerManager.startActivityPoll`. Per-workflow opt-out exists via `meta.json` `disableRssGuard` for legitimate heavy jobs (kernel compiles, big LTO links). Reviewers and handoff reflections always run under the global cap regardless.
- Subprocesses spawn `detached: true`; `killProcessTree` takes down descendants on cancel.
- Linux `/proc`-based activity probe (`processActivity.ts`, polled 1.5s) drives the CPU-pulse UI + silent-exit supervision. See `server/src/workers/limits.ts`.
- **Operator-terminal (PTY) reaping mirrors worker reaping, but with NO RSS auto-kill (§K2).** node-pty children are *not* `detached`, so a clean exit reaps them — but a hard kill reparents them to init and leaks them. So they get the same durable-registry treatment: `server/src/terminal/ptyPidRegistry.ts` persists pids to `~/.arco/terminal-pids.json` (a **separate** file from `worker-pids.json`, so the two reap sets can't clobber each other), and `reapOrphanTerminals()` runs right after `reapOrphanWorkers()` before `listen()`, SIGKILLing stale pids via the shared `forceKillPid`. **Crucial difference: do NOT add an RSS auto-kill of operator shells.** A worker is autonomous and bounded by `RSS_KILL_BYTES`; a terminal runs processes the *operator started intentionally* (dev server, build, REPL), so SIGKILLing their shell on a memory threshold would be destructive and violate the operator-in-control fence. Orphan reaping (a *prior crashed run's* leak) is safe; killing a *live* operator session on a heuristic is not. Any future guard must default to observation-only. Two windows can attach to one session (multi-listener `Set` in `PtySession`); closing one window disposes only its listener, not the co-viewer's.
- **The §3.1 stuck-worker breaker pauses a worker that keeps FAILING THE SAME WAY — error-persistence, not action-pattern.** `workers/stuckBreaker.ts` keys per workflow on the signature `toolName|inputHash|outputHash` (the watchdog's NORMALIZED `outputHash` from `providers/outputSignature.ts`, so "the same error" with drifting digits/timestamps collapses to one signature) and counts a streak of FAILING results (`failed` status OR non-zero `exitCode` OR an `[error]` sentinel) with no success interleaved: ≥`DETECT_AT` (3) → `nudge`, ≥`ESCALATE_AT` (5) → recommend a reversible `pause`; any success or different signature → `recovered`. It NEVER kills/prompts a worker itself — it returns a verdict `seedWorker` turns into a visible `worker_stuck` event. Action-pattern loop-detection was FALSIFIED on real logs (a model legitimately re-runs the same shape of command); error-persistence is the signal that holds. Cross-turn + cross-provider (consumes `worker_tool_use` from any transport carrying `outputHash` — claude-native + codex today). Composes with the in-turn Native H9 guard.
- **H46 RSS/heap trend logging is OFF by default and observation-only.** `memoryMonitor.ts` samples `process.memoryUsage()` every ~5 min (plus a `boot` baseline and a `shutdown` high-water line) into a file log ONLY when file logging is enabled — a cheap trend the operator greps, never a kill trigger. The `RSS_KILL_BYTES` tree-RSS guard above stays the only auto-kill. Don't wire an action onto the trend.

## Provider / worker plumbing

- **Provider logins run in Arco's terminal, never spawned by the server.**
  `POST /api/auth-login` returns `{ command }` — each CLI's headless mode
  (`claude auth login`, `codex login --device-auth`, `env NO_OPEN_BROWSER=1
  cursor-agent login`, `gh auth login --web`, `env NO_BROWSER=true gemini`) —
  and the client's `LoginCommand` pre-fills it into the terminal (§K3 fence:
  the operator presses Enter). Don't reintroduce detached `spawn('claude',
  ['auth','login'])` with `stdio:'ignore'` or desktop-terminal launches: with
  no reachable browser/display they no-op silently, and when the operator is
  on another device over the tailnet the browser opens on the wrong machine.
  Claude Native's PKCE redirect is `localhost:53692`, so its paste-the-URL box
  is ALWAYS offered, not only on `manualFallback`.

- **A failed `claude auth status` probe is "unknown", never "logged out".**
  `getClaudeAuthStatus` retries once, then returns the last known answer with
  `probeFailed:true`; only a parsed `loggedIn:false` counts. `HeaderAuthChip`
  ignores `probeFailed`, and App.tsx keeps polling while logged out. Prior
  incident: one timeout flipped loggedIn=false, the poll stopped, and the chip
  stuck red until clicked — the near-daily false re-login prompt.

- **Chat mode is an execution boundary, not a CSS toggle.** `kind:'chat'` must
  become `WorkerConfig.interactionMode:'chat'` on every turn. Keep project
  prompt expansion, context refs, trust discovery, skills/CLAUDE.md/knowledge,
  plans, MCP wiring, preview sniffing, and checkpoints behind
  `projectContextEnabled`. The hidden `~/.arco/chats/<wfid>` cwd exists only
  because persistence and subprocess APIs require a cwd; never treat it as a
  project. Native/local providers must omit project tools; an armed Ollama Chat
  gets only `WebSearch` / `WebFetch`. Codex/Cursor must remain
  read-only and isolated. Gemini/Antigravity stay rejected server-side even if
  a stale client crafts a create request.

- **Web access is the ONE tool a Chat tab may hold, and it is per-tab operator
  state.** `chatWebArmed(config, provider)` in `server/src/chatMode.ts` is the
  single source of truth: unset `chatWebAccess` means armed for a saved Chat and
  disarmed for Incognito (the query leaves the machine), and only
  `CHAT_WEB_PROVIDERS` (claude, codex, ollama, strata) can honor it. Every transport
  states it
  outright rather than inheriting a provider default — CLI Claude
  `--tools WebSearch,WebFetch`, Native `buildChatWebTools()` (web_search +
  web_fetch only; NOT `buildClaudeServerTools`, whose `code_execution` would
  hand a projectless chat an interpreter nobody armed), Codex exec + app-server
  `web_search="live"|"disabled"`; Ollama advertises only its local
  SearXNG-backed `WebSearch` / `WebFetch` pair and auto-executes that narrowed
  pair even though Chat has no Agent AUTO toggle. `ollamaToolCallAllowed()`
  repeats the web-only check at dispatch because a model can invent a known but
  unadvertised tool name; never rely on `tools[]` alone as the Chat boundary.
  Ollama Chat transcript replay must use `preserveThinking:false`: hidden
  reasoning from an earlier offline turn can quote the old no-web system prompt
  and override the newly armed state by recency. The latest user turn also gets
  `ollamaChatWebReminder()` so visible historical refusals are explicitly stale.
  The system prompt must move with the toolset —
  `chatSystemPrompt(armed)` — or the model refuses lookups it can actually do
  (2026-09-10: "Not in this Chat tab, I don't have web access here" while the
  operator was asking for a search). Nothing else about the boundary moves: no
  filesystem, no shell, no MCP, no project.

- **The strict-incognito config fence has exactly three exceptions.**
  `INCOGNITO_SAFE_CONFIG_FIELDS` in `server/src/index.ts` lets an
  `update_primary_config` carrying ONLY `model` / `effort` / `chatWebAccess`
  through; every other field (`remoteEnabled` above all) still gets
  "unavailable in strict Incognito Chat". The exception holds because those
  three choose what the next turn spawns and nothing more — an incognito chat's
  cwd is the private per-process `incognitoChatCwd()` dir (0700 mkdtemp under
  the temp root — never the shared world-writable root itself, whose planted
  CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md / .claude/settings.json provider CLIs would load), so
  the handler's meta write finds no meta.json to update. Widening this set means re-proving disklessness for the new field.

- **Incognito Chat must be diskless at every Arco boundary.** `incognito:true`
  is Chat-only and cannot resume. Do not add it to `workflowCwds`, call
  `initWorkflowStore`/`persistInitialMeta`, or let it through the Zustand
  `partialize` jobs map. Stateless providers rebuild prior turns from
  `WorkflowState.incognitoEvents` (not `/tmp/.arco`); streaming text is
  coalesced there to avoid chunk-count growth. Claude CLI must receive no
  session id (`--no-session-persistence`), ordinary Codex Chat uses `exec --ephemeral`,
  and Computer alone uses verified app-server `thread/start {ephemeral:true}`
  (reject resume or a non-ephemeral/pathful response before inference),
  and Z.ai must use Native. `server/logger.ts` suppresses the whole incognito
  async tree plus any delayed line carrying its workflow/worker id; CLI stderr
  forwarding, notifications, remote reporting, and the durable PID registry
  are also disabled. Claude framing/history rides stdin, never argv or an
  append-system-prompt temp file. Cursor has no ephemeral-session switch and
  llama.cpp has an unavoidable shared `~/.arco/llamacpp.log`; both are
  rejected rather than given a misleading privacy label. The protocol fence
  rejects attachments, skills, primitives, and all non-chat workbench actions.
  On the last observer disconnect, a 10-second reconnect grace ends in
  `handleRemoveWorkflow`, which kills workers and drops transcript memory.
  “Incognito” describes Arco's persistence; remote API providers retain
  requests under their own policies.

- Workers inherit `cwd` from the workflow.
- `kill()` in every provider snapshots `wasRunning` BEFORE mutating status, then emits a synthetic `'error'` so `WorkerManager`'s promise resolves. Don't regress — cancelled workers will otherwise hang the workflow busy forever.
- `WorkerConfig.sandbox?: 'read-only' | 'workspace-write' | 'danger-full-access'` (in `shared/src/protocol/workflows.ts`) gates Codex sandbox mode. `codex.ts:buildArgs` prefers `config.sandbox`, falls back to `skipPermissions ? 'danger-full-access' : 'workspace-write'`. Other providers ignore. Claude reviewers still gate via `READ_ONLY_CLAUDE_TOOLS`.
- **Codex resume drops sandbox flags.** `codex exec resume` doesn't accept `--color`, `-s <mode>`, `-C <cwd>`, or `--add-dir`. `buildArgs` branches on `isResume` and drops them. Sandbox maps to `--full-auto` (workspace-write) or `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` (danger-full-access). `cwd` rides on `spawn({ cwd })`. `-c` overrides (`reasoning_effort`, `model_context_window`, chat `web_search`) are shared by both sides; `--ephemeral` and the chat `--ignore-user-config` pair are exec-only. Don't add new flags to the resume path without checking which side accepts them.
- **Codex placeholder UUID reconciliation is mandatory.** First-turn Codex doesn't have a thread_id until the `thread.started` event fires; Arco mints a placeholder UUID for `meta.cliSessions[]` and reconciles it in place via `reconcileLatestCliSession()` after the real id arrives. Without this, the next resume fails with "no rollout found."
- **Session rotation (handoff/spawn) MUST call `startSessionWatcher` in every path** — both pre-minted (`initialSessionId`) and async-discovered (`onSessionDiscovered`) branches. The watcher's internal dispose-then-start makes repeated calls safe. Missing this pins the watcher to the archived JSONL; new writes never become `session_entry` events and finalized assistants vanish from chat on `primary_complete`.
- **Concurrent-writer guard**: JSONL watcher detects entries with `entrypoint !== 'sdk-cli'` (a native `claude --resume` terminal touching the same session), emits debounced `system_notice` (30s window), and `handleUserMessage` refuses to start a new turn within 60s of the last foreign write. Contaminated sessions recover via Handoff, not in-place. Watcher starts with `byteLength` offset so past touches don't retrigger.
- **API resilience** (Claude): `StreamParser` catches `{"type":"error"}` envelopes (top-level + text-delta variants) and surfaces them as `system_notice` events. `ClaudeWorkerProcess` retries on pre-result hard exits with 15s/30s/60s backoff, up to 3 attempts, reusing the same session-resume id (idempotent — the prompt never reached the API on pre-result exit). `kill()` clears the retry timer. Don't retry inside the CLI's internal retry window — gate is "exited non-zero without result," not mid-stream.
- **Ultracode result-coalescing is load-bearing — under `config.ultracode`, the turn settles on PROCESS EXIT, not the first `result` event.** An ultracode turn runs the harness WorkflowTool's subagent fleet in the **background**, so one `claude -p` process emits **two `result` events**: an early "workflow launched" ack, then the real synthesis when the fleet finishes (the process exits 0 only after the last). WorkerManager settles on the FIRST `result` (its `settled` flag), so without intervention the operator gets the ack and the synthesis is dropped. Fix, gated on the flag, in `claude.ts`: `parser.on('result')` does NOT emit under ultracode — it HOLDS the result in `_heldResult` (emitting a superseded prior as an `[ultracode] workflow checkpoint` progress `system_notice`); the `exit` handler FLUSHES the last held result as the canonical `result`. **Don't move the finalize back onto the first `result`, and don't drop the exit-flush** — either regresses to the ack-as-answer bug. Don't ungate it either: normal CLI turns emit exactly one `result` then exit, and must stay byte-identical (they never hit the hold path). The flush fires on ANY exit when a held result exists (so a workflow that crashed mid-flight surfaces what it has rather than hanging the turn busy). The activity probe is safe for the long quiet gap: its silent-exit killer only fires on `!alive` past a grace window, never on an alive-but-quiet process, and the exit-flush settles the worker before the probe's next tick. See `docs/PROVIDERS.md § Claude § Ultracode` and `docs/ULTRACODE_PLAN.md`.
- **The ultracode FLEET watcher is read-only transient telemetry — `ultracode_fleet_progress` is NEVER persisted.** `server/src/ultracodeFleetWatcher.ts` polls the CLI WorkflowTool's background subagent transcripts (`<sessionId>/subagents/workflows/wf_*/…`) and emits a per-turn snapshot via `broadcastTransient` — it replaces the prior in place (like `context_usage`), so it never lands in events.jsonl. Turn-scoped (baselines pre-existing `wf_*` dirs so only this turn's runs count), started only when `ultracode && wf.primarySessionId`, torn down in the settle `finally`. It never writes run dirs and never steers (four-question test). Don't document it as an events.jsonl record and don't add a persist path.
- **Auth-expired notices use a `[AUTH:<provider>]` content sentinel.** `ClaudeWorkerProcess` special-cases `authentication_error` in the `stream_error` handler (hard stop — does NOT set `_lastTransientError`, retrying doesn't refresh tokens). Cursor ACP is stranger: its long-lived process answers with ordinary assistant text `Please sign in to continue`, so `CursorAcpTurn` holds only that short candidate prefix, recognizes the exact settled response, emits `[AUTH:cursor]`, and retires the cached worker. This retirement is load-bearing — the auth-status poll reads `~/.config/cursor/auth.json` fresh but an already-running ACP child keeps its old auth state and binary. `ChatMessage` detects the prefix via `parseAuthNotice` and renders `AuthReloginNotice` instead of the italic system line; inline **RE-LOGIN** hits `/api/auth-login?provider=<name>`, whose router allowlist must include every supported sentinel provider (especially `cursor`, or it silently falls back to Claude). The same sentinel is emitted client-side by `TerminalInput`'s TUI-only slash-command guard (`/login`, `/logout`). Keep the emitters, parser allowlist, and HTTP router in sync.
- **TUI-only slash commands are intercepted client-side.** `TerminalInput.handleSubmit` checks the first token against `TUI_ONLY_SLASH_COMMANDS` (18 Claude Code TUI commands: login/logout/context/clear/model/config/help/compact/cost/status/doctor/memory/agents/permissions/init/ide/bug/release-notes). Match → drop a local system message instead of sending. Arco runs `claude -p` so these never reach the interactive dispatcher; without the guard they hit the API as literal prompts (and expired `/login` attempts come back as 401s). Toolbox entries pass through — names don't collide.
- **Claude effort levels use doc overrides on top of `/v1/models`.** The live Anthropic catalog can lag newly-published model behavior; on 2026-05-30 it reported Opus 4.8 without `xhigh` while the current API and Claude Code docs list `low/medium/high/xhigh/max`. `effortsForModel()` unions live catalog levels with `CLAUDE_MODEL_EFFORTS` for known Claude IDs so pickers match the documented surface. Fable 5 / Mythos 5 and Opus 4.7/4.8 are adaptive-thinking models with `low/medium/high/xhigh/max`; keep 4.6/Sonnet 4.6 at `low/medium/high/max` (no `xhigh`). `ultracode` is intentionally not in `EffortLevel`: it is a Claude Code session setting (`xhigh` plus dynamic workflow permission), not an API effort value.
- **ARG_MAX overflow on `--append-system-prompt`** (Claude). Long appendSystemPrompts can push argv past Linux's per-arg limit (~128 KB); the CLI `E2BIG`s the spawn. Mitigation in claude.ts: above `APPEND_SP_ARG_MAX` (64 KB) it writes the value to `/tmp/arco-prompts/append-sp-<id>-<pid>.txt` and passes it via the **separate** `--append-system-prompt-file` flag (a documented peer of `--append-system-prompt` in the CLI's bare help) — not the same flag with a path. If the tmpfile write fails it falls back to inline `--append-system-prompt`. Tmpfiles are cleaned up on settle and on server exit.
- **A CLI that is installed but crashing must NOT collapse into "not installed" — `probeVersion` returns `'unknown'` (absent, ENOENT) vs `'broken'` (present, non-zero exit), and the PROVIDERS popover hides only the former.** 2026-09-03: the popover's own codex update command (`npm install -g @openai/codex@latest`, `capabilities.ts` `UPDATE_COMMAND`) silently skipped the 129 MB `@openai/codex-linux-x64` platform binary — it's an **optional** dependency, so npm exited 0 and reported success — leaving a launcher that threw `Missing optional dependency` on every invocation. `probeVersion` caught the throw and returned `'unknown'`, the popover's `version === 'unknown'` filter dropped the row, and CODEX vanished from the UI entirely: no error, no version, nothing to click. Auth compounded it — `getCodexAuthStatus` shells `codex login status`, so every profile reported logged-out despite intact `auth.json` files, sending the operator after a credential problem that did not exist. Three invariants: (1) keep the absent/broken distinction in `probeVersion` — collapsing them is what made the failure invisible; (2) `runCliUpdate` must not infer success from the version string merely *changing* (`'unknown' !== '0.153.1'` read as `updated: true` and rendered `→ vunknown`) — a post-update probe that can't report a version is a **failure**, and `probeFailureReason` re-runs the binary to surface its first `Error:` line; (3) an update failure is sticky in the popover (it survives the 6 s auto-clear) — auto-dismissing the only line that explains the breakage is how this stayed unexplained. Note the recovery trap: capabilities re-probe only at boot and every 4 h (`index.ts periodicCapabilityProbe`), and the sole manual re-probe is clicking the provider row — which is hidden while broken, so a service restart was the only way out.
- **Provider updates resolve an installable version BEFORE installing — `@latest` is not automatically installable.** `npmPlatformResolve.ts` (`resolveInstallableVersion`, called from `runCliUpdate`) asks the registry whether the `latest` wrapper's **self-published** platform build exists for this `platform-arch` and, if it doesn't, walks back to the newest version that has one and reports a `notice`. This closes the 2026-09-03 / 2026-09-04 codex breakage at the cause rather than after the fact. The cause is a **publish race**, which is why it recurs: upstream moves the `latest` dist-tag when the wrapper is published and the six platform tarballs land separately. Observed directly on 2026-09-04 — at 19:12 local `@openai/codex@0.153.3` was `latest` with no `0.153.3-linux-x64` in the registry (the `linux-x64` dist-tag still read `0.153.2`); by 19:35 the build had appeared and the identical install succeeded. Anyone clicking update inside that window gets a broken codex; clicking again later silently fixes it. Three invariants: (1) **the `ownPackage` narrowing in `findPlatformDep` is load-bearing** — plenty of CLIs optionally depend on someone *else's* platform build and run fine without it (`@google/gemini-cli` → `@lydell/node-pty-linux-x64`), so gating on a third-party package would downgrade a working CLI for no reason; only a build the package publishes for itself counts. (2) **Never infer success from npm's exit code** — a skipped optional dep exits 0; success is "the binary runs afterwards," which is why `runCliUpdate` still probes and still keeps the post-flight `--force` retry for the case where npm skips a build that *does* exist. (3) **A walk-back is a downgrade, not a fix** — it is capped at `MAX_WALK_BACK` (25) so a package that genuinely stopped shipping this platform fails loudly instead of silently installing something ancient, and the operator-facing notice says to re-run once upstream finishes publishing. Registry lookups are pure HTTP against `registry.npmjs.org`; the abbreviated packument (~4 MB for codex) is fetched **only** on the walk-back path, and any registry failure returns null so the updater falls back to plain `@latest` rather than blocking an update on a network blip. Parser shape pinned by `npmPlatformResolve.test.ts`.
- **There are two `codex` binaries on this machine and that is deliberate.** `~/.npm-global/bin/codex` (npm, earlier in `PATH`, the one Arco actually spawns — every call site uses bare `'codex'`, there is no configurable binary path) and `/usr/local/bin/codex` (distribution package `codex-cli`, manifest-tracked, immune to the optional-dep failure above because its PKGBUILD extracts the platform tarball explicitly). Do **not** "consolidate" by removing the npm copy: `UPDATE_COMMAND.codex` is hardcoded to `npm install -g`, so the popover's update button would simply recreate it. Same shape as `claude-code`.

## Claude Native transport (Phase 7a)

- **Cache discipline is load-bearing.** The forced first system block (`"You are Claude Code, …"`) is byte-stable across every Native tab; the appendSystemPrompt block is byte-stable across iterations within a turn. Never insert per-turn-variable content (timestamps, request IDs, operator name) above the cache marker — that invalidates every prior turn's cache on every tab. System-block ordering is fixed (canonical list in the `buildSystemBlocks` header comment: FORCED_FIRST_BLOCK → north-star → cluster plan → role → efficiency/progress doctrine → delegation contract → sandbox nudge → env note → turn discipline → workspace facts → appendSystemPrompt → systemPrompt → output style; a Chat tab short-circuits to FORCED_FIRST_BLOCK → chat prompt); reordering on a deploy invalidates every active tab's cache. The hardcoded `FORCED_FIRST_BLOCK` constant in `worker.ts` is the single source of truth — operator overrides were intentionally rejected (§14 Q4). **Symptom when it is missing (observed 2026-09-01 while probing the wire fingerprint):** on the OAuth subscription path Anthropic answers a request without the identity block with a bare `429 rate_limit_error {"message":"Error"}` — no `retry-after`, no ratelimit headers — on every model except Haiku. It looks exactly like a usage cap and isn't one; the same request with the block returns 200. Any raw probe / diagnostic (`/api/auth/claude-native/probe`, one-off scripts) must send the block.
- **Stateless Native helpers live in siblings, re-exported from `worker.ts` (P0 carve-out).** To stay under the 2000-line cap, the pure leaf helpers were moved to `modelParams.ts` (token/thinking/effort/ctx), `contentBlocks.ts` (content-block types + image/message/server-tool/context-mgmt/strip-thinking helpers), and `workerHelpers.ts` (`replayMessagesFromEvents`, `mergeUsage`, `truncateOutput`, `planParserAdvisories`, `sleepWithSignal`). `worker.ts` re-exports the previously-public names so `from './worker.js'` imports (including the test files) keep working — **add new pure helpers to the sibling modules, not back into `worker.ts`**; only stateful class logic belongs there. The `FORCED_FIRST_BLOCK` constant stays in `worker.ts` (single source of truth, see cache-discipline bullet above).
- **Replay-on-restart is the canonical recovery path.** On `ensureStarted` the worker tail-reads `events.jsonl` and reconstructs `messages[]` from `user_message_echo` + `primary_text` entries. Tool_use / tool_result events are NOT replayed (the model gets a clean USER/ASSISTANT view; reconstructing tool ids with full fidelity is deferred — §14 Q7). Don't add a periodic snapshot path without first measuring whether replay cost actually bites. The reconstruction logs to stderr under `[claude-native:<wfid>]`; that's the forensic surface.
- **`replayMessagesFromEvents` pair-flushes — solo user / solo assistant entries are dropped.** `flushPair()` only emits a (user, assistant) tuple when BOTH `userBuf` and `assistantBuf` are non-empty. An in-flight `user_message_echo` with no `primary_complete` yet, or an orphan `primary_text` from a reflection turn that landed in events.jsonl without a preceding echo, both fall on the floor. Reason: a trailing user-only entry would duplicate against the next operator prompt's fresh user push; an orphan assistant would invert into the `[assistant, user, …]` shape Anthropic rejects. Don't "fix" the asymmetry by emitting partial pairs.
- **`replayMessagesFromEvents` resets `messages[]` on every successful `handoff_artifact_ready`.** Anything before the most recent successful handoff event is discarded; only the post-handoff seed turn and what follows seeds the replayed worker. This is what makes Handoff a true rotation rather than an additive seed prompt. Don't change the reset to "merge" semantics — the rotated-out session's tool history would re-enter the worker's view and break the operator's mental model of "handoff is a fresh start."
- **Server compaction block must be resent verbatim** (`compact_20260112`, opt-in `claudeNativeCompact`). The response's `{type:'compaction', content}` block stays in the assistant message in stream order and goes back on every later request — the API ignores everything before it, and dropping it (the pre-2026-09-25 behaviour) re-compacted every round. Never render it as assistant text. Once one is in history the `compact-2026-01-12` beta rides every request even with the toggle off. It is not a history edit under preserved thinking (the prefix check restarts at the block), but never re-insert earlier assistant turns with their thinking after it. Replay is text-only, so a restart may re-compact once.
- **Server Fold (`context_folded {engine:'server'}`, `compact-2026-09-04`) keeps the prefix FROZEN and persists the RAW kept turns — both deliberate exceptions.** Kept thinking stays valid only while `system` + `tools` match the requests that produced it, so when turns are kept `serverFoldInPlace` does NOT call `resetPrefixFreeze` (Fold/compact do); instead it marks `deliveredSystem = frozenSystem` and rebuilds `activeToolKeys` from the frozen base + tool changes surviving in the tail (`activeToolKeysAfterServerFold`), so system/tool deltas that lived in the summarized range are restated after the NEXT user turn — never between the block and the kept turns (that breaks their thinking). With zero kept turns it resets the freeze like Fold. And unlike `keptTail`, `keptMessages` is the raw message array (thinking signatures, tool_use/tool_result pairs): the API requires kept turns unchanged, so live AND replay both consume that same persisted array via `serverFoldSeed` (byte-identity pinned by `serverFold.test.ts`); the 256 KB cap keeps it out of the way of the 1 MB exact-replay window. The signed block must go back exactly as returned and FIRST in `messages` — never re-project, re-render, or scrub it, and don't put summarized messages in front of it (`compaction_block_misplaced`). The cut must fall before an operator user message (not a tool_result message) so no tool call straddles it and the first kept role differs from the last summarized one.
- **Operator in-place compact (`native_compacted`) is replay-load-bearing — the live seed and the replay seed MUST be the same helper.** `worker.compactInPlace()` runs one summarization round, replaces `messages[]` with `compactSummarySeed(summary, keptTail)`, and persists a `native_compacted { summary, keptTail? }` event. On restart, `buildMessagesFromReplayEvents` and `summarizeClaudeNativePriorTurns` reset at that marker (same semantics as `handoff_artifact_ready`) and re-seed via the **same** `compactSummarySeed` — so a live worker and a replayed worker land on byte-identical history. If you fork the seed wording between the two sites, the prompt cache splits and a transcript diff shows phantom drift across a reload. `compactSummarySeed` lives in `resumeSummary.ts` (not the worker) precisely so both sites share it; don't inline a copy.
  - **Manual COMPACT must cold-acquire Native workers.** A restarted service, rehydrated tab, or post-handoff disposal can leave `workersByWorkflow` empty even though `events.jsonl` has completed turns to compact. `compactClaudeNativeWorker(workflowId, config)` must acquire a worker when needed and let `ensureStarted()` replay before `compactInPlace()` runs. Do not regress to a map-only lookup that tells the operator to "run a turn first" for an existing Native transcript.
  - **Native `inFlight` is per-provider-round, not durable busy state.** `streamOneRound()` installs a fresh `AbortController` for each Anthropic `messages.create`; successful turns and hard API failures MUST clear it before returning idle. `compactInPlace()` uses a non-aborted `inFlight` as the last defense against compacting during a live request, so a stale controller after a completed/terminated turn makes COMPACT falsely fail with "a turn is in flight" even though `wf.busy` and the UI are idle.
  - **COMPACT summarizes a clean conversational projection, never raw live tool protocol.** A cancelled/interrupted Native tool loop can leave an assistant `tool_use` without the immediately-following user `tool_result` Anthropic requires. Normal turns call `pruneOrphanToolUse()` before appending the next operator prompt; `compactInPlace()` must do the same before its summary round and emit the same visible `ctx_action`. The summary request then uses `projectCompactSummaryMessages()` so even older completed `tool_use` / `tool_result` blocks are stripped before hitting `messages.create`; replay never reconstructs those protocol blocks either, and the compact summary only needs the visible user/assistant text.
  - **Phase 2 keep-last-N tail rides the EVENT, not the post-marker event stream.** The kept verbatim tail (last ~3 user/assistant pairs, `projectKeptTail` in `workerHelpers.ts`) consists of messages that occur BEFORE the marker, so replay (which resets at the marker) would lose them — they're persisted in `native_compacted.keptTail` and re-appended via the SAME `compactSummarySeed(summary, keptTail)` at both sites. The projection mirrors replay's own shape: it skips tool_use/tool_result/image-only/empty messages (replay never reconstructs those), collapses consecutive same-role messages to the most recent, and drops any leading assistant so the tail starts on a `user` boundary (the seed ends on an assistant ack — a leading assistant would invert the order Anthropic rejects). Don't persist raw `messages[]` (tool_use blocks) into `keptTail` and don't change the projection on only one of the two sites — the live `messages[]` and the replayed array must consume the SAME persisted `keptTail`. Byte-identity is pinned by `npm run test:resume-summary -w server` ("compact keep-tail: live seed and replay reseed are byte-identical").
  - **Phase 3 compaction takeover must always have a clear path — it can't strand.** `native_compacting` (emitted from `compactInPlace` BEFORE the summary round, once for both the manual button and the auto path) flips the client `JobState.compacting` overlay. The flag is cleared on FOUR signals so it can never stick: `native_compacted` (success), a compaction-failure `system_notice` (the no-`native_compacted` path — the empty/failed summary leaves history untouched and emits ONLY a `Compact failed` / `Compaction skipped` notice; matched by `isCompactionFailureNotice`, unit-pinned by `npm run test:compacting-notice -w client`), `primary_turn_started` (a real turn resumed), and `turn_cancelled`. Do NOT clear it on `primary_turn_ended` — the manual COMPACT path fires a transient `primary_turn_ended` right after `native_compacted`, and clearing there would wipe the post-compaction "Continue" affordance the operator hasn't clicked yet. `native_compacting` is advisory on replay (the takeover is live-only; replay never re-opens it).
  - **Auto-continue is opt-in and goes through the normal operator path.** `nativeCompactAutoContinue` (default false) is the ONLY thing that makes `maybeAutoContinueAfterCompact` dispatch the continuance turn (`COMPACT_CONTINUE_PROMPT`) after a successful compaction — via `handleUserMessage`, so it's a real, visible turn in `events.jsonl`. Default is MANUAL (the operator clicks ▸ CONTINUE in the composer) because between-turn compaction ends the turn, so auto-continuing is autonomous continuation (CLAUDE.md autonomy fence). The auto path only fires when `ws`/`session` are in scope (the operator's connection) — the `__debug_native_auto_compact_probe` smoke passes neither, so it never auto-continues (no API spend). Both the manual (`compactHandlers`) and auto (`turnEngine`) paths call the SAME `maybeAutoContinueAfterCompact`; keep them in sync.
  - **Between-turn auto-compaction (`maybeAutoCompactNative`, Phase 2) reuses this path.** Armed via `efficiencyAutoCompact` (default off; `conservative`→compact at 80% of the real window, `aggressive`→70%, clamped [50,95]). It fires at the settled turn boundary (same post-turn site as `maybeAutoHandoff`, after `wf.busy` resets), is bounded once per turn (`wf.autoCompactFired`, reset on context-drop), fail-soft (a failed/empty summary leaves history untouched), and calls `compactClaudeNativeWorker()` — the SAME path as the manual COMPACT button. It runs AFTER auto-handoff so a successful handoff (which zeroes `lastContextPercent`) makes it no-op. Do NOT make it fire when `efficiencyAutoCompact === 'off'`, and do NOT re-enable the server-side `compact_20260112` beta to "help" — that's the separate mid-turn mechanism Phase 1 turned off precisely because it evicts the working set. The summarization call rides the worker's live OAuth client with `FORCED_FIRST_BLOCK` leading the system (OAuth rejects requests without the Claude Code identity) and NO tools / NO context-management edits. It's idle-only (the WS handler gates on `!wf.busy`) and operator-initiated, which is what keeps an operator-clicked summarize-replace on the right side of the "no second-LLM summarizer in the agent loop" fence — same eligibility class as Handoff.
- **OAuth refresh races.** `proper-lockfile` serializes the read-refresh-write cycle on `~/.arco/auth.json`. The lock is **not** held across the SDK call — only across the token rewrite. Two workers refreshing simultaneously: both acquire the lock in turn, the second sees the freshly-written token from the first and skips its own refresh. Don't hold the lock across `messages.create`; you'll deadlock the second worker against a 30-second SDK request.
- **Anthropic SDK pins `authToken` at construction; rebuild on rotation.** `new Anthropic({ authToken })` captures the token by value — refreshing the token file on disk does NOT update an existing client. A long-lived Native worker that survived a RE-LOGIN keeps its stale token and 401s every subsequent call until the tab closes. `ClaudeNativeWorker.ensureStarted()` stamps `clientTokenObtainedAt` from `ensureLiveTokens()` and rebuilds the SDK client on mismatch (logs `tokens rotated — rebuilding Anthropic client`). The rotation path skips `discoverHooks` and the `init` emit — both are first-time-wiring concerns. Don't pin the SDK client across a token rotation; the auto-refresh path inside `ensureLiveTokens` benefits from the same rebuild.
- **Callback port (53692) EADDRINUSE.** When the local OAuth callback server can't bind, `oauth.ts` falls back to manual-paste mode automatically and surfaces a flag the UI shows. Don't try a different port — Anthropic's redirect URI is registered against the constant. The operator's remote-access setup (only port 5173 forwarded) hits this fallback path normally.
- **H10: the Native worker honors Anthropic's OWN retry timing on 429/529/overload — and with the SDK's internal retries off (`maxRetries:0`) this ladder is the ONLY backoff.** `rateLimit.ts resolveRateLimitWait` reads `retry-after` (delta-seconds or HTTP-date) first, else the soonest `anthropic-ratelimit-*-reset` RFC-3339 timestamp; `classifyRateLimit` distinguishes a genuine rate limit from an overload. When no usable header is present (mid-stream SSE errors carry none) it falls back to the fixed 15/30/60s ladder. Don't re-enable SDK `maxRetries` to "help" — you'd double the backoff and lose the header-honoring wait.
- **Wire fingerprint pin is deliberate, not tracked.** `claude-cli/2.1.280` today (`wireFingerprint.ts`; 2.1.75 from Pi → 2.1.257 for Fable 5.1 → 2.1.280 for Opus 5.5). Bumping is a deliberate revalidation against an observed Anthropic-side rejection — not a routine `npm update` or `claude update`. Same rule for `@anthropic-ai/sdk@^0.91.1` (verify `Anthropic({ authToken })` field name + stream-event shape on bumps).
- **`anthropic-dangerous-direct-browser-access` IS sent on Claude Native OAuth requests.** Current Pi sends it on the SDK `authToken` path together with `x-app: cli`; Arco follows that wire fingerprint. Older Arco docs said Pi did not send it — that was stale after re-checking `earendil-works/pi` on 2026-05-31.
- **Native web search/fetch are Anthropic server tools, and their response `usage` is NOT the CTX bar.** `buildToolDefs()` appends Anthropic `web_search_20260209` / `web_fetch_20260309` plus `code_execution_20260120` (required for the efficient 2026 dynamic-filtering path). The response usage fields can include internal search/fetch processing tokens from a single request; those are real billing/processing telemetry but they can be hundreds of thousands of tokens larger than the retained conversation context. Do not drive `CTX: N / window` from `response.usage` on server-tool turns. Worker code prunes completed-turn server-tool evidence / citation metadata / hidden thinking from retained Native history, then uses `messages.countTokens()` with client tools only to estimate the next-turn prompt footprint. Do not add local `WebSearch` / `WebFetch` to `tools.ts` unless the product goal changes to operator-owned search providers; that would route browsing through Arco's dispatcher instead of Claude's native server-tool path.
- **Never edit the LATEST assistant message — not its thinking blocks, not the blocks around them.** Anthropic freezes the last assistant message in `messages[]`: *"`thinking` or `redacted_thinking` blocks in the latest assistant message cannot be modified. These blocks must remain as they were in the original response."* This is a much wider rule than the whole-prefix binding `enforcesPreservedThinking()` gates on (Fable/Mythos 5.1) — Opus 4.8 enforces it too — so protection must be unconditional, not model-keyed. The trap: Anthropic server tools return their evidence INSIDE the same assistant message that carries the final answer, so `pruneServerToolEvidenceAfterTurn` (which deletes `server_tool_use` / `*_tool_result` blocks and appends a retained-history note) was rewriting exactly that message. It now skips the latest assistant message; mid-turn assistant messages are still prunable. **The one intentional opt-out is `scrubServerToolEvidenceOnAbort` (`protectLatestAssistant: false`)** — an aborted round leaves a partial assistant message Arco assembled from a truncated stream, and its dangling `server_tool_use` is itself a guaranteed 400, so there the edit is mandatory. Same helper, opposite requirement; `npm run test:orphan-tooluse -w server` pins the abort side and `test:append-only` pins the settled side. **Symptom when regressed (2026-09-05, workflows `1f308e4c` / `b27a3dce` on opus-4-8):** the prune logs normally, `count_tokens` 400s ~150ms later, and the tab is then dead — every subsequent turn replays the same rejected history, and COMPACT can't rescue it because `generateCompactSummary` replays that history too. REWIND / rehydrate is the escape hatch (replay never reconstructs thinking blocks). **Protecting that message is necessary but not sufficient — see the mirror invariant below.** The same rule constrains `stripTrailingThinkingBlocks`, which mutates `this.messages` in place across the whole unsent tail — it has never actually fired in production (0 occurrences in 1466 opus-4-8 turns; `display:'omitted'` rarely ends a message on thinking), so it was left alone, but it is a live source of the identical 400 if that changes.
- **The 400-recovery matcher must cover BOTH preserved-thinking wordings.** `isThinkingBindingError` originally matched only the signature form (*"Invalid `signature` in `thinking` block"* / *"bound to a different conversation"* / `prefix_mismatch_behavior`). The latest-assistant-message form shares none of that vocabulary, so it fell through to the fatal branch and wedged the tab instead of triggering the one-shot `stripAllThinkingBlocks` retry. Any new Anthropic wording for "you edited history we froze" belongs in that matcher — the recovery is the same either way. Relatedly, `bindingStripUsed` re-arms per turn (`runTurn`), not per worker lifetime: it exists to stop a strip-retry loop within a round (already bounded by `attempt`), and a worker that self-healed once must not spend the rest of its life unable to recover again.
- **Edit refuses if file wasn't Read this turn.** Mirrors Claude Code's "blind edit" footgun — operators sometimes report "the model wrote a file it never looked at" on CLI; Native enforces the discipline at the dispatcher level. Per-turn read-set is reset at the top of `runTurn`. Reads from a prior turn don't carry over.
- **Background Bash → KillShell registry.** `Bash(run_in_background:true)` returns a `shellId` and registers `{kill, backgrounded:true}` on the worker's per-turn shell map. Foreground Bash also registers (cleared on close) so KillShell can interrupt a stuck foreground command. The map is per-worker (not per-turn) so a background shell started in turn N is killable from turn N+5. Operator cancel + tab close also iterate the map and kill everything.
- **Hooks fire inside the dispatcher; they're not the steer-drain hook.** `PreToolUse` / `PostToolUse` fire around each tool call inside `dispatchTool(...)`. The Phase 5 steer-drain hook fires between tool-result append and the next API round-trip in the agent loop. Both can fire on the same iteration without conflict — they have separate purposes (settings.json hooks observe individual tool calls; steer drain delivers operator-typed mid-turn input).
- **Handoff disposes the cached worker.** After `handoff_complete`, `disposeProviderResources(workflowId)` is called which tears down the cached `ClaudeNativeWorker`. The next operator turn rebuilds the messages array from the post-handoff transcript. Don't skip this — the worker's in-memory `messages[]` array would otherwise still reference the pre-handoff thread.
- **Original-worker handoff may fall back to fresh-summarizer.** Native original-worker reflection can fail after a long/expensive turn when Anthropic terminates the stream (`Anthropic HTTP 0: terminated`) or the source session is otherwise degraded. `runExportContext` keeps the same handoffId/artifactName and falls back to the configured fresh summarizer when original-worker returns an error. It also enforces a bounded original-worker reflection timeout (`ARCO_HANDOFF_ORIGINAL_WORKER_TIMEOUT_MS`, default 180s, clamped 30s-600s) so a degraded old session cannot hold Handoff for minutes before the fallback path starts. This is still operator-initiated Handoff, not an autonomous second-LLM loop: the operator clicked the primitive, the fallback is visible as a system notice, and the final artifact flows through the normal `handoff_artifact_ready` / `handoff_complete` path. Client upsert must clear a prior handoff error if the same handoffId later succeeds.
- **`handleHandoffRequest` disposes the Native worker BEFORE the pre-warm, for every mode — not just `original-worker`.** Originally the dispose was gated on `effectiveMode === 'original-worker' && transport === 'native'` because only that path ran the reflection on the cached worker; fresh-summarizer ran the reflection on a separate CLI summarizer worker, so the cached Native worker was thought untouched. That gating was wrong: any prior operator turn that was cancelled mid-tool-dispatch (CANCEL click / RSS guard / abort mid-tool-loop) leaves the cached worker with a trailing orphan `assistant(tool_use)` in `messages[]`. Push the seed prompt on top → next `messages.create` 400s with `tool_use ids were found without tool_result blocks immediately after`. Always dispose the cached Native worker for native handoff so the seed turn boots fresh from `replayMessagesFromEvents` (which skips tool_use entries entirely).
- **`pruneOrphanToolUse` is the defense-in-depth backstop, called at the top of every `runTurn`.** Same orphan-trailing-`assistant(tool_use)` shape can arise outside handoff — any subsequent operator turn following a cancel-mid-tool-loop in the same worker would have hit the 400 first. The dispatcher pops any trailing assistant message whose content contains `tool_use` blocks before appending the new user message, and emits a `ctx_action { action: 'prune_orphan_tool_use', trigger: 'recovery', detail: 'unpaired tool_use ids: ...' }` for the audit trail (silent context manipulation is a hard fence — see `docs/REFACTOR_PLAN.md` §11). Don't add mid-array pruning without an explicit reason — only the tail case is reachable from our code paths today.
- **The Native tool loop continues on Arco-owned pending WORK, not on `stop_reason`.** `runTurn` dispatches another round when the assistant response contains a client `tool_use` block Arco must execute, or when Anthropic returns the documented `stop_reason === 'pause_turn'` server-tool continuation. It deliberately does NOT gate on `stop_reason === 'tool_use'`. It also deliberately does NOT loop on a bare `server_tool_use` under `end_turn`: Anthropic server tools (`web_search` / `web_fetch`) execute inside the Messages API, and their result belongs in the same assistant turn unless the API explicitly pauses. Reason (2026-05-30/31, Opus 4.8): Opus 4.8 returns `stop_reason: 'end_turn'` for a response that can still contain an unexecuted client tool. Gating on stop_reason abandoned the client tool. But treating every server tool as pending caused the opposite failure: Native re-fed server-tool turns, searched repeatedly, and diverged from Claude Code's one-pass behavior on the same prompt.
  - **(1) `tools 0B`, dies in ~6s.** A response with an unexecuted client `tool_use` alongside a `server_tool_use` (parallel `Read` + `web_search`) came back `end_turn`; gating on stop_reason broke the loop → the client tool never dispatched (orphaned → pruned by `pruneOrphanToolUse`), the search never resolved.
  - **(2) Re-search spiral / stale-source answers.** A later workaround continued on unresolved-looking server tools under `end_turn`. That made Native over-drive web_search/web_fetch, especially when context editing had already cleared older search results. Same prompt, same model, same effort worked cleanly in the real Claude CLI; Native was the outlier. The fix is to match the documented server-tool contract: continue for client tools or `pause_turn`, otherwise let the assistant settle.
  - **(3) Parallel tool use stays enabled.** A temporary follow-up disabled parallel tool use (`tool_choice.disable_parallel_tool_use=true`) after seeing duplicate `Read` / search bursts. Re-checking current Pi showed that was not parity, and a same-prompt NVIDIA repro after the Pi request-shape fixes stayed clean with parallel tools enabled. Do not re-add a global parallel-tool ban without a fresh failing transcript; it slows legitimate independent work and diverges from Pi/Claude Code behavior.

  Regression guards: `server/src/providers/claude-native/workerParity.test.ts` pins the pure continuation decision; `scripts/smoke/web-search-tool-loop.ts` (`npm run smoke:web-search-tool-loop`, spends real quota) covers the mixed local Read + server web_search path end-to-end.
- **An orphan `server_tool_use` permanently wedges a long-lived Native worker — and `pruneOrphanToolUse` must drop it, not just client `tool_use`.** A `server_tool_use` (web_search / web_fetch) pairs with its `*_tool_result` in the SAME assistant message. If a round is aborted/interrupted after the call streams but before the result does (or a `pause_turn` continuation dies on the next round), the assistant message at line 1206 is pushed with the call and no result. Anthropic then 400s `web_search tool use ... was found without a corresponding web_search_tool_result block` on EVERY later `messages.create` — and because the worker is long-lived, it never self-heals until it replays from disk (restart / Handoff-dispose / tab reopen, all of which skip tool blocks). Two-part guard: (1) `pruneOrphanToolUse` (the top-of-turn recovery pruner) detects unpaired *server* tool ids too, via the pure `orphanToolUseIds` helper in `contentBlocks.ts` — the old version filtered `type === 'tool_use'` only and walked right past the orphan; (2) every early-return path in `runTurn` (hard stream error, mid-tool abort, iteration cap) calls `scrubServerToolEvidenceOnAbort(turnMessageStart)` so the orphan is stripped on the way out, not left in `messages[]` to also break a between-turn `countTokens` / auto-compact. The normal-completion `pruneServerToolEvidenceAfterTurn` already strips all server_tool_use regardless of pairing — the bug was only that the abort paths skipped it. Do NOT narrow `pruneOrphanToolUse` back to client tools, and do NOT drop the scrub from any new early-return you add. Regression guard: `npm run test:orphan-tooluse -w server` (`orphanToolUse.test.ts` pins the pure detector AND drives both worker recovery methods against a synthetic wedged history — the inert Native constructor makes this deterministic and zero-quota, unlike `smoke:web-search-tool-loop` which can't hit the interrupt window reliably).
- **The mirror wedge: a `*_tool_result` whose `server_tool_use` was pruned out from under it.** A `pause_turn` splits a server-tool call from its result across TWO assistant messages — the paused message ends on the bare `server_tool_use`, the continuation opens with the `*_tool_result` carrying that id (worker.ts pushes the paused message and loops, per the resend-as-is contract). Because `pruneServerToolEvidenceAfterTurn` protects only the LATEST assistant message, it deleted the call from the paused message while the protected continuation kept the result, and Anthropic 400s the exact inverse of the orphan above: *"unexpected `tool_use_id` found in `web_search_tool_result` blocks: srvtoolu_… Each `web_search_tool_result` block must have a corresponding `server_tool_use` block before it."* Same permanence — every later `messages.create` replays it (2026-09-05, hours after the latest-assistant protection landed; FOLD / COMPACT / restart heal it because all three project history to text-only). Two-part guard, mirroring the orphan one: (1) the prune collects the `tool_use_id`s the protected message's results reference and KEEPS those `server_tool_use` blocks wherever they live — everything else still goes, including a self-contained pair in the same mid-turn message; (2) `stripDanglingServerToolResults` (`retainedContext.ts`) runs at the top of every turn next to `pruneOrphanToolUse` and removes any result block with no preceding call, emitting a `ctx_action` / `trigger:'recovery'` — a no-op on healthy history, there so no future path can wedge a worker for good. Note the asymmetry that makes this legal: a call in message N answered in message N+1 is a shape Anthropic itself produces, so pairing is checked over all PRIOR blocks, not within one message. Regression guard: `npm run test:append-only -w server` (both directions, plus the ordering case where a result precedes its call). Ids are unique per call — never write a fixture that reuses one across rounds, or the pairing rule will (correctly) treat the earlier round's call as load-bearing.
- **An assistant message must never END with a `thinking` block.** Anthropic 400s with `messages.N: The final block in an assistant message cannot be 'thinking'`. With interleaved thinking Opus 4.8 routinely emits a round whose last streamed block is `thinking` (it reasons after its final tool_use, then the response ends); we push `round.contentBlocks` verbatim, so that trailing thinking is a landmine — the whole history is re-validated on every resend, so a stale earlier message (`messages.15` mid-turn, 3 min into a heavy research turn) detonates a later request. `stripTrailingThinkingBlocks()` runs in the pre-request scrub path (next to `scrubEmptyTextContent`, before every `messages.create`) and drops the contiguous trailing run of thinking from every assistant message. Mid-message thinking (preceding a tool_use / text) is KEPT with signatures for reasoning continuity — only the trailing run, which the API forbids keeping anyway, goes; a pure-thinking message becomes a placeholder text block so it never strips to empty. Emits `ctx_action { action: 'strip_trailing_thinking', trigger: 'recovery' }`, which is persisted to `events.jsonl` for the audit trail — but the client (`handleServerMessage.ts`) deliberately does NOT render it as a chat `ctx-divider` (unlike the rare `prune_orphan_tool_use` / `scrub_empty_text_blocks` recoveries). It fires on most multi-round 4.8 turns, so a divider per turn is noise, not signal; the audit record in the event log is the surface, not the conversation. Don't "fix" the missing divider by re-adding it. Pure core is `stripTrailingThinkingFromContent` (exported) with deterministic unit tests: `npm run test:thinking-strip -w server`.
- **An empty `image` block permanently wedges a long-lived Native worker — 0-byte uploads must never become image blocks.** A laptop paste once uploaded a 0-byte PNG; `/upload` accepted the empty body with a 200, the client staged it, and `buildImageBlock` read the file fine and encoded `source.data: ''`. Anthropic 400s that (`messages.N.content.0.image.source.base64: image cannot be empty`) — and because the operator message is pushed into `messages[]` BEFORE the request, every later turn failed at the same index, including retries with good images (2026-08-07, `messages.375`). Three guards, all load-bearing: `/upload` rejects empty bodies with a 400 (`http/router.ts`); `buildImageBlock` returns null on a 0-byte read so the attachment falls back to the `[attached file: …]` text ref; and `scrubEmptyTextContent` drops empty image blocks (top-level and nested in tool_result content) in the pre-request scrub pass so an already-poisoned live worker self-heals without a respawn. Replay-on-restart was never affected — it rebuilds history text-only. Don't remove any single guard on the grounds that the other two cover it: the upload check protects every consumer of `uploads/`, the builder check protects steer/spawn paths that bypass `/upload`, and the scrub is the only one that heals history that already went bad. Regression guard: `emptyImageScrub.test.ts`.
- **Capability lookups key on `capModel()`, never on a raw alias.** The `opus` / `opus[1m]` aliases are absent from `/v1/models`, so a catalog lookup keyed on the alias returns `null` and silently disables ctx-management/compact gating — the same class of miss that once gave alias tabs `max_tokens: 8192` and a dead effort knob (Opus-5 plan Phase 2). The worker resolves capabilities through `capModel()` (echoed concrete id, persisted to `meta.resolvedModelId`, restored on rehydrate only while `config.model` is still an alias, falling back to the catalog alias resolution before the first echo). Static alias entries in `modelParams.ts` / `models.ts` cover turn 1 before any echo. If you add a new capability decision to the worker, route it through `capModel()`, not `this.config.model`. Turn-1 shape pinned by the alias test in `workerParity.test.ts`.
- **The request `model:` field must be a CONCRETE id — never a family alias.** The Messages API has no alias resolution: `model: "opus"` returns `not_found_error: model: opus`. Only the Claude Code *CLI* resolves `opus` / `sonnet` / `haiku` / `opus[1m]`, client-side, before calling the API — which is why the aliases are safe on the CLI transport and fatal on Native. The Opus-5 commit's claim that "the request `model:` field stays the alias so auto-tracking survives" was wrong and left every Native alias tab dead on turn 1 (2026-07-28). Dispatch now goes through `requestModel()` → `resolveClaudeAliasFromCatalog()` (newest in-family id by `created_at`, `CLAUDE_ALIAS_FALLBACK` when the catalog is cold; `ensureStarted` warms the catalog once for alias tabs). The tab's configured model stays the alias, so auto-tracking survives where it actually belongs — in the resolution, not the wire. If you add a new `messages.create` / `countTokens` call site, use `this.requestModel()`, not `this.config.model`.
- **A controlled `<select>` whose `value` matches no `<option>` silently renders the FIRST option — and fires no `onChange`.** This is how the alias bug stayed invisible: the creator, CONFIG, and tab-context-menu model pickers all build options from the live catalog (which excludes aliases) while their value came from stored defaults, so a tab on `opus` *displayed* "OPUS 5" everywhere, and re-picking the displayed option in CONFIG was a no-op that couldn't repair it. Creator + context menu now reconcile state to the option list on change; CONFIG renders the unmatched value as an explicit `(unlisted)` option instead of hiding it. Any new picker fed by a live catalog needs one of those two treatments — never assume the stored value is in the list.
- **A refusal is a 200, not an error — and never regex-detect it.** `stop_reason: 'refusal'` arrives on a successful response, so `errorClassifier` (which only sees thrown errors) can never surface it; `runTurn` branches on the stop reason, emits a `system_notice` naming `stop_details.category`/`explanation` (both nullable — guard) and a durable `primary_refusal` event. The malformed turn needs no special pruning: the per-request `scrubEmptyTextContent` / `stripTrailingThinkingBlocks` pass repairs an empty or thinking-terminated assistant message before the next `messages.create`. Do NOT add a text/regex refusal detector — server-side model judgment on content is on the wrong side of the CLAUDE.md § Non-goals fence; `stop_reason` is the structured ground truth.
- **Reviewers / handoff seeds always run on CLI transport.** They leave `claudeTransport` unset, the dispatcher falls through to the CLI provider. Don't thread `claudeTransport` through `handleReviewRequest` / `handleHandoffRequest` / `handleSpawnFromHere` / `runExportContext` — Native sessions are Arco-internal and the handoff destination needs a `--resume`-able CLI session on disk.
- **Per-tab toggle does NOT dispose a running worker.** Flipping CONFIG-tab `cli` ↔ `native` mid-turn is a no-op against the in-flight turn. The next operator turn picks up the new transport. Mirrors Codex's Phase 6e contract.
- **Plan-mode gates `Write` / `Edit`, NOT `Bash`.** EnterPlanMode flips a per-worker boolean that short-circuits the `Write` and `Edit` dispatchers. `Bash` is intentionally not gated — detecting "write intent" in arbitrary shell commands generically (sed -i, redirection, grep -l & rm pipelines, in-place git operations) would be a regex arms race with high false-positive cost. Operators relying on plan mode as a hard write barrier should rely on the surrounding sandbox or CI contract; the per-tool gate is for the *common* footgun of the model patching files before sketching the plan, not a universal write firewall. Plan-parser advisories surface from `EnterPlanMode` as part of the tool result (warnings only, never reject — model corrects on next turn).
- **Cross-tab `TaskOutput` is rejected.** The `tasksByTaskId` map lives on the spawning parent worker. Only the parent that called `Task` can read its child's accumulated output via `TaskOutput`; a `TaskOutput` call against a `taskId` not present in the local map returns `[error] no such task`. Map entries drop on parent dispose, so a child tab promoted to standalone (rare; operator-only) becomes unaddressable from the parent's side. Don't rebuild this as a global registry — it would let any Native worker peek at any other's children, breaking the depth-1 cap's containment story.
- **`Task` is depth-1 only.** Children spawn with `claudeNativeTaskDisabled: true` in WorkerConfig; the worker checks that flag at construction and `getNativeToolDefinitions({ taskDisabled: true })` drops `Task` from the tool list sent to Anthropic. The Task tool dispatcher also early-returns `[error] Task is depth-capped at 1` if the model finds another path to invoke it. Don't loosen this — non-goals (`docs/REFACTOR_PLAN.md` §11) explicitly forbid autonomous orchestration trees, and depth-1 is the right ceiling for the operator-visible spawn-from-here UX.
- **Registry MCP sessions MUST be closed on `dispose()` — no leaked stdio children (E1-Native).** The Native worker is an MCP host: it opens a long-lived `McpSession` (`connectServer`) per operator-allowed registry server and keeps the map across operator turns, reconciled at the top of `runTurn` (`reconcileMcpSessions` — open newly-allowed, close newly-removed). `dispose()` (tab close + after `handoff_complete`, via `disposeClaudeNativeWorker`) calls `closeMcpSessions()` which force-kills every child — a leaked stdio child would otherwise outlive the worker. `cancel()` deliberately does NOT close sessions (they survive an operator CANCEL by design and are torn down only on full disposal). Don't move the close into `cancel()` thinking it's "cleanup" — that would respawn every server on the next turn. If you add a new teardown path that bypasses `dispose()`, it MUST also call `closeMcpSessions()`.
- **The MCP dangerous-tool denylist is enforced on Native at TWO points: omission (primary) + a dispatch-time guard (defense in depth).** `buildToolDefs()` → `registryToolDefs()` includes a registry tool iff it's safe (`!classifyDangerous`) OR its bare name is in the server's operator-ticked `allowedDangerousTools`. A dangerous-unticked tool is **never added to the tool defs**, so the model never sees it (no-hidden-expansion / four-question test) — this mirrors the cluster peer-tool registration gate (advertise iff allowed). But omission alone is NOT a hard boundary: a model can emit a `tool_use` for a name it was never advertised — guessing the namespace off an advertised safe sibling (`mcp__reg-x__read_file` → `mcp__reg-x__delete_file`), or replaying one from earlier context — and the server session is open, so dispatch would execute it. So `dispatchTool` ALSO calls `isRegistryToolAllowed(config.mcpRegistryServers, key, tool)` (the exact omission rule) before routing to the session, and returns `[error] … not enabled for this tab` on a dangerous-unticked name. This gives Native the same guarantee the CLI path already has (Claude Code's `--disallowedTools` rejects at its own dispatch). Keep BOTH: do not relax the omission and rely only on the dispatch reject (the model shouldn't see capabilities it can't use), and do not drop the dispatch guard trusting omission (un-advertised names bypass it). Tool names use underscores only (`mcp__<key>__<tool>`) — Anthropic's tool-name regex rejects dotted forms, same rule as peer tools.
- **H20: spawned MCP children get an ALLOWLISTED env, never the host environment.** `mcp/client.ts buildSafeEnv` passes only a small safe set (`PATH`/`HOME`/`SHELL`/`USER`/`TERM`/locale `LC_*`/`TMPDIR`/`XDG_*`) PLUS the operator-declared `def.env` (the intended secret channel) — everything else is dropped. Before H20 both spawn paths merged the full `process.env`, handing every untrusted MCP server the Claude OAuth token, a GitHub PAT, and provider keys. Don't re-add `{ ...process.env }` to an MCP spawn; `mcp/safeEnv.test.ts` asserts no host secret survives the filter.
- **DD11b: a per-MCP-server `disabledTools` list OMITS a tool outright and DISABLE WINS — even over a dangerous-tool tick.** `isRegistryToolAllowed` returns false for a disabled name BEFORE the dangerous/ticked check, and `registryToolDefs` skips disabled tools first. The operator-declared disable-list is preserved across a config update when the caller omits it (`workflowMeta.ts`). Distinct from the dangerous-tool denylist (name-token classification) — this is an explicit per-server operator off-switch that a `classifyDangerous` tick can't override.
- **DD11a: plan mode hides non-readonly MCP tools (omission) AND rejects them at dispatch.** With plan mode on, `registryToolDefs(planMode=true)` OMITS every tool not provably read-only (annotation-first, verb fallback, fail-closed) — mirroring how `Write`/`Edit` short-circuit; and `isRegistryToolReadOnly` re-checks at dispatch so a model emitting an un-advertised mutating MCP name still can't run it (the same dispatch-time guarantee the CLI gets from `--disallowedTools`). Keep BOTH the omission and the dispatch guard, exactly like the dangerous-tool denylist pair above.
- **AskUserQuestion has no timeout.** A pending question stays open until answered or the turn is cancelled. Don't add a timer — operators step away, and a timeout-driven `[error]` returned to the model would prompt it to invent answers it doesn't have. Cancel mid-question rejects the awaiting Promise with `AbortError` so the tool result reflects the cancellation cleanly. Only one open question per worker — a second `AskUserQuestion` call returns `[error] another question is awaiting response` and the model is expected to sequence them.
- **Context-management beta-header strings are NOT what the catalog keys imply.** Anthropic's `/v1/models` advertises capability keys `clear_thinking_20251015`, `clear_tool_uses_20250919`, `compact_20260112`. The actual beta-header values are `context-management-2025-06-27` (umbrella for the two clear edits) and `compact-2026-01-12` (compact). Sending `clear-thinking-2025-10-15` / `clear-tool-uses-2025-09-19` directly returns 400 invalid_request_error. Source: `https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/context-editing.md` + Phase 7c-followup probe. Don't infer a beta-header string from a catalog capability key.
- **`clear_thinking` / `clear_tool_uses` are NOT default-on in Native.** They were initially shipped as "Claude Code parity," but the Opus 4.8 NVIDIA-driver A/B test disproved that assumption: the real CLI preserved web-search evidence and answered cleanly, while Native with clear edits could surface `Search result was cleared`, repeatedly search, and draw stale conclusions. Native now leaves those clear edits off by default and relies on `compact_20260112` + Arco auto-handoff as pressure valves. If a future operator-triggered clear surface is added, remember ordering still matters: `clear_thinking_20251015` must precede `clear_tool_uses_20250919` or Anthropic returns 400.
- **Legacy server-side compact true values migrate off.** Anthropic `compact_20260112` is not the same as Arco COMPACT: it fires inside `messages.create`, can evict the working set mid-turn, and was a root cause of slow/re-read spirals. New tabs default `claudeNativeCompact` off. Older `meta.json` files with `claudeNativeCompact: true` but no `claudeNativeCompactUserSetAt` marker are treated as legacy defaults and migrated to false on rehydrate / before the next Native turn. If the operator re-enables the CONFIG toggle after that, `update_primary_config` stamps `claudeNativeCompactUserSetAt` and the opt-in sticks.
- **Context-management betas are gated against the live `/v1/models` catalog.** The Native worker calls `getCachedModelCapabilities(modelId)` per request and skips unsupported betas/body fields when the cached entry returns `null` (cold-start, fetch failure) or the model entry doesn't advertise the relevant capability key. The only context-management body Arco can send today is `compact_20260112`, and only after an explicit server-side compaction opt-in; `clear_thinking` / `clear_tool_uses` remain off even if the catalog supports them. The `[claude-native:<wfid>] → messages.create … ctx_mgmt=compact` log line shows what actually went out.
- **`max_tokens` for adaptive thinking follows model caps, not an Arco effort table.** Adaptive thinking has no explicit budget — the model picks how much to think, and `max_tokens` bounds total output (thinking + text) **in a single response**. Flat 8K silently truncated mid-thinking on heavy turns (Newgame incident, 2026-05-08), and 32K/64K were still Arco-only guesses. Current model metadata advertises **128K** output for Fable 5 / Mythos 5, Opus 4.6/4.7/4.8, and Sonnet 5, and **64K** for Sonnet 4.6, so `chooseMaxTokens()` mirrors those model-family caps. We deliberately do NOT auto-continue a `max_tokens` truncation — a response cut mid-`thinking` leaves an incomplete thinking block that isn't cleanly resumable (same family as the trailing-thinking landmine above).
- **Task children always spawn on the Native transport — never reintroduce a conditional.** The Task tool only exists on Native workers, so a Task child's parent is Native by construction. The old spawn code (`index.ts`) pinned `claudeTransport: 'native'` only when the parent tab carried an *explicit* per-tab override; tabs riding the settings cascade sent `undefined`, and provider selection (`providers/index.ts`, strict `=== 'native'`) dropped those children onto the per-turn CLI — where they failed closed in ~0.5s (`Not logged in · Please run /login` became the tool_result) and the parent silently planned around four dead delegations (operator project incident, 2026-07-01).
- **The Native system prompt carries a standing turn-discipline block — don't remove it as "prompt bloat."** The forced first block is only the one-line Claude Code identity; the real CLI ships extensive turn-completion doctrine that Native historically lacked, which is why Fable checkpointed mid-task in Arco ("Continuing now." → end_turn → idle) but not in Claude Code. `buildSystemBlocks` appends the doctrine after the env note and before operator prompts: keep working until done or blocked, never end a turn on a status update/promise, report failed Task delegations explicitly. Cache-stable, unconditional, no cache breakpoint of its own.
- **Adaptive-thinking display defaults to `omitted` — a long Fable/Opus think is invisible by design.** `buildThinkingParams` sends `thinking: {type: 'adaptive', display: 'omitted'}` (Claude Code parity), so during an adaptive-thinking stretch the SSE stream carries only `ping` events — nothing bumps `lastPrimaryEventAt` and the client's 30s stuck banner fires on a perfectly healthy worker (first seen with Fable 5 @ high, 2026-07-01: 547s of legitimate silence mid-turn). The per-tab CONFIG `THINKING VISIBILITY` picker (`claudeNativeThinkingDisplay`, `'omitted' | 'summarized'`) flips to `summarized`, streaming thinking deltas into the Activity tab (`primary_thinking` rows) which also refresh the stuck clock.
