The stream pipeline (`thinking_delta` → `emit('thinking')` → `primary_thinking`) already existed for budget-thinking models — don't "optimize" it away for adaptive ones. Primitives (reviewers / handoff seeds / DOCS / GIT) leave the field unset ⇒ omitted, mirroring outputStyle's forced `'normal'`.
- **Interleaved-thinking beta is skipped for adaptive-thinking models by default.** Current Pi comments that adaptive-thinking models have interleaved thinking built in, so it appends `interleaved-thinking-2025-05-14` only for non-adaptive Anthropic thinking paths. Arco mirrors that by default. The CONFIG tab has a Native-only debug toggle (`claudeNativeForceInterleavedThinkingBeta`) that forces the legacy beta anyway on the next turn for A/B testing if a future model starts repeating searches or missing tool-result evidence. Keep the default off unless a fresh failing transcript justifies the experiment.
- **Image attachments build proper content blocks.** `buildUserMessageContent` reads `.png/.jpg/.jpeg/.gif/.webp` files and base64-encodes them into Anthropic image content blocks; non-image attachments fold to `[attached file: <path>]` text refs (matches exec-mode codex behavior). Anthropic supports png/jpeg/gif/webp via base64; up to 5 MB per image, up to 20 images per request. **Replay-after-restart limitation:** `replayMessagesFromEvents` reconstructs from `user_message_echo` text only; past-turn images aren't re-attached on resume because attachment paths aren't persisted on the echo event. Lower-priority follow-up.
- **Tool-loop iteration cap is a runaway-loop safety net, not an expected workload boundary.** Default `MAX_TOOL_TURNS = 1000` (override via `ARCO_CLAUDE_NATIVE_MAX_TOOL_TURNS` up to 10000). CLI is unbounded; the cap exists purely to catch a stuck model burning tokens indefinitely on a multi-tab Arco. Real workloads top out in the low hundreds; if you ever see this fire on a legitimate task, the cap should go away entirely.
- **The §2.3 budget meter (`context_budget`) reads against the EFFECTIVE window, not the raw one.** `contextBudget.ts` computes `pct = usedTokens / (windowSize − reservedOutput)` — the reserved output tokens (`chooseMaxTokens`) are unusable for input, so a percent against the raw window under-reports how close the prompt is to the wall the API enforces. Pure + LLM-free (the worker passes three ground-truth numbers: `lastPromptTokens`, `KNOWN_CTX`, `chooseMaxTokens`). This meter — NOT the CTX bar's `context_usage` — is the signal the Fold/auto-fold trigger reads. Emitted alongside every `primary_complete`; transient (derived per turn, not persisted).
- **§2.1 Fold (`context_folded`) is a deterministic in-place trim — and like COMPACT it MUST re-seed from the SAME helper at the live and replay sites.** `foldClaudeNativeWorker` replaces a mid-history prefix with a deterministic `foldDigest` (NO model call — task / files / cmds / searches / errors / recent-asks / open-todos extracted from the folded prefix + the live todo list) plus a verbatim token-bounded tail, persists `context_folded { summary, keptTail }`, and continues the SAME session. Replay resets at that marker and re-seeds via `compactSummarySeed(summary, keptTail)` — the exact dual-site byte-identity rule as `native_compacted` (don't fork the wording between sites). It is a DISTINCT event from `native_compacted` so the UI/transcript can tell an instant deterministic Fold from an LLM `/compact`. Manual via the composer FOLD button (`fold_workflow`, idle + Native-gated); auto via the per-tab `autoFold` config (default OFF, set through `update_primary_config`) whose `foldAt` sits BELOW `autoHandoff.warnAt` so the cheap Fold is tried before a handoff is recommended. Anti-thrash: a fold whose `byteDelta < MIN_EFFECTIVE_FOLD_BYTES` increments `foldIneffectiveCount`; ≥2 ineffective folds (`MAX_INEFFECTIVE_FOLDS`) stop auto-fold.
- **`tool_evicted` (§2.2 aggregate spill) is DISTINCT from `tool_filtered` (Phase C per-pack trim) — don't conflate them.** `toolResultEviction.planTurnSpill` fires only when a turn's SUMMED tool-result token cost exceeds the turn budget, then replaces the largest results with a semantic 1-liner receipt (`[Bash] npm test → exit 0 · 47 lines`) largest-first until the retained sum is back under budget. Operator-armed via the `ARCO_TOOL_EVICT` env var (`toolEvictEnabled()` — `on`/`1`/`true`; a per-tab CONFIG toggle is the planned layer on top of this floor). Cache-safe: rewrites only the just-finished turn's results before they form a cache breakpoint. The cross-turn `planTailEviction` (which DOES rewrite prior turns — a deliberate cache break) runs ONLY inside the §2.1 Fold path. Both gzip the raw output to `rawArtifactPath` (fully reversible), same convention as `tool_filtered`.
- **§4.1 tool posture filters `buildToolDefs()` to a DETERMINISTIC bundle; `toolPosture.ts` is the single source of truth (clusterEfficiency.ts pattern).** `full` (default) is a no-op (byte-identical tab). `coding`/`review`/`chat` keep a curated CORE set — but tools OUTSIDE the known universe (operator-opted-in `mcp__*` / `peer_*` / `ctx_*` dynamic tools) ALWAYS pass through: a posture must never silently strip an explicit operator opt-in. Two guards: a non-empty fallback (a bundle that would empty the set falls back to `full` + logs, so a turn never bricks on zero tools) and `scrubCrossRefs` (drops "X is still available" clauses in surviving descriptions that name a now-removed tool). Session-local on `WorkflowState` (NEVER meta.json — a reload returns to `full`), but the pick IS broadcast/persisted in events.jsonl via `tool_posture_set` for review. Flipping posture re-breaks the cached `tools[]` prefix once, then re-warms. Native-only; `set_tool_posture` takes effect next turn.
- **H50 fuzzy Edit apply NEVER writes back the model's drifted `old_string` — it splices the file's ACTUAL bytes.** `editApply.ts` runs an exact-first chain (`exact` → `escape-drift` → `trailing-ws` → `indent`); each fuzzy strategy must be UNAMBIGUOUS (exactly one match) unless `replace_all`, and the splice replaces the matched region's real bytes (so a one-space / re-indent / over-escaped-`\n` drift applies cleanly instead of returning "old_string not found" and burning a turn). On total no-match it returns a "closest lines" hint so the model corrects without re-Reading. MultiEdit/batch is out of scope. Don't let a fuzzy strategy write the drifted string — that would corrupt the file with the model's mis-remembered whitespace.
- **H54 workspace facts are CACHE-STABLE — probed ONCE at worker start, with NO live dirty-status.** `workspaceFacts.ts` detects the project's real verify/build/test commands + package manager + dev runner deterministically (lockfiles + package.json scripts + Makefile targets + ecosystem markers; no model call) and folds them into a `cache_control`'d system block. Git branch is captured ONCE; live `git status` dirtiness is deliberately EXCLUDED because it flips every turn and would break the prefix cache (§ cache discipline above). `primaryVerify` also seeds Handoff/Spawn docs. Any signal you add here must stay stable across a turn or it cache-busts every request.
- **H47 sensitive-path write floor gates Write/Edit to credential / VCS / key paths on an explicit operator OK — even outside plan mode.** `isSensitiveWritePath` matches `.env` / `.env.*`, `id_rsa`/`id_ed25519`/etc., `*.pem`, and any path component `.git` / `.ssh` (a plain `.gitignore` file does NOT match — it's a file, not the `.git` dir). The gate reuses `AskUserQuestion` (no parallel approval system) and is FAIL-CLOSED — a non-Native caller / no approval channel REFUSES the write rather than allowing it. It's a floor (model-proposes / operator-one-clicks / reversible), not a tiered policy.
- **DD2 Bash output NEVER kills on the cap — it spills the COMPLETE output to a tempfile and keeps a bounded RAM tail.** `outputAccumulator.ts` decodes with a streaming `TextDecoder` (UTF-8 chars don't straddle pipe-chunk boundaries → no mojibake), keeps a bounded tail in RAM, and once total output exceeds the cap spills the full stream from byte 0 to a tempfile the model can re-read. The spill sink is INJECTED (the bash tool wires a real tempfile; tests wire an in-memory sink) so the accumulator does no I/O itself. Don't restore a kill-on-cap path — a truncated-and-killed command loses the very tail (compiler/test errors land at the end) the operator and model need.
- **DD3: Native Write/Edit serialize per real file path through a MODULE-LEVEL mutation queue — the fix for silent lost writes between cluster members on one cwd.** `file-mutation-queue.ts` keys `withFileMutationLock` by `realpath()` (symlink aliases collapse to one queue), and the queue `Map` is module-level so every Native worker in the process (all members of one cluster) serializes against each other, not just a worker against itself — distinct files still run in parallel. The enqueue critical section (async `realpath` + tail read/write) is itself serialized via `registrationTail` to close a registration race for a brand-new path. Emits `file_mutation_queued` ONLY when a write actually waited (its presence in events.jsonl IS the contention signal). NOT proper-lockfile (that's cross-process; DD3 is in-process — CLI claude edits in a child and is out of scope).
- **H62: a Write after only a PARTIAL Read of the file this turn surfaces a non-blocking warning (cluster-correctness).** A `Write` replaces the whole file; if the model Read only a slice (`offset`/`limit`) it never saw the tail, and the write silently destroys the unseen lines. The worker's `partialReadSet` (paths Read with offset/limit this turn; a later FULL Read of the same path clears it) drives a warning note on the tool result + a `tool_gate_triggered` event — NOT a gate (a wholesale rewrite is sometimes intended). `Edit` is exempt (targeted splice). Reset per turn alongside `readSet`.
- **H30: a Task child inherits the parent's `outputStyle` — thread it through `runPrimaryTurn`, NOT `derivePrimaryConfig`.** `derivePrimaryConfig` drops `outputStyle`, so the child silently ran `normal` regardless of the parent's OUTPUT STYLE pick until the child turn passed `outputStyle: parent.config.outputStyle` explicitly. The Task tool description also instructs the child to END with a verifiable self-report (files changed / verify commands + outcome / anything undone) so the parent can trust the result without re-deriving it.
- **H9 in-turn tool-loop guard (`toolLoopGuard.ts`, Native) is warnings-only by default; hard-stop is opt-in.** Two per-turn signals (reset at `primary_turn_started`): identical-repeat (same `tool`+`argsHash` N×, higher threshold — paging/polling is legitimate) and no-progress (same `tool`+`argsHash`+normalized `outputHash` — the model re-calls on a byte-identical result, lower threshold). Default `warn` emits a `tool_loop_warning` + `system_notice`; `ARCO_TOOL_LOOP_GUARD=stop` aborts the loop on first trip; `off` disables detection. Event metadata is `{toolName, argsHash}` ONLY — never raw args (secrets / large pastes). Sibling of the cross-turn §3.1 stuck breaker (see § Worker safety) — the two compose: H9 catches a tight same-turn spin in a few iterations, §3.1 catches a worker failing the same way across turns.
- **H8 aggregate `@`-ref budget gate (`contextRefs.ts`, all providers) soft-warns at ~25% and HARD-REFUSES any ref past ~50% of the window.** The per-ref 64 KB cap bounds ONE ref; H8 sums estimated tokens across every `@file:` / `@diff` / `@git` ref in a turn and, once the running aggregate would cross the hard ceiling, refuses that ref AND every ref after it — so a stack of pastes can't bury the operator's actual instruction. Conservative default window (200k) for unknown models so the gate still bites. Runs at the send path in `handleUserMessage`, not in a provider — so it's transport-agnostic.

- **The Native request prefix is FROZEN per worker and only ever extended (FABLE_51_APPEND_ONLY_PLAN.md, 2026-09-01).** Claude Fable 5.1 binds every thinking block's signature to the top-level `system`, the name-sorted `tools` set, and every earlier message; editing any of them invalidates every later block (400 on accounts created on/after 2026-08-31, `drop_block` degradation when the request opts in — Arco opts in). `resolvePrefixForRequest()` snapshots `system` + `tools` on the worker's FIRST `messages.create` and puts that snapshot on the wire forever after. `buildSystemBlocks()` / `buildToolDefs()` still run per request, but their output is DIFFED (`appendOnlyPrefix.ts`), not sent: a changed system block set becomes an appended `{role:'system'}` message (`ctx_action system_delta`), a changed tool set becomes `tool_addition` / `tool_removal` blocks (`ctx_action tool_set_change`, beta `mid-conversation-tool-changes-2026-07-01`; newly-seen tools are appended to the frozen array with `defer_loading:true`, never inserted). Models without `role:'system'` support (Sonnet 5, Opus 4.7 and older) keep the old rebuild-per-request path — they don't enforce binding. **Never "fix" a stale system prompt by rebuilding it mid-worker, and never reset the freeze on a config change** — `resetPrefixFreeze()` is only for a conversation restart (Fold, Compact, replay, seed). Description-text changes on a still-present tool (posture `scrubCrossRefs`, `webSearchMaxUses`) are ignored while frozen and logged.
- **Per-turn text goes on the USER message, never in `system` (`WorkerConfig.turnNotice`).** The peer-mailbox notice and the plan-mode arm nudge used to be `firstIteration`-gated folds into `appendSystemPrompt`, which flipped the system prompt between consecutive requests of one auto-continued turn. On Native they now ride `turnNotice` → `appendTextToLastUserMessage` (a trailing `text` block on the operator's message, kept in history for good — deleting it next turn would be the same edit). CLI / Codex still get them inside `appendSystemPrompt`. Don't add a new per-turn reminder to `buildSystemBlocks()`; add it to `turnNoticeParts` in turnEngine.
- **Thinking blocks are never deleted from history; on binding-enforcing models the end-of-turn evidence prune is skipped.** `pruneServerToolEvidenceAfterTurn` keeps `thinking` / `redacted_thinking` on every model (the pre-2026-09 shape deleted them, so no reasoning ever crossed a turn and Fable/Opus 5 started every turn cold). On `fable-5-1` / `mythos-5-1` / `opus-5-5` the whole prune is skipped after a normal turn — removing server-tool evidence from the turn's own messages would invalidate that turn's later thinking. The abort-path `scrubServerToolEvidenceOnAbort` still runs everywhere (an orphan `server_tool_use` is a guaranteed 400 — correctness beats cache). Pre-request scrubs (`scrubEmptyTextContent`, `stripTrailingThinkingBlocks`) walk only `unsentTail()` — messages past `sentThrough`, which advances after every accepted request.
- **A `role:'system'` message directly followed by a user message is a 400.** `runTurn` calls `popTrailingSystemMessages` before pushing the operator turn; `resolvePrefixForRequest` only appends when the last message is a user turn (else the delta waits a round and logs `pending`). Arco's own system messages carry a private `arco:` marker that `toWireMessages` strips — Fold / Compact / replay projections already skip non-user/assistant roles, so they never leak into a summary seed.
- **Binding controls are on for Fable 5.1 / Mythos 5.1 / Opus 5.5 (`enforcesPreservedThinking`): `thinking.block_binding.prefix_mismatch_behavior:'drop_block'` + beta `thinking-binding-controls-2026-08-01`.** Opus 5.5 (2026-09-22) ships the same preserved-thinking check as Fable 5.1; Opus 5 does NOT and 400s on `block_binding` — every gate keys on `opus-5-5` explicitly because `includes('opus-5')` matches both (pinned in `appendOnlyPrefix.test.ts`). Every `message_start` then carries `input_transformations`; the worker logs `input_transformations=N` per round and emits `ctx_action { action:'thinking_dropped_by_api', trigger:'api' }` when N>0. **Empty on every round is the proof the harness is append-only** — a `prefix_binding_mismatch` entry is a bug to chase (something before `path` changed since the previous request); `model_binding_mismatch` right after a live model swap is expected and unbilled. Two 400 recoveries in `handleApiError`: a signature/binding rejection strips every thinking block once and retries (`strip_thinking_binding_mismatch`); a rejection naming `tool_addition` / `tool_reference` / `role 'system'` / `block_binding` / `clear_at` THAWS the worker (`prefixThawed`, Arco system messages removed, rebuild-per-request for the rest of its life) and retries — worst case is the pre-plan behaviour, never a dead tab.
- **`claudeNativeThinkingDisplay:'updates'` is a third display mode (Fable 5 / 5.1 / Mythos 5.1 / Opus 5.5 only).** On Opus 5.5 the between-tool-call notes Opus 5 returned as `text` come back as thinking blocks, so under the default `omitted` an Opus 5.5 tab's Activity goes quiet during long tool loops — use `summarized` (or try `updates`). Sends `display:'updates'` + beta `thinking-display-updates-2026-08-18`; the model's between-tool-call progress notes come back as non-empty `thinking` blocks (reasoning stays hidden) and stream through the existing `primary_thinking` rows. `resolveThinkingDisplay` degrades it to `omitted` on any other model so an unknown enum value never 400s. Echo them back unchanged like any thinking block. **Observed 2026-09-01:** on a real Fable 5.1 cluster session (medium effort, ~30 fast tool rounds) and in a raw SDK probe, the API accepted `updates` but every thinking block came back signed and EMPTY — zero progress notes. The mode is wire-valid and free, but today it looks identical to `omitted`; `summarized` is the option that reliably produces Activity rows. Don't file that as an Arco bug — the stream handler forwards any non-empty `thinking_delta`, and there were none.
- **The Claude-Code wire fingerprint (`user-agent: claude-cli/<ver>`) is model-gated by Anthropic — `wireFingerprint.ts` is the single source of truth.** 2026-09-01: every Fable 5.1 Native turn failed with `Claude Code 2.1.75 does not support this model; version 2.1.251 or newer is required` while every other model kept working. Repeated at the Opus 5.5 launch (2026-09-22) → bumped to 2.1.280. The pin is deliberate (a `claude update` must not silently change what the edge sees), so a new model can make it too old without any Arco change. Bump the constant, never the three call sites (worker `messages.create`, `catalog.ts` `/v1/models`, the router's diagnostic probe) individually, and log the rejection text in the constant's history comment.

## Codex

- **Codex's reasoning-effort enum is NOT capped at `xhigh` any more — GPT-6 Astra added `max` and `ultra` above it.** Astra shipped 2026-09-03 with `supportedReasoningEfforts: [low, medium, high, xhigh, max, ultra]` (`ultra` = "Maximum reasoning with automatic task delegation"), and Codex's own catalog reports `isDefault: true` for it. Arco dropped both new levels on release day because the surface was pinned to the old ceiling in four independent places, each failing silently: (1) `client/src/utils/format.ts effortsForModel` intersected the live catalog against a hard-coded `low|medium|high|xhigh` allowlist, so the picker showed four levels while the catalog advertised six; (2) both transports' `baseMapEffort` / `baseMapEffortToReasoning` collapsed `max` → `xhigh` (correct when Codex genuinely stopped at xhigh, a silent downgrade of every MAX turn afterwards); (3) `codex-appserver/catalog.ts EFFORT_RANK` had no entry for either, so both ranked 0, sorted to the FRONT, and corrupted the `efforts[efforts.length - 1]` "highest supported effort" clamp both transports rely on; (4) `CodexReasoningEffort` and the ts-rs `ReasoningEffort` binding both stopped at `xhigh`. Invariants: **every value Codex can return must be ranked in `EFFORT_RANK`** (the clamp is positional — an unranked value silently breaks it); **`EFFORT_RANK` in `shared/src/protocol/providers.ts` is typed `Record<EffortLevel, number>` so a new level cannot be added without ranking it** — keep it total; **never widen `effortsForProvider('codex')`** (the no-catalog fallback stays `low|medium|high` — a model that really stops at `high` must not be offered more, and the per-model catalog is the only authority); and **`ultra` is Codex-only** — it is in the shared `EffortLevel` union so the picker can represent it, but no Claude/Gemini/local model advertises it, so `effortsForModel` is what keeps it off every other provider. The ts-rs binding is hand-edited (upstream's generated enum still lags); re-apply after any regen. Pinned by `client/src/utils/codexEfforts.test.ts` (`npm run test:codex-efforts`).
- **Codex context size has a conservative default and a larger catalog maximum.** As of 2026-09-11, Astra and the GPT-5.6 workers (and GPT-6 Sol / Luna from 2026-09-22) advertise `context_window:272000`, `max_context_window:872000`, and `effective_context_window_percent:95`, producing 258.4K STANDARD or 828.4K MAX. Arco defaults per-tab `codexContextPolicy` to MAX and reads these values from the selected account's live `$CODEX_HOME/models_cache.json`; never hardcode the override because model/account entitlements can change. Both exec and app-server must receive the same policy. Changing policy/model/account must change the app-server bootstrap signature so the child recycles and resumes the durable thread on the next turn. Missing metadata omits the override and fails safe to Codex's own default. The stream-reported `model_context_window` still wins in the CTX bar.
- **No concurrent-writer guard for Codex rollouts (yet).** Claude's JSONL watcher detects foreign `claude --resume <uuid>` writes and refuses turns for 60s. Codex doesn't have an equivalent. Probe (2026-04-26): every rollout file at `~/.codex/sessions/<y>/<m>/<d>/rollout-*.jsonl` carries a unique UUID in its filename — strong evidence that each `codex exec` and `codex exec resume <uuid>` invocation creates a NEW rollout file rather than appending to the existing one (the long 1k-line rollouts in the wild are from the interactive `codex` TUI, not from `exec --json`). If that holds for current Codex CLI versions, two concurrent `exec resume` calls against the same `thread_id` produce two separate rollout files and there's no interleaved-write corruption to worry about. **Caveat: this hasn't been verified by triggering the race directly.** If a future Codex CLI version flips to append-mode, port the Claude pattern from `sessionJsonl.ts` (entrypoint-detection + 60s refusal). For now, the operator-self-inflicted concurrent-resume risk is treated as acceptable and the guard is deferred.
- **Codex `cached_input_tokens` is a SUBSET of `input_tokens`, not disjoint.** OpenAI and Anthropic report cache fields with opposite semantics. For **Anthropic/Claude**, `input`, `cacheRead`, and `cacheCreate` are disjoint and sum to the total prompt — Arco's ctx for Claude is `input + cacheCreate`. For **OpenAI/Codex**, `input_tokens` IS the full prompt size and `cached_input_tokens` is informational ("of those input_tokens, this many were served from cache"). Confirmed against real rollout: `input=8402, cached=7296, total=8641` — math only checks out if cached is a subset of input. Arco's ctx for Codex is therefore just `input`. Adding cacheRead would double-count the cached portion (was the source of an inflated "89k after 3 prompts" report on 2026-04-26 before this was fixed). The client's `contextTokens(usage, provider)` branches accordingly; don't unify the formula and don't flip Codex back to a sum.
- **GPT-5.5's "400K window" is misleading — it splits 272K input + 128K output.** Codex itself reports ~258K via `model_context_window` (272K × 0.95 effective-window multiplier). Confirmed against a real rollout's `model_context_window: 258400` field and tracked in upstream Codex issues #19319, #19409, #19208. Arco now captures the stream-reported value directly: `codex-stream.ts` parses both `turn.completed` envelopes and `event_msg::token_count` payloads, forwards `model_context_window` as `result.modelContextWindow`, which `WorkerManager` puts on `worker_complete.modelContextWindow`, which `seedWorker` puts on `primary_complete.modelContextWindow`, which the client store keeps as `JobState.modelContextWindow`. The TokensFooter prefers this observed value over the hardcoded fallback (`client/src/utils/format.ts:codexModelWindow()`, default 272K). The dot indicator next to the percentage flips ● (reported) vs ○ (estimate). When OpenAI ships a Codex model with a different window, the stream value picks it up automatically — only update the hardcoded default if a fresh-spawn-pre-first-turn estimate matters. Auto-handoff for Codex and Z.ai app-server must use the post-settle rollout `last_token_usage` snapshot, not aggregated `turn.completed.usage`; Z.ai native uses the final Chat Completions `prompt_tokens` via `lastPromptTokens`. Explicit compact must clear `wf.lastContextPercent` so a pre-compact percentage cannot fire later.
- **TokenUsage carries Codex-only fields too.** `reasoningOutput` is a subset of `output` representing the model's hidden reasoning trace (high-effort GPT-5.x turns can put significant tokens here, and they roll forward into the next turn's prompt — explains why session ctx grows faster than visible chat would suggest). `total` is the provider-reported total when present. Both are captured from `turn.completed.usage.reasoning_output_tokens` / `total_tokens` and from `event_msg::token_count.info.last_token_usage` if Codex emits the TUI envelope. Don't sum them with input/output manually — they're informational subsets/totals, not additive.
- **`codex exec --json`'s `turn.completed.usage` aggregates across internal LLM calls within an operator turn — that's a billing number, not a context-window-fill number.** GPT-5.5 high is agentic: one operator turn can drive 5+ internal model calls (think → tool → think → tool → respond). The `usage` block on `turn.completed` reports `input_tokens` summed across all those internal calls. For a 60-character prompt that triggered a heavy investigation, this can land at 400k while the model's actual per-call context fill was ~80k. To recover the per-call reality, the server tails `~/.codex/sessions/<y>/<m>/<d>/rollout-*-<thread_id>.jsonl` after `turn.settled` and reads the latest `event_msg::token_count.info.last_token_usage` (per-call snapshot) and `model_context_window` (the only honest source — exec --json doesn't surface it). Broadcast as `codex_token_snapshot`; client overrides `lastTurnTokens` so the CTX bar reflects per-call reality. `totalTokens` (cumulative billing) keeps the aggregated number — that's the right metric for "you've burned X input tokens this turn" reporting. The post-settle read is awaited before `maybeAutoHandoff()` so threshold checks use the fresh percentage. Code: `server/src/workflows/transcripts.ts` + the post-settle path in `server/src/ws/turnEngine.ts`. Don't simplify back to using `turn.completed.usage` for the CTX bar.
- **Original-worker Handoff on a near-full Codex thread will fail at `exec resume`.** Arco's reflection prompt is fixed-length (a few KB), so prompt size isn't the bottleneck. The risk is the thread itself: `codex exec resume <thread_id>` loads the full prior context internally, and if that context already saturates the model's window, the resume errors out before reflection runs. Not original-worker-specific — any `exec resume` on a near-full thread has the same limit. Mitigation if it bites: fall back to `fresh-summarizer` mode (renders the transcript through a fresh Codex worker, which sees only the rendered text + summary prompt). The mode picker exposes both; operator picks. No automatic threshold-based fallback today.
- **Exit-code-0 must emit a synthetic result if the parser never settled.** Codex exiting cleanly without the parser ever firing `done`/`result` (abrupt stdout close, stream `end` racing past process exit) used to leave the worker stuck running forever. The exit handler now mirrors `claude.ts`'s done-fallback (its `process.on('exit')` handler) for code 0. Don't drop this — the parser-only path is not always reached.
- **App-server transport invariants (Phase 6).** When `codex.transport === 'appserver'`, Codex's lifecycle / cancel / steer model is fundamentally different from `exec`. Don't regress these:
  - **One long-lived `codex app-server` child per workflow tab**, not per turn. Cached in `server/src/providers/codex-appserver/index.ts:workersByWorkflow`, disposed only on `handleRemoveWorkflow`. Reviewers and handoff seed turns hard-code `'exec'` (in `handleReviewRequest` / `handleHandoffRequest` / `handleSpawnFromHere` / `runExportContext`) so the per-tab cache never picks them up — don't thread `codexTransport` through those paths.
  - **Cancel is a two-step `turn/interrupt` RPC, not SIGKILL.** Operator cancel sends `turn/interrupt`, waits up to 5 s for the `turn/completed { status: 'interrupted' }` notification, then settles. If the ack times out or the RPC errors, fall through to SIGKILL the app-server child and emit a fatal — the next operator turn re-resumes the thread on disk via `thread/resume`. Don't shortcut to SIGKILL on the happy path; the structured cancel keeps the rollout consistent.
  - **`turn/steer` requires `expectedTurnId` and races against `turn/completed`.** The steer drain hook fires on every `item/completed`. Between drain and round-trip the turn can end; Codex enforces `expectedTurnId` and the steer fails with a mismatch error. Treat that as expected and re-queue via `WorkerConfig.requeueSteer`: `activeTurnNotSteerable {turnKind:'review'|'compact'}` → re-enqueue to **steer** bucket; mismatched turn id or generic transport error → re-enqueue to **follow-up**. Don't paper over the failure or land the steer in a future turn implicitly.
  - **The toggle flips per-tab, not the running process.** `WorkflowConfig.codexTransport` switches what the *next* operator turn picks up; an already-spawned app-server child stays mounted until the tab closes, regardless of toggle direction. Don't add a "kill on toggle to exec" path — it'd break parity with the documented contract and risk a half-stopped tab. The idle child's RSS is O(100 MB) so the leak window is cheap.
  - **Refresh cached-worker config every turn.** The app-server child is long-lived, but `WorkerConfig` is per-turn. `CodexAppServerTurn.spawn()` must call `worker.updateConfig(config)` before `ensureStarted()` / `beginTurn()` so current-turn attachments become `localImage` input items, non-image attachments fold into the text tail, and live model/effort/cwd/sandbox changes don't reuse stale values from the first turn that created the child. Claude Native has the same pattern. If a current-turn image shows in `user_message_echo.attachments` but the Codex rollout has `images:[]` / `local_images:[]`, this invariant is the first thing to check.
  - **Clarification questions are separate from approvals.** The app-server worker intercepts `item/tool/requestUserInput` for explicit operator answers; approval requests keep deterministic declines. Async `agentMessage.delivery=async` question cards survive successful turn settlement and reply through normal operator messages. RPC handles close with their turn; cancel/transport death closes all handles. Reconnect snapshots must include `operatorQuestions: []` when none remain, so replayed questions cannot silently reactivate after a restart. Redact secret answers before publishing/persistence.
  - **NDJSON over stdio, no JSON-RPC envelope.** Codex's app-server emits bare `{id, method, params}` / `{id, result}` / `{method, params}` lines, not the `"jsonrpc":"2.0"` form. `jsonRpc.ts` parses both directions accordingly. If a future Codex bump starts emitting the envelope field, allow it — don't reject.
  - **`serverOverloaded` is model capacity, not provider quota.** Codex app-server used that enum for the live `gpt-5.6-sol` error “Selected model is at capacity” on 2026-08-30. Marking it throttled promoted one model's transient capacity miss into a provider-wide 30-minute cooldown, made the header claim an invented quota reset, and stopped operator-armed Goal Runs. Only `usageLimitExceeded`, an explicit HTTP 429, or a 429/Too-Many-Requests fallback message changes quota state. Keep `serverOverloaded` as the visible turn error and let the operator retry or choose another model. Regression: `npm run test:codex-quota`.
  - **Operator compact (`thread/compact/start`) must stay locked until the compact turn's own `turn/completed`.** Codex models compaction as an internal turn, but the `Turn` binding carries NO `turnKind`; `compacting` isolates it and `compactTurnId` records the id from `turn/started` without adopting it into operator `current`. `thread/compacted` means the summary landed, **not that the turn is idle**—never resolve on it. 2026-09-07 incident: Astra compacted a 86%-full ~922K thread in 158s; the old 120s timeout returned `ok:true` at 120s, Arco unlocked, and two sends during the remaining 38s bounced as `ActiveTurnNotSteerable { turn_kind: Compact }`. The waiter must be registered BEFORE `thread/compact/start` so a fast completion cannot race ahead of it. The 10-minute ceiling is a hard failure: kill/recycle the app-server worker, emit `Compact failed`, and resume the durable thread on the next turn—never claim timeout as success. `teardownProcess()` resolves waiters as `transport-ended`, also a failure. Pinned by `npm run test:codex-compact-lifecycle`.
    Manual COMPACT is a valid pressure valve, but it is intentionally lossy: Codex replaces older conversation detail with its compacted summary. Use Handoff or source artifacts when exact historical wording is load-bearing.
  - **Arco adopts ONLY the turns it starts, and clears Codex's native thread goal on every bootstrap.** Codex ships its own persistent-goal feature — `get_goal` / `create_goal` tools backed by a `goals_*.sqlite` in the CODEX_HOME. With an active goal, app-server auto-starts a fresh turn ~3 ms after every `turn/completed`, injecting `<codex_internal_context source="goal">Continue working toward the active thread goal`, and keeps doing so turn after turn. That is standing operator-absent authority over the workflow (CLAUDE.md § Non-goals, fence A) arriving through Codex's tool surface rather than Arco's, and it was invisible: the tab rendered the turn as ended while Codex kept spending. **2026-07-28 incident** — a JWLibrary tab (gpt-5.6-sol) called `create_goal` mid-turn; the operator turn settled at 11:54:04 and Codex started turn `9dc13d3c…` 3 ms later, burning 5.4M input tokens over 13 unattended minutes. Two guards, both load-bearing: (1) `turn/started` adopts an id only when `current.startedAt` is set — `beginTurn()` stamps it *before* the `turn/start` RPC, so a real operator turn always has it and a Codex-initiated turn never does. Adopting a phantom turn latched `current.turnId` with no wrapper listening, so it could never settle and every later send threw "already has a turn in flight" with no escape short of closing the tab. (2) `clearThreadGoal()` fires `thread/goal/clear` after every `thread/start` / `thread/resume` (best-effort, never fails bootstrap), and a `thread/goal/updated` notification re-clears + surfaces a `system_notice`. Arco's NORTH STAR is the operator-owned equivalent — don't let the model own one. Related: **only bootstrap failures bump the spawn-failure breaker** in `codex-appserver/index.ts` — a `beginTurn` pre-flight throw means the child is healthy, and counting those burned strikes only a tab close could clear.
  - **Consecutive `agentMessage` items need an explicit `\n\n` separator.** Codex emits a fresh `agentMessage` item for each narration block between tool calls; the `item/agentMessage/delta` deltas within one item are continuous, but Codex inserts NO separator between items. Streaming each delta straight into `appendText` therefore glued the blocks into a wall of text (`…release process.Kernel.org confirms…`). `worker.ts` tracks `current.lastAgentMsgItemId` (from the delta's `itemId`) and appends `\n\n` when the id changes (guarded on prior-text-exists so the first block gets no leading break). Don't drop the tracker — the boundary is the only place the paragraph break can be reconstructed (no delimiter survives downstream to split on). The `exec` transport (`codex-stream.ts`) has the same latent issue but emits whole items rather than deltas; if you see walls there, the fix is the same item-boundary `\n\n`.
- **Effort enum is `none|minimal|low|medium|high|xhigh`, not just `low/medium/high`.** Pre-2026-05 mappers downgraded `xhigh`/`max` → `high` thinking Codex topped out there; the actual ceiling is `xhigh` ("Extra high" in the CLI picker). Pass `xhigh` through to the appserver and `-c reasoning_effort=xhigh` to exec. Arco's `max` (an Arco-only level) collapses to Codex's `xhigh`. **Catalog-aware downgrade**: if the active model's catalog entry doesn't list the requested effort under `supportedReasoningEfforts`, mappers fall back to the highest supported effort instead of letting the appserver 400. See `docs/PROVIDERS.md § Codex § Live model catalog`.
- **Hardcoded `CODEX_MODELS` in `shared/src/protocol/models.ts` is a fallback only** (protocol types now live under `shared/src/protocol/`; `protocol.ts` is a thin re-export barrel). The picker reads from the live `/api/codex/catalog` snapshot; the static list serves only on cold-start or when the appserver isn't reachable. Don't keep the hardcoded list in sync as a primary source of truth — operator-facing model freshness comes from the catalog poll. The `CODEX_MODEL_INFO` static effort table is a tertiary fallback for `effortsForModel` (after live catalog → CODEX_MODEL_INFO → effortsForProvider).

## Cookbook download/disk guards (G4)

`server/src/cookbook/install.ts` pulls model weights and writes the llama.cpp catalog — the highest-risk Cookbook surface. The invariants, each load-bearing:

- **Exec-free pulls.** Ollama = HTTP `POST /api/pull` via `getOllamaBase()` (honours the operator's remote base URL); HF = a streamed `fetch`. There is **no `ollama pull` subprocess and no `execFile`/`child_process`/shell string anywhere in the file** — it's pure HTTP + `fs`. Don't "simplify" a pull into a subprocess; that would drop the remote-base-URL honouring and reopen a shell-injection surface.
- **Path confinement.** HF GGUF weights land **only** in `~/.arco/models/`. `sanitizeModelFilename` reduces the repo filename to a safe **basename** (rejects empty, separators `/ \`, NUL, `.` / `..` / any `..`, leading `-`, non-`.gguf`); `resolveWithinModelsDir` then resolves the join and **prefix-checks** (trailing-separator) that it stays inside the resolved models dir. A malicious repo/filename can neither escape the dir nor smuggle a flag. Never widen this to accept a path.
- **Disk pre-flight refuses BEFORE downloading.** `preflight(size, free, margin)` (free from `fs.statfs` via `freeBytesOn`; 2 GB margin) runs **before the first byte** and refuses an oversized pull, surfacing `sizeBytes`/`freeBytes`/`neededBytes`. The disk is never filled. Don't move the pre-flight after the stream starts.
- **Cancel/error deletes the partial.** The download streams to a `.part` temp and only `fs.rename`s into place on full completion. A cancel (`AbortController`) or any error deletes the `.part` — **no corrupt `.gguf`, and no catalog entry for an incomplete pull.**
- **Atomic register only after a fully successful download.** `buildCatalogEntry` (alias derived + sanitized + made **unique** vs the existing catalog, **absolute** `ggufPath`, ctx from the fit engine) is persisted via the existing **atomic** `writeCatalog` (`.tmp.<pid>` + rename, 64 KB cap) — never hand-rolled. Ollama pulls write **no** catalog entry (auto-discovered via `/api/tags`).
- **Never auto-pull.** The only caller is the WS router's `cookbook_download_request` case — an explicit operator click (or a confirmed G3 rec). No timer/hook/orchestrator pulls weights. Visible (start/progress/done/refused/cancelled persist to `events.jsonl` via the `workflowId`), reversible (delete the file + remove the entry). Clears the four-question test.

## Media ops FFmpeg wrapper (H6 audio + H7 video)

`server/src/media/ffmpeg.ts` shells out to `ffmpeg`/`ffprobe` — the fence-critical media surface. Every invariant is load-bearing; the director reads this file line by line:

- **`execFile` with an argv array ONLY.** The binary is the **fixed literal** `'ffmpeg'`/`'ffprobe'`; every argument is a separate array element. **Never** `exec`, **never** a shell string, **never** `shell: true`. There is nothing for a shell to parse. Don't "simplify" a run into a template string — that reopens shell injection.
- **Typed-spec → args, never operator text.** `buildArgs(spec, in, out)` is **PURE + total** over the `MediaOpSpec` union (audio AND video ops). The only operator-influenced argv values are validated numbers (clip/extract/fade/resample/spans) or whitelisted enums (`AUDIO_FORMATS` codec / bounded bitrate / mono|stereo / bounded sample-rate; `VIDEO_FORMATS` container+codecs / `VIDEO_VCODECS` override). No operator string is ever concatenated into a flag or path. `validateSpec` runs first (in<out, known format, …). The smoke asserts the exact argv per op.
- **Video resolution/codec/container are whitelisted/validated too (H7).** `-c:v` comes only from `VIDEO_FORMATS` defaults or the bounded `VIDEO_VCODECS` set; the container comes from a `VIDEO_FORMATS` key (→ output ext). **Resolution never flows raw into `-vf`:** a preset maps to a **fixed** `scale=-2:<h>` string we control, and an explicit `{w,h}` is validated (positive integers ≤ `VIDEO_DIM_MAX`) then composed by us into `scale=w:h`. Never the operator's `-vf`/`-c:v` text. **`runAudioOp` was renamed `runMediaOp`** — one confine/cancel/cleanup body for both; `runAudioOp`/`runVideoOp` are aliases (do not add a second ffmpeg spawn path).
- **Input AND output gated by the N2 access fence** (was `resolveWithinCwd`; see § Media access fence below). The output basename is also sanitized (`sanitizeOutputBasename`: no separators / `..` / leading-dash / NUL). An out-of-fence path is rejected (`code: 'bad-path'`).
- **Output is a NEW file; never overwrites the source.** `deriveOutputBasename` → `<stem>.<op>.<ext>`, `uniqueBasename` appends `-2/-3/…`, and there's an explicit `outputAbs === inputAbs` refusal + ffmpeg's `-n` (never overwrite). Three independent guards. The source file is read-only.
- **Cancel kills the child + cleans up the partial.** The route wires `req.on('close')` → `AbortController.abort()`; the signal `child.kill('SIGKILL')`s the ffmpeg child and `runMediaOp` **unlinks the (possibly partial) output**. No half-written file is ever returned as success. A cancel is `code: 'cancelled'` and is NOT broadcast as a failure.
- **Graceful when ffmpeg is missing.** `ffmpegAvailable()` (cached `ffmpeg -version`) gates every op; absent → `200 { code: 'ffmpeg-missing' }` with an `OPERATIONS.md` pointer, never a spawn crash / 500. (Restart the server to re-probe after installing.)
- **`/api/media/raw` is read-only.** Range-able (`206`), no write, no exec — the on-disk waveform/playback source. **Since N2 it gates `path` through the same access fence** as the op route (not bare `resolveWithinCwd`), so a scratch / unconfined path can be previewed.

## Media access fence (N2)

`server/src/media/ffmpeg.ts::resolveMediaInput` / `resolveMediaOutputDir` widen the studio from "cwd-only" to a general tool — **carefully**. The fence is the security core; don't loosen a tier or skip a gate.

- **Three tiers, in order:** (1) inside the workflow cwd → always; (2) inside Arco's `media-scratch` (`server/src/media/scratch.ts::isWithinScratch`) → always; (3) anywhere else → **only** when `mediaUnconfined !== false`. A path clearing none throws `bad-path`. The output dir is gated by the **same** `gate()`.
- **`unconfined: false` MUST reproduce the pre-N2 fence exactly** (cwd + scratch only). The positional `runMediaOp(cwd, path, spec)` form passes no `opts`, so `unconfined` defaults to `false` — this is what keeps the `audio-ops`/`video-ops` smokes' path-escape assertions (`/etc/hosts`, `../../`) green. Don't flip the default to open.
- **Default-OPEN is deliberate but tunnel-sensitive.** `mediaUnconfined` undefined ⇒ open (`!== false`); the route reads it per-op from the cascade (`readSettings(cwd)`) so a project `.arco/settings.json` can pin it. The operator switches to CONFINED on an exposed instance — keep the Settings toggle + the `bad-path` message that points at it.
- **Scratch is always allowed but bounded.** Upload writes to a **fresh per-upload dir** under a **sanitized** basename (can't escape regardless of `?name=`), with a per-upload byte cap, a 24h TTL, and a total-size cap (oldest evicted). `isWithinScratch` is a pure path-containment check — we wrote the bytes, so no symlink-following is needed. Don't gate uploads behind `mediaUnconfined` (imported-file conversion must work even when CONFINED).
- **Operator-chosen output never escapes the never-overwrite invariant.** `outputBasenameFor` keeps the stem but **forces** the op's target ext (`targetOutputExt`); `uniqueBasename` + the `outputAbs === inputAbs` refusal + ffmpeg `-n` still hold. `relPath` is returned **only** for an in-cwd output (drives REVEAL IN FILES); out-of-cwd returns the absolute `outputPath` only.

## Advanced media ops (N3)

The N3 ops (`framegrab` / `gif` / `geometry` / `speed` / `gain` / `spectrogram` / `join` / `swap` + the `inspect` endpoint) extend `server/src/media/ffmpeg.ts` — same fences, a few new load-bearing rules:

- **Multi-input ops gate EVERY input.** `join.extraInputs` and `swap.replace.audioPath` flow through `specExtraInputs(spec)` → `resolveMediaInput` (the same tiered fence) + a `stat` **before any path reaches `buildArgs`**. `buildArgs` receives only **resolved abs paths**, never the raw spec strings. Don't pass a spec string straight into the argv — an out-of-cwd extra must be rejected exactly like the primary when the fence is locked (`media-advanced-ops` asserts it). `JOIN_MAX_INPUTS` caps the fan-in.
- **Audio-vs-video is a `video` boolean on the spec, set by the PANEL — not a probe, not free text.** `speed.video` / `join.video` pick whether a video filtergraph (`setpts`, `[i:v]` concat labels, `v=1`) is built. The AUDIO panel sends `false`, the VIDEO panel `true`. This is why an audio file never gets a `-vf`/`[:v]` it has no stream for. (Edge: a *silent* video + `speed` fails on `-af` — surfaces as `ffmpeg-failed`, not a crash. Acceptable; don't "fix" it by probing in `buildArgs` — that breaks purity.)
- **`buildArgs` stays PURE + total over RENDER ops.** `inspect` produces no file, so it is **NOT** a `MediaOpSpec` — it's `inspectMedia` behind `POST /api/media/inspect`. Don't fold a "render nothing" op into the union; it would force a fake `buildArgs` case and a different result shape. `framegrab`/`gif` build their **own** argv (the `-ss`/`-t` seek goes **before** `-i`, so they don't use the shared `pre`); everything else extends `pre`.
- **Filter strings are composed only in the dedicated builders** (`atempoChain`, `geometryFilter`, `animFilter`) from **already-validated** numbers/enums. `atempo` only accepts `[0.5, 2.0]` per link → the chain covers `[0.25, 4]` in ≤2 links. A geometry preset maps to a fixed filter; a crop `{w,h,x,y}` is validated (pixel dims) then composed. Never interpolate an unvalidated value into a filter.
- **New media-producing ext is in `targetOutputExt` ONLY.** framegrab → the still ext, gif → the anim ext, spectrogram → `.png`; the rest keep the source container. The derive/uniquify/never-overwrite path is otherwise untouched.

## Demucs stem separation (N4)

`server/src/media/demucs.ts` is a **separate runner** from ffmpeg — long-running, GPU, multi-output, `spawn` not `execFile`. Don't fold it into `runMediaOp`/`MediaOpSpec`; it has its own request/result shapes and broadcast.

- **Same fence, but errors must be RE-KEYED.** It reuses `resolveMediaInput`/`resolveMediaOutputDir`, which throw `MediaOpError('bad-path')` — a *different class* than `DemucsError`. `runDemucsStems` wraps both gate calls in `gateOrThrow`, which re-throws as `DemucsError('bad-path')`. **Without this the route's `instanceof DemucsError` check misses it and returns a 500** instead of a structured `200 { code: 'bad-path' }`. If you add another shared-resolver call, route it through `gateOrThrow` too. (The smoke asserts the out-of-cwd reject.)
- **Fresh dir we own → safe whole-dir cleanup.** Output goes into a freshly `mkdir`'d uniquified `<track>.stems[-N]/`. A cancel/failure `fs.rm`s the **entire** dir — correct *only because we created it* (never point this at a pre-existing dir). demucs forces a `<model>/` subdir; `flattenAndCollect` moves the files up and removes it (read the subdir back, don't assume its name).
- **Presence check is a PATH scan, NOT a spawn.** `demucs --help` imports torch (seconds). `binaryOnPath` scans `$PATH` + `~/.local/bin` (pip --user) for an executable. Cached; restart re-probes. Don't "verify" by running demucs.
- **No timeout; cancel is the only stop.** `runDemucs` (spawn) has no timeout (a long separation is normal) and bounds the stderr tail to ~4 KB. The `AbortSignal` SIGKILLs the child; the route wires `req.on('close')`. **Device is auto** — no forced `-d` (cuda-if-available else cpu) so it stays cross-platform.
- **mp3 is the safe default; wav/flac need `torchcodec`.** Some torchaudio builds route wav/flac saves through `save_with_torchcodec`. The runner detects the `/torchcodec/i` stderr and returns a `demucs-failed` with a use-mp3/install-torchcodec hint — keep that detection if you touch the error path.
- **Visible.** Success/failure broadcasts `media_stems_result` (in the `ServerMessage` union); it lands in `events.jsonl` like `media_op_result`. A cancel is **not** broadcast as a failure.

## Strata

- Reuse the OpenAI HTTP worker, but keep Strata's identity, endpoint and discovery
  separate from LM Studio/Ollama. Discover `meta.n_ctx` and live image modalities;
  a text-only service must never get a vision badge from its model name.
- Saved Chat advertises only its armed web pair and checks the allowed names
  again at dispatch. Agent/primitive tool restrictions are enforced there too.
  Never register Strata for Incognito while its service logs/image cache persist.
- Strata owns its memory cgroup. Optional operator-registered local profiles
  may switch through their named user services on the next turn. Hold Strata's
  serial slot through the entire tool loop; never alias two weight sets to the
  same running engine. Confirm the served model before inference, preserve
  startup guards/cgroup limits, and restore the old profile on failure/cancel.
  The switch and FREE VRAM share one exclusion guard; remote/unregistered
  endpoints stay HTTP-only. Selecting a dropdown never interrupts a turn.
  Inspect VRAM and cgroup/RAM headroom before changing its image encoder; keep
  the configured expert-cache cap and desktop reserve unless the operator opts in.

## DeepSeek / Xolotl

- Keep port 8150, its registry and Xolotl `--ctx`/Engram validation separate
  from Qwen/Strata on 8081. No Ollama import, downloader or direct engine spawn.
- Start/stop only through the registered guarded launcher and existing user
  units. Never stop a miner; preserve asset/mount/RAM/VRAM guards and cgroup limits.
- Hold the whole-turn serial slot and shared local-operation guard through
  switch/cleanup/recovery. Manual controls refuse all local work; inference
  preparation excludes only its own serialized DeepSeek turns. Reject external
  in-flight/queued requests and mismatched service/model/context identities.
- Exact/Light remain selectable offline. A prepared model is not proof of
  residency; service active and engine loaded are separate. FREE VRAM stops the
  managed DeepSeek service and confirms endpoint loss. Idle reload is guarded.
- Preserve native reasoning/tool-call pairs and configured total output budgets.
  Vision comes from validated encoder assets/config, never a model-name guess.
  Text-only profiles reject images before loading; live vision must match the
  selected prepared profile. Keep the lazy CPU encoder within service limits.
  Strict Incognito, clusters and Computer selection
  remain excluded until their boundaries are explicitly implemented.
- Version checks read metadata/Git only, never load a GPU engine. Strata's
  offline BUILD.json fallback keeps its row visible while stopped. Xolotl source
  revision drift does not establish a built-binary update or semver ordering.

## Ollama

- **Cache prefix invariance.** Ollama's prefix cache hits on byte-identical message stack prefixes. Anything that mutates the system prompt or transcript bytes between turns flushes the cache: timestamps, random ids, switching `effort` mid-workflow (low → high invalidates because `/no_think` toggles), changing `OLLAMA_PRIMARY_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, or per-request `options` overrides. Plan §5.5 deliberately doesn't surface inline `num_ctx`/sampler overrides for this reason — those live in the Modelfile so they stay byte-stable across spawns.
- **Single-slot, single-model.** `OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=1` and `OLLAMA_MAX_LOADED_MODELS=1` are required for cache reliability. Concurrent workflows hitting the same daemon serialize at the daemon level. Without these, Ollama allocates separate decode slots per request and the prefix cache fragments.
- **Polite eviction default.** `keep_alive: '10m'` per request — model evicts 10 minutes after the last Arco turn, freeing VRAM for Resolve / games. `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE` env at the daemon level does NOT win over the per-request value (only matters for non-Arco clients hitting the same daemon).
- **`MAX_TOOL_TURNS = 50` default cap.** Hard limit per primary turn, tunable with `ARCO_OLLAMA_MAX_TOOL_TURNS` up to 200. A workflow that hits the cap is suspect; split the task or open an issue. Reset on the next operator turn.
- **Preparatory-only local replies are not final.** Ollama models can stream text like "Let me inspect the diff" with no tool calls; without a guard, Arco would mark the turn complete because `/api/chat` has no stronger "I am done" signal than "no tool_calls". `looksPreparatoryOnly()` allows up to two continuation prompts before accepting a no-tool reply as final. Keep this guard ahead of cluster-report enforcement so a worker gets a chance to do the work before being told to report.
- **Cluster Ollama workers must `peer_send` before finalizing.** For non-director cluster members, `OllamaWorkerProcess` tracks tool names seen this turn. If the model tries to finish without `peer_send`, Arco injects one private continuation telling it to send PASS/FAIL or DONE/BLOCKED to the assigning peer. This is intentionally bounded to one retry; do not make it unbounded, or a model that cannot call tools can trap the turn.
- **Think-tag streaming, two paths.** Native-parser models (qwen3.5/qwen3.6 with `RENDERER qwen3.5` / `PARSER qwen3.5`, qwen3-vl-thinking, deepseek3, gpt-oss) emit reasoning in `message.thinking` separate from `message.content`. `ollama.ts` reads both: `message.thinking` is forwarded straight to the `thinking` event channel; `message.content` runs through `ThinkSplitter` for legacy non-native-parser models (heretic-tools, qwopus) that still embed `<think>...</think>` in content. Native-parser models also need `think: true` in the request body — `ollama.ts` sends this as `effort !== 'low'`. Don't reintroduce a hand-rolled TEMPLATE that omits the qwen3.5 RENDERER/PARSER directives — capability detection breaks (no `thinking` advertised) and reasoning silently disappears.
- **`config.tools` is now an allowlist for Ollama.** Reviewer spawns pass `READ_ONLY_OLLAMA_TOOLS = ['Read','Grep','Glob','WebSearch','WebFetch']` to restrict the registry. Primary spawns pass `tools: undefined` to get the full kit. The Ollama reviewer also overrides `skipPermissions: true` so tools execute (SAFE mode would otherwise return denial strings, defeating the purpose of a code-reading reviewer). Allowlist is the safety net; AUTO/SAFE doesn't apply when no destructive tools are on the menu.
- **Read tool cap is 32 KB, not 256 KB.** Local model windows are smaller than Claude's; a single big Read can overflow context, and 5 parallel Reads definitely will. Models that need more from a file should use the Claude-shape `offset` (1-based start line) and `limit` (max lines) args. Don't bump the cap without a similar bump to `num_ctx` in every Modelfile.
- **Vision routing trap.** When a model has a vision projector blob (CLIP `mmproj`), Ollama routes loads through llama.cpp instead of its Go-side runner. llama.cpp's vendored snapshot is older than upstream master and may not know the model's architecture (current case: `qwen35`). The fix for Qwen3.6-27B was to strip the projector by `FROM`-ing the text-only blob directly — see `<local-llm-tools>/models/qwen3.6-27b-coder.Modelfile` for the worked example. Vision-capable workflows should pick a model whose arch IS in the vendored llama.cpp (qwen3vlmoe, llava, mllama, etc.).
- **Tool runtime is server-side.** Ollama's `/api/chat` returns `tool_calls`; Arco executes them in `OllamaWorkerProcess.dispatchToolCalls()` (see `ollamaTools.ts`). Path validation lives in the executor, not the loop. v1 allowlist is Read/Glob/Grep/Write/Bash/WebSearch/WebFetch — don't expand without a security pass on the new tool's blast radius.
- **SAFE mode = denial string, not protocol pause.** SAFE mode (operator's `skipPermissions: false`) returns a denial string as the tool result so the loop converges without a new approval-protocol surface. Operator-level cancel still works at any point. This is intentional per plan §5.4 design choice (a).
- **Stream EOF drains the trailing buffer.** `streamOneTurn`'s NDJSON loop synthesizes a `\n` and re-splits when `reader.read()` returns `done:true` with non-empty `buf`. Without this, daemons that close the stream right after the final `done:true` envelope (no trailing newline) drop token counts (`prompt_eval_count` / `eval_count`) and `total_duration`, breaking the CTX-bar denominator. Don't simplify the loop back to plain `if (done) break`.
- **Malformed tool-arg JSON surfaces an explicit `argError`, not a defaulted `{}`.** When `message.tool_calls[].function.arguments` is a JSON string that fails to parse, the call carries an `argError` and `dispatchToolCalls` short-circuits with `[error] tool "X" arguments rejected: ...`. A silent `args = {}` was firing tools against the wrong target and producing confusing tool results. The model needs to see the parse failure to retry correctly.
- **WebFetch pre-resolves DNS and re-validates resolved IPs.** `dns.lookup({ all: true })` runs before `fetch()` and every resolved IP gets re-checked through `isPrivateOrLoopbackHost`. Hostname-string matching alone is bypassable via DNS rebinding (attacker returns public IPs to our lookup, loopback IPs to fetch's lookup). There's a residual TOCTOU window when fetch re-resolves; documented in the code. Don't drop the pre-resolution.
- **`tool_choice` / `functions` make Ollama HANG — strip them (M2).** litellm's hard-won lesson: Ollama's `/api/chat` does NOT understand the OpenAI-style `tool_choice` / `functions` fields and **hangs** if they're present. `streamOneTurn` has a belt-and-suspenders `delete raw.tool_choice; delete raw.functions` before send. Arco doesn't currently set them, but the guard is there against a future regression; don't remove it, and don't add either field to the request shape.
- **`format` and `tools` are mutually exclusive on the wire (M5).** The structured-output `format` field is only sent when **no** tools are advertised (`format && !(tools?.length)` in `streamOneTurn`) — `format` constrains the sampler to JSON and suppresses `tool_calls`, so sending both yields a tool-less turn. The Review path exploits this deliberately: the tool-loop runs free (native tools, no `format`), then ONE schema-constrained synthesis turn sends `format` with tools omitted. Don't "simplify" by always attaching `format`.

### Ollama multi-backend (M4)

- **NEVER cool the only/last healthy backend (the load-bearing guard).** `reportFailure` in `ollamaResilience.ts` skips the cooldown when `healthyServingCount <= 1` and emits a `cooldown_skip_last` event instead. Cooling your sole remaining host is self-sabotage: the operator then sees "no backend available" when the host was about to recover (litellm's SINGLE_DEPLOYMENT rule). We keep using it and let the per-request error surface. The caller MUST count healthy serving backends *including* the just-failed host (the guard asks "would cooling this strand the operator?") — `ollama.ts` counts with `isBackendCooled` over the serving set. Don't "simplify" the guard away or move the count to *after* recording the failure.
- **400 (context overflow) must NOT cool the backend.** `classifyOllamaError` returns `{retryable:false, cooldown:false}` for any 4xx except 429. A 400 is the transcript's fault, not the host's — M9 (context-window fallback) owns that path. Cooling a host on a 400 would penalize a perfectly healthy daemon for an oversized prompt.
- **Cooldown TTLs are SHORT (12s remote / 6s local), not minutes.** odysseus's hard-won lesson: a single transient blip used to trip a 60s lockout and the operator thought the model died. Lazy recovery — `isBackendCooled` is a pure query; expired entries are dropped on the next `selectBackend` pass. There is **no background health-prober** (rejected as overkill for 1–3 hosts); don't add one.
- **Zero backoff is the failover speed.** `backoffMs(hasHealthyPeer=true, …) === 0`. Jittered exponential applies ONLY when down to the last/only host. If you see slow failover, check that a healthy peer is actually being detected (serves-model + not-cooled + not-attempted), not that backoff is misconfigured.
- **Connect timeout is a SECOND AbortController, cleared once headers arrive.** `streamOneTurn` wires a connect-timeout controller (default 3s) and clears the timer the moment `fetch` resolves — decode stays unbounded (a real model turn can take minutes). The operator-abort bridge stays wired through streaming so a cancel still aborts the body read. The timeout abort carries `CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MARKER`; classification keys off it to tell a dead-host timeout (retryable) from an operator cancel (bail).
- **Mid-stream drop after bytes were emitted does NOT fail over.** Failing over to a peer after partial output already streamed would double-emit a fresh response. `streamOneTurn` stamps `_emittedBytes` on the error; `streamWithFailover` treats a mid-stream-with-output error as terminal. Pre-byte connection drops do fail over.
- **Tailscale fallback is best-effort and MUST fail soft (cross-platform).** `resolveViaTailscale` shells out via `execFile` (argv-only, no shell) trying `tailscale` on PATH + `/usr/bin/tailscale` + the macOS app-bundle path, 2.5s timeout, returns null on any error. A missing/erroring Tailscale must never break normal operation. Only fires on a DNS (`ENOTFOUND`) failure, never on a loopback host. Don't make it a hard dependency or add a `which tailscale` precondition that errors when absent.
- **Back-compat: empty `backends` is the degenerate single-backend case.** `getOllamaBackends` synthesizes one backend from the legacy `baseUrl`→`OLLAMA_URL_BASE`→localhost cascade when `backends` is unset. Probe/status paths (`probeOllama`, `probeOllamaLive`, auth) still use `getOllamaBase()` (the primary backend) — multi-backend changes only the chat path. Don't fan auth/status probes across all backends without an explicit decision (it'd multiply boot-time probe cost).

### Ollama prefix-cache byte-stability (M3 / M10 / M11)

The prefix cache is the **load-bearing invariant of this whole program** — Ollama only re-prefills the changed tail, so the early region of the request (system block + tool schemas + applied `options`) MUST be byte-identical across a workflow's turns or every turn pays full prefill cost.

- **The `options` envelope is the single M3 insertion point — treat any change to it as cache invalidation (M3).** `applyOllamaParams` merges the preset blob into `options` (+ `keep_alive` at root). Don't inject anything per-turn-variable here (timestamps, random seeds you didn't pin, a `num_ctx` that drifts). Trusted-only `num_ctx`: `clampNumCtx` emits the value only when a real `/api/show` probe exists, clamped to the Modelfile `num_ctx` or — when the Modelfile sets none (stock tags) — the arch max `<arch>.context_length`; no probe ⇒ omit, never a guess. The arch max is a *ceiling only*: the CTX-bar / compaction window is `effectiveNumCtx` (applied operator value → Modelfile value → the daemon default measured from `/api/ps` after a no-`num_ctx` turn), and M9 escalation still targets Modelfile values only. The daemon default is VRAM-derived on current Ollama (32768 on the 5090), not 2048 — before 2026-09-29 stock tags silently dropped every operator `num_ctx` (SET TO FIT included), never auto-compacted, and painted a 100% CTX bar.
- **Tool subsetting ranks against the STABLE system block, not the last message (M10).** `ollamaToolSubset` computes the advertised set **once per turn** from the system-block signal so `tools[]` stays byte-identical turn-to-turn. Ranking against the volatile last user message would re-order/re-pick the schema region every turn and flush the cache. The core set (`Read/Grep/Glob/Bash` + cluster tools) is never trimmed; cap is 9 (`ARCO_OLLAMA_TOOL_CAP`), disable with `ARCO_OLLAMA_TOOL_SUBSET=0`. Don't move the ranking signal to the last message.
- **The fenced-protocol prompt is folded INTO the (stable) system block and must stay PURE (M11).** `buildFencedToolPrompt` is a pure function of the (already-subsetted, already-stable) tool list — no clock, no per-turn user text. Folding it into the system block means it's prefix-cache-stable AND counted by the M6 estimator for free (the fenced text rides every request where native schemas would). Don't make it depend on per-turn input or the cache breaks and the M6 fill estimate goes wrong.

### Ollama compaction tool-message invariants (M8)

Front-trimming the transcript to compact can corrupt the tool-pairing Ollama validates positionally — these repairs are hard-won (ported from odysseus); don't skip them.

- **Pairing is POSITIONAL — there is no `tool_call_id`.** Ollama's native message shape pairs a `role:'tool'` result to the immediately-preceding assistant `tool_calls` by adjacency. `sanitizeToolMessages` runs after any cut to drop/repair orphans: a `role:'tool'` with no preceding `tool_calls` (the classic front-cut orphan) and a dangling assistant `tool_calls` with no following result. An orphan makes the daemon 400.
- **Truncate oversized tool-call args, don't drop the message (M8).** A single tool turn can itself overflow; `truncateToolCallArgs` shrinks the `arguments` to a `{_truncated_for_context: N}` placeholder, **preserving name/type** so pairing survives. Don't delete the whole turn (orphans its result).
- **Keep system + current + last-10 verbatim; summarize only the older half.** `ARCO_OLLAMA_COMPACT_KEEP_LAST`=10. The leading system block is preserved (a naïve split that drops it breaks the prefix cache and the safety footer). Compaction **fails open** — `deterministicDigest` produces a usable recap when the M7 summary call returns `null`; never fail closed.
- **Gate at 0.85 / warn-only at 0.75 / max 1 per turn.** The Ollama worker is stateless per turn, so `maybeCompact` fires at the **front edge** of `run()` (before the tool-loop), not at the end like llama.cpp. Don't move it mid-tool-loop.

### Ollama context-window fallback — escalate, don't re-compact (M9)

- **M9 escalates the window; M8 owns the transcript.** On a detected overflow, `planCtxEscalation` re-dispatches to a larger window — order is `numctx_bump` (same backend, full probed ceiling, only when `>` the failing window) then `peer_backend` (a known-larger probed window). It does NOT re-summarize; compaction already shrank the transcript and re-running it would lose more for no window gain. Bounded to `MAX_CTX_ESCALATIONS` hops (default 2, no re-pick), with a mid-stream guard so a re-dispatch can't double-emit.
- **A 400 (context overflow) stays non-retryable AND keeps the host HOT.** This is the same `classifyOllamaError`/`contextOverflow` rule as M4 — a 400 is the transcript's fault, not the host's. M9 is the path that consumes it; cooling the backend on a 400 would penalize a healthy daemon for an oversized prompt.

### Ollama RAG injection is discipline-only (M14)

- **`ragInjection.ts` is pure primitives with NO live retriever feeding it.** A survey at ship time confirmed no scored retrieval-injection path exists today. Recall is backward-looking FTS5 archive search (never injected); `buildKnowledgeBlock` is an **unranked pinned-facts dump**, not a ranked retriever; codebase-memory is an external MCP tool. Don't mistake `buildKnowledgeBlock` for a retriever, and don't assume `fuseRRF`/`selectTopK`/`buildRagBlock` are wired into any turn — they're the shaped landing zone for a future retriever at the `turnEngine.ts` knowledge-injection site.

### Ollama utility job — timeout + thinking (`ollamaUtility.ts`)

The utility/summarizer call is `stream:false`, and two non-obvious facts make it silently return the deterministic digest (or an empty summary) if regressed:

- **`stream:false` ⇒ TTFB == total.** Ollama sends NO response bytes (not even headers) until generation finishes — verified empirically. So the ENTIRE cold-load + generation lives in the headers-arrival window. The hot path's 3s `OLLAMA_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS` is therefore **wrong for this call** — any cold load or any summary taking >3s would abort and fall back to the digest, so a remote/large utility model would *almost never actually run*. The fix: a cheap liveness probe (`GET /api/version`, the short connect budget) fast-fails a dead/hung host, then the real call gets `UTILITY_TOTAL_TIMEOUT_MS` (180s) covering cold load + full generation. Don't "restore" the short timeout on the generation call.
- **`think: false` is REQUIRED, not optional.** A thinking-capable model (qwen3.5, qwen3, …) left to its default burns the whole `num_predict` budget on `message.thinking` and returns EMPTY `message.content` — a silently empty summary that reads as "the summarizer returned nothing." `buildUtilityRequest` sets `think:false` explicitly; it's a harmless no-op on non-thinking models. Don't drop it.
- **Fail-soft still applies.** When the host is genuinely unreachable (liveness fails) the caller falls back to the deterministic digest — that's correct. The bugs above were it failing soft when it *shouldn't* have (cold but reachable).

### Ollama VRAM context-sizer (`ollamaVram.ts` `computeOllamaFit`)

**MEASURE, don't guess.** Max-num_ctx capacity depends on the daemon's KV-cache quant (`OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE` — f16/q8_0/q4_0 swing it ~4×) and flash attention, which a static arch formula can't know. Two earlier static approaches were both wrong: a param-count heuristic (off ~50% on GQA), then an exact-arch f16 calc (still 4×+ low for an operator running q4_0 KV, which is the *normal* "maximize context" setup). The only reliable signal is the model's actual VRAM.

- **`estimateMethod: 'measured-live'` is the trusted path.** When the model is loaded, `/api/ps` gives its real `size_vram` at its real `context_length`. Back out KV/token = `(size_vram − weights) / context_length` (base = on-disk weights, NOT weights+overhead — overhead falls into the slope, which keeps it conservative and, critically, never negative so a tiny q4_0 KV doesn't false-negative to 'needs-load'). Then `maxNumCtx = (totalVram − weights) / kvPerTok`, capped at `contextMax`. This reflects q4_0/q8_0/flash-attention automatically. ONLY this path drives SET TO FIT + the warning.
- **`'needs-load'`** — dense model not loaded. Don't fabricate a number (can't know the KV quant). Tell the operator to run a turn so it can be measured. No warning.
- **`'sliding-window'`** (gemma: `*.attention.sliding_window` in `model_info`) — KV is window-bounded, context isn't the VRAM limiter; report the full trained `contextMax`, never warn. (gemma also omits `head_count_kv`, so even a dense calc is impossible.)
- **`'unknown'`** — no GPU / no metadata. Informational only.
- **`kvCacheType`** is read best-effort from the local daemon (`systemctl show ollama`, Linux+systemd only) for display — the math doesn't depend on it (the live measurement already reflects it).
- **Don't reintroduce a static f16 max-context number.** It reads as authoritative and is wrong for anyone running a quantized KV cache.

## Antigravity

- **RPC-only transport** — Antigravity turns go through the local language-server RPC endpoint. Do not reintroduce composer DOM injection, chat scraping, `antigravityMode`, or `antigravityCdp`.
- **Debug port is not transport** — `ANTIGRAVITY_CDP_PORT` is still used as a cheap desktop liveness probe, but prompt/response traffic is RPC.
- **No mid-turn cancel** — `kill()` stops Arco's RPC poll loop, but the desktop app's agent work can keep running in the background.
- **No safety footer** — `WorkerManager.spawn` skips the `[Arco safety reminders...]` blob for Antigravity. The footer derails the underlying Gemini model into Arco talk instead of the intended task.
- **Profile choice matters** — the isolated user-data-dir at `~/.arco/antigravity-data/` is not signed in by default. Use `ANTIGRAVITY_USE_DEFAULT_PROFILE=1` when you want the operator's subscription-backed desktop profile.
- **Opaque context** — Arco stores the cascade id for resume, but Antigravity owns context, cache, and compaction. Don't show invented CTX percentages for this provider.

## Cursor

Cursor is an ACP provider (`cursor-agent acp`). Full detail in `docs/CURSOR_TRANSPORT_PLAN.md`; these are the load-bearing traps.

- **Cluster peer tools ride a loopback HTTP MCP, never stdio.** Cursor ACP advertises `mcpCapabilities:{http:true,sse:true}` and **no stdio** — the stdio child Claude-CLI/Codex members spawn is silently ignored (a stdio `mcpServers` entry is accepted but its tool never called). Cursor members get peer tools from an in-process loopback **StreamableHTTP** MCP server (`server/src/mcp/clusterMcpLoopback.ts`). Don't try to route Cursor through `mcpConfigWriter` / the stdio child.
- **`clusterMcpTools.ts` is the single source of truth for the tool set.** The stdio child (`clusterMcpServer.ts`) and the loopback both call `registerClusterTools(server, ctx)`. Don't fork tool names / descriptions / REST wiring between transports — edit the factory once.
- **Fresh-only membership — the exclusion is deliberate.** `mcpServers` is fixed at `session/new`, so the loopback URL can only be injected at worker bootstrap. A Cursor member must be spawned already inside the cluster (cluster creator / FRESH-spawn). A standalone Cursor tab is excluded from `AddWorkerSection`'s EXISTING candidates on purpose — joining it would produce a mute member (session opened without the MCP). **Do not "fix" this by adding cursor to the EXISTING candidates without also solving ACP session re-attach** (session/load with a populated `mcpServers`, preserving the transcript) — otherwise you ship a peer-toolless member.
- **The loopback listener is per-member, not per-cluster.** `peer_send` stamps `from: workflowId`, so each member bakes its own identity + can't share a URL. It's torn down on `dispose` (`closeClusterLoopbackMcp`); the ctx is refreshed each `beginTurn` so a mid-life task-board attach/detach lands.
- **`resolveClusterOverrideProvider` skips the mismatch check for explicit `cursor` picks.** Cursor proxies the claude-\*/gpt-\*/glm-\* model families, so a Cursor member on `claude-4-opus` is legitimate — inferring `claude` from the id and rejecting it would be wrong. Only bare `auto` + `grok-*` infer to `cursor`. The reverse (requesting `claude` with a grok id) still trips the mismatch, correctly.
- **`GET /api/usage-summary` is the ONLY endpoint carrying the included-allocation denominator — don't re-derive it.** The "Included in Pro" gauge (`CursorIncludedGauge`) needs a used/limit denominator that the other dashboard endpoints don't expose for individual accounts: `get-hard-limit` returns only `{noUsageBasedAllowed}`, the legacy `/api/usage` has `maxRequestUsage: null` on usage-based plans, and the endpoint list can't be scraped from the JS bundle (paths built dynamically; the authed page 307s through a separate web-auth). `/api/usage-summary` (GET, session cookie, no CSRF) → `individualUsage.plan.{used,limit,remaining}` cents + `{total,auto,api}PercentUsed` + billing-cycle bounds. `fetchIncludedUsage` is **non-fatal** — if it drifts, `included` is absent and the card falls back to the charged-$ line; don't make it throw.
- **The gauge bar fills from Cursor's `totalPercentUsed`, NOT `used/limit` — don't "fix" it.** Cursor weights first-party vs API usage differently, so `totalPercentUsed` (0.23 with used=44¢, limit=2000¢) is deliberately not `44/2000`. Filling the bar from `used/limit` would disagree with cursor.com's own Spending page. The dollar caption uses `used`/`limit`; only the *fill* uses the blended percent. `fmtPct` shows `<1%` for a real-but-sub-1% value rather than rounding live usage to `0%`.
- **Cursor's separate `Other Models` meter is `apiPercentUsed`, not a share of local event totals.** The R-dot hover renders it as a second bar alongside the blended included-plan gauge. Keep both percentages sourced directly from `/api/usage-summary`; dashboard events are bounded/display-oriented and cannot reconstruct either quota safely.
- **Cursor bakes reasoning effort into the model id — the worker IGNORES `config.primaryEffort`.** Catalog ids carry the effort (`grok-4.5-xhigh`, `-low`/`-high`/`-max`), so there is no separate effort surface. The picker (`CursorModelSelect`) moves family×effort×flags coordinates and then LOOKS UP the exact catalog id (`resolveCursorModelId`) — it never concatenates one; effort display parses the effort back OUT of the id (`parseCursorModelId`), never `config.primaryEffort`. The worker maps the bare id to the ACP bracket-param form (`grok-4.5[effort=high,fast=true]`, Auto = `default[]`). Don't synthesize model ids or thread `primaryEffort` into a Cursor turn.
- **Cursor COMPACT must send `/compress` as the ENTIRE ACP prompt.** Normal Cursor turns prepend north-star + cluster plan/role/doctrine before `session/prompt`; sending `/compress` through `beginTurn()` therefore bypasses Cursor's slash-command parser and wastes a model turn. `compressConversation()` is the dedicated unprefixed path, gated by the cached `available_commands_update` snapshot when one has arrived. The footer says `CTX: Cursor-managed` because Cursor ACP still emits no `usage_update` / retained prompt count; dashboard billing totals are not a context-fill signal. No signal means no Cursor threshold auto-handoff — don't manufacture a percentage from bytes or billed tokens.
- **`sandbox: 'read-only'` is the exclusive signal that routes a Cursor turn to the stateless print path — primitives MUST NOT reuse the ACP worker cache.** Reviewer / fresh-summarizer / DOCS / research / compare primitives carry the *initiating* tab's `workflowId`; the ACP worker is cached per `workflowId` (`workersByWorkflow`), so reusing it would run the primitive INSIDE the operator's live Cursor session (transcript pollution, primary model, read-write).
