The dispatcher routes `name === 'cursor' && config.sandbox === 'read-only'` to `CursorPrintProvider` (`cursor-agent -p --mode ask`, read-only Q&A) instead. Primary turns never set `sandbox: 'read-only'`; the handoff/summarizer spawn does (`exportContext.ts`, load-bearing for this routing). Task-child / spawn-from-here tabs carry their own `workflowId` and no read-only sandbox, so they keep the full ACP worker.

## Chat / UI

- **Store retention is already comprehensive — don't "fix" a memory report by re-adding caps.** Every hot array is capped on append (`memoryTrim.ts`: messages/sessionChat 500, activity 500, historyEvents 1000, workers 80, turnTokenUsage 200, plus per-field byte caps), `removeJob` drops the `JobState` + revokes attachment blob URLs on tab close, persist `partialize` strips workers, and an A1 audit of every component interval/observer/listener + the `useWebSocket` reconnect path found no leak. If a session still grows in RAM, **measure before changing code**: enable the `MemoryHud` (`?memhud=1`) and read `getRetentionStats()`. Heap up + counts flat ⇒ the leak is non-store (detached DOM, highlight cache, blob URLs, the Preview iframe) — chase that, don't lower a cap. A per-tab count climbing unbounded ⇒ a specific append path is bypassing its cap — fix that path. Invariants: `client/src/stores/retentionStats.test.ts`.
- **Auto-scroll uses imperative `el.scrollTop = el.scrollHeight - el.clientHeight`**, not `virtualizer.scrollToIndex`. Two fire paths in `ChatPanel`: a `useLayoutEffect` keyed on `[virtualizer.getTotalSize(), visibleRows.length]`, and a `ResizeObserver` on the inner height wrapper. Don't reintroduce `scrollToIndex` in the auto-scroll path — its cached-offset arithmetic is always one frame stale during streaming.
- **Voice intent re-pins the selected transcript.** `chatFollowStore` retains
  one transient targeted request across panel mounting/reuse. Voice selection,
  back and successful sends/nudges to the selected worker resume follow;
  failed sends, unrelated recipients and background output do not. A later
  manual scroll-up still unpins. Never force follow on every streamed chunk.
- **`displayMessages` must be memoized on `[job.sessionChat, job.messages]`** — a fresh array ref every render makes dep-less effects fire on every keystroke. `visibleRows` (the virtualizer input, which layers the PRIOR-SESSIONS pill on top) must also be memoized on `[displayMessages, priorSessionsExpanded]` for the same reason.
- **`ChatPanel` virtualizer passes `getItemKey`** from `visibleRows[i]?.key`. Message rows key by `message.id`; the synthetic prior-sessions pill keys by the literal string `'prior-sessions'`. Default index-keyed measurements leak stale row heights onto new rows when `session_entry` arrivals sort-insert. Don't let a new row type land in `visibleRows` without a stable `key`.
- **Virtualizer uses a per-row `estimateSize`, not a flat constant.** Rows span wildly different heights (tool-use pill ~36px, system ~44px, user 80px, prior-sessions pill 44px, `LongUserMessage` pill ~55px, assistant scaled by content length clamped 90-640px). A flat estimate causes visible scroll jitter when off-screen rows settle to real heights on scroll-up — `getTotalSize()` jumps hundreds of pixels and shifts every row's `vi.start` mid-scroll. `overscan: 16` (raised from the tanstack default) lets more off-screen rows pre-measure before entering the viewport. Don't collapse back to a constant without confirming jitter regression.
- **Jump-to-bottom button uses a 200px threshold, separate from the 40px `pinnedToBottom` slack.** The button surfaces only on meaningful scroll-away, not on row re-measure drift. `pinnedToBottom` stays as a ref (load-bearing for auto-scroll, can't re-render per scroll event); `showJumpToBottom` is separate state that flips only on threshold crossings. React's same-value update dedup keeps the scroll handler cheap even though it calls `setState` every tick.
- **Passthrough-bubble dedup has two paths, not one.** Exact-prefix match against `sessionAssistantConcat` is the fast path; when it fails (whitespace drift between streamed deltas and JSONL writes, or a `tool_use` interleave collapsing a gap in the concat), a whitespace-normalized coverage check decides whether to drop the streaming bubble or keep it pinned. Dropping when normalized `sessionChat` covers ≥90% of the streaming content loses a few in-flight tokens for one frame (until `primary_complete` flips `isStreaming: false`) but avoids rendering the whole turn twice, which is what the old pin-to-bottom fallback did. Don't simplify back to exact-prefix-only.
- **Optimistic user `timestamp` is carried onto the sessionChat echo**, not just `attachments`. The optimistic add uses client `Date.now()`; the sessionChat echo uses Claude's JSONL wall-clock (different clock, can land seconds later). Sorting by the echo's timestamp caused large pastes to visually reorder relative to streaming/system messages already above them. Same content-prefix + 5s proximity key used for the attachments merge.
- **Both render paths sort by timestamp.** The merged path (sessionChat + extras) sorts at the end. The early-return path (when `sessionChat` is empty — non-Claude providers, or fresh workflow before JSONL is populated) **also** sorts. Without this, frames that arrive out of order on the wire (long pauses between text chunks, reconnect replay) render in arrival order and the chat looks scrambled. Both branches must stay sorted.
- **The queue strip's source of truth is `job.queue`, not `job.messages.queued`.** Phase 4 introduced server-authoritative queue snapshots: the server emits `queue_update` on every mutation (enqueue / drain / cancel-one / cancel-all), and the strip in `QueuedMessagesIndicator.tsx` renders directly from `job.queue.steering` + `job.queue.followUp`. The optimistic `ChatMessage.queued` flag is still set on the originating tab so the operator sees their own send echoed instantly, but the strip's render no longer reads from there. This gives replay-on-reconnect coherence (the latest `queue_update` in events.jsonl is the definitive state) AND survives the 30 s `onRehydrateStorage` rule that prunes stale optimistic queued messages on reload.
- **Interrupt and send preserves the primary claim through provider teardown.** `kind:'interrupt'` is a priority entry in the steering bucket, excluded from `drainSteerForWorker`; its correction runs separately before older queued messages. Never implement this as client CANCEL + resend: CANCEL clears the queue and revokes the claim before asynchronous teardown. CLI interrupt waits for actual child exit; Native waits for `runTurn` and carries forward a correction cancelled before `turnInputAccepted`; Codex app-server deduplicates concurrent interrupt/STOP waiters. STOP/removal must still prevent a queued correction from starting. Interrupted work never arms or extends Goal Run. Queued picks, refs, attachments and IDs follow their own instruction. Pinned by `test:interrupt-send` + isolated `smoke:interrupt-send`.
- **Steer vs follow-up delivery depends on transport.** Claude Native and local loops drain between tool results and the next API request; Codex app-server drains on `item/completed`. Single-shot transports drain after completion. The server orders pending interrupts first, then remaining steering before follow-ups. A normal steer never aborts an API request to arrive sooner.
- **Mid-turn steer delivery boundaries (Phase 5, Ollama + llama.cpp).** The hook lives in `OllamaWorkerProcess.run()` / `LlamacppWorkerProcess.run()` between `messages.push(...toolResults)` and the next `streamOneTurn`. Server-side `drainSteerForWorker` *splices* the whole steering bucket (drain-all, not shift-one) and broadcasts `queue_update` + `queued_messages_drained`; the originator's `user_message_echo` was emitted at enqueue time in `handleUserMessage` (Phase 4 shape, unchanged) and is the authoritative transcript record — the drain hook does not re-echo. Three invariants here that future edits must not break:
  - **Splice is destructive; same-iteration re-call returns `[]`.** This is the only mechanism preventing double-deliver under llama.cpp's auto-continue path (which fires on the same iteration after the drain). If you replace `splice(0)` with a non-destructive read, you must also gate the call site against re-entry.
  - **Recovery arms in llamacpp.ts (`reasoning_runaway` / `stall` / `wall_clock` / `empty_completion`) `continue` *before* reaching the drain hook.** They inject their own synthetic user message and intentionally don't see the queue; they're conversation-repair flows, not normal-flow delivery points. If a future recovery arm wants to flush pending steers, it must call the drain hook explicitly — placing the hook above the recovery branches would cause their injected messages to interleave incorrectly.
  - **Mid-turn steer attachments fold to text references (`[attached file: <path>]`), not re-encoded binaries.** The post-turn merged drain still encodes attachments via the provider's normal `buildMessages` path, so a follow-up-with-image queued on a busy tab continues to work as before. Mid-turn image re-encoding for Ollama's `images: [...]` / llama.cpp's `image_url` parts is deferred until a real ask.
  - Steer pending while a worker is mid-API-call delivers on the *next* iteration. Arco does not abort in-flight provider calls to land a steer faster — same posture as the post-turn drain.
- **Pinned Arco skills store references, never body snapshots.** `WorkflowConfig` / `meta.json` carry only `{scope, slug}`; `turnEngine` re-runs `resolvePinnedSkills` and rebuilds the standing system block every primary turn. This is what makes SKILL.md edits immediate and lets pins survive Native Fold/compaction, Handoff, reload, and restart. Keep one-shot `pickedSkills` in the user-prompt path and pinned skills in the system-prompt path. Pin replacement is bounded (`MAX_PINNED_SKILLS` + aggregate byte cap), persisted before `pinned_skills_set`, and project pins intentionally bypass DD7 discovery suppression because clicking PIN is the operator's explicit vouch. Missing/deleted skills are skipped visibly, never silently auto-unpinned.
- **The composer's busy-Enter / Alt+Enter / Alt+↑ / Esc bindings are load-bearing.** `TerminalInput.tsx` `handleKeyDown` checks `isComposing` (IME guard — Enter inside an open candidate window must commit, not submit) and disambiguates by modifier:
  - Idle + `Enter` → send (start a fresh turn)
  - Busy + `Enter` → enqueue as steer
  - Busy + `Alt+Enter` → enqueue as follow-up
  - Busy Claude/Codex + `Ctrl/Cmd+Enter` → interrupt and send next
  - `Shift+Enter` → newline (preserved in every state)
  - `Alt+↑` → recall most recent queued message into the editor (last follow-up else last steer)
  - Busy + `Esc` → cancel turn; if queue non-empty, `window.confirm("Drop N queued messages?")` first
  - Busy + `Shift+Esc` → force-cancel without the prompt
  - Idle + `Esc` (empty composer) → cancel last queued message (legacy muscle-memory; rare since the strip's [×] is the primary path)

  Cancelling an in-flight turn drops the queue too — this matches today's behaviour but is now made explicit (the confirm prompt names the count). Don't add additional Enter behaviours without rechecking IME composition + modifier disambiguation.
- **Queued interjections are hidden from the chat log, not just tagged.** `ChatPanel.tsx` filters `m.queued === true` out of `displayMessages` in both the normal extras pipeline AND the `sessionChat.length === 0` early-return path (both filters must stay — the early-return window covers fresh workflows, post-reload replay gaps, and non-Claude providers that never emit `session_entry`). Queued entries surface only in the composer-adjacent `QueuedMessagesIndicator` (pill + preview modal) until the corresponding `session_entry` (Claude's JSONL user-turn write) arrives — at that point the `session_entry` case in `client/src/ws/handleServerMessage.ts` removes every queued optimistic whose 120-char content prefix appears as a substring anywhere in the drained prompt, and lets the sessionChat entry render as the sole visible copy. Removal-on-`session_entry`, not flip-on-echo: the server's `user_message_echo` fires at enqueue time, minutes before Claude actually processes the drained prompt, so using it to flip `queued:false` would (a) surface the message in chat during the wait, and (b) leave the later sessionChat entry to double-render once the 5s content-prefix dedup proximity window blows. The match rule is **substring, not equality**, because the server merges the entire queue into one `\n\n`-joined follow-up turn — a single `session_entry` can resolve N queued optimistic entries at once. Scanning is `queued:true`-only, so a regular (non-queued) send that happens to quote an old queued prefix can never accidentally remove one. Do NOT route queued messages through the 5s sessionChat dedup by unhiding them and relying on proximity; that window cannot span an arbitrary queue wait. See `docs/QUEUE_FIX_PLAN.md` for the original audit and the 2026-04-23 DEVLOG entries for the merge flip.
- **Queue drains as a single merged turn, not one turn per queued message.** the turn-drain loop in `server/src/ws/turnEngine.ts` `splice(0)`s both `wf.queue.steering` and `wf.queue.followUp` (steering first) after the current turn settles, joins contents with `\n\n`, flattens attachments, and runs one follow-up turn. Prior behavior drained one-per-iteration; flipped on 2026-04-23 because operators typically stack queued messages as cumulative thoughts and want one integrated reply. Don't revert without thinking through the client-side consequences — the `session_entry` substring match above depends on the merged prompt containing every queued prefix; going back to one-per-turn needs the match to revert to "equals" + oldest-tiebreak in the same commit.
- **`onRehydrateStorage` drops `queued:true` entries older than 30s.** A genuinely-just-queued optimistic transitions to visible-in-chat within seconds of the server drain (via the `session_entry` removal above), so 30s+ is definitionally stale — either a ghost from before the removal path landed, or a server-restart case where the client kept the optimistic after the in-memory `pendingInterjections` queue was lost. Dropping (not clearing the flag) is safe because sessionChat already has the real copy once Claude processed the prompt; keeping the stale optimistic around would double-render on that arrival, since the 5s content-prefix dedup can't bridge the gap.
- **`FileViewerModal` is portaled to `document.body` via `createPortal`** so `position: fixed` escapes the chat virtualizer's transformed ancestor. Don't regress anywhere the modal is used.
- **Long-session hydration is tail-first**: server splits `loadSessionJsonl` into `earlier` + `tail` (last 200), emits only `tail` as `session_entry`, stores `earlier` on `WorkflowState.pendingEarlierEntries`, sends `SessionHistoryStatusMessage { hasEarlier, earlierRemaining }`. Client renders "↑ LOAD EARLIER MESSAGES" pill; click dispatches on `earlierSource` (`'ws'` for JSONL queue, `'transcript'` for REST paging). Scroll preserved via pre-click `scrollHeight` capture + post-layout shift. `MAX_CHAT_MESSAGES` cap removed from `upsertSessionChat` (would drop oldest-of-tail first under paged inserts). events.jsonl replay on reconnect is deliberately untouched — lastSeq-gated; partial replay risks stranding streaming `primary_text` without matching `primary_complete`.
- **Chat renders Claude tool_use blocks inline** as `ToolCallPill` between assistant bubbles. `toSessionEntries` emits `tool_use` entries alongside `message` entries (discriminated `SessionEntryMessage.entry`); `ChatMessage` dispatches on `message.toolUse`. Claude-only (uses session JSONL). Inline pills for Codex would need a rollout-file watcher parallel to Claude's session-JSONL watcher.
- **Seed-bubble pills are detection-string-coupled to the server — three consumers.** `HandoffSeedPill` matches `startsWith('[HANDOFF — new session initialized for this tab.')` emitted by `handleHandoffRequest`; `ChatPanel`'s PRIOR-SESSIONS compression uses the same prefix to find handoff boundaries; `server/src/primitives/reviewContent.ts` uses it again to scope the `current-session` Review option. `SpawnSeedPill` matches suffix `\n\n---\n\nNew direction: ` or `\n\n---\n\nRead the handoff above. Acknowledge with "READY"` emitted by `handleSpawnFromHereRequest`. Change the seed string and all four sites go silently dormant — compression stops grouping, Review `current-session` degrades to `full`, pills render as regular long-user messages. Change detection + server strings together.
- **Long-user-message auto-collapse thresholds** (`ChatMessage.tsx`): 800 chars OR 12 lines. Pastes over either threshold route to `LongUserMessage` pill (expandable, same pattern as `HandoffSeedPill`). Attachment chips stay visible on the collapsed pill so operators can see what was sent. Content is always sent verbatim to the agent regardless of render state — only composer transform is `value.trim()` at `TerminalInput.handleSubmit`.
- **Prior-sessions compression collapses everything through the _most recent_ handoff seed** — one pill at the top, not nested per-handoff. Expanding re-inlines the full history with intermediate handoff seeds rendering as their own `HandoffSeedPill` pills (stacked handoffs still navigable). Message count for the label filters out `toolUse` entries so "N messages" reflects real turns, not tool-call rows.
- **Operator-message and `LongUserMessage`-expanded text is linkified via `utils/linkify.tsx`**. Operator messages render as plain text (no markdown, intentionally — operator markdown in the composer would be surprising), so the linkifier is the only path that makes URLs clickable there. Assistant messages go through remark-gfm + the `a → target="_blank"` override and don't need it. Linkifier strips trailing punctuation (`. , ; ) ]`) off the URL so "see https://example.com." links the URL and leaves the period.
- **Attachment pills open `FileViewerModal`, not a custom viewer.** `AttachmentPill` (used by `ChatMessage` + `LongUserMessage`) opens the uploaded file's absolute path via `/api/file-content`. Uploads live at `<repo>/uploads/<id>-<name>` (see `POST /upload`); the file-content endpoint resolves arbitrary absolute paths, so no extra plumbing is needed for the attachment case.
- **Client `sessionChat` is deliberately NOT cleared on `handoff_complete`** — pre-handoff content stays visible above the `[HANDOFF]` marker for continuity.
- **`ChatMessage.tsx` ReactMarkdown overrides `a → target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`** so URLs the primary emits don't navigate the Arco tab away. Other ReactMarkdown sites (modals/dialogs) deliberately unchanged until a concrete report hits them.
- **`TerminalInput.isBusy` is a three-way OR**: `(job.busy === true) || hasActiveWorkers || hasStreaming`. Any code path that ends a turn must clear all three, or the composer stays locked in interjection mode. Normal turn-end works because `primary_complete` fires `finalizeMessage` (flips the live message's `isStreaming: false`) and `primary_turn_ended` fires `endPrimaryTurn` (flips `busy: false`). The cancel path skips `primary_complete` — `endPrimaryTurn` therefore also walks `job.messages` and finalizes any `isStreaming: true` entries itself (matches `applyWorkflowSnapshot`'s behavior when it sees `busy: false`). `recordTurnCancelled` does the same defensive clear so the cancel path doesn't depend on a trailing `primary_turn_ended` to unstick the composer. If you add a new "turn ended" event, either route it through `endPrimaryTurn` or replicate the streaming-finalize step explicitly.
- **The composer diagnostic chip (`b·w·s`) is intentional, not debug noise.** Renders next to CANCEL whenever `isBusy` is true. Tells the operator (and the next agent debugging a stuck composer) which of `busy` / `activeWorkers` / `streaming` is keeping the composer in queue mode. If you remove it, you lose the one-glance diagnostic for stuck-composer reports.
- **Late `primary_text` frames must be finalized AND coalesced, not opened as fresh streams nor split one-bubble-per-delta.** The server's turn-settle path resolves on a microtask, so a `worker_text` event dispatched between the parser's `result` and that microtask broadcasts AFTER `primary_turn_ended` has already cleared `isStreaming` and `busy` on the client. The naive `if (!ref[key]) { mintFreshStreamingMessage(...) }` path created a new `isStreaming: true` message that nothing would ever finalize (`primary_complete` already fired), and the composer stayed pinned in queue mode (`b0·w0·s1`) until the next turn. The first fix (commit `435f060`) pushed each not-in-progress chunk as its own *finalized* message — which unstuck the composer but introduced a worse symptom: a late burst is rarely one chunk (the final answer can stream as dozens of deltas after `primary_turn_ended`), so the answer fragmented into a stack of one-token `< ARCO :: PASSTHROUGH` rows. That fragmented state then persisted to localStorage and reappeared verbatim on reload, while a clean seq-ordered RESUME replay (where the text sits *before* `primary_turn_ended` on disk) showed it coalesced — the exact divergence operators reported ("reload looks shattered, resume looks fine"). Current fix in `handleServerMessage.ts`'s `primary_text` handler: in the not-in-progress branch, reuse `ref[key]` as a coalesce target — first chunk mints ONE `isStreaming:false` bubble, subsequent chunks append. Same path also covers a `rehydrate_workflows` replay whose window opens mid-turn (`clientLastSeq` fell between turn start/end, so the bracketing `primary_turn_started` was never re-sent → `turnInProgress` stays false for the whole replayed burst). Because the late branch now SETS `ref[key]`, `primary_turn_started` must also null the phase refs (it didn't before) so a stale late ref can't leak into the next turn and absorb its text. Don't simplify back to per-delta messages. Regression guard: `client/src/ws/lateText.test.ts` (`npm run test:late-text -w client`). (Originating fix: commit `435f060`; coalesce fix: this branch.)
- **Help-mode CSS depends on the `help-system-ui` escape hatch class.** When `body.help-mode` is set, `styles/contextual-help.css` rules disable pointer events on `#root` and re-enable them only on `[data-help]` and `.help-system-ui` elements. The help-system-ui class is the only thing keeping `HelpButton`, `HelpBanner`, `HelpPopover`, and `DocViewer` interactive while help mode is on — rename or drop it without rewriting the CSS and the help system itself goes inert (button click does nothing, no way to exit besides Esc). The class also survives the `[data-help] *` pointer-events:none cascade because the CSS rule applies to `.help-system-ui` and all descendants. Nested native button/select help targets need a noninteractive wrapper:
  the help-mode CSS deliberately disables native controls inside `[data-help]`.
  ProvidersPopover's outside-click handler must ignore `.help-system-ui` so
  entering help leaves its version chips mounted. Originating commit:
  contextual help system landing; Strata audit follow-up 2026-10-15.
- **`DocViewer` Escape uses capture-phase to beat `UserManual`'s window-level Escape.** Both modals listen for Escape at the window level — without capture-phase ordering, hitting Esc inside DocViewer would close both the file viewer AND the manual underneath it (visible as the manual flashing closed alongside DocViewer). DocViewer's handler is registered with `{ capture: true }` and calls `stopPropagation()` so the manual's bubble-phase handler never sees the event. If you add a third stacked modal in this family, register its Escape handler with capture too and stop propagation — don't rely on z-index alone.
- **`handleCancel` broadcasts turn-end unconditionally** (`server/src/ws/workflowHandlers.ts`). Prior version only called `wf.manager.killAll()` + `wf.busy = false` and relied on the worker-kill path's synthetic-error → `settled` → `seedWorker`'s `primary_turn_ended` emission. That only fires when a worker was actually in `running`/`spawning` state. For ghost cases (already-settled primary worker, post-restart orphan state, emission-ordering bugs) the kill loop is a no-op and the UI stays stuck forever with every CANCEL click silently doing nothing. Fix is an escape hatch: `handleCancel` now also sends `turn_cancelled` (the only emitter of this message type — its client case in `client/src/ws/handleServerMessage.ts` was dead code until this fix) and `primary_turn_ended` directly. Idempotent with the worker-kill path; don't collapse back to "only kill the worker."

## Themes / shaders

- **Composer drafts live in `stores/draftStore.ts`, NOT `appStore` — this is a performance boundary.** Zustand notifies *every* subscriber of a store on every `set()`, and appStore has several hundred mounted subscriptions (some looping over clusters/members). While drafts lived there, each keystroke paid the whole fan-out: measured 2026-08-06 on the EliteBook, same page / same instant, composer **37.2ms** median per keystroke vs the NORTH STAR field (plain local state) **4.2ms**. Long Animation Frames put `styleAndLayout` at 0ms on every long frame, i.e. none of it was CSS/layout — it was 100% script in the `oninput` listener. It also explains why cluster tabs typed slower than single-worker tabs: more subscribers mounted, identical keystroke. `setDraft` / `clearDraft` remain on appStore as **delegating wrappers** so ReviewTab, the terminal→composer bridge and the seed-prompt handler are unchanged. Don't move drafts back, and don't "simplify" the wrappers away.
- **`TerminalInput` must not subscribe to the draft.** The textarea and the `@`-ref chips are separate memoised components (`ComposerTextarea`, `ContextRefChips`) that own the subscription; TerminalInput's handlers read it non-reactively via `readDraft()`. Reintroducing a `useDraftStore(s => s.drafts[...])` in TerminalInput drags ~2,500 lines (toolbar, chips, status, nine dialogs) back into every keystroke.
- **`ChatMessage` is memoised and its props must stay referentially stable.** ChatPanel virtualises with `overscan: 16`, so ~30 rows are mounted, each rendering react-markdown + rehype-highlight. Without memo, any ChatPanel re-render re-rendered all of them — and in a live cluster ChatPanel re-renders constantly (several components subscribe to whole `s.jobs` / `s.clusters` maps, which change identity on every worker event). The memo only works because `writtenFiles` / `onMakeTask` are `useMemo`'d in ChatPanel and `message` is a store object. Add an unstable prop and it silently stops helping.
- **No `[style*="..."]` attribute-substring selectors in theme CSS.** An attribute-substring match on `style` is re-matched whenever any element's inline style attribute changes, and the composer's auto-resize writes `style.height` on every keystroke. One was added and removed the same day (2026-08-06) for the Arch theme's pulse override; the class-based sites carry it instead.
- **The decorative shader and xterm.js are code-split behind lazy gates — don't import them statically.** `ThemedBackgroundShaderImpl.tsx` (three.js 636KB + 120KB of `?raw` GLSL) is reached only via `ThemedBackgroundShader.tsx`, which checks `shaderPreset` *before* touching the heavy module, so `shaderPreset: 'none'` themes never fetch the chunk. `XtermView.tsx` (~290KB) is reached only via `XtermViewLazy.tsx`. A static import from anywhere collapses the split and puts ~866KB back in the main chunk (4,367 → 3,501KB when it was done).
- **A new theme needs three coordinated edits — the `VALID_THEME_IDS` allowlist is the always-forgotten one.** A palette theme needs (1) its definition file under `client/src/themes/definitions/`, (2) registration in `client/src/themes/registry.ts` (`THEME_REGISTRY`), and (3) its id added to `VALID_THEME_IDS` in `shared/src/protocol/themes.ts`. Miss (3) and the theme silently reverts to the default on select (the id fails the shared allowlist gate) with no error — the hardest of the three to diagnose because the def + registry look complete. `provider-auto` is a sentinel in the list, not a palette; provider tints (e.g. `PROVIDER_THEMES.cursor`) resolve under it and are NOT separate `VALID_THEME_IDS` entries.
- **Decorative shader rAF loop is throttled and visibility-paused.** `ThemedBackgroundShader` routes its per-frame work through `client/src/utils/rafThrottle.ts`, which caps the loop and stops calling rAF entirely while `document.hidden`. Don't reintroduce a raw `requestAnimationFrame(tick)` loop. Reason: Firefox 150 on Wayland drives rAF at the monitor's true refresh — on a 144Hz panel an uncapped loop runs shaders at 144fps and a 5090 idles around 60% utilization for nothing. The cap is intentionally lower than 60fps for most presets (see next bullet) because the *Wayland compositor surface upload*, not the shader work, is the dominant cost.
- **Per-preset fps cap, not a global one.** `LOW_FPS_PRESETS` in `ThemedBackgroundShader.tsx` runs discrete/noisy/slow-atmospheric presets (matrix-rain, amber-crt, blueprint-grid, film-grain, star-drift, aurora, nebula, sentinel, cicada) at 24fps; smooth fast-motion presets (liquid-glass, watercolor) stay at 30. The eye picks up stutter on continuous fast motion but not on cell-quantized or slow content. Theme switches restart the rAF with the right fps. Don't unify back to a single fps without measuring — 24 vs 30 is a real ~20% compositor-upload reduction on the matched presets.
- **The renderer is `alpha: false` (opaque framebuffer).** `ThemedBackgroundShader` creates its WebGLRenderer with `alpha: false` and clears to `0x000000`. On Firefox/Wayland/NVIDIA an alpha-channel canvas forces a per-pixel alpha-blend compositor pass against the page below; an opaque canvas can use a direct-scanout path. Visually identical because the body bg matches the clear color (both come from `--color-bg`), and the CSS `opacity: 0.32` on the canvas container still applies for shader dimming. Don't set `alpha: true` back without a concrete need.
- **The bloom-overlay companion canvas was removed (2026-04-28).** It used to run a second WebGL pipeline through CSS `blur(32px)` + `mix-blend-mode: screen` to bleed phosphor haze over the UI. The screen-blend forces a fullscreen compositor read-back per frame; that was the dominant remaining cost on Firefox/Wayland/NVIDIA. The visual contribution was haze rather than identity — per-element glow on text/borders/LEDs comes from CSS `text-shadow` / `box-shadow`, not bloom. Don't reintroduce a global bloom canvas; if you need a halo on a specific element use CSS shadows.
- **Firefox/Wayland/NVIDIA has a continuous-WebGL P-state floor.** Once any continuous WebGL render loop is alive the GPU jumps to a higher power state and stays there. nvidia-smi reads this as a high "% GPU" regardless of whether the shader cores are saturated. Solar (`shaderPreset: 'none'`) idles to 0% / ~60W; any theme with a shader floors at ~70W. This is environmental (Mozilla bug 1750457 — WebRender Compositor Wayland) and is *not* fixable by halving fps, dropping DPR, or removing CSS effects below the floor. Symptom of the issue, not a bug in the code.
- **Background-shader rule: no smooth radial gradients on dark themes.** 8-bit alpha compositing produces visible posterization rings on low-amplitude radial vignettes / noise washes / breathing blobs against near-black. Even with per-pixel ±1/255 dithering the bands persist because the gradient lives across only a few alpha bytes. Strip such effects entirely; rely on structural elements (particles, streaks, grids, dots, vein lines, scanlines, hard-edged cells) to carry the theme. Originating pass: 2026-04-27 sweep across Sentinel, Cherenkov, Sonar, Tarkovsky, Bloodmoon, DSKY, Oscilloscope, Cassette Futurism, Noir, Aurora, Nebula, Midnight Tokyo, Abyssal, Cicada, Kintsugi, Night City, Stealth, Concrete, Voyager, Arrakis, Amber CRT, Infrared. Light-mood themes (Solar Paper, Frutiger, Blueprint, Dark Academia, Braun, Holographic, Seismograph) untouched — banding doesn't show against light backgrounds.
- **Star-twinkle / shimmer was operator-rejected on Aurora & Nebula.** Stars there now drift on per-star hashed velocity vectors at fixed brightness, wrapping the viewport. If you re-add brightness modulation, gate it behind a setting; default off.
- **`.arco-led` base style has NO `filter: drop-shadow`.** Filters force every LED into its own GPU compositor layer regardless of state, so the previous base-class drop-shadow was paying layer-promotion cost for every nominal/idle LED forever. The 3-layer `box-shadow` provides the halo; the visual diff is subtle on small round LEDs. Warning/critical keyframes reapply `filter: drop-shadow` for the duration of their animation only — that's where it pulls visual weight. Don't put `filter:` back on the base.
- **Nominal LEDs are static (no breathing animation).** `.led-breathe-nominal { animation: none }` — only warning and critical breathe. Most LEDs in Arco's UI are nominal most of the time; animating them was constant compositor work (filter + box-shadow interpolation per LED) for no signal. Don't reintroduce a "calm pulse" — a steadily-glowing dot reads as "on" without animation, and reserving motion for the meaningful states (warning / critical) makes those states more legible.
- **`body::before` tension vignette is gated on `body.tension-active`.** `useTension` toggles the class when the scalar crosses 0.05; CSS only creates the pseudo-element under the active class. Earlier the element was unconditional with `opacity: calc(var(--tension) * 0.18)` — a fixed full-screen layer at z:9998 sits in the compositor tree even at opacity:0, costing per-frame compositor work when nothing's wrong. Don't drop the gate.

## Cross-platform

- **Arco runs on Linux (primary dev machine) and macOS (secondary).** Any server-side shell-out or spawn needs to work on both. GNU-only flags, Linux-only binaries, and `/proc/` reads without a fallback are the usual traps. Concrete rules:
  - **No `setsid` binary.** Not installed by default on macOS. Node's `spawn(..., { detached: true })` already calls `setsid(2)` on POSIX and gives you the same new-session / no-controlling-terminal behavior for free. `nohup` is also redundant once `stdio: 'ignore'` + detach is in place. Originating incident: commit `54aad6c` (Mac agent fixed a restart path that `spawn('setsid', ...)`'d and silently `ENOENT`'d on Darwin).
  - **Shell scripts target bash 3.2+.** macOS ships bash 3.2 by default. `set -uo pipefail`, POSIX traps, `||` short-circuits are all fine. Bash-4 features like associative arrays (`declare -A`), `[[ =~ ]]` with `BASH_REMATCH` quirks, `readarray`, or `${var,,}` lowercase-expansion do not round-trip.
  - **`/proc/` reads need a platform check or a fallback.** `processActivity.ts` is Linux-only by design (gated); copy that pattern for anything else that peeks at `/proc`.
  - **`pkill -f` works on both platforms** but beware: macOS's `pkill` is BSD, which matches against `argv[0]` truncated at 16 chars by default for `-f` purposes; Linux matches full cmdline. If you're doing pattern-based process targeting, test on both or switch to PID-based targeting.

## Persistence / workflow lifecycle

- **Every JSON/text store writes through `server/src/atomicFile.ts`** (`atomicWriteFile`, or `atomicWriteFileSync` for the two PID registries that write from the shutdown path). Do NOT hand-roll `tmp + rename` in a new store — there used to be ~20 such sites and 11 byte-identical private `atomicWrite` helpers, all of them missing the fsync and the failure-path cleanup. The temp MUST stay in the target's own directory: `rename` is only atomic within one filesystem, so a temp in `os.tmpdir()` silently degrades to a copy across mounts.
- **The fsync before the rename is the load-bearing part.** `rename` orders the directory entry, not the bytes — without the flush a crash can leave a **zero-length file where a good one used to be**. Pass `durability: 'advisory'` ONLY for best-effort telemetry that already swallows its own write failures (`recent-files.jsonl`, `skill-usage.json`). Never for `meta.json`, `trust.json`, `settings.json`, `known-cwds.json`, or the PID registries. The parent directory is deliberately NOT fsynced: losing the rename leaves the previous good file, which is safe; a truncated file is not.
- **An atomic write does NOT make a read-modify-write safe.** Unique temp names stop two writers colliding on the rename; they do nothing about two readers loading the same old state and the second write dropping the first one's mutation. Any store that reads-then-writes must wrap the whole critical section in `withFileLock(key, fn)` — currently `patchSettings()`, `writeTrustDecision()` / `removeTrustDecision()`, `rememberCwd()`, `recordSkillRead()`, `updateMeta()` (every `workflowMeta.ts` RMW helper), cluster meta setters, the cluster plan bridge, `planStore.ts` index mutators, `session-index.json`, and task-ledger comment appends. The lock is not re-entrant — a locked export called from inside another's critical section on the same key deadlocks; use the `*Unlocked` twin. Adding a field to one of those is fine; moving the read outside the lock re-opens the bug.
- **`events.jsonl` is append-only and must never be written through `atomicFile.ts`.** It goes through `workflowStore.ts`'s append path; rewriting it whole would break seq continuity and lossless replay.
- **Cluster mailbox writes MUST stay atomic (`clusterMailbox.ts`).** Not for crash-durability — mailbox files really are ephemeral — but because `drain` runs concurrently with `peer_send` (two members, same process, independent async tool calls). `readMessageFile` collapses ANY parse error to `null`, and `drain` treats `null` as "stale" and **unlinks the file**. So a reader that observes a half-written message destroys it outright, with no `peer_message_received` in the recipient's log — silent cross-member message loss, the same class DD3's file-mutation queue closed for edits. The safety comes from the temp being renamed in under a `.tmp` name while every reader filters `.endsWith('.json')`. Do NOT "simplify" these back to `fs.writeFile`; `npm run test:atomic-file` pins the property.
- **The snapshot manifests in `clusterSnapshots.ts` are the real exception** — written inside a hidden staging directory that is itself atomically swapped, so a plain `writeFile` there is correct and converting them buys nothing. Same for the `tempDir` paths in that file: those are directory swaps, not file replacements, and `atomicFile.ts` does not apply.
- **Any new workflow-creation path MUST `await initWorkflowStore(cwd, wfid)` before first broadcast** — otherwise `seq` restarts from 0 and events.jsonl replay corrupts on next reconnect.
- **Calibration `{ratio, overhead, lastGroundTruth}` is persisted to `meta.json`**; `lastContextPercent` and `zonesCalibrated` are in-memory only. Both the `handleRehydrateWorkflows` and `handleCreateWorkflow` resume paths read calibration back into `wf.calibration`. `zonesCalibrated` is deliberately reset on restart so the next zone crossing re-samples against whatever the session has grown into; every successful calibration (auto or manual) calls `writeCalibrationToMeta` to update disk.
- **Two `cwd → ~/.claude/projects/<hash>` encoders must agree**: `resolveSessionJsonlPath` (`server/src/sessionJsonl.ts`) and `encodeProjectDir` (`server/src/http/fileRead.ts`, imported by `workflowDataRoutes.ts` / `purgeWorkflow.ts` / `sessionDiscovery.ts` / `recall/index.ts`). Claude replaces any non-`[A-Za-z0-9-]` char with `-` (so `Sample_Project` → `Sample-Project`). Earlier versions of one or the other only replaced `/`, which silently broke context-fill + session watching + the Resume picker for any project path with `_`, `.`, or whitespace. If you touch one, check the other.
- **Snapshot-only fallback on rotation** (`workflowStore.ts`): when `clientLastSeq + 1 < firstSeq`, events have rotated/fallen off disk and partial replay isn't possible. Server sends `workflow_state_snapshot` with `snapshotOnly: true` and `snapshotReason: 'events-rotated'`; client force-sets its `lastSeq` to the server's head, accepting that some history is lost. Don't try to "be helpful" by falling back to partial replay — it strands streaming events without their completes.
- **Worker PID registry persists** to `~/.arco/worker-pids.json` and is read on every boot's `reapOrphanWorkers()`. **H44 — each entry now pairs the pid with its process START TIME (`{ procs: [{ pid, startTime }] }`); `reapProc` (`workers/pidRegistry.ts`) only SIGKILLs when the live process's start time matches the recorded one** (a recycled pid that now belongs to an unrelated process is left alone → `reused`; a missing pid is `gone`). Unknown start-time on either side fails toward kill (preserving the old behaviour), and a legacy `{ pids: [...] }` file still reads. The same start-time-verified reaping applies to the operator-terminal pid registry (`reapOrphanTerminals`). Don't revert to "SIGKILL anything still listed" — a boot can land on a reused pid. Don't disable or skip the reap — orphan workers from a server crash eat CPU until the kernel reaps them, and they hold detached file descriptors on their session JSONLs.

## Server-authoritative workspace (mirrored tabs)

- **The open-tab set is server state** (`server/src/workspace.ts` → `~/.arco/workspace.json`; `workspace-<port>.json` on any port but 3001 so a smoke server sharing `~/.arco` can't clobber prod's). Every browser reconciles to it; don't reintroduce a client-only open/close path, and don't send `rehydrate_workflows` before the `workspace_snapshot` reconcile — rehydrating unknown ids recreates them server-side, which is exactly how one browser used to resurrect tabs another closed.
- **The open hook is `send()`'s `workflow_created` branch** (after fan-out, so the creator sees `workflow_created` before `workspace_changed`). Every creation path — create / resume / spawn / handoff / bake-off / genesis — already announces there; a new path that bypasses `send()` (raw `ws.send`) won't open a tab. Replays and `handleClusterMemberRehydrate` use raw `ws.send` deliberately so they never re-open.
- **Close = `handleRemoveWorkflow`, before its registry guard** (a tab can be open while unregistered after a restart). Cluster close rides `broadcastCluster(cluster_deleted)`; cluster open is `handleClusterSubscribe` success.
- **Former members inherit tabs only if the cluster was open** (`handleClusterDelete` / collapse check `isClusterOpen` first). Orphan clusters' members are boot-registered too; deleting one from CLOSED CLUSTERS must not pop its members into every browser.
- **Live reconcile removes only ids that were in the previous snapshot.** A member between its `workflow_created` and its cluster's `cluster_created` is briefly in neither list; an initial-style "drop everything absent" on `workspace_changed` would close it.
- **Incognito never enters the workspace** (`trackable()` + client filter). It stays per-browser — the memory-only boundary.
- **A never-seen tab replays tail-first, sequentially** (2026-10-14 OOM: a fresh MacBook full-replayed 73 mirrored tabs from seq 0 in parallel — three 103 MB task-child logs — and killed the shared server under the workstation's browser). Any `rehydrate_workflows` entry with `tail: true` or `lastSeq: 0`, and any gap over 5000 events, goes through `replayTailEvents` (last 3 MB, starting at a `user_message_echo` boundary, one `replay_batch`, a per-browser `[MIRRORED]` notice). `handleRehydrateWorkflows` awaits each entry in the client's order (focused tab first) and replays ONLY the message's entries — it used to re-replay every session id from 0 on each call. The per-rehydrate task-lifecycle / Z.ai scans stream via `scanEventsOfTypes` instead of parsing the whole log. Don't restore parallel or whole-file paths here.
- **Task children are never workspace entries** (hidden, and their logs are the big ones). **Only an unseeded server adopts a browser's local tabs** — a second browser's stale localStorage must not push old tabs onto every screen.
- **Active tab is per-browser by design.** Only the open set, order (`settings.tabOrder`, now refetched live on `settings_updated`), names and config mirror.

## Plans

- **`handleNativeEnterPlanModeSet` must NEVER write the shared cluster plan.** For a cluster member it emits `plan_candidate_ready` and lets the operator pick via `PlanCandidateBanner` (save-to-worker / attach-to-cluster / discard); ATTACH TO CLUSTER routes to `cluster_plan_set` → `handleClusterPlanSet`. `plan_set` still fires either way for the events.jsonl audit.
  - **Why this is load-bearing:** from 2026-05-19 (`683bbb3`) to 2026-09-01 this branch called `setClusterPlanBody()` directly, which bypassed every guard the deliberate model-driven path enforces. `peerPlanWrite` (the `cluster_plan_write` peer tool) denies mailbox-only members and rejects a destructive body with `destructive_write_requires_operator` via `validatePlanWritePreservesCheckboxes` — doctrine: a model may author or non-destructively restructure the plan but "must preserve existing checkbox tasks and their status". EnterPlanMode did a wholesale body replace with none of that, so one member could wipe every task and its status for every other member — the cluster plan is folded into all of their system prompts.
  - It was collateral in a plan-storage refactor, not a decision: the same commit updated `PlanCandidateBanner.tsx`'s ATTACH TO CLUSTER semantics, `plan_candidate_ready` survived only in the `catch` block, and the function's own doc comment still described the gate. `scripts/smoke/phase-2.6.ts` caught it (17/18); it is back to 18/18.
  - The two legitimate write paths are the operator's (`cluster_plan_set`) and the guarded peer tool (`peerPlanWrite`). Do not add a third.
- **`cluster_plan_changed` is an audit record and must persist.** `broadcastCluster` is live-push only (no seq, no events.jsonl), which is fine for `cluster_state` but silently made plan rewrites untraceable — despite the client handler and `peerTools.ts` both documenting that it lands in events.jsonl. `broadcastCluster` now special-cases this one type and mirrors it to each member via `broadcastWorkflow`. The client handler is a no-op, so the duplicate delivery is inert. Don't route it around that helper.


- **Deleted plan files leave the attachment dangling.** If `<cwd>/.arco/plans/<planId>.md` is removed externally, the next turn's `buildPlanPrefix()` returns empty and the agent runs without the brief. No error surfaced — graceful degradation. Operator notices when the agent stops referencing the plan. The trade-off is intentional (don't break a turn because of file-system drift), but worth knowing.
- **`plan_create` / `plan_attach` / `plan_list` are the sanctioned model path (MCP server `arco`).** `server/src/mcp/planMcpTools.ts`, served by the `arcoMcpServer.ts` stdio child (CLI Claude via `mcp-config.json`, both Codex transports via `-c mcp_servers.arco.*`) and by Cursor's loopback for members. Deliberately a SEPARATE server from `arco-cluster`: those tools are cluster-scoped and their wire names (`mcp__arco-cluster__peer_send`) are referenced in prompts and the gpt-5.6 code-mode addendum, so they must not move — and a solo tab shouldn't advertise peer tools it can't use. **Unlike the cluster entry, this one is wired for every workflow with a cwd**, so the three MCP call sites (`turnEngine` CLI-Claude branch, `providers/codex.ts`, `codex-appserver/worker.ts`) no longer gate on cluster/codebase-memory/registry. `plan_create` **rejects** a body with no `###` headings or no top-level `- [ ]` lines (`lintPlanBody`) and returns the problems plus the `allowUnstructured` escape hatch — catching at write time what used to surface much later as an empty per-phase Review. Keep the lint in sync with `parsePlan`'s regexes. Claude Native has no in-process registration (its `EnterPlanMode` already creates+attaches); Ollama / LM Studio / Antigravity / Gemini have no MCP wiring and still rely on the prefix note.
- **A model cannot attach a plan by writing a file, and `buildPlanPrefix` now says so.** Plan existence is defined by `<cwd>/.arco/plans/index.json` — `listPlans` reads only that index, never the directory — and plan ids must match `PLAN_ID_PATTERN`. So a hand-written `my-plan.md` is invisible twice over. Attach is reachable from a shell (`POST /api/plans` with `attach:true` + `workflowId`, or `POST /api/plans/attach`; both call the same `handlePlanAttach` as the WS message), but only Claude Native's `EnterPlanMode` hook reaches it via a tool — so on Codex / CLI Claude / Cursor / Ollama the model had to guess Arco has a local HTTP API and reverse-engineer the route. **2026-07-28:** one worker did get there after several turns of curl+jq probing; another wrote markdown into its artifacts dir and reported "Plan attached to the Arco workflow." When no plan is attached, `planAuthoringNote()` now injects the endpoint and the body contract. Keep the "don't create a plan unless asked" clause — the note rides every plan-less turn and without it reads as an invitation.
- **Plan body structure is parsed, not decorative.** `parsePlan` (`reviewContent.ts`) keys phases off `### ` (H3), closes a phase on any `## ` (H2, intervening content discarded), and counts a task only from a **top-level** `- [ ]` / `- [x]` line — indented bullets are continuations, and `- **4.1**` is a fallback for agents that stripped the checkboxes. A model-authored plan without checkboxes therefore parses to **zero tasks**: per-phase Review and the operator's item picker have nothing to offer, even though the plan reads fine to a human. That's the failure mode behind a "poorly designed plan" complaint — it's a parser contract, not taste. `planAuthoringNote()` states the shape; if you change these regexes, change that note too.
- **`planLastSeenUpdatedAt` drives plan-edit refresh.** When the plan body's `updatedAt` exceeds the recorded last-seen, the next turn injects a "plan was edited since last turn — re-read the file" marker. If you change how plan injection composes the prompt, keep this signal — without it the agent might miss mid-session plan revisions.
- **`buildPlanPrefix` always emits a one-liner — never the full body.** Three variants (first-attach / edited-since-last-turn / stable) all fit on a single bracketed line. Earlier behavior injected `<plan>…</plan>` on first attach and on every `updatedAt` bump, which created two problems: (a) the agent's own edits to the plan file (the directive encourages tick-offs and inline findings) bumped `updatedAt` and re-injected the body the very next turn — a feedback loop where every working turn cost 1.5–5KB of plan prose, and (b) Claude's session JSONL captures prompts verbatim so the body re-rendered as a duplicate user bubble in chat. Don't reintroduce the full-body branch — let the agent re-read the file on demand instead.
- **`stripPlanPrefix` in `toSessionEntries` is load-bearing for chat cleanliness.** The plan prefix is meant to be invisible to the operator (server-side prompt construction only), but Claude's session JSONL records the full prompt. Without this strip, the operator sees their message rendered twice — once clean from `user_message_echo`, once with the plan prefix prepended from `session_entry`. Regex `^\[Active project plan[^\n]*\]\n(?:<plan>\n[\s\S]*?\n<\/plan>\n)?\n` is anchored at message start (won't touch user-typed content that quotes the bracket form later) and includes the optional `<plan>…</plan>` branch to handle entries written before the one-liner-only switch.
- **Plans survive Handoff.** `wf.planId` persists in `meta.json` so the new session inherits the attached plan. The Handoff seed prompt acknowledges the attachment so the new session knows what's pinned. If you change handoff seed composition, don't lose this acknowledgment.
- **Handoff resets `meta.planLastSeenUpdatedAt = 0`.** The new session has zero context of the prior plan attachment; treating it as a fresh attach is correct. Without the reset, `buildPlanPrefix` would emit the *stable* one-liner ("file: …") on the seed turn, and the new session might miss the "you have a plan, read it" signal. With the reset, the seed turn gets the *newly-attached* form ("Read the brief at .arco/plans/<id>.md to orient"). Best-effort meta write — failure means the new session sees the stable form, which is still functional, just less directive.
- **Spawn does not auto-copy the parent's plan.** Spawn mints a new workflowId with a fresh `meta.json`; `planId` is never carried over by default. The SpawnDialog's "Include plan from parent" checkbox is the *only* way a spawn ends up with the parent's plan attached. Server-side, `handleSpawnFromHere` honors `msg.includePlan` by writing `planId` into the new meta, calling `recordAttachment` (so the spawn shows up in the plan's "active in" list), acquiring the watcher, and broadcasting `plan_attached` to the new tab. The seed turn's `buildPlanPrefix` then emits the "newly attached" form because the new meta starts with `planLastSeenUpdatedAt = 0`. If you change the spawn flow, preserve the opt-in default — accidentally copying the parent's plan creates surprising attach-span entries.
- **fs.watch on `<cwd>/.arco/plans/` is ref-counted, debounced, and skip-flagged.** `acquirePlansWatcher`/`releasePlansWatcher` ref-count by attached workflow + cwd; the watcher debounces 200ms and filters to `*.md`. Operator-driven REST writes (`PATCH` body, `DELETE`) push the planId onto `watcherState.skipNext` BEFORE the disk write so the inevitable subsequent watcher fire is consumed silently — without this every operator edit would deliver a duplicate `plan_attached` (or `plan_detached`) to clients ~200ms after the direct broadcast. Skip is keyed per planId per cwd; consume-once on fire. PATCH that touches only `completedAt` does not write the body and skips the skip-flag too.
- **Watcher reconcile owns title for new files only.** `reconcilePlanFile` extracts the H1 from the body when seeding a brand-new entry (agent created the `.md` directly), but for existing entries it bumps `updatedAt` and leaves the title alone. The operator owns titles via the UI/PATCH; the agent owns the body. Don't rewrite title from H1 on every edit — it would let body content silently retitle plans the operator already named.
- **Mark-complete auto-detaches; reopen does not auto-attach.** `PATCH { completedAt: <ts> }` runs `detachAllForPlan` server-side: closes every open span, broadcasts `plan_detached`, clears `meta.planId`, releases the watcher. `PATCH { completedAt: null }` only clears the timestamp — operator must re-click to attach. The asymmetry is deliberate: a completed plan shouldn't keep getting injected into turns, but reopening it should let the operator decide whether it's worth re-engaging.
- **Attachment spans are append-only with idempotent open-spans.** `recordAttachment` is a no-op when an open span for the (planId, workflowId) pair already exists, so rehydrate replay (server restart → meta.planId resurfaces → re-acquire watcher → recordAttachment) doesn't double-add. `closeOpenSpansForWorkflow` runs on tab close to flip every workflow's open spans to closed (preserves history rather than deleting). `workflowName` is snapshotted at attach time — if the operator renames the tab afterward, the span stays stale; rare enough to skip the bookkeeping.
- **`PlanCandidateBanner` autosave fires once per candidate.** `autosaveFiredRef.current` guards against re-entry; the candidate stays in store until either the save succeeds (cleared via timeout) or the operator hits DISCARD. `turn_cancelled` clears `pendingPlanCapture` (the *arm*) but NOT a candidate that's already captured — once the agent's reply is in hand, cancellation of a later turn shouldn't void it.
- **Plan body fetch surfaces failure rather than silently rendering empty.** `PlanTab` shows `Body fetch failed: HTTP 4xx — <reason>` above the `<pre>` when the GET fails. Old behavior (`setBody('')` on error) made `(empty plan)` ambiguous between "real empty body" and "fetch failed", which masked a class of bugs. Don't revert.
- **`<pre>` body needs `flexShrink: 0`, not `overflow: hidden`, to scroll.** PlanTab's outer container is a flex column with `flex: 1, minHeight: 0, overflowY: auto`. A `<pre>` flex item with `overflow: hidden` reports its flex-min-size as 0 and flex compresses it to fit, clipping the body and starving `overflowY: auto` of anything to scroll past. `flexShrink: 0` keeps the pre at content height so the parent actually scrolls. Confirmed regression on 2026-04-26.
- **Checkbox renderer regex matches `- [ ] item` / `- [x] item` only.** `CHECKBOX_RE = /^(\s*)-\s+\[([ xX])\]\s+(.*)$/`. Indentation is preserved (so nested `- [ ]` works); other markdown patterns (numbered lists, plain bullets) pass through as preformatted text. Click handler does an optimistic body update + `PATCH /api/plans { body }` and reverts on failure; the server's broadcast confirms the same value via the body-refetch useEffect.
- **Plan reference docs live beside the plan, not in the index.** `<cwd>/.arco/plans/<planId>.refs/<slug>.md`, directory-listed by `listPlanRefs` — deliberately the opposite of plan existence (index-only), so an agent with plain file tools can add a ref by writing a file. The `.refs` suffix and the watcher's non-recursive `*.md` filter mean a ref write never triggers `reconcilePlanFile`; keep both properties (`planRefs.test.ts` pins the reconcile no-op). Consequence: a ref written directly doesn't bump the plan's `updatedAt` — only the REST PUT/DELETE do (`announcePlanTouched`) — so the UI also refetches on focus. `deletePlan` removes the dir; `movePlan` copies it before dropping the source. Over-cap ref bodies are **refused**, unlike plan bodies (silently truncated at 256 KB) — a clipped spec is worse than an error the agent can split around.
- **The refs hint rides `buildPlanPrefix`'s one line — slugs only.** Slug charset `[a-z0-9-]` can't contain a newline, so the single-line `PLAN_PREFIX_RE` strip still holds; ref *titles* are never put in the prefix. Capped at 12 slugs. For cluster members the slugs go in a trailing `#### Plan reference docs …` heading appended to the folded cluster plan text — a heading on purpose, because `planOutline` keeps every heading, so the line survives the outline fallback. `cluster_plan_read` returns `refs` + `refsDir`.
- **Every path that hands an agent plan work goes through `server/src/planRefContext.ts`.** `refsDoctrine` is the one wording for the per-turn plan header and the cluster-plan trailer, and it **must stay one line** (`PLAN_PREFIX_RE` — `planRefContext.test.ts` pins it). `cluster_task_show` attaches `referenceDocs` (resolved from `ref:` tokens in the task title / notes / comments against the cluster plan + any `kind:'plan'` task link; dangling slugs come back `exists:false`, not dropped; it also adds the refs of the task's **originating plan item** — `planItemContextFor` text-matches the title to a cluster-plan checkbox, nested stage checkboxes included (exact, then a shared leading item id like `P6.9`/`T1`, then longest containment ≥16 normalized chars; ref links and their labels are stripped before comparing) and takes that item's nested block + its phase preamble, so members never have to copy links into tasks they create). Plan-scoped Review inlines the linked refs of in-scope items (16 KB each / 48 KB total). The `ref:` token grammar (`[l](ref:s)`, `[[ref:s]]`, bare `ref:s` not preceded by `[\w/:-]`) is duplicated in the client's `REF_LINK_RE` — change both, or the operator's chips and the agent's resolved refs disagree.
- **Lean-plan lint warns, never refuses.** `leanPlanWarnings` (`planMcpTools.ts`) flags a body over 12 KB or a task with >400 chars of continuation prose and returns `warnings` on the `plan_create` success result. The structural `lintPlanBody` stays the only refusal. Don't promote the lean check to a refusal: a long plan is a style problem, a checkbox-less plan is a parser contract breach.

## Handoff prompt overrides

- **The `[HANDOFF —` seed-prefix is load-bearing across four call sites.** Three of them are detection-only (`HandoffSeedPill` `startsWith` match, `ChatPanel` PRIOR-SESSIONS compression, `reviewContent.ts` `current-session` scope) and the fourth (`server/src/index.ts` `handleHandoffRequest`) is the *emitter* that produces the seed turn. The literal string is also exported from `server/src/handoffPrompts.ts` as `HANDOFF_SEED_PREFIX` and exposed to operator-edited prompts as the `{{seedPrefix}}` substitution variable so a custom prompt referencing the prefix doesn't fork the constant. **Do not let the prompt editor rewrite the seed itself** — only the *reflection / summarizer* prompts are operator-editable; the seed-prompt assembly lives in `handleHandoffRequest` and stays hard-coded. Changing the prefix means coordinated edits at all five sites; Phase 3 left it untouched.
- **Handoff prompt validation is advisory, not blocking.** `validateOverride` warns when an `original-worker` body omits `{{handoffPath}}` or `[HANDOFF_COMPLETE]`, surfaced as a banner in `HandoffPromptsEditor.tsx`. Saves are never blocked: operators occasionally want to experiment, the file-existence check after the reflection settles is the actual safety net (`runOriginalWorker` reads the file or surfaces `original-worker did not write …`), and a prompt that genuinely doesn't need the variable (e.g., a shorter test-only template that prints a literal path) shouldn't be rejected at the editor. Don't tighten the validator into a save-blocker without a real incident — false negatives there force operators to work around Arco.
- **Empty / whitespace-only override files are treated as absent.** `loadEffectivePrompt` falls through to the next tier (project → global → default) when a file exists but `body.trim().length === 0`. Saving an empty body via the UI is therefore *not* the way to "blank out" an inherited prompt — use [RESET TO DEFAULT] (which calls DELETE on the override file) instead. This rule exists so an accidental empty save can't silently strip the agent's instructions and produce empty handoff documents.
- **`runExportContext`'s `ctx.cwd` is the override-resolution cwd in both Handoff and Spawn paths.** For Handoff this is the workflow being rotated; for Spawn-from-here it's the *new* workflow's cwd, which is normally the same as the parent's (Spawn doesn't change cwd). If a future feature lets Spawn target a different cwd, the override resolution will follow the new cwd — that's the desired behaviour, but worth knowing.

## Context calibration

- **`appendActivity` must NOT auto-correct `busy` / `turnStartedAt`.** Prior versions stamped `turnStartedAt = Date.now()` on any incoming `primary_tool_use` / `primary_thinking` to cover reconnect-mid-turn. But `events.jsonl` replay re-emits every historical tool_use on every reconnect, so the auto-correct re-stamped wall-clock-now against stale replays and any workflow with an orphaned post-`primary_turn_ended` tool_use showed permanent `CHURNING Nm Ns`. Authoritative signal for busy + turnStartedAt is `workflow_state_snapshot` via `applyWorkflowSnapshot`; keep it that way.
- **First-observation `fromPercent = 0`, not `currentPercent`.** `maybeCalibrate` builds a range `[fromPercent, currentPercent]` and picks the highest uncalibrated zone that overlaps. Earlier logic used `fromPercent = currentPercent` on first observation (prev undefined), producing a zero-width range — if `current` sat between two zone ranges (e.g. 34%, between A 20–30 and B 45–55), nothing overlapped and calibration never fired. Resumed mid-range sessions stayed on byte-count forever. Using `0` catches every uncalibrated zone the session has already passed through.
- **Calibration runs `claude --resume <sid> --print "/context"`, not a pty.** The TUI spends 10+ seconds re-rendering prior conversation on large sessions before accepting slash-command input — the 10s pty timeout fired every time on a 247k-context Opus 1M session. `--print` hits the same slash-command dispatcher, returns the report, and exits in ~3s. Don't reintroduce pty-based automation here.
- **`/context` output parser must handle both legacy and markdown formats.** Claude TUI emits `**Tokens:** 247k / 1m (25%)` + a table row `| Messages | 223.8k | 22.4% |` (label sits *before* the numbers). Earlier builds emitted `62k/200k tokens` + `Messages: 44k tokens` (label *after*). `parseContextOutput` keeps both regex shapes. `parseTokenCount` also needs the `m` suffix path (`1m → 1_000_000`) for the 1M-context denominator.
- **Every `claude --print "/<cmd>"` writes three rows to the session JSONL** — a `<local-command-caveat>` user row with `isMeta:true`, a `<command-name>` user row (no isMeta), and a `system` row with `subtype:"local_command"` holding stdout. The system row is filtered by `toSessionEntries`' user/assistant-only type check; the two user rows must be filtered by the isMeta flag + content-prefix sniff for `<command-name>` or `<local-command-caveat>`. Without the filter, every background calibration surfaces as fake operator turns in chat. `SessionJsonlEntry.isMeta?: boolean` exists for this exact check.
- **Manual recalibrate goes through the same busy guard as auto.** `handleRecalibrateRequest` refuses when `wf.busy` is true — two `claude` processes touching the same session JSONL is the corruption pattern the concurrent-writer guard catches. The client-side button is also disabled while busy, but server-side guard is the authority.

## Chat message attachments

- **Attachments live on the optimistic `job.messages` entry only** — Claude's JSONL user-turn content is a multi-block array where image blocks sit alongside text blocks, and `toSessionEntries` only extracts text. When the `session_entry` echo arrives and the `ChatPanel` dedup filter picks it over the optimistic entry, the attachments are gone unless explicitly merged. `ChatPanel` walks `job.messages` for user sends with `attachments` and folds them onto the matching sessionChat entry by content-prefix + 5s timestamp proximity (same key used for dedup) before the final sort. Don't remove the merge pass unless you extend `toSessionEntries` to materialize image/file blocks from the JSONL.

## Review primitive

- **`ReviewContextRef` is a tagged union, not a string literal.** Protocol: six transcript-scope variants (`last-turn`, `last-n`, `current-session`, `full`, `git-diff`, `since-event` with `anchorEventSeq`) plus three plan-scope variants (`plan-whole`, `plan-phase`, `plan-items` — all carry `mode: 'verify' | 'critique'`). Server-side scoping reads `.kind` and dispatches to `buildReviewContent` (transcript) or `buildPlanReviewContent` (plan); artifact frontmatter flattens to a single string via `contextRefToString` for easy grepping. `ReviewRequestMessage.includeGitDiff?: boolean` layers a `git diff HEAD` block onto any transcript scope (plan scopes ignore it). Don't re-narrow `contextRef` back to a literal.
- **`buildReviewContent` skips in-flight turns.** The turn extractor only emits turns with a `primary_complete` event — a prompt that Claude is still replying to isn't included. `last-turn` scope therefore rejects with "Primary agent has not replied to the last prompt yet" if the operator hits Review mid-turn. Don't "helpfully" include partial replies; reviewers critiquing half-generated work produce useless feedback.
- **`current-session` scope depends on handoff-seed detection** (the same prefix `HandoffSeedPill` and `ChatPanel` compression use). If the seed string changes server-side, `current-session` silently degrades to the full transcript. Safe failure, but noted.
- **Review `git diff HEAD` has a 10MB `maxBuffer`.** Bigger diffs are almost always lockfile churn or generated code and would blow the reviewer's context window anyway. Not-a-repo / git-not-installed errors surface briefly inline in the diff block so the reviewer knows the diff is empty by accident, not by absence.
- **Review verdict is regex-extracted.** `Verdict:\s*(PASS|MINOR|BLOCKING)` (case-insensitive). If the reviewer's markdown deviates, `verdict` stays undefined and the badge shows `?`. The body is still readable. Don't tighten the regex to require exact formatting — reviewers occasionally indent or italicize the verdict line.
- **Reviewer artifact body is NOT persisted to events.jsonl** — only on disk. If the artifact file is deleted, the Review tab still shows the entry but `GET /api/workflows/artifact` 404s.
- **Local-reviewer addendum is provider-gated.** `runReviewer.ts` builds framing as `BASE_FRAMING + LOCAL_REVIEWER_ADDENDUM` only when `provider === 'ollama' || provider === 'llamacpp'`. Frontier reviewers don't need it (and stuffing it in would dilute the framing). The addendum tells the local reviewer to use its read-only tools (Read/Grep/Glob/WebFetch/WebSearch), with a verdict rule that "PASS without using tools" should be downgraded to MINOR. Don't extend the addendum to all reviewers without a real rationale.
- **Cluster-routed review dispatch uses `sendMessage`, NOT `operator_peer_send` (2026-05-11).** When the operator picks a cluster peer as reviewer (STEP 2 row in `ReviewDialog`), `App.tsx → onReviewViaMember` calls `buildClusterReviewTask` and dispatches via `sendMessage(reviewerWfid, content)` — a real operator turn on the reviewer. `peer_send` was the obvious-but-wrong choice: it stuffs the mailbox without waking the worker, and with AUTO-PICKUP off (the default) the task sits in mailbox limbo forever. Don't refactor the dispatch to peer_send "for consistency" — the path is operator-driven and the reviewer needs an immediate turn. The cluster-routed review explicitly tells the reviewer NOT to forward findings (no hidden forwarding); the operator forwards via `→ TRANSCRIPT`. The sub-worker path (default) still uses the existing review primitive (`requestReview` → `runReviewer`) and writes a `.md` artifact; cluster-routed reviews don't. Don't conflate the two paths in callers — they have different artifact shapes and different REVIEWS-tab visibility (sub-worker shows up; member-routed doesn't).

## Plan-review parsing invariants

- **H2 closes phases.** An `##` header inside a phase ends it; intervening content (until the next `###`) is silently discarded. Without this, headers like `## Open Questions / Blockers` between phases leak into the previous phase's preamble and corrupt review bodies. If you want prose between phases, structure it as inline prose within the previous phase, not as an H2 section.
- **Numbered-bullet fallback recognizes naked numbered tasks.** `parsePlan()` treats `- **N.M** Task` (no checkbox) as an unchecked task. This is a foot-gun mitigation — agents (especially Qwen) sometimes strip checkboxes when "updating" a plan, and review surfaces would otherwise break silently. Indented variant `  - **1**` is a subtask. Plain indented bullets (`  - Some text` with no checkbox or numbered prefix) are continuation prose, not separate tasks. Don't tighten the regex to require checkboxes — that re-introduces the foot-gun.
- **Preamble is reviewable in VERIFY mode.** A phase with zero `[x]` items but containing prose (e.g., "Phase 3 Completed: we did X, Y, Z") renders the prose for the reviewer. Completion-claims-as-prose is a valid claim. Decision rule: render phase if `taskCount > 0 OR preambleLength > 0`; don't reject only because there are no checked items. Only reject VERIFY scope when both the checked-task count AND the preamble are empty.
- **Item ID instability.** `${phaseIdx}/${taskIdx}` IDs are stable across check-state toggles but break on reorder/delete. If the operator edits a plan between selecting items in the picker and dispatching the review, unresolvable IDs trigger a reject naming the missing items. Don't try to "rescue" by fuzzy-matching surviving items — operator must re-pick.
- **Mode doesn't filter `plan-items`.** VERIFY/CRITIQUE filtering (show only `[x]` vs show all) applies only to `plan-whole` and `plan-phase`. For `plan-items`, the operator's explicit selection is authoritative; mode only affects the reviewer's framing/instructions. All selected items render regardless of check state.

## DOCS / GIT composer buttons

- **DOCS prompt explicitly excludes `.arco/plans/`.** Load-bearing reason: plan files have checkbox / H3 phase structure that the Plan tab parses for per-phase review and the spawn-from-task surface. When agents (especially Qwen) "update docs" without this constraint, they rewrite plans as prose and silently break those features. Don't loosen the prompt to "doc updates including plans" — keep the exclusion explicit. The original incident is the reason this button exists.
- **GIT button never auto-pushes protected branches.** `PROTECTED_BRANCH_RE = /^(main|master|release\/.*|prod|production)$/i`. The pattern is checked client-side in `inferDefaultAction(status)` (drives the smart action default) and embedded into the structured prompt (which tells the agent to pause and confirm). Don't remove the client check — the smart default depends on it. Don't auto-push protected branches — operator must confirm.
- **Server-side `checkoutBranch` refuses on dirty trees.** Hard reject if `git status --porcelain` is non-empty; operator must commit or stash first. Moving uncommitted changes across branches creates confusion (file shows as "modified" on the new branch with no clear context). The refusal happens before any branch state changes — no partial state.
- **Branch name validation is `/^[A-Za-z0-9._/-]+$/`.** Runs on both switch and create paths. Rejects with HTTP 400. Don't widen this to allow shell metacharacters — a name containing `;` or `$()` would be unsafe even with execFile.
- **RUN AS spawn clears session IDs.** `spawnConfigFromRunAs()` sets `resumeSessionId: undefined, resumeWorkflowId: undefined` to force a fresh dispatch in the new tab, not a resume. GIT and DOCS are one-off work, not session-rooted; resuming a parent's session into a different model would seed it with foreign tool history. Don't preserve those IDs in the spawned config.

## Protocol

- **Protocol rename (Phase 8):** everything that was `orchestrator*` is now `primary*` — `PrimaryTextMessage` / `PrimaryCompleteMessage` / `PrimaryModelMessage` / `PrimaryArtifactMessage`, `PrimaryPhase`, `PRIMARY_PROVIDERS`, `WorkflowConfig.primaryProvider/Model/Effort`, `WorkflowDefaults.primary*`. Only the read-compat shims in `workflowMeta.ts` + `settings.ts` still mention the old names, to absorb on-disk state from before the rename. Don't reintroduce `orchestrator*` names in new code.
- **Neither dispatcher is exhaustiveness-checked — a missing `case` fails silently.** The server's `ClientMessage` switch (`server/src/index.ts` `ws.on('message')`) and the client's `ServerMessage` switch (`client/src/ws/handleServerMessage.ts`, split out of `useWebSocket.ts`) have no `default`/`never` guard, so a new type added to the union but not to the switch is dropped with no `tsc` error and no log. When adding a message type, add the `case` on the receiving side in the same commit (and a smoke that asserts the round-trip). Don't widen `msg` to `unknown` to paper over a narrowing error — the discriminated union is the only type safety left here.

## Operator session-cookie gate (WS + HTTP)

- **`/ws`, `/terminal`, `/upload` and every non-agent `/api/*` route need the browser session cookie** (`server/src/http/wsAuth.ts`). Origin guard alone admitted every Origin-less local process — the 2026-10-10 incident was a cluster Director driving `cluster_dispatch_once` and `update_primary_config { goalRun }` over a Node `ws` script with every automation toggle OFF. Don't add an Origin-less or cookie-less bypass for convenience; scripted clients use a test server with `ARCO_WS_AUTH=off`.
- **The signing key is memory-only and per-boot.** Never persist it or put it in env/a file — anything an agent can `cat` defeats the gate. Consequence: every restart invalidates cookies, so every client connect path must `await ensureWsSession()` first (both `useWebSocket.ts` and `XtermView.tsx` do). A new socket opener that skips it works until the first deploy, then 401-loops.
- **Mint requires browser fetch metadata** (`Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin`, `Sec-Fetch-Mode: cors|same-origin`, `Sec-Fetch-Dest: empty`, `Origin`). Not an OS sandbox: a same-user process that forges those headers can mint. The gate makes scripting the control plane deliberate circumvention, and logs every refusal.
- **Cookie name carries the server port** (`arco_session_<port>`) — cookies ignore ports, so dev and prod on one host would otherwise clobber each other.
- **Agent tool routes stay open** — `/api/clusters/:id/peer/<op>` (CLI/Codex/Cursor MCP children, Antigravity bridge) and `/api/plans` + `/api/plans/attach` (plan MCP tools) are called from agent processes by design (`isAgentToolRoute`). A new MCP tool that calls back into Arco over HTTP must be added there or it 401s; never widen the exemption to a prefix.
- **Cookie is session-only (no `Max-Age`)** so it never lands in the on-disk cookie DB. The client keeps it alive itself: `installSessionFetch` (401 → re-mint → retry once), `startSessionRefresh` (20 min + on tab focus), both called from `main.tsx` before any window renders. `<a href>`/`<img>` loads of `/api/*` can't retry — they rely on the refresh.

## Goal run (north-star auto-continue)

`docs/GOAL_RUN_PLAN.md`. Operator-armed, per tab, default OFF; while armed, one continuation turn is dispatched after each settled turn. It is the sanctioned answer to "keep working toward the north star", built specifically so the 2026-07-28 Codex `create_goal` incident can't recur through Arco's own surfaces. Load-bearing:

- **The loop never asks a model whether to continue.** `evaluateGoalRun` is a pure function of facts the server already holds (iterations left, token ceiling, busy, queue depth, last-turn error, throttle, context pct). Continuation is *budget remaining*, termination is *budget spent* — never a completion judgment. A model signal may only ever SHORTEN a run; wiring one that extends a run, routes work, or spawns anything re-creates the archived failure (CLAUDE.md § Non-goals, fence A). Don't add a "the model said it's done" branch, and don't add a "the model said it needs more turns" one either.
- **Iteration budget is mandatory and finite** (default 10, `GOAL_RUN_MAX_ITERATIONS` 500, clamped server-side — never trust the client's number). The whole point of the incident write-up is that Codex's equivalent ran with a NULL budget.
- **`remaining` is decremented BEFORE the dispatch await.** A fast re-entrant settle would otherwise read the pre-decrement count and spend one iteration twice.
- **Counters are NOT mirrored to meta.json.** A server restart ends any armed run. This is deliberate: the restart already killed the turn, and a deploy silently resuming an unattended loop is precisely the surprise the feature is designed to avoid. Don't "fix" this by persisting the counters.
- **Busy / queued is a skip, not a stop** — no iteration consumed, next settle re-evaluates. Only the six reasons in `GOAL_RUN_STOP_TEXT` end a run, and each one is announced with its reason.
- **`maybeGoalRun` runs LAST** among the post-settle armed actions (after auto-fold, auto-handoff, native auto-compaction). Each of those reshapes the context the tick would run against.
