- **Arming requires a north star**, and clearing the north star mid-run stops it (`no_north_star`) — otherwise the loop dispatches turns pointing at nothing.
- **Operator messages don't disarm.** Steering mid-run is expected; stopping is always explicit (the chip or `/goal run stop`). The chip renders only while armed, so an unattended loop can never be running without a visible counter and a one-click stop above the composer — don't hide it behind a panel.

## Project trust (DD7)

The gate (`server/src/projectTrust.ts`) suppresses a cloned repo's `CLAUDE.md` /
project skills (`.arco/skills`) / project `.arco/settings.json` from the model
turn until the operator vouches for it — the cloned-repo prompt-injection vector.
Load-bearing invariants:

- **Two-pass bootstrap — trust is resolved from USER/GLOBAL scope ONLY.** `gateProjectResources(cwd)` reads `~/.arco/trust.json` + the global `settings.json`'s `defaultProjectTrust` BEFORE any project-local surface is read. A cloned repo must never be able to self-grant trust by shipping a `.arco/settings.json` that sets `defaultProjectTrust: 'always'` — `readDefaultProjectTrust()` calls `readSettings()` with NO cwd (global-only branch). Don't "fix" it to read the cascade with a cwd.
- **The gate is threaded as `includeProject`, not enforced inside the leaf functions.** `readSettings`/`listSkills`/`discoverClaudeMdFiles` take an optional `{ includeProject }` (default `true`) so UI/HTTP callers still see project state; only the turn path in `turnEngine.ts handleUserMessage` passes the resolved trust. Don't gate inside the leaf functions globally — it would blank the settings UI's project overrides.
- **Fail-safe, not fail-open.** If `gateProjectResources` throws, the turn-path catch sets `includeProject: false`. An error must suppress, never leak.
- **Nearest-ancestor, deepest-wins.** `decideTrust` picks the deepest recorded ancestor of the cwd, so a `never` on a subdir overrides an `always` on its parent. Clusters get one decision per cwd tree for free (keys are canonical paths).
- **Operator-prompt only; deduped per cwd.** `markPromptPending`/`clearPromptPending` raise one `project_trust_prompt` per cwd per server lifetime (cleared when a decision is recorded). The turn ALWAYS proceeds with resources suppressed — the prompt only grants trust for future turns. The gate never auto-trusts. `'never'` suppresses silently (no per-turn notice); `'ask'`/fail-safe log the suppression to events.jsonl.
- **The one-shot prompt is not the only way in — keep the standing surfaces working.** The dedup above plus the fact that the banner renders in ONE tab's composer (`TerminalInput`) means it's routinely missed: in a cluster the prompt lands in whichever member's turn hit the gate first, and the parent tab has no CONFIG tab, so the operator sees only the per-turn `[PROJECT TRUST] … suppressed` notice with no button. The standing surfaces are `GET`/`POST /api/project-trust` (`http/projectTrustRoutes.ts`) behind `useProjectTrust`: the global SettingsPanel **PROJECT TRUST** section, the per-tab CONFIG row, and the `ClusterRightPanel` **TRUST** chip. Don't "simplify" any of them back onto the workflow-scoped `set_project_trust` WS message — the settings panel has no workflowId, which is why the routes are HTTP.
- **`decision: 'clear'` must re-arm, not just delete.** `removeTrustDecision` drops the entry AND the route calls `clearPromptPending(cwd)`; without that the cwd would sit at `ask` with its one-shot prompt already consumed — suppressed forever with no prompt. HTTP writes also fan a `project_trust_set` broadcast to every live workflow the key covers (events.jsonl visibility + clears stale banners in every cluster member).

## Skills, primitives & config interpolation (Phase 8)

- **`/SKILLS` is provider-native; `+SKILL` is Arco-owned — never merge the catalogs.** `/api/toolbox` asks the active runtime what it can actually invoke: Claude CLI's zero-cost init metadata supplies exact names (plugin skills stay namespaced), while Codex app-server supplies `skills/list` after `plugin/list` completes asynchronous plugin synchronization. The UI MUST use the returned `invocation` (`/name` for Claude, `$name` for Codex). Claude Native does not run Claude Code's skill runtime and must return `supported:false`; `.arco/skills` remain the explicit provider-agnostic manifest/inlining path through `+SKILL`. Showing Claude plugin skills on GPT tabs or inserting bare `/frontend-design` recreates the original bug.

Eight borrowed-improvement features share one rule: **operator-visible, deterministic, reversible — never a server-authoritative judgment.** Load-bearing invariants:

- **Skill-usage telemetry (`skillUsage.ts`, §5.2) is fed at ONE point: the `worker_tool_use` case in `seedWorker.ts`.** That's the cross-transport convergence — every provider's tool_use lands there before broadcast. The signal is a `Read` whose `file_path` is a `.arco/skills/<slug>/SKILL.md` (segment-aware match, not basename). It's fire-and-forget (`void recordSkillRead`), serialized via a module-level write-chain, fail-safe (a write error is swallowed). Counts attach in `listSkills`, NEVER in `buildSkillsManifest` — telemetry must not enter the model prompt. Don't re-hook per-transport.
- **Primitive drift guard (`primitiveDrift.ts`, §5.1) is FAIL-OPEN.** `checkModelDrift` flags only when the injected catalog probe (`catalogDriftProbe` in clusterHandlers — cluster-member providers only, others/cold-catalog return true) affirmatively says the model is gone. A probe error / unsupported provider / empty input never flags. It FLAGS (a `system_notice`), never blocks the primitive. Don't turn it into a hard refuse.
- **DD8 operator-only skills: `buildSkillsManifest` FILTERS `operatorOnly`; full bodies enter context only after an explicit operator action.** `operator-only: true` frontmatter → omitted from the model manifest (removes model authority). **APPLY ONCE** rides `pickedSkills` on `user_message`, applies on the FIRST iteration only, and emits a byte-delta `system_notice`. **PIN TO TAB** rides `update_primary_config { pinnedSkills }`, persists stable refs, and re-resolves bodies into the system prompt every turn until removed. Either click is the operator's explicit action, so project skills apply REGARDLESS of the DD7 discovery gate. `renderSkillFile` must keep writing `operator-only: true` or an edit silently strips it.
- **DD12 config interpolation: the badge introspects WITHOUT resolving; resolved values are NEVER logged.** `describeConfigValue` classifies `!cmd` / `${ENV}` / literal for the `{configured, source}` badge with no spawn / no env-value read. `resolveConfigValue` runs the command (argv-only, no shell, 10 s timeout) or env-expands (`$$`→`$`, `$!`→`!`), caches successes by raw string, and is consumed lazily (Z.ai key in `providerSecrets.ts`; Ollama/LM Studio base URLs — URL-validated AFTER resolution, fail to the default). A file-stored `${ENV}` ref is source `'env-ref'`, distinct from `'env'` (process ZAI_API_KEY set) — don't conflate, the UI disables input only for the latter.
- **DD10 `/prompt` commands expand BEFORE the turn is recorded, and gate project scope on the raw trust DECISION.** Expansion runs first in `handleUserMessage` (before context-refs, before the echo) so `events.jsonl` carries the expanded turn. A project template (`<cwd>/.arco/prompts/*.md`) needs `resolveTrustStatus(cwd) === 'always'` — NOT `gateProjectResources.includeProject` (a prompts-only repo has no other gated resource, so includeProject would be true and let an untrusted template through). Global templates (`~/.arco/prompts`) are ungated.
- **H17 hardline command floor is ALWAYS ON (not operator-disablable) and checked FIRST in the Native dispatch.** `checkHardlineCommand` refuses Arco self-restart/self-kill + `rm -rf` of repo/home/root. It's a FLOOR for the cooperative self-restart mistake, NOT an adversary sandbox — trivial obfuscation slips through and that's accepted. Don't add an operator opt-out.
- **H21 OSV malware pre-check is FAIL-OPEN + block-with-override.** `checkCommandForMalware` flags only an `MAL-` advisory (ordinary CVEs don't block); a network error / unreachable OSV resolves to allow. On a hit the create is NOT applied — the server echoes the payload back with `overrideMalwareWarning: true` so the operator can re-send to proceed. Don't make a network failure block the add.
- **H35 skill audit is DIAGNOSTIC ONLY — there is deliberately NO install verdict.** `auditSkillContent` returns invisible-unicode + threat-pattern findings; it must never carry `should_allow_install` / `blocked` / `verdict` (that's border-A — a server-authoritative quality judgment). The operator reads the findings and decides; nothing is auto-blocked. The unit test pins the absence of a verdict field.

## Local-model picker (Phase 9)

Two deterministic, operator-readable fit/diagnosis helpers. Both pass the four-question test (operator-triggered readouts, fixed rules, reversible). Load-bearing invariants:

- **DD15 Apple-Silicon fit: the darwin probe presents a UNIFIED-MEMORY BUDGET as VRAM, or every model scores NO.** Before DD15 `doProbe()` returned `totalVramBytes:0` on darwin, so `scoreFit` → `available 0` → `NO` for everything. `probeAppleSilicon()` now parses `system_profiler SPDisplaysDataType -json` (`parseAppleGpu`, **"apple"-name guard** — an Intel Mac's non-Apple GPU must NOT get a budget, it falls back to the no-VRAM profile), derives `appleUnifiedMemoryBudget` (size-scaled 0.67/0.75/0.80, **`sysctl iogpu.wired_limit_mb` override wins when >0**), and sets it as `totalVramBytes` + `gpus[0].vramBytes`. Both `system_profiler` and `sysctl` are **execFile argv-only, darwin-gated, never-throw**. Throughput uses `APPLE_BANDWIDTH_BY_CORES` (fit.ts, ~100→800 GB/s keyed by `hw.gpuCores`; flat `metal=4e11` is only the cores-unknown fallback) — NOT the old flat metal bandwidth, which mis-estimated ~8×. Constants are PORTED (hard-coded), not imported.
- **DD13 serve-failure diagnosis is a DETERMINISTIC ordered regex registry — never a model verdict.** `diagnoseLlamacppServeFailure` (pure) classifies the stderr tail: **order matters — OOM is checked before ctx-too-large** (an OOM tail often mentions the KV cache; the OOM remediation must win), **binary-missing before gguf-missing**. Unmatched → `{matched:false, cause:'unknown'}` with no invented fix. Only `set-ctx-size`/`set-ngl`/`add-extra-arg` fixes exist (they map onto the catalog editor's three fields); advisory-only causes (port/path/install) carry `fixes:[]` and rely on `suggestions` text. `modelHealth.ts` computes it ONLY when `managerState === 'failed'`, feeding the failed loadout's current ctx/ngl so a fix suggests a concrete halved value. The panel's fix button mirrors `LlamacppCatalogEditor`'s apply+reload EXACTLY (GET catalog → mutate → POST full catalog → `/api/llamacpp-load`) so it's reversible; `add-extra-arg` appends argv tokens idempotently. vLLM/SGLang entries from the upstream reference are DROPPED — llama.cpp subset only.

## Restore-points / Phase-9 forks (REWIND CONVERSATION)

In-tab rewind/branch over the append-only `events.jsonl` (`docs/PHASE_9_FORKS_PLAN.md`). Native-first. Load-bearing invariants:

- **`meta.leafSeq` ABSENT ⇒ byte-identical to pre-Phase-9.** The branch-aware replay pre-pass (`filterEventsToBranch`/`walkBranchSeqs` in `resumeSummary.ts`) only runs when `meta.leafSeq` is set. Absent ⇒ flat replay, no filtering, fast path, no whole-file read. Never make the filter unconditional — that is the no-regression guarantee.
- **`leaf_move` is a REDIRECT node — that is what prunes the abandoned branch, NOT `parentEventSeq` alone.** `persistAndStampSeq` stamps the linear predecessor as `parentEventSeq` (no write-path change for Phase 9), so after a rewind the post-rewind events' parents still point at the physical tail. The walk excludes the abandoned span ONLY because, when it lands on a `leaf_move`, it jumps to `targetSeq` instead of following `parentEventSeq`. Don't "simplify" the walk to pure parentEventSeq.
- **Replay walks from the PHYSICAL TAIL, not from the stored `leafSeq` value.** `meta.leafSeq` being set just means "branch mode on"; `replayMessagesWithResumePolicy` starts the walk at the greatest seq present. Each rewind appends a fresh `leaf_move` at the tail and every post-rewind turn appends after it, so the newest event is always on the active branch and the latest redirect is on its ancestry path. This is why post-rewind turns are picked up **without advancing `leafSeq` per turn** — do not add per-turn `leafSeq` bumps.
- **Branch mode bypasses the byte-range summary path** (reads the whole file) because the lineage walk needs the full event set to resolve parents/redirects across the tail boundary — the §5 "walk before summarization" rule. `walkBranchSeqs` reports `truncated` when ancestry has rotated off the size-capped log; that surfaces as `ResumeReplayResult.reconstructed` and a loud worker `console.warn` (hygiene-B: never silently degrade a lossless rewind into a lossy one). `inScope` holds only surviving events.
- **The rewind disposes the live Native worker** (`disposeClaudeNativeWorker`) so the next turn cold-rebuilds from the branch — reuses the proven post-handoff teardown rather than force-mutating a live `messages[]`. Busy-guarded: rejected mid-turn.
- **Native-only in v1.** `handleRevertToPointRequest` resolves the effective transport (tab override → cascade) and rejects non-Native: on CLI/Codex `meta.leafSeq` is never read, so a rewind would silently no-op. The client hides the button for non-Claude providers. **Named REWIND CONVERSATION, deliberately distinct from `revert_to_checkpoint`** (the git working-tree RESTORE FILES) — two REVERT verbs on one HISTORY tab would confuse.
- **Reversible by construction:** abandoned branches stay byte-identical on disk; "undo the undo" is just another `leaf_move`. Contrast Purge, which destroys.
- **`branch_summary` is RESERVED + UNEMITTED — forks ship via `leaf_move`, not `branch_summary`.** The `BranchSummaryMessage` type exists in the protocol (declared early so events.jsonl/HISTORY readers recognize the kind without a second schema bump) but NO server path emits it. The fork record is the `leaf_move` redirect event + `meta.leafSeq`. Don't wire `branch_summary` into the rewind path thinking it's the fork marker.

## DEBAG durable review gate (review safe-mode)

Operator-armed HITL approval gate (`docs/DEBAG_PLAN.md`). Native-only. When `WorkflowConfig.reviewSafeMode === 'on'`, the Native worker's `dispatchTool` pauses on a *sensitive* call (`gateReview.ts` static allowlist: Bash, KillShell, write-outside-cwd, git push/reset --hard/clean) and surfaces an editable proposal. Load-bearing invariants:

- **Two-condition firing, default OFF.** `evaluateGate(armed, …)` returns a proposal ONLY when safe-mode is armed AND the call is sensitive. Safe-mode off ⇒ null ⇒ the hot path is byte-for-byte untouched. The gate slots in AFTER the hardline/config/fact-force guards (a call refused there never reaches it) and sees the EFFECTIVE post-hook-rewrite `input`.
- **The standing-approval signature is NON-lossy — never reuse `outputHash`.** `gateSignature` hashes the full canonicalized (sorted-key) tool name + args. `outputHash` strips digits (`\d+→#`) for error-loop detection, so `rm -rf dir1`==`rm -rf dir2` there; reusing it would broaden a standing approval to commands the operator never saw (a nudge toward border-A). Over-prompting (key reorder is canonicalized away, but any value change re-prompts) is the safe direction; under-prompting is forbidden.
- **`gate_required` is written BEFORE the Promise parks** (events.jsonl + the per-workflow sidecar `.arco/workflows/<wfid>/gate-approvals.json`). Writing it after reopens the lost-on-crash hole the whole feature exists to close. Sequential tool dispatch means the for-loop simply suspends on the `await` (plan §4.4); batch-settle for parallel `Task` is deferred (`Task` isn't sensitive).
- **Restart-resume is a deliberate discontinuity, NOT a bug (§4.5).** A gate that survived a `systemctl restart` cannot resume the dead in-process tool-loop — the parked Promise is gone. `rehydrateGatesOnce` (Set-guarded, on `rehydrate_workflows`) re-emits `gate_required` from the sidecar so the card reappears regardless of the client's event-replay cursor (the synthetic `gate::<id>` message dedupes, so replay + rehydrate double-delivery is harmless). When the operator then resolves it, `routeClaudeNativeGateReply` finds no live park → `resolveOrphanedGate` clears the sidecar, emits `gate_resolved`, and for approve **feeds the approved action as the next-turn input** (`handleUserMessage`, like `maybeAutoContinueAfterCompact`) — an honest re-issue, not a seamless continuation. If safe-mode is still armed the re-issued call gates AGAIN, live (a fresh park resolved normally) — that second approval is the documented cost of a restart-orphaned gate, not a loop.
- **Cancel drains the parked gate AND its sidecar record** (`cancel()` rejects every `pendingGates` entry; the park's catch clears the sidecar) so a cancelled turn's gate doesn't resurrect on the next rehydrate.
- **Fence:** operator-armed (default off), deterministic allowlist (no model judgment about what's dangerous), every gate_required/gate_resolved in events.jsonl, reject + disarm + (sub-phase 5) revoke make it reversible. The model only *proposes*; the server pauses and waits.

## Server lifecycle

- **`tsx watch` restarts on `server/` and `shared/` saves and takes spawned CLIs with it.** During active turns, editing server source kills the worker mid-stream. Warn before editing, or pause the watch via the launcher script.
- **Provider `worker_error` MUST retain `workflowId` through the `error` protocol message.** The client routes scoped errors to that workflow and only uses `activeJobId` for truly global errors. Dropping the id recreates the 2026-07-30 incident where Builder 2's provider refusal appeared in a newly focused unrelated tab; it also prevents the failure from landing in the source workflow's `events.jsonl`.
- **Restart path is SIGKILL + 500 ms grace, not SIGTERM + port-poll.** Earlier 5-second grace + port-binding poll raced concurrently/tsx teardown; ports were freed by kernel sync and the new process bound before the old fully exited. SIGKILL is immediate; ports release on socket-close which kernel does in the same syscall. Don't loosen back to graceful shutdown for the dev wrapper — the `arco` launcher script is the supervisor.
- **Shutdown handler flushes events.jsonl before exit.** `flushAllWorkflowEvents()` clears in-memory pending writes synchronously. Don't add async work between the SIGTERM handler and `flushAll()` — it widens the crash-loss window.
- **`handleUserMessage`'s `.catch` MUST broadcast `system_notice` + `primary_turn_ended` and clear `wf.busy`.** A bare `console.error`-only catch was leaving the UI mid-turn forever on any internal exception. Don't simplify the catch back to a log-only path; the operator sees nothing if you do.
- **`sessionJsonl.startWatcher` dedups its own restart timer.** A flapping fs error fires the watcher's `error` handler multiple times; without the `restartTimer` guard, each fires a new `setTimeout(startWatcher, 2000)` and they fan out. Cleanup must clear `restartTimer`.
- **Launcher port-fallback exports `ARCO_PORT` + `ARCO_CLIENT_PORT`.** When 5173/3001 are held by non-Arco processes, the launcher walks forward to the next free port (cap +50) and exports the chosen values to `npm run dev`. `vite.config.ts` honors them with `strictPort: true` so vite fails loudly if it can't bind the launcher-chosen port (a silent slide would diverge from the launcher's `wait_ready` URL). Server reads `ARCO_PORT`. Don't hardcode 3001/5173 anywhere new.
- **H43 code-skew banner: prod runs the COMPILED `dist`, so a rebuilt/pulled checkout can silently outrun the running build.** `diagnostics/codeSkew.ts` snapshots the git HEAD at boot (the rev the running build started from) and the `core.code-skew` Diagnostics check (`diagnostics/checks.ts`) WARNs "N commits behind" when the on-disk HEAD has advanced past it. SKIPPED under tsx-watch dev (source is live there, so skew is meaningless) and when not a git checkout. Read-only `git` via `execFile` (argv array, cwd pinned to the three-levels-up repo root, timeout). If you change the dist layout, keep that repo-root resolution honest — the systemd unit sets no `WorkingDirectory`, so process cwd is not the repo.
- **H42 systemd shutdown-window probe warns when the unit's `TimeoutStopUSec` is below the graceful-shutdown budget** — the SIGKILL-the-worker-groups + reap-PTY/LSP + flush-every-workflow's-events work `onSignal` does before exit. Read-only `systemctl show`, Linux + `INVOCATION_ID`-set only. Part B is the `onSignal` forensics line (`formatShutdownForensics` — what the stop interrupted: in-flight turns by provider, PTY count, uptime/RSS) logged inside the ~100 ms flush budget. Don't add async work to `onSignal` between the handler and `flushAll()` — it widens the crash-loss window (existing rule above) and eats into the systemd window H42 is checking.

## Service mode boot resolution (B1)

- **`@arco/shared` MUST resolve to compiled JS under bare `node`, or `node server/dist/index.js` (production / `arco service`) crash-loops on boot** with `ERR_UNKNOWN_FILE_EXTENSION: Unknown file extension ".ts"`. `shared/package.json` carries a conditional `exports` map: `development`→`./src/index.ts` (Vite serve + `tsx --conditions development`), `types`→`./src/index.ts` (tsc, `moduleResolution: bundler`), `import`/`default`→`./dist/index.js` (bare node). **Do not revert it to `"main": "./src/index.ts"`** — that's the original bug. The server dev script + `test:*` scripts pass `--conditions development`; drop that flag and `npm run dev` loses live `shared` source resolution. Guarded by `scripts/smoke/service-cli.ts` (static exports assertion + a bare-node import boot check). Full rationale: `docs/SERVICE_MODE.md § Build & @arco/shared resolution`.
- **The prod static-serve is gated on `ARCO_SERVE_CLIENT=1` (set only by the systemd unit), NOT `NODE_ENV`.** Unset → the server returns its normal 404 and Vite owns the client (dev unchanged). The fallback runs after every API/preview route, GET-only, and is path-traversal-guarded (resolved path must stay inside `client/dist`); a missing asset 404s, a missing non-asset path falls back to `index.html`. Don't key it on `NODE_ENV` — dev sets nothing and must stay inert.
- **Promotion bakes `ARCO_HUB_URL` + `ARCO_NTFY_URL` into the unit — and a promoted box has NO hot-reload.** When the server is hub-linked (Tier 5), install with both vars in the shell so `renderSystemdUnit` writes them as `Environment=` lines; omit them and the compiled server's `initHubLink`/`initNtfyTransport` stay inert (working service, but offline to the hub + silent on ntfy). **workstation is promoted (2026-06-03)** — it runs `arco.service` from `server/dist`, so editing `server/`/`shared/` does NOT auto-restart it. To ship a change: `npm run arco:service -- install` (rebuilds dist) then `systemctl --user restart arco.service`. To iterate in dev: `systemctl --user stop arco.service arco-agent.service`, run `npm run dev`, then re-`start` the units. (The tsx-watch-kills-workers hazard only applies while on `npm run dev`.)

## LSP diagnostics (§C3)

- **The post-write hook is fire-and-forget and MUST NOT be able to break a Write/Edit.** `claude-native/tools.ts` calls `try { ctx.onFileWritten?.(abs); } catch { /* best-effort */ }` AFTER the successful `fs.writeFile` in both `Write` and `Edit` — never awaited, never allowed to throw into the write path (the write path is P0). The worker's wiring additionally `void`s the returned promise and `.catch()`es it so an async rejection can't surface as an unhandled rejection. Don't make the hook awaited, don't move it before the write, and don't let any LSP error propagate to the tool's return.
- **Absent language server = silent no-op.** When no LSP server is on PATH for the changed file's language, `resolveServer` returns null and the subsystem does nothing — NO error, NO crash, NO chat notice. This is intentional (bring-your-own-upstream, same as the caveman output style). Don't "helpfully" surface an error when a server is missing; that would spam every operator who hasn't installed `typescript-language-server` et al. `tsserver` is NOT a candidate — it speaks the TSServer protocol, not LSP.
- **0-based → 1-based conversion happens exactly once, in `mapPublishDiagnostics` (`server/src/lsp/client.ts`).** LSP positions are 0-based; the protocol `DiagnosticItem.line/column` are 1-based (`+1` at that boundary). The client never re-converts. If you add another consumer of LSP positions, convert at the same boundary — don't push raw 0-based numbers across the wire.
- **Server lifecycle mirrors `llamacppManager`: one process per `(cwd, languageId)`, dedup, idle-reap, shutdown-kill.** `lspManager.shutdown()` is wired into the server's SIGTERM/SIGINT handler (`server/src/index.ts`) and SIGTERM+SIGKILLs every live child. Idle-reap evicts after `ARCO_LSP_KEEPALIVE_MS` (default 5 min). **Orphan gap (noted, accepted):** unlike worker subprocesses, LSP servers have NO durable pid registry / boot-sweep — a hard `kill -9` of the Node server can leak a language-server child until the OS reaps it. Acceptable because they burn no API tokens and idle-reap bounds them; if it ever matters, mirror `pidRegistry.ts`. See `docs/LSP.md`.
- **An empty `items[]` clears the file's diagnostics.** `store.setDiagnostics` drops the per-file key on an empty array, so "fix the error → badge drops" just works. Don't change `diagnostics` to accumulate — each message is the authoritative full set for its `file`.
- **The DIAGNOSTICS row click opens the file but does NOT scroll to the line yet.** The `FileViewerModal` has no line-scroll affordance; the row shows `line:col` and the tooltip carries the line. Best-effort per the §C3 spec — noted gap, not a bug.

## Runtime observable states (C1)

- **Two orthogonal axes, never one.** The connection axis (`ConnectionState`) and the per-workflow axis (`WorkflowRuntimeState`) are derived separately and must not be collapsed into one enum. The taxonomy maps to ARCO_OPS_PLAN.md §C1's (a)-(f): `disconnected` = (a) browser/network WS drop, server alive; `server-down` = (b) server process gone; `busy` = (c) workflow working with recent output; `stuck` = (d) busy but silent; `idle` = (e) operator's turn; `replay-recovered` = (f) just rehydrated from disk. `connecting` is the pre-first-open boot state. Both live in `client/src/runtime/runtimeState.ts`.
- **The `/healthz` probe is the ONLY thing that distinguishes `disconnected` from `server-down` — don't conflate them.** A closed WS alone can't tell you whether the *server process* is dead or just the *socket* dropped (browser slept, network blip, Vite proxy hiccup). The WS hook (`useWebSocket.ts`) fires a `fetch('/healthz', { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(HEALTHZ_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS) })` on close + each reconnect tick: 2xx → server alive → `disconnected`; reject/timeout/non-2xx → `server-down`. A null probe (not yet resolved) stays optimistically `disconnected` so there's no spurious SERVER DOWN flash in the gap before the probe lands. The probe reuses the same relative base-URL convention as `/api/restart` + `/api/transcript` (Vite proxy in dev, same-origin in prod) — do NOT fork a second server-URL derivation.
- **`stuck` is derived from ABSENCE of primary content, NOT from `primary_activity`.** `primary_activity` is change-gated — it only broadcasts when the descendant-process set changes, so it is NOT a heartbeat and a quietly-working turn can go minutes without one. The stuck check is `now - lastPrimaryEventAt >= STUCK_THRESHOLD_MS`, where `lastPrimaryEventAt` is bumped by the genuine primary signals (`primary_turn_started` / `_text` / `_thinking` / `_tool_use` / `_complete`, plus `_activity` as a weak extra). Crucially it is NOT bumped by `worker_*` deltas — a child Task streaming does not prove the primary is alive. Don't "fix" stuck-ness by treating `primary_activity` as the liveness source; it would let a hung worker read as busy forever.
- **The deriver is pure — no `Date.now()` / `new Date()` inside it. `now` is always injected.** This is what lets `scripts/smoke/runtime-states.ts` assert every state (and the killing-the-server-vs-silent-worker contrast) deterministically with a fixed clock and no live socket. The clock-reading impurity lives in the component layer (`RuntimeStateBanner`, which ticks once a second while a transient state shows) and in `SystemHeader`. If you add a new state, add its case to the deriver and a smoke step — never read the clock inside the deriver.
- **The threshold constants live in the deriver, exported, with rationale.** `STUCK_THRESHOLD_MS` (30s — clears a long-but-live tool call, surfaces a genuine hang within a watched turn), `REPLAY_RECOVERED_WINDOW_MS` (12s — long enough to notice "came back from disk", short enough not to shadow idle), `HEALTHZ_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS` (2s — clears a healthy localhost round-trip with margin). Tune them there, not by hard-coding numbers in components.
- **`connectionState` is ephemeral (not persisted) and `connected` stays load-bearing.** `connectionState` is absent from the store's `partialize` so a reload always re-derives it from a live socket rather than a stale snapshot; `recoveredAt` / `lastPrimaryEventAt` are likewise not in the persisted job shape. The existing `connected` boolean is unchanged — other code reads it; `connectionState` is the richer display overlay layered on top.

## Robustness invariants (C3)

- **The audit harness is pure by design — keep it clock-injected and headless.** `scripts/smoke/robustness-audit.ts` exercises all nine enumerated failure cases as timelines of input frames over the §C1/§C2 derivers — no live server, no spawned provider, no real socket. The moment it grows a `Date.now()` read, a real `fetch`, or a WS connection it stops being deterministic and the audit rots (same rule as `runtime-states.ts` / `retry-paths.ts`). New failure case → new pure scenario, not a live probe.
- **The connecting gap must NEVER flash `server-down`.** On fresh boot and on client reload the socket is `connecting`/`closed` with `everOpened` false and an unresolved (`null`) probe — that derives `connecting`/`disconnected`, NOT `server-down`. Only an explicit `healthzReachable === false` promotes to `server-down`. Don't "tidy" the deriver into treating a null probe as a failed probe; CASE 9 guards this and it's the difference between a calm reload and a scary SERVER DOWN flash on every refresh.
- **A silent provider crash must surface as `stuck`, never `idle`.** When a provider process dies WITHOUT a clean `primary_turn_ended`, `busy` stays true and the only honest read after `STUCK_THRESHOLD_MS` of no primary content is `stuck` (→ CANCEL TURN), not `idle`. A clean exit flips `busy`→false → `idle`. CASE 4i/4ii pin both. Don't add a "process exited → force idle" shortcut: a silently-dead worker would then read as the operator's turn with the dead turn never cancelled.
- **The audit asserts end-states for the three non-deriver mechanics — it does not fake them.** Codex resume correctness (CASE 5), Native replay-not-blocking-the-UI (CASE 6), and terminal orphan reaping (CASE 7) are transport / perf / process-lifecycle properties, not deriver properties. The harness asserts only the observable end-state each produces when it works; the internal correctness lives in `smoke:phase-4` / `smoke:compare`, the Native replay manual recipe, and `smoke:terminal` respectively (see `docs/OPERATIONS.md § Robustness audit`). Don't move that internal verification into the pure harness — it can't model it honestly.

## HISTORY tab (Phase 8)

- **Lifecycle events re-broadcast on rehydrate regardless of `clientLastSeq`.** `task_started` / `task_status` (Phase 7b) and `operator_label` (Phase 8) are persisted on the parent workflow but the client-side state they drive (`taskChildren`, the HISTORY filter) is rebuilt from the events themselves. A workflow whose only Task fan-out happened pre-refresh would otherwise come up showing `0s · RUNNING` forever (events sit below the seq cutoff). Re-broadcast is safe because the corresponding store actions are idempotent — `recordTaskStarted` / `recordTaskStatus` on `taskId`, and `operator_label` flows through `narrativeEventFromMessage` → `appendHistoryEvent`, which dedupes on `seq`. Don't add a "skip if seq <= clientLastSeq" gate to lifecycle replay — it'll silently regress the refresh path.
- **Pinned label anchors are by `targetEventSeq`, not by chat-row id.** A label pins to the seq of an event in `events.jsonl`; if the chat-row id changes (e.g. session JSONL renames it), the label still resolves because HistoryTab reads `event.seq → row` rather than chasing the row id. Don't try to "stabilize" labels by switching to row ids — the seq is the load-bearing identity.
- **`since-event` Review framing names the operator label.** When `anchorEventSeq` points at an `operator_label` event, `buildReviewContent` lifts the label's `text` into the framing (`research checkpoint` becomes the named anchor); otherwise the framing falls back to the bare seq. If you change the label-event shape, update `buildReviewContent` too — silent fallback to the bare seq is honest but loses operator context.
- **Spawn cutoff is honored in fresh-summarizer + Native original-worker, IGNORED in CLI original-worker.** `cutoffEventSeq` filters the rendered transcript and stops Native pre-warm replay at the cutoff; CLI `--resume` carries the parent's full session and there's no clean way to retroactively trim its view. The dialog tells the operator to choose fresh-summarizer when a hard cutoff matters; don't try to enforce the cutoff CLI-side without rebuilding the resume mechanism.
- **PURGE is irreversible and the type-the-name gate is the ONLY guard.** `DELETE /api/workflows/:wfid` (`purgeWorkflow.ts`) deletes the workflow tree + checkpoints (git refs + tar) + recall row + cluster mailbox; the `nuclear` tier also deletes the bound provider session files. The route requires `confirm:true`, and `nuclear` requires `confirmName` === `meta.name` (server-enforced, not just client). Deletion order matters: read `meta.cliSessions` for the nuclear provider-file paths **before** `rm`-ing the workflow dir (which holds meta.json). Each step is best-effort/isolated so one failure can't strand the rest; the client drops the tab via `removeJob` on success (the server already ran `handleRemoveWorkflow`). Don't add a soft-delete/trash — purge means gone.

## Git worktrees per task (§K5)

`server/src/project/taskWorktree.ts` spins an optional isolated `git worktree`
per ledger task. Load-bearing invariants (mirror the checkpoint store's):

- **Worktrees live ONLY under the managed root** (`~/.arco/worktrees/<cwd-hash>/<taskId>`), never inside the user's tree. `removeTaskWorktree` asserts the resolved path is under that root before any destructive `git worktree remove --force`. Don't relax this — it's the only thing standing between a bug and `rm -rf` on a path the operator owns.
- **Removal NEVER deletes the branch.** `git worktree remove --force` + `git worktree prune` only — the `arco/task/<id>` branch survives so committed work isn't lost. A "clean up everything including the branch" path is intentionally absent.
- **The `worktree` field is server-set only.** Clients ask via `task_worktree_create` / `task_worktree_remove`; the field is set through `persistLedgerMutation` → `applyUpdate` (the same event-sourced path as every other ledger mutation). The `task_ledger_update` WS message deliberately has no `worktree` member — a client can't forge one. Keep it that way.
- **Create needs a repo + at least one commit.** On an unborn HEAD `git worktree add` fails; we pre-check and throw `TaskWorktreeError('no-commit', …)` so the route returns a clean error, not a 500. Same for a non-repo (`'not-a-repo'`).
- **All git calls are `execFile` argv arrays** — never `shell:true`, never string interpolation (same rule as `gitSnapshots.ts`).
- **Checkpoints inside a worktree are fine and intended.** A linked worktree shares the main repo's object store, so `createCheckpoint({ cwd: worktreePath, … })` works unchanged and dedupes objects across sibling worktrees. Don't special-case worktree cwds in the checkpoint store.

## arco-agent — signed lifecycle supervisor (§K1)

`scripts/agent/` + `scripts/arco-agent.ts` is the per-host supervisor the K2 hub drives over a tailnet WS.
Load-bearing invariants — relaxing any one re-opens the §1 trust boundary:

- **The inbound vocabulary is the fixed `LIFECYCLE_COMMANDS` allowlist, nothing else.** No file access, no workflow access, no free-form shell. `renderLifecycleCommand` maps a command to an **argv array** (never a shell string) — there is no interpolation surface. A command not in the enum is rejected `not-allowlisted` and never reaches the runner.
- **Execute ONLY after full verification, in this order:** structural → allowlisted-cmd → Ed25519 signature → instance match → freshness (issuedAt skew) → single-use nonce. `verifyCommand` returns the FIRST failure and is **pure** (never records the nonce) so a forged signature can't poison the replay set. The nonce is recorded in `handleSignedCommand` only *after* a clean verify and *before* exec, so a duplicate in flight is `replay`, not a double-run.
- **Canonical payload serialization is fixed-field-order** (`canonicalizePayload`), never `JSON.stringify(payload)` directly — object key order is not guaranteed and signer/verifier must hash byte-identical input.
- **The hub holds the PRIVATE key; agents only ever get `hub.pub`.** `keygen` writes `hub.key` mode `600`. Never commit either key; never ship the private key to an agent.
- **Forging tests must flip a LEADING base64 char**, not the trailing `=` padding — padding decodes to identical signature bytes, so a padding flip does NOT corrupt the signature (this bit the first selftest; see the comment in `arco-agent.ts doSelftest`).
- **The runner is injected.** `handleSignedCommand` / `createAgentConnection` take a `CommandRunner` so the smoke asserts the dispatched argv WITHOUT spawning systemctl (and runs on macOS). Production supplies `systemctlRunner()`. Don't hard-wire `execFile` into the handler.
- **macOS has no autostart in v1** (§13.3) — `install` writes a systemd unit on Linux only; on macOS the lifecycle subcommands print a manual notice. The agent there runs only when launched from the app launcher.
- **The live hub WS endpoint is K2.** `run` connects outbound to `ARCO_HUB_URL`; until the hub exists there is nothing to connect to. The command-handling core is fully tested via `npm run smoke:arco-agent` (fake in-process hub). Verify offline with `npm run arco:agent -- selftest`.

## arco-management hub (§K2)

`scripts/management/` is the tailnet-only hub the K1 agents register to. Invariants:

- **`serve` must NEVER bind a public/all-interfaces address.** `arco-management serve` refuses `0.0.0.0`/`::` and the deployed unit binds the **tailnet IP only** (`<hub-tailnet-ip>` on hub-host). No Funnel, no public path — this is §1 rule 1, and the hub has no auth of its own beyond tailnet reachability + the signed-command layer, so a public bind is a real breach.
- **The hub holds the PRIVATE key and is the only signer.** `keygen` writes `hub.key` mode `600` under `~/.arco/hub/` and refuses to overwrite. Agents get only `hub.pub`. The hub signs every lifecycle command; the agent verifies. Never move the private key onto an agent.
- **The hub is a ROUTER, not an authority on validity.** `routeAction` forwards a workflow action to the owning instance's socket; the *local Arco instance re-validates* against its current pending request (that handler is its own integration — the hub does not decide whether an action is still valid). Don't add validity logic to the hub.
- **Offline never deletes a registry entry.** `InstanceRegistry` keeps last-seen so the console shows *which* machine is asleep; `deriveState` returns offline but the entry stays. Only explicit `remove` drops it. Lifecycle dispatch to an offline/socketless instance returns `instance-offline`, not a hang.
- **Lifecycle replies correlate by nonce, with a timeout.** `sendLifecycleCommand` keys a pending resolver on the command's nonce and clears it on `lifecycle_result` or a `commandTimeoutMs` timeout — so a never-answering agent yields `timeout`, not a leaked promise.
- **hub-host is a shared box.** It runs nginx/postgres/redis/PM2/rift/<app>/gunicorn/docker. The hub install is strictly additive (a new `--user` unit on `:3500`); never restart or reconfigure anything the hub didn't create. See `docs/REMOTE_MANAGEMENT.md` §6 recon.
- **Build-local-then-deploy.** The hub code is developed + smoked on localhost (`npm run smoke:management-hub` wires a real K1 agent to a real hub in-process); the VPS deploy is a separate operator-reviewed step.

## Workflow-reporting layer (§ workflow layer)

The live Arco *service* reporting workflows + applying reply/stop/continue. `server/src/management/{hubLink,initHubLink,identity}.ts` + the hub's service socket. Invariants:

- **INERT unless `ARCO_HUB_URL` is set.** `initHubLink` returns immediately with no env — an ordinary `npm run dev` / production boot must stay byte-unchanged. Never make the hub link connect by default.
- **Two connections, one card.** The agent (lifecycle, role `agent`) and the service (workflows, role `service`) both register under the SAME `instanceId` (shared `~/.arco/instance-id`). The hub keeps `agentSockets` + `serviceSockets` separately but ONE registry entry. Lifecycle commands route to the agent socket; workflow actions to the service socket. Don't collapse the two socket maps.
- **The local instance re-validates — the hub only routes.** `initHubLink.applyAction` looks up the live workflow and checks state (stop requires `busy`; reply requires non-empty body) BEFORE applying. The hub's `routeWorkflowAction` never decides validity. Keep validity logic server-side.
- **Apply via the existing turn primitives, synthetic ws+session.** stop → `handleCancel`; reply/continue → `handleUserMessage` with a `readyState: CLOSED` fake ws + a synthetic `Session` (mirrors the cluster auto-turn dispatch in `clusterAutoTurn.ts` — persistence runs, socket writes skip, events land in the workflow's events.jsonl). Don't invent a parallel turn path.
- **`hubLink.ts` stays turn-engine-free.** It takes `snapshotWorkflows` + `applyAction` as injected seams so it's smoke-tested without the server (`smoke:workflow-reporting`). The ONLY file coupling to server internals is `initHubLink.ts`. Keep that boundary.
- **`ARCO_HUB_URL` is shared by agent + service; path is cosmetic.** The hub WSS accepts any path and disambiguates by `role` on register. So one env var lights up both connections.
- **Desktop hub-link indicator is the ONLY management element in the Arco UI; status lives in an isolated store.** `client/src/stores/hubLinkStore.ts` (not the central appStore — keep it isolated so this optional surface can't destabilize the load-bearing store). Fed by `hub_link_status` (broadcast on change + on client connect). The chip renders nothing unless a hub is configured (`hubUrl` present). The click toggles `hub_link_set_enabled` → `setHubLinkEnabled` (local kill switch / reconnect). Everything else management-related stays in the separate console (trust boundary §1 rule 4) — don't grow the desktop surface.
- **Remote handoff is same-worker only + rejected mid-turn.** The `handoff` workflow action calls `handleHandoffRequest` with `mode:'original-worker'` (same provider/model, default summarizer) and `initHubLink.applyAction` returns `busy` if `wf.busy` — handoff summarizes *settled* context, so don't allow it mid-turn. It's fire-and-forget (the rotation streams through the workflow's normal events); the action result just confirms acceptance.
- **Transcript is reconstructed by the SERVICE, never read by the hub.** The hub has no filesystem access to the workstation (it's remote on hub-host in prod), so `GET …/transcript` triggers `transcript_request` → the owning service's `getTranscript` reads its OWN `events.jsonl` (bounded 8MB / 800 turns; `user_message_echo`=operator, `primary_text`=assistant, skip thinking) → `transcript_response`, correlated by requestId with a timeout. Don't make the hub read transcripts off disk — it breaks the trust boundary and won't work remotely.
- **Decision context comes from tailing `events.jsonl`, not in-memory transcript.** `initHubLink.readLastMessage` reads the bounded tail (1MB) and reconstructs the **full** last assistant message from `primary_text` events (skip `phase:'thinking'`), back to the last `primary_turn_started` (capped at `MAX_MESSAGE_CHARS=200k` only to bound a runaway — keeps the tail if exceeded). Best-effort + never throws. Don't replace this with a per-broadcast in-memory hook without measuring — the tail read is cheap and avoids coupling to the broadcast path. The 1MB bound is load-bearing: `task_ledger_snapshot` events are large + frequent and crowd the tail, and the full message must be recoverable past them. The console renders it in a ≤60vh scrollable block.

## SSH lifecycle fallback (§K4)

`scripts/management/sshLifecycle.ts` + the `sshTargets`/`sshExec` path in `createHub`. Invariants:

- **SSH is the LAST resort — only when the agent socket is down.** `sendLifecycleCommand` tries the agent socket first; it falls back to SSH only in the `instance-offline` branch, and only if a target is configured. A healthy agent always wins. Don't reorder.
- **The SSH path is NOT Ed25519-signed — that's intentional.** The agent path is signed because the agent verifies; over SSH there's no agent, so the **tailnet + ACLs are the auth** and `nonce:'ssh'` is a sentinel (it correlates nothing). Don't try to sign the SSH path or route it through `pending`.
- **Same allowlist template, wrapped — never a shell string.** `renderSshLifecycle` calls the SAME `renderLifecycleCommand` and passes the remote argv after `ssh … -- `, so there's no interpolation surface. It returns `null` for an off-platform/off-allowlist command (e.g. `arco-agent.restart` on macOS) and the hub reports `instance-offline` rather than running anything. Don't build the remote command as a string.
- **Targets are operator-supplied, never discovered, never committed.** `~/.arco/hub/ssh-targets.json` keyed by instanceId OR friendly name (`sshTargetFor` tries id then name). `parseSshTargets` drops malformed entries / bad platforms / non-JSON → empty map (no SSH fallback). The file holds host/user identity → keep it out of git.
- **`systemctl --user` over SSH needs the user session.** The target must SSH as the **user that owns the units** (`user:"operator"`, not root) so `systemctl --user` hits that user's bus; this requires `enable-linger <user>` (B1 install does it) or an active session. SSHing as root would target the wrong (system) manager. The path is verified the moment workstation becomes a real systemd `--user` service.

## ntfy external transport (§J2)

`server/src/ntfyTransport.ts` + the `registerNoticeTransport` seam on `server/src/notifications.ts`. Invariants:

- **INERT unless `ARCO_NTFY_URL` is set.** `initNtfyTransport` returns `false` and registers nothing with no env — an ordinary `npm run dev` must stay byte-unchanged (same gating contract as `initHubLink`). Never push by default.
- **Mute is enforced UPSTREAM — never re-check it in the transport.** `emit()` returns `null` for a muted category *before* the transport fan-out, so a transport only ever sees surviving notices. This is the load-bearing single-chokepoint promise: muting an in-app category suppresses the push for free. Don't add mute logic to `ntfyTransport.ts`.
- **Transports are fire-and-forget; they can never break the in-app path.** `emit()` calls each transport in a try/catch (sync throws caught) and swallows async rejections (`.catch(()=>{})`). A slow/down ntfy must not delay or fail the `workbench_notice` broadcast. Don't `await` transports inside `emit()`.
- **ntfy header values are ascii-safe + single-line.** `headerSafe()` transliterates em/en-dashes + smart quotes to ASCII, strips remaining non-ascii, collapses whitespace, caps 200 chars — because ntfy `Title`/etc. are latin-1 HTTP headers and a unicode title (Arco uses em-dashes liberally) would otherwise break the request. The body (POST payload, not a header) keeps unicode. Don't pass raw titles as headers.
- **`Priority` never uses `urgent`.** error/warning→`high`, success→`default`, info→`low`. `urgent` (max) can bypass Do-Not-Disturb — deliberately unused.
- **Console tap-link is derived, not hardcoded.** `deriveConsoleUrl(ARCO_HUB_URL)` maps `ws→http`/`wss→https` and drops the path (the console is the hub's HTTP root on the same port). `ARCO_CONSOLE_URL` overrides. Don't hardcode the console URL.
- **Deployed ntfy is tailnet-only + topic-is-the-secret.** On `hub-host`, ntfy `listen-http` binds the **tailnet IP `:3501`, never `0.0.0.0`**. `upstream-base-url: https://ntfy.sh` forwards only an opaque wakeup id (content stays on the tailnet). No auth in v1 — the **topic name is the secret**, so it lives in `<hub-host>:/root/ntfy/topic.txt` + the server `ARCO_NTFY_URL` and is **never committed** to the repo/docs. Runs under PM2 (`arco-ntfy`), not apt/systemd-wide, so the shared box is untouched.

## Per-workflow REMOTE opt-in (§1.4)

`WorkflowConfig.remoteEnabled` (default OFF, persisted to meta.json) is the documented `REMOTE CONTINUATION` gate. Invariants:

- **One flag, three gates, ALL on the owning instance.** Visibility (`initHubLink.snapshotWorkflows` `continue`s past opted-out workflows), actions (`initHubLink.applyAction` returns `remote-disabled`), and notifications (the ntfy transport's `isRemoteEnabled` predicate). The hub needs no change — filtering at the snapshot source means an opted-out workflow is never sent, so the VPS console omits it. Don't add hub-side filtering; keep the gate at the source.
- **Default OFF is load-bearing.** Absent/false ⇒ invisible + uncontrollable + silent. A workflow only reaches the phone/console after the operator flips `◉ REMOTE` in the composer. Never default it on, and never report a workflow the operator hasn't opted in.
- **A toggle re-pushes immediately.** `handleUpdatePrimaryConfig` calls `refreshHubReporting()` (→ `link.pushNow()` + `emitStatus()`) only when the value actually changed, so the console + header monitored-count update at once instead of on the 4 s timer. Guard on a real change to avoid spamming pushes.
- **`handleUpdatePrimaryConfig` lives in `server/src/ws/updatePrimaryConfig.ts`** (split out of `workflowHandlers.ts` for the size budget, re-exported there). The in-memory and meta.json field lists must stay in sync — add new per-tab config fields to BOTH blocks.

## Remote Control v2 (REMOTE_CLUSTER_CONTROL_PLAN.md — A/R phases, 2026-07-17)

- **The cluster switch OVERRIDES member flags, both directions.** `isWorkflowRemoteVisible` (initHubLink) is the ONE visibility rule: a clustered workflow follows `ClusterState.remoteEnabled` and its own `remoteEnabled` is ignored — a member flag flipped before joining must not leak the cluster, and the cluster switch must not need N member flips. The same rule gates snapshot, applyAction, monitored-count, AND the ntfy predicate in `index.ts` — change one, change all four (that's why it's one function).
- **Gates arming is a SECOND switch, never implied — and cluster-level for members (2026-07-18).** Remote conversation must never grant remote gate-approval authority. `areGatesRemoteResolvable` (initHubLink) is the one rule: clustered workflows follow `ClusterState.remoteGatesEnabled` (the cluster ⛨ GATES chip; member flags ignored), solo tabs their own `remoteGatesEnabled` (composer chip). `gate_resolve` re-validates server-side (`gates-disabled`); the console renders unarmed gates read-only. No remote arg-editing — approvals run the parked proposal as-is.
- **The console mailbox path uses `peek()`, never `drain()`.** Draining destroys the message files (§ Cluster mode); every remote mailbox surface is read-only reconstruction. Same for the R5 activity feed — bounded `events.jsonl` tail reads, no state mutation.
- **A1 forces at most ONE refresh per turn, and only when a retry slot remains.** The per-turn flag stops a dead refresh chain from looping; the `attempt < MAX_RETRY_ATTEMPTS - 1` guard stops a discarded-'retry' silent failure. `forceRefreshTokens(staleObtainedAt)` skips the refresh when another worker already rotated the chain — don't remove the obtainedAt guard (concurrent workers would burn each other's rotated tokens).
- **The manual NUDGE is not the idle nudger.** The idle nudger stays operator-facing-only (never prompts workers). The remote NUDGE verb is one operator tap = one canned visible prompt through `handleUserMessage` — per-tap, no standing behavior. Keep them distinct.
- **`cluster_member_reorder` must be a full permutation.** `setMemberOrder` rejects length/set mismatches so a stale drag can't drop or duplicate members. Order is display-only — nothing may start routing on members[] position.
- **initHubLink ↔ clusterHandlers is a deliberate ESM cycle** (refreshHubReporting one way, cluster action handlers the other). Both sides touch the other's exports only at call time — never add module-evaluation-time usage across that edge.

## Task children (Phase 7b)

- **Depth-1 cap is enforced at construction.** Children spawn with `claudeNativeTaskDisabled: true` in `WorkerConfig`; the worker checks the flag and `getNativeToolDefinitions({ taskDisabled: true })` drops `Task` from the tool registry sent to Anthropic. The dispatcher also early-returns `[error] Task is depth-capped at 1` if the model finds another path. Don't loosen — non-goals (`docs/REFACTOR_PLAN.md` §11) explicitly forbid autonomous orchestration trees.
- **`tasksByTaskId` map is per-worker, not global.** Only the parent that called `Task` can read its child's accumulated output via `TaskOutput`; cross-tab `TaskOutput` calls return `[error] no such task`. The map drops entries on parent dispose, so a child tab promoted to standalone (rare; operator-only) becomes unaddressable from the parent's side. Don't rebuild this as a global registry — it would let any Native worker peek at any other's children, breaking the depth-1 cap's containment story.
- **Parallel `Task` dispatch is `Promise.all` for `Task` blocks, sequential for everything else.** Task children share no filesystem state so concurrent dispatch is safe and substantially faster on fan-out turns. Other tools stay sequential because they often share the cwd's filesystem state (Edit-after-Read invariant, etc.).

## Preview tab

- **Stale `vite.config.js` shadows `vite.config.ts`.** Vite resolves `.js` before `.ts`. A stray `tsc` run that compiles `client/vite.config.ts` to `vite.config.js` (and `.d.ts`/`.js.map`) means every subsequent edit to the `.ts` is silently ignored — Vite loads the frozen `.js`. The three artifacts are gitignored but easy to forget. Symptom: Vite changes don't take effect even after restart; `npx vite --debug config` shows the old shape. If you see this, delete `client/vite.config.{js,js.map,d.ts}`. The repo's typecheck script must use `tsc --noEmit`; don't introduce a build step that emits next to the source.
- **Vite's bypass needs the bless map for transitive imports.** When `/src/main.ts` imports `/src/render/foo.ts`, the browser's Referer for the sub-import is `http://localhost:5173/src/main.ts` — NOT the iframe URL. A naive Referer regex (`/api/preview/<wfid>/`) misses the entire transitive import graph and they fall through to Arco's own Vite, which 404s as text/html → empty MIME → blocked module. Fix: every URL the bypass proxies gets blessed (path → wfid, 60 s TTL); subsequent requests whose Referer points at a blessed path also resolve. Bless by **pathname only** — Vite's pre-bundled deps include `?v=hash` query strings but the lookup uses `URL(ref).pathname`. Don't store `path?query` as the bless key.
- **The bypass rewrites `req.url` to include the explicit `/api/preview/<wfid>/` prefix.** Once a request is identified as preview-bound, it's forwarded with the rewritten URL so the server's `PREFIX_RE` matcher catches it without needing Referer. The browser still sees the response on its original request, so its module URL stays `/src/main.ts` — keeping the import graph consistent.
- **Preview proxy responses set `cache-control: no-store` + `connection: close` + drop the upstream `etag`.** Earlier symptoms were "iframe stays white after restart": Firefox had cached previous empty-MIME failures (when the proxy or port wasn't ready) under the canonical URL, then Etag-revalidated to 304 against Vite's index.html and kept the bad cached body. `no-store` prevents future caching, dropping etag prevents revalidation reuse, `connection: close` prevents response-boundary misalignment on parallel keep-alive fetches through the double-proxy chain (5173 → 3001 → 5181). All three together. If you remove any, expect a regression. The proxy also strips RFC 7230 hop-by-hop headers (`connection`, `keep-alive`, `transfer-encoding`, `upgrade`, `proxy-*`, `te`, `trailer`) in both directions — forwarding them confuses HTTP parsers.
- **Iframe is given a fixed logical viewport (width 1920, height per operator-selected aspect ratio: 1440 / 1200 / 1080 for 4:3 / 16:10 / 16:9), CSS-scaled to the largest fitting box-of-that-ratio.** Don't change the iframe to `width: 100%; height: 100%`; the dev server inside renders to its viewport size and most apps assume a desktop-class width. The fixed logical size + scale is what gives "no clipping at any pane size." `ResizeObserver` on the wrapper recomputes scale on every pane resize AND on aspect change. Aspect choice persists to localStorage `arco.preview.aspect` (global, not per-workflow). Width was originally 1280 — bumped to 1920 on 2026-04-28 because real desktop apps overflowed at 1280. Frame is centered in the pane both axes (`alignItems: center`); top-aligning leaves a tall black band below short content.
- **`previewAutoDetect` never overrides `manual: true`.** When the operator types a port and hits BIND, the binding is sticky — Layer 1 / Layer 2 detection still runs (in case the agent later spins up a fresh server) but cannot replace a manual entry. Don't unify the paths; the operator's choice always wins.
- **`freshlyBound: true` only on real binds, not hydration replay.** The auto-flip-to-PREVIEW UX hinges on this flag: replays stamp `false`. If you ever broadcast `preview_set` from a path that isn't a fresh operator/auto bind (e.g. a periodic refresh), set `freshlyBound: false` or the user gets yanked into the preview tab on every server restart.
- **`PreviewTab`'s scaling `useLayoutEffect` must depend on `src`.** The effect observes `fitWrapRef.current` to set `boxStyle`, but that wrapper div only mounts when `src` is non-null. Object refs don't notify effects when the underlying DOM node attaches — so on first BIND the wrapper appears in DOM but the effect (deps `[logicalH]` only) never re-runs, `boxStyle` stays null, and the iframe never renders. Symptom: BIND appears to do nothing until you switch tabs and back (which remounts the component). Keep `src` in the dep array.
- **Preview history is per-workflow and persisted to `meta.previewHistory`.** Every successful `set_preview_port` appends an entry (most-recent first, capped at 5, consecutive same-port dedupes to a timestamp refresh). `preview_history` rebroadcasts on hydrate alongside `preview_set` so reconnect doesn't lose the chain. If you add a new bind path (a third-party detector, an API trigger), make sure it goes through `handleSetPreviewPort` so history stays in sync — don't write `wf.preview` directly.
- **Right-pane tab state is per-workflow.** `RightPanel` stores `Record<workflowId, RightTab>` under `localStorage['arco.sidePanelTab.byWorkflow']`. Earlier this was a single global key, which leaked tab state across workflows (PREVIEW on one tab dragged every workflow onto PREVIEW). Default for unknown workflowIds is `'files'`. Don't reintroduce a single global key.

### Preview verification / capture (G1 — `docs/PREVIEW_VERIFY.md`)

- **The dev server is NOT a CDP endpoint.** It serves HTTP. "Capture the preview" means launch a *browser* and point it at `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/`. Don't try to connect CDP to the dev-server port — there's nothing listening for it there.
- **No bundled browser — ever.** The operator's box is LFS (manifest-tracked installs, never `curl|sh`); puppeteer/playwright download a Chromium on `npm install`, which is exactly what we can't do. `captureViaCdp.ts` drives a *system* Chrome/Chromium over the existing `ws` dep (no new dependency). If you're tempted to add puppeteer for "robustness", don't — extend `findBrowserBinary` instead.
- **Capture must degrade, never throw.** `capturePreview` always resolves; no browser / no port / nav failure all land in `{ ok:false, error }` (or `ok:true` with a navigation-failure console entry for a refused connection). The `finally` block *always* kills the headless child + removes the temp `--user-data-dir`, even on error — a leaked headless Chrome per capture would pile up fast.
- **The report references the screenshot by filename — no base64 data-URI embed.** Embedding bloats the report and, when G2 attaches it to a Review, ships a huge image the reviewer can't read (and pays tokens for). The UI shows the image via `/api/workflows/artifact-raw`. If you "improve" the report by inlining the screenshot, you'll regress both.
- **`/api/workflows/artifact-raw` must be matched BEFORE `/api/workflows/artifact`.** The JSON reader's prefix (`/api/workflows/artifact`) also matches the longer raw path; route order in `workflowDataRoutes.ts` is load-bearing. Same path-safety guards apply (sanitized name, resolved-prefix escape check).
- **`previewCaptures` is ephemeral (not replayed).** It lives only in the client store, not in `events.jsonl` replay — the artifacts on disk are the durable record. After a reconnect the strip is empty until the next capture; that's expected, don't add replay plumbing for it.

## Efficiency (Phase A/B/C/D/E)

- **Hook stdout parser is tolerant — never throws.** `parseHookOutput` (`server/src/providers/claude-native/hooks.ts`) treats non-JSON, malformed JSON, and non-object top-levels as `{}`. A hook that prints log lines to stdout is safe; only well-formed JSON whose top level is an object yields recognized fields. Don't wrap the parser in try/catch in callers — that's the contract; surface-area regressions break rtk integration.
- **`hook_rewrote_input` must fire on every PreToolUse rewrite.** Without the event, transcript review can't tell why a tool input differs from what the model emitted — looks like a model hallucination of the input. The chain is: `parseHookOutput(stdout).hookSpecificOutput.updatedInput` non-empty → `workflowEventBroadcast({type: 'hook_rewrote_input', ...})` → `broadcastWorkflow` → `events.jsonl`. If you add a new rewrite-capable surface (e.g. PostToolUse input rewrites in a future revision), emit the same event.
- **Last-write-wins across multiple hooks for `updatedInput`.** `{ ...input, ...updatedInput }` is applied in hook iteration order; each rewrite emits its own `hook_rewrote_input` event so the operator can see the chain. Matches Claude Code's documented behavior — don't switch to first-write-wins or deep-merge.
- **`config-protection` matches by basename, not regex on the full path.** `checkConfigProtection` (`pretoolGuards.ts`) resolves the input path against `cwd` then matches `basename()` against `PROTECTED_BASENAMES` plus a small regex set for `tsconfig.*.json`. A nested package's `tsconfig.json` is just as protected as the root's. Don't over-broaden the patterns (regression risk: blocking a source file named `eslint.config.ts` in user code). Don't use absolute-path globs unless we're explicitly carving out an exception path — they hide where the gate fires.
- **`config-protection` is per-tab relaxable via `efficiencyConfigProtection` — default ON, opt-out is explicit.** The gate now reads `(this.config.efficiencyConfigProtection ?? 'on') === 'on'` in `worker.ts:dispatchTool` before calling `checkConfigProtection`. Absent field ⇒ gate ON (safety default), so a missing value never silently disables it; only an operator flip to `'off'` (CONFIG-tab → CONFIG-PROTECTION GATE, Native-only) relaxes it. Wired exactly like `efficiencyFactForce` (protocol → meta.json → updatePrimaryConfig live-set + rehydrate restore → turnEngine per-turn resolve → seedWorker/WorkerManager) **except** there is NO new-tab default stamp: fact-force explicitly stamps `'off'` for new tabs so the workflow-created echo and saved metadata agree, while config-protection's `?? 'on'` fallback means its default is uniform and needs no stamping. Don't add a config-protection stamp — it would make an absent field and an explicit `'on'` indistinguishable on disk for no behavior gain. The gate is Native-only because `checkConfigProtection` only runs in the in-process Native `dispatchTool` (same protocol limit as fact-force — see the Codex note below); `turnEngine` hard-zeros non-Native transports to `'off'` so the flag is never a meaningless no-op flag on a CLI/Codex tab.
- **`tool_gate_triggered` is the audit signal — keep firing it from every guard.** Even if a guard becomes "default off in CONFIG-tab", the event still fires when the guard ran and decided "allowed". Operators reading transcripts depend on knowing which guards inspected which calls.
- **Auto-threshold (`efficiencyAutoCompact`) fires Arco COMPACT, not Anthropic compact.** The old signal-only version was replaced by between-turn auto-compaction. It must call `compactClaudeNativeWorker()` so the same visible `native_compacting` / `native_compacted` markers, keep-tail semantics, and cold-acquire behavior apply. Do not wire auto-threshold to `claudeNativeCompact` / Anthropic `compact_20260112`; that reintroduces hidden mid-turn context mutation.
- **Per-turn token telemetry uses API ground truth.** `primary_turn_token_usage.input/output/cacheRead/cacheCreate` come from the Anthropic / Codex API response; never estimate them. `thinkingBytes`/`toolResultBytes` are byte sums (deliberately labeled "B"/"KB" in the UI, not "tk") because no public Anthropic tokenizer matches their billing tokenizer. CLI/Codex transports set `toolResultBytes = -1` (unknown) — render as "—" not 0; 0 implies "no tool calls happened" which is wrong.
- **`turnMessageStart` index in Native must come BEFORE the operator message push.** The byte-counting walk (`worker.ts:runTurn`) starts from this index and sums thinking/tool_result blocks added during the turn. If you move the operator-message push above the index capture, the walk also counts the operator's message — but operator messages don't have thinking/tool_result blocks so the bug is silent until someone audits the numbers.
- **`TURN_TOKEN_USAGE_CAP = 200` is a sliding window, not the durable record.** `events.jsonl` has every turn forever; the client store caps at 200 because rendering 5000 rows in the Activity tab tanks scroll perf. If you need full history, write a `tokenStats(workflowId)` helper that scans `events.jsonl` server-side — don't bump the cap.
- **Filters never operate retroactively (cache invariant).** Phase C's `filterToolResult` runs only on the *current* turn's tool_result before it enters `messages[]`. Prior turns are never rewritten — that would invalidate every upstream cache breakpoint and turn a token-saving feature into a token-burner. If a future "rewrite history to save more" idea surfaces, the right surface is Phase 7c's operator-clicked `clear_tool_uses` (already cache-busts under operator control), not silent retroactive mutation.
- **Raw-tools artifacts must round-trip — operator must recover what the model didn't see.** When `tool_filtered.originalBytes !== filteredBytes`, the worker MUST write the pre-filter raw bytes to `artifacts/raw-tools/<turnId>-<toolUseId>.txt.gz` AND stamp `rawArtifactPath` on the event. Skipping the artifact write because "the filter looks safe" is the failure mode that turns Phase C from a transparent reduction into a silent context manipulation. The chat chip's path-copy affordance is operator-facing proof that recovery exists; if the file isn't there, the chip lies.
- **`branch_on_exit` is critical for compiler / test packs: errors land at the end.** `cargo test`, `pytest`, `jest` all emit the failure summary AFTER the test run. A naive `head_cap` on a failed run truncates exactly the bytes the operator and model need. Pack authors set `branch_on_exit: true` and the dispatcher flips `head_cap → tail_cap` when `exitCode !== 0`. Forgetting this on a new pack will silently bury errors — review every new pack's red-path test (`makeCargoTestRed` / `pytest red run` shape) before merging.
- **Filter pipeline applies BEFORE PostToolUse hook augmentation.** Order in `dispatchTool`: `executeNativeTool` → filter (Phase C) → PostToolUse hook stderr/`additionalContext` append. If you flip the order, hook output gets caught by `replace[]` / `skip[]` patterns meant for the tool's own output and operator-curated context disappears.
- **`tool_filtered` is emitted even when the filter is a passthrough (no byte delta).** Operators reading transcripts depend on knowing "the filter ran and chose not to shrink" vs "the filter never fired" — same shape rule as `tool_gate_triggered`. The `rawArtifactPath` field is the truth source for "did bytes change": absent ⇒ passthrough; present ⇒ recovery available.
- **Filename safety in `persistRawArtifact`.** `toolUseId` is sanitized via `replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9_-]/g, '_').slice(0, 64)` and prefixed with `currentTurnId.toString(36)`. Anthropic's tool-use ids today are URL-safe, but tightening to a regex-passable charset costs nothing and inoculates against a future SDK change that ships colons or slashes. Don't loosen back to raw-string.
- **Aggressive mode is identical to conservative until packs are promoted.** Phase C ships only conservative packs (`bash-cargo-test`, `bash-jest-vitest`, `bash-pytest`, `bash-git-status`, `bash-find`, `bash-grep`, `bash-ls`); `aggressive` resolves the same list. Promoting `bash-npm-install` / `bash-cargo-build` / `bash-docker-build` / `bash-git-push` from named-deferred to shipped requires a follow-up phase with operator data showing the conservative wins are real and the aggressive packs don't bury operator-relevant errors. Don't ship aggressive defaults without that data.
- **Bash dispatcher classifies by leading token only — piped commands count as their pre-pipe tool.** `classifyBashCommand` matches `head + sub` (e.g. `git status`, `cargo test`) on the first whitespace-delimited tokens after stripping `sudo` / `time` / env-var prefixes. Pipes, redirections, and command substitutions are NOT parsed: `find . -type f | wc -l` classifies as `find` and emits a `tool_filtered` event keyed to the `find-head-cap` pack — even though the actual tool stdout is `wc`'s 6-byte count. `originalBytes` / `filteredBytes` always refer to the FINAL tool output (post-pipe), not the unpiped command's hypothetical output. This is correct behavior (Phase C ships filtering on the bytes the model actually receives, not on shell-pipeline internals) but inflates `tool_filtered` event counts and produces lots of equal-byte passthrough events when the model defensively pipes. Don't "fix" this by parsing pipelines — that path leads to a shell parser inside the dispatcher.
- **`codebase-memory-mcp` ships a `--skip-config` flag — Arco MUST use it.** The upstream binary auto-edits user agent config files (`~/.claude/settings.json` and friends) on install / run unless `--skip-config` is passed. Both spawn sites hard-code it: the Phase F reindex route (`/api/workflows/:wfId/codebase-memory/reindex` in `server/src/http/clusterRoutes.ts`) and the F9 `serve` entry (`buildCodebaseMemoryEntry` in `server/src/mcp/mcpConfigWriter.ts`). Any future code path that invokes the binary inherits this invariant — grep for `codebase-memory-mcp` before merging anything that spawns it.
- **ADR is the only LLM-write surface in `codebase-memory-mcp`; operator-prompted only.** The binary itself is deterministic (tree-sitter graph + BM25 + SQL queries). The only path that triggers a write is `manage_adr`, and it fires only when the operator explicitly asks ("make an ADR for this"). Don't add auto-extraction surfaces ("after every primary_complete, save a fact"), don't surface ADR creation as a background action, don't let the model write ADRs unprompted. Those patterns are the canonical bad shape per `docs/EFFICIENCY_PLAN.md` §2 and the autonomy four-question test.
- **Phase F9 attach is spawn-time MCP registration, gated on project context.** `WorkflowConfig.codebaseMemory.attached` threads to `WorkerConfig.codebaseMemoryAttached` / `codebaseMemoryIndexPath` (`indexPath || cwd`) in `turnEngine.ts`. CLI Claude gets a `codebase-memory` entry in `.arco/<wfid>/mcp-config.json` via `mcpConfigWriter.writeFor`; both Codex transports layer the same child via `-c mcp_servers.*` overrides (app-server folds the index path into its bootstrap signature, so a path change recycles the child). Native ignores it. Chat tabs (`projectContextEnabled` false) never attach.
- **Fact-force gate must be silent on second+ edits per turn (`filesEditedThisTurn` set).** `checkFactForceFirstEdit` (`pretoolGuards.ts`) consults the worker's `filesEditedThisTurn` Set and returns `blocked: false` for any path already in the set — including a path that was blocked earlier this turn and is now being re-issued. The worker MUST `filesEditedThisTurn.add(resolvedPath)` on both the blocked AND the allowed branches (see `worker.ts:dispatchTool`); skipping the add on the blocked branch would re-fire the gate every time the model re-issued the edit and create an infinite ping-pong. The set is cleared at the top of `runTurn`; cancel paths return before mutating it, so a cancelled turn leaves the prior set in place — defensible because the gate engages on the NEXT operator message regardless.
- **Fact-force defaults OFF (2026-09-02).** It is a cognitive nudge, not a verifier: the synthetic failed tool result makes the model recite importers, API surface, and the operator instruction, but Arco does not check whether those statements are true. Operator use showed that the per-file failure + prose + retry cost polluted the transcript and append-only context more than it helped. New workflows explicitly stamp `'off'`; absent values also resolve off; a saved or newly supplied explicit `'on'` remains honored. Keep it available as a per-tab CONFIG opt-in, but do not restore a default-on path without measured operator evidence.
- **Pre-tool-use guard order is load-bearing: config-protection first, fact-force second.** Two reasons: (1) config-protection is a cheap path-shape check (no state), so running it first short-circuits without touching the fact-force state. (2) If both rules fire on the same path (e.g. operator armed fact-force AND the model edits `tsconfig.json`), the operator gets the more specific config-protection message — "fix your code instead of weakening the config" beats "list importers and re-issue." Don't reverse the order; the model's re-issued edit after fact-force would then bounce off config-protection on the second pass, which is twice the synthetic-result churn.
- **Fact-force is Native-only at first ship — CLI parity is F4 deferred.** The gate lives inside `claude-native/dispatchTool`, which only runs on the Native transport. CLI Claude reaches the same surface via the `.arco/<wfid>/hooks.json` shim documented as F4 in `docs/MASTER_REFACTOR.md` §3.3. Don't add a CLI codepath inside `pretoolGuards.ts`; the canonical site for CLI parity is the hooks shim, not the gate function. If operator data shows CLI tabs need it before F4 ships, prefer the shim over a parallel implementation — the gate semantics MUST match Native exactly or transcript review on cross-transport sessions will be confusing.
- **Fact-force (and most other Native-only efficiency knobs) being absent for Codex is a protocol limit, not an oversight — don't "port" it.** The Native gate works because tool dispatch is synchronous and in-process: Arco returns a synthetic `tool_result` *before* the edit lands. Codex gives no equivalent pre-execution gate — `exec` only surfaces tool calls in the final aggregated output (no mid-turn hook at all), and `appserver` *has* approval bindings (`FileChangeRequestApprovalParams`, `CommandExecutionRequestApprovalParams`) but they're dropped in `onNotification`'s `default` case and there is **no approval-response RPC path** wired to send a denial back. Wiring it is ~500 LOC against an undocumented approval-response shape (needs a live Codex harness to pin down) and would bolt a pre-execution gate onto Codex's async-after-completion event model. Same root reason server-side compaction, interleaved-thinking beta, tool-result filter, and sandbox tools can't exist for Codex today: they all mutate the in-process Anthropic request (body / beta headers / `tools[]` / system blocks), which an external subprocess doesn't expose. Output style is the narrow exception for Codex app-server / Z.ai because Arco owns the stable per-turn input prefix there. If fact-force on Codex ever becomes worth it, it's a standalone initiative starting from the approval protocol, not a parity shim. (The F4 hooks shim above is for **Claude CLI**, which Codex does not read.)
- **`ctx_action` sentinels must be emitted from every code path that mutates `messages[]` in a way the operator should see during transcript review.** Today's emit sites (`worker.ts`): `prune_orphan_tool_use` (trigger: recovery) on the orphan-drop at the top of `runTurn`, and `compact` (trigger: auto-threshold) inside `maybeEmitAutoThreshold`. Normal turn-flow pushes (operator message, assistant message, tool_results, drainSteer items) do NOT emit ctx_action — they're additions, not reshapes. `seedMessages` (Spawn-from-here init), `replayMessagesFromEvents` (rehydrate), and `dispose` (tear-down) are lifecycle paths that also don't emit. Server-side `compact_20260112` mutates Anthropic's prompt prefix, not our local `messages[]`, so it doesn't emit ctx_action from Arco either — it rides as a per-turn body field, not an event. Same rule applies if `clear_thinking` / `clear_tool_uses` ever return behind an explicit operator action. Forgetting the sentinel on a NEW path that does mutate local `messages[]` (e.g. a future "cancel-the-last-turn-and-reissue" surface) breaks transcript review silently; the operator sees turn N+1 without knowing turn N was rewound.
- **`primary_tool_counter` is the worker-authoritative chip signal — don't recount from events.** Phase 6b.1 shipped the chip reading `JobState.toolCallsSinceHandoff` (a client-side recount of `primary_tool_use` events). Phase D §8b added `JobState.toolCallsSinceCompact` mirrored from the worker's `toolCallCountSinceCompact`. The chip prefers the worker mirror; the recount stays as the audit fallback for non-Native tabs and for the first paint before the worker emits.
