Worker rebuild after `handoff_complete` is the implicit reset (the new worker starts at counter=0); the client also eagerly zeroes the mirror on `handoff_complete` to avoid a stale paint between the handoff and the first post-handoff tool dispatch. Auto-threshold compact firing inside `maybeEmitAutoThreshold` emits a follow-up counter=0 event after the reset; without it, the chip would stay at `n` until the next tool dispatch.
- **Phase E sandbox.db MUST be cleared on tab close (`remove_workflow`) or the operator's explicit Clear-DB click.** `destroySandbox(cwd, wfId)` closes the cached `better-sqlite3` handle and unlinks `sandbox.db` + `sandbox.db-wal` + `sandbox.db-shm`. Skipping the tear-down on tab close would leak per-tab state across workflows that share a cwd — defeating the per-tab opt-in contract. The teardown path is in `handleRemoveWorkflow` (`server/src/index.ts`) and the operator clear hits `POST /api/workflows/:wfId/sandbox/clear`. Both fire WS events (`sandbox_db_cleared`) so transcript review can see the truncation. If a future code path adds another lifecycle endpoint that should also clear (e.g. a hypothetical "reset workflow"), call `destroySandbox` from there too.
- **Sandbox subprocess executor MUST kill the process group on timeout, not just the immediate child.** `sandboxExecutor.ts` spawns with `detached: true` so the child becomes the process-group leader, and the timeout path calls `process.kill(-child.pid, 'SIGTERM')` (negative PID — the kernel interprets that as the process group) with a 1 s SIGKILL escalation. A python script that forks workers or a shell script that `&`-launches background jobs would leak grandchildren past a naive `child.kill('SIGTERM')`. Don't refactor to the simpler form — the test that surfaced this on context-mode's PolyglotExecutor (the inspiration) was a `python -c "import multiprocessing; multiprocessing.Pool(4).map(time.sleep, [60]*4)"` script that leaked 4 sleepers per call.
- **`node:sqlite` (Node 22+ builtin) has NO FTS5 module — Phase E uses `better-sqlite3` instead.** The plan brief listed `node:sqlite` as a fallback option; in practice Node 22.14.0's bundled SQLite is compiled without FTS5 so `CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE … USING fts5(…)` returns `Error: no such module: fts5`. Don't switch back to `node:sqlite` without verifying FTS5 availability on the target Node version. The `SandboxStore` class encapsulates the choice; if a future Node release lights up FTS5 (or someone wants a loadable-extension build), swapping is a single-file refactor.
- **Phase E FTS5 tokenizer is `unicode61` (the default) — NO stopwords applied.** Spot-checks show 'added' / 'fix' / 'the' all survive tokenization and match exactly. The original brief warned about stopword false-negatives; the inverse is the actual invariant — DO NOT switch to a tokenizer that adds stopwords (e.g. `porter` with a stopword set) without measuring the impact on technical search. The fact that short common English words match is load-bearing for the kind of "search for 'added the foo handler'" the model is likely to issue.
- **Phase E nudge ordering: cluster plan + role BEFORE sandbox nudge BEFORE operator's appendSystemPrompt / systemPrompt / output style.** `buildSystemBlocks()` appends in that order so cluster framing wins over the sandbox nudge (cluster context is more load-bearing) but the sandbox nudge wins over operator prompts only insofar as it comes earlier — Anthropic's system block ordering doesn't enforce precedence, but the prompt-cache implications (sandbox nudge gets its own cache-control:ephemeral block) and the model's reading order both rely on this sequence. Don't insert the sandbox block before plan / role; don't append it after the operator's blocks. Documented as a forced-block invariant in `docs/EFFICIENCY_PLAN.md §14`.
- **Sandbox tools are per-tab, not per-cluster — each cluster member has its own sandbox.db.** Cluster mode and sandbox mode interact orthogonally. If a future operator wants a shared knowledge graph across a cluster, the answer is "attach codebase-memory-mcp" (Phase F), not "extend the sandbox to be cluster-wide." Cluster-wide sandbox would (a) re-introduce a shared-state coordination problem on the path Phase E deliberately took to avoid, and (b) cross the per-tab opt-in contract.
- **`update_primary_config` field threading needs FOUR call sites or the toggle dies silently.** Adding a new per-tab config field (e.g. `efficiencyToolFilter`, `outputStyle`) requires edits to: (1) `WorkflowConfig` and `WorkerConfig` in `shared/src/protocol/workflows.ts` and `UpdatePrimaryConfigMessage`, (2) `useWebSocket.updatePrimaryConfig`'s undefined-check + spread, (3) `handleUpdatePrimaryConfig` in `server/src/ws/updatePrimaryConfig.ts` (`wf.config` mutation + `meta.json` mirror — both blocks), and (4) `appStore.updateJobPrimary`'s parameter type AND its setter logic. Skipping (4) is silent: TS structural typing accepts the wider object passed from `App.tsx`'s `onUpdatePrimary` arg into the narrower `updateJobPrimary` action, the WS message goes through, the server persists — but the local optimistic update never lands so the CONFIG-tab button highlight never moves. Surfaced 2026-05-09 on Phase C smoke-test; same trap will catch any future field. When adding a new field, grep for the existing `claudeNativeCompact` threading as the canonical four-place template.
- **Z.ai Native provider history must preserve `reasoning_content` exactly.** `zai-native/worker.ts` persists hidden `zai_native_history` snapshots because GLM preserved thinking depends on resending prior assistant `reasoning_content` alongside assistant `tool_calls` and matching tool results. Do not summarize, trim, normalize whitespace, emit it as `primary_thinking`, or convert that field into visible chat text. If a tool round is incomplete on replay, prune the dangling assistant/tool pair rather than inventing a placeholder; sending orphaned tool-call state poisons the next Chat Completions request. Operator COMPACT is the explicit exception: it is a visible context replacement that emits `native_compacted`, replaces old native history with a summary seed, then persists a fresh `zai_native_history` snapshot for exact post-compact replay.

### Output style (OUTPUT_STYLE_PLAN.md)

- **Addendum text is checked into the repo — not LLM-generated, not session-modified.** `outputStyleAddendum(level)` (`server/src/providers/outputStyle.ts`) is the single source of truth for the per-level provider-prompt text. Editing it requires a code change + commit; no operator-facing knob can mutate the body. This is the autonomy-test guardrail: an LLM can't curate its own instructions. If a future "let the model self-tune the addendum" idea surfaces, that's the canonical bad pattern from `CLAUDE.md`'s hard fences — reject without a plan-doc update + operator sign-off.
- **Review / Handoff seed / DOCS / GIT primitives MUST run with `outputStyle: 'normal'` regardless of source-tab pick.** Hard-coded at four sites (plan §6): `runReviewer.ts` (reviewer WorkerConfig), `exportContext.ts` (CLI handoff path, Native handoff path, fresh-summarizer path), and `handleUserMessage` (overrides outputStyle when `UserMessage.primitive === 'docs' | 'git'`, set by the in-tab DOCS / GIT composer dialogs). DOCS / GIT new-tab spawn forces normal client-side via `spawnConfigFromRunAs`. Terse-style reviews regress operator comprehension; terse handoff seeds reduce the *next* worker's input fidelity. Don't make this a per-spawn knob. If a future operator argues for a terse review, treat it as operator data toward changing the default — not as a config-knob escape hatch.
- **Spawn-from-here DOES inherit the parent's `outputStyle`.** Different from the four primitives above. `SpawnDialog` spreads `...parentConfig`, so `outputStyle` flows through; the operator can flip in the child tab. Don't add an opt-out at `SpawnDialog` for `outputStyle` — terse → terse is the explicit operator pick on the parent and they kept it for a reason. Inheritance is the right default for a fresh-but-related tab.
- **Addendum text MUST preserve code blocks / error strings / file paths verbatim AND MUST NOT compress security warnings, irreversible operations, or order-sensitive multi-step sequences.** Each level's text leads with explicit "auto-clarity" rules and a verbatim-preserve list. A new level (e.g., a hypothetical `ultra`) shipped without these rules will silently regress technical correctness — the model will compress a sed command's flags or shorten an error string. Don't ship a new level without the verbatim list; review the existing levels' text as the floor.

## Cluster mode (CLUSTER_MODE_PLAN.md Phase 1)

- **BLIND is context isolation, ISOLATED is filesystem isolation.** BLIND
  keeps the project cwd but receives no plan/board/roster/role/focus/efficiency
  or progress context and gets only `peer_send` / `peer_read`. ISOLATED is
  Claude-Native-only, executes in
  `~/.arco/clusters/<clusterId>/isolates/<wfid>/workspace`, removes Bash,
  hooks, project prompts/refs/attachments/settings/skills/knowledge/goal,
  codebase memory and registry MCPs, and still persists events/meta under the
  real project workflow directory. Do not weaken this to a prompt-only rule.
- **A BLIND member cannot transition its assigned board task; completion returns by mail.** Task assignment/transfer may still point at a BLIND workflow so the operator and STANDARD members can track ownership, but every plan/task tool is intentionally absent and the shared peer-ops layer returns `access_denied` if the member guesses the real REST shim. The member must finish the substantive work, `peer_send` its report, and ask a STANDARD member/operator to update the board. `BLIND_CLUSTER_DOCTRINE` is shared by Native, local, and string-form providers and explicitly forbids probing Arco HTTP APIs/workflow storage to recover hidden state. Keep this centralized: the 2026-07-30 Cursor/Kimi incident had an impossible “move task to review” assignment, so Kimi spent four shell/grep probes guessing `/api/tasks` routes before correctly escalating.
- **Entering ISOLATED always means replacement.** Reusing a workflow/session
  that previously saw the project would leak retained context. ISOLATED rejects
  transcript import and task transfer. Replacement keeps the old workflow on
  disk, atomically swaps the cluster slot, and discards its mailbox.
- **Isolated artifacts cross the boundary only through mailbox copies.**
  `peer_send artifact_paths` accepts paths rooted in the isolated workspace,
  at most 8 files / 256 KB total. On drain Arco writes copies under the
  recipient's `.arco/peer-artifacts/<msgId>` (or its own isolated scratch).
  Never expose the project cwd to an isolated sender as an artifact shortcut.

- **Mailbox is per-recipient, not per-cluster — don't index it the wrong way.** Files live at `~/.arco/clusters/<clusterId>/mailbox/<recipientWorkflowId>/<msgId>.json`. `clusterMailbox.enqueue/peek/drain` take a single `wfId` (the recipient) and resolve the cluster via `clusterStore.getClusterIdForWorkflow`. A bug that scans `mailbox/` flat would commingle all recipients' inboxes; a bug that scans every cluster looking for `<msgId>` would cost O(clusters × messages) on every operation. Stick to the per-recipient layout — `peek(wfId)` reads exactly one directory.
- **Peer tools must be gated on `worker.config.clusterId` — leaking them into non-cluster workflows breaks the no-orchestrator invariant.** `worker.ts:buildToolDefs` returns peer descriptors only when `clusterId !== undefined`; the dispatch branch in `dispatchTool` mirrors the same gate. A workflow that the operator never opted into a cluster has no business seeing `peer_send` in its tool list — the four-question test (operator-opted-in) fails the moment a non-cluster worker can address peers it has no relationship with. If a future change adds a "tool list debug dump" or similar, do not bypass this gate.
- **Tool names use underscores, not the plan's dotted form.** Anthropic's tool-name regex is `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,64}$`; `peer.list` would 400 at API call time. Wire-level names are `peer_list / peer_send / peer_read`. Plan §13 carries the decision. When wiring CLI / Codex transports in Phase 3+ via MCP, the namespace prefix (`mcp__arco-cluster__peer_list`) similarly must avoid periods inside the tool-name segment.
- **Pre-turn peer-mailbox handling is depth-notice, NOT auto-drain (superseded — see the 2026-05-11 afternoon entry "Peer mailbox no longer auto-drains pre-turn" below for the canonical contract).** The original Phase 1 shape called `drainPeerMailbox` before `user_message_echo` so `peer_message_received` events landed first; that was reverted because the subsequent `peer_read` then returned empty (drain already consumed the files) and the model conflated "nothing to read" with "no work to do." Current code (`server/src/ws/turnEngine.ts`) reads `clusterMailbox.depthFor(workflowId)` on `firstIteration` and folds a `--- PEER MAILBOX ---` notice with the pending count into `appendSystemPrompt`; the actual drain + `peer_message_received` events fire on the worker's `peer_read` call. The drain-destroys-files / events.jsonl-is-the-durable-record invariant still holds — the drain just moved to `peer_read`. Don't re-introduce pre-turn auto-drain.
- **The `peerMessagesPrologue` system block bypasses prefix cache for that turn.** The prologue text is per-turn variable (different mailbox contents), so it cache-busts the `appendSystemPrompt` block from the operator's pick onward. This is fine — peer messages are new content the model has never seen — but don't try to "stabilize" the prologue by hashing or omitting timestamps. The unique line-per-message + timestamp shape is what makes injection traceable; the cache cost is the price of that traceability.
- **Drain destroys the mailbox file; the durable record lives in `events.jsonl`.** `clusterMailbox.drain` removes the file after returning the message — the corresponding `peer_message_received` event in events.jsonl is the audit trail. If you want a "view the full mailbox history" UI in a later phase, scan events.jsonl, don't try to re-keep mailbox files. Phase 1 keeping mailbox files post-drain only grows unboundedly with no read path.
- **The send budget resets per-sender on `primary_turn_started`, not per-cluster or per-message.** `clusterBudget.reset(workflowId)` fires from `seedWorker.runPrimaryTurn` for every member starting a new turn. A member that hits the 15-cap can keep sending again only after the operator advances *its* turn — not after a peer's turn or a wall-clock interval. If you ever add a "system-initiated turn" path (auto-handoff, scheduled refresh) and want it to count against the budget, call `recordSend`; if you want it not to count, leave the counter alone — but don't quietly skip `reset` because the turn was system-initiated.
- **`peer_send_rejected` MUST emit on every refusal — silent budget violations are a hard fence (plan §6.2).** Three rejection paths: budget exhausted, unknown peer, content too large (and in principle `recipient_not_in_cluster`, though the resolver catches that as `unknown_peer`). All four return an `[error]` string visible to the model AND fire the WS event. If you add a fourth rejection class, follow the same shape: model-visible `[error] <code>: ...` + `peer_send_rejected` event with a stable `reason` code.
- **No depth-2 clusters; no cross-cluster membership.** `clusterStore.createCluster` rejects when any candidate workflowId already lives in a cluster (`member_already_in_cluster`). Reversal path is `DELETE /api/clusters/<id>` — clears the membership index, then the same workflowIds are re-clusterable. Don't add a `move-member` API: the right shape is delete + re-create, which makes the change visible at the events.jsonl level.
- **`createCluster` is the one validation site for the "no nested clusters" fence.** Today it rejects only a wfid already in `memberIndex` (depth-2 / cross-cluster). The cluster tab is keyed by `clusterId`, not a workflow, and although `WorkflowConfig.kind` admits `'cluster'` (skipped by the busy sweep and hub reporting), no server path creates one — so the "member is itself a cluster" case is still forward-compat. If a path ever mints a `kind:'cluster'` workflow, add an explicit reject clause here. The fence belongs at the validation site, not at the peer.send dispatch (where it would silently neuter cross-cluster sends instead of refusing the cluster).
- **REST endpoints are the only Phase 1 surface — no WS messages yet.** `POST /api/clusters`, `GET /api/clusters/:id`, `DELETE /api/clusters/:id`. Phase 2 adds `cluster_*` WS messages and a UI tab; until then, operator wires clusters via curl. Don't pre-emptively add WS handlers — the shape isn't stable until the UI design lands.
- **Member display names default to the 8-char workflowId prefix.** `clusterStore.displayNameFor(wfid)` returns `wfid.slice(0,8)`. The plan §12.5 open question (operator-editable names) is deferred to Phase 2 with the UI. When you add the editable name in Phase 2, persist it on the *cluster*'s `meta.json` rather than the member workflow's meta — names are cluster-relative (the same workflow could in principle be re-clustered with a different display name, though the no-cross-cluster fence makes that simultaneous case impossible).
- **Handoffs serialize per-cwd via `cwdHandoffChain` (`server/src/server/state.ts`, consumed by `ws/handoffHandlers.ts`).** Two cluster members sharing a cwd would otherwise race to read git state + prepend DEVLOG.md and observe each other's mid-handoff writes (reflection turns are read-then-write against shared root docs). `handleHandoffRequest` installs a `Promise<void>` slot on `cwdHandoffChain.get(cwd)` after the sync validation gates; subsequent handoffs in the same cwd `await` the prior promise before reading repo state. The slot releases from a `try { ... } finally { releaseHandoffSlot() }` tail so every return path (settle failure, no-transcript reject, clean completion) frees the next queued handoff. **Don't move the queue setup before the wf-busy / cwd validation** — those need to return errors synchronously without occupying a slot. After the `await`, re-check `workflows.has(wfid)` and `wf.busy` since the wait can span an unrelated cancel or operator-driven turn-start. The chain key is `cwd`, not `clusterId` — standalone tabs in unrelated cwds run in parallel; only same-cwd handoffs serialize.

### §D3 — Task board + task peer-tools (CLUSTER_MODE_PLAN.md §10.6)

- **`done` / `archived` are operator-gated AT THE PEER LAYER — `peerTaskUpdate` rejects them before touching the ledger.** The §D1 invariant ("only the operator's `task_ledger_update` reaches `applyUpdate` with a terminal status") now spans the cluster peer surface. `clusterPeerOps.peerTaskUpdate` returns `{ ok:false, code:'operator_gated' }` when `status === 'done' || status === 'archived'`, so `applyUpdate` never sees those statuses from a worker. This is **not** a global block — the operator's WS path still reaches `applyUpdate(done/archived)` and succeeds. Don't "simplify" by moving the peer-layer check into `applyUpdate` itself: that would also break the operator path, which is the legitimate terminal-status setter. Asserted by `scripts/smoke/task-board.ts` steps (d) + (e); regressing either is a hard fail.
- **The task peer-tools are gated on `clusterId` via `getPeerToolDefinitions()` — same gate as `peer_*` / `cluster_plan_*`.** `cluster_task_list/show/create/claim/update/heartbeat/link/unlink/comment/block/complete` are in `PEER_TOOL_NAMES` and the schema array, both consumed by `worker.ts:buildToolDefs` only when `clusterId !== undefined`. `buildToolDefs` enumerates via the function, not a hand-maintained name list, so the gate covers them automatically. Don't add a separate registration path that bypasses the clusterId check — a non-cluster workflow has no shared cwd ledger to read.
- **`cluster_task_list` returns a COMPACT model-facing view (`taskLedger.formatBoardForModel`), NOT the raw ledger — don't "simplify" it back to a full dump.** The raw `JSON.stringify(tasks)` dump is all statuses, oldest-first, full notes; the worker's tool result is hard-capped at 64KB (`MAX_TOOL_OUTPUT_BYTES`, applied by `truncateOutput` on Native / the transport on CLI/Codex), so the byte-slice lopped off the NEWEST/active rows and left invalid JSON cut mid-object. On a 128-task board (120 done) the live rows were never visible — a worker couldn't see its own active work. The formatter drops done/archived from `tasks` (counts them in `completedCount`, lists the latest 10 in `recentlyCompleted`; any task still fetchable by id via `cluster_task_show`), strips notes to a one-line preview + length, sorts active newest-updated-first, and bounds to valid JSON under a 48KB budget (dropping oldest from the tail, reporting `omittedActive`). Applied at BOTH model-facing seams — `peerTools.ts` (Native) and the `/task-list` REST endpoint (read by the MCP child for CLI/Codex). The operator UI is untouched: it renders from `task_ledger_snapshot` WS broadcasts, which still carry the full task set. Pinned by `npm run test:board-view -w server`.
- **`cluster_task_claim` concurrency guard lives INSIDE the per-cwd lock, not before it.** The owner check runs inside the `persistLedgerMutation` mutate callback (which holds the per-cwd lock), so two concurrent claims serialize and the second sees the first's `assignee`. A pre-lock read-then-claim would race: both members would read "unclaimed" and the second would stomp the first. If you refactor the claim path, keep the guard under the lock.
- **cwd is derived from `clusterStore.readCluster(clusterId).cwd`, NOT the caller's config.** A cluster has one authoritative shared cwd on its record; a member workflow may be unhydrated (`workflows.get(wfid)` misses). The plan-ops resolve cwd the same way. Don't switch the task-ops to `workflowCwds.get(callerWfid)` — that's the workflow-scoped operator path's source and can disagree with the cluster's cwd.
- **Task mutations broadcast `task_ledger_snapshot` CLUSTER-wide, plan-tick-style — not workflow-scoped.** The operator WS path broadcasts on the originating workflow (its observers look at that cwd); the peer-ops must reach every member's board, so they take a `broadcastCluster` callback exactly like `peerPlanTick`. The durable `task_ledger_event` is still appended to the *acting* member's events.jsonl via `persistLedgerMutation` → `broadcastWorkflow`, so rebuild folds it. Don't drop the cluster-wide snapshot in favor of the per-workflow one — other members' boards would go stale until their next snapshot.
- **Task comments/runs are source-of-truth event payloads, not only snapshot decoration.** `cluster_task_comment`, `cluster_task_block`, `cluster_task_complete`, and note-bearing `cluster_task_heartbeat` append a `TaskComment` in the `task_ledger_event.payload.comment` field; `cluster_task_heartbeat` also carries `payload.run`. `saveKernel()` materializes them into `tasks.json`, and `loadKernel()` rebuilds them from event payloads when the snapshot is missing/corrupt. If you add more lifecycle tools, carry durable handoff text through `payload.comment` and run state through `payload.run`; otherwise details vanish after snapshot rebuild.
- **H48: `cluster_task_complete` enforces hallucinated-id + ownership guards INSIDE the per-cwd lock.** A worker can only complete a task that EXISTS in this cluster's ledger AND has `assignee === itself` — both checked inside the `persistLedgerMutation` mutate callback (under the lock, so a concurrent claim can't slip an owner change past the check). An id not in the ledger → `not_found` (stops a member fabricating/guessing an id); another member's / unassigned task → `not_owner` (with a "coordinate via peer_send" / "claim it first" hint). Complete moves `in_progress → review`; the operator's task-ledger update path stays the ONLY `done`/`archived` setter. Mirrors `cluster_task_claim`'s in-lock owner pre-check — don't hoist either guard above the lock.
- **`cluster_dispatch_once` is explicit-assignee-only and persists before prompting.** The dispatcher does not infer from role/tier/focus and does not ask a model to route. It scans ready tasks deterministically, rejects anything without a concrete idle supported assignee, writes `dispatch_fired` with the updated task plus `payload.run` under the per-cwd lock, then starts the synthetic worker turn. `dispatch_skipped` is durable for every ready-task refusal. `rebuildRunsFromEvents()` must keep folding `dispatch_fired.payload.run`; otherwise run history disappears after snapshot rebuild.
- **The armed task dispatcher daemon is just repeated one-shot dispatch behind a persisted OFF-by-default gate.** `dispatcher.enabled` lives in cluster meta and mirrors on `cluster_state`; absent means OFF. The interval loop may call only the same deterministic dispatch kernel as `cluster_dispatch_once`. Do not add a second routing path, role/tier/focus inference, or model-based assignment in the daemon.
- **Worktree-required dispatch is also operator-armed.** `LedgerTask.worktreeRequired` is just a task flag. The dispatcher must skip it with `worktree_required` unless the task already has a server-set `worktree` or cluster dispatcher `autoWorktree` is explicitly true. When auto-provisioning, create the managed worktree before `dispatch_fired`, persist the pointer in that same mutation, and include the path/branch in the prompt. Do not silently switch the member workflow cwd; Stage J only prompts the worker to edit in the task worktree.
- **Daemon sweeps must not double-dispatch open runs, including after restart.** The open-run check belongs inside the persisted dispatch path, not only in daemon memory. A server restart forgets timers but not `dispatch_fired.payload.run`; after replay, a task with a `claimed`/`running` run must be skipped with `active_run`, never fired again.
- **Disarming the daemon stops new fires only.** `cluster_dispatch_toggle(enabled:false)` clears/sets meta state and cancels future interval work, but it must not complete, fail, reclaim, or delete already-open runs. Worker lifecycle stays on `cluster_task_heartbeat`, `cluster_task_block`, `cluster_task_complete`, and later explicit recovery controls.
- **The dispatcher is not a terminal-status path.** Fired tasks move `ready -> in_progress`; workers complete via `cluster_task_complete` into `review`; only the operator's task-ledger update path marks `done` / `archived`. Do not add auto-done, quality verdicts, or server-side "is this complete?" checks to the dispatcher.
- **The cluster BOARD drawer may expose DONE / ARCHIVE only through the operator WS path.** The drawer's status selector calls the same `task_ledger_update` sender as the standalone LEDGER tab, using a cluster member workflowId only to resolve the cwd. Do not route terminal statuses through `cluster_task_update` / peer tools, and do not let Director acceptance auto-apply terminal state. The operator click is the acceptance boundary.
- **`task_run_recover` mutates exactly one selected run.** Reclaim / terminate / reassign / fail all look up `runId` inside the per-cwd lock and reject if it does not belong to `taskId`. Never "recover the task" by bulk-closing every open run; sibling attempts must stay intact unless the operator targets them too. Reclaim returns the task to `ready` and clears assignee, reassign resolves the target cluster member and sets assignee, terminate blocks immediately, and fail blocks only after the configured consecutive-failure limit. None of these actions may set `done` / `archived`.
- **Stale reclaim must be checked server-side, not only by the button state.** The BOARD UI disables `RECLAIM` until an open run has no heartbeat for five minutes, but the authoritative guard is `staleOnly` in `handleTaskRunRecover`, using `lastHeartbeatAt ?? startedAt` under the lock. Keep that check even if the UI grows better stale indicators; otherwise a stale-looking row from an old snapshot could reclaim a fresh heartbeat.
- **Stage I swarm creation is graph construction, not orchestration.** `cluster_swarm_create` should create ordinary ledger tasks and dependencies: root blackboard, N workers, verifier depending on all workers, synthesizer depending on verifier. It must not create a parallel swarm store, infer assignees through a model, or mark non-root tasks `done`. If the operator asks to dispatch after creation, call the existing deterministic dispatcher path and keep the same terminal-status fence; the smoke target is `scripts/smoke/task-swarm.ts`.
- **Stage K orchestration assist is proposal-only until explicit accept.** Assist requests may draft task graphs, assignments, swarm layouts, recovery actions, synthesis comments, and provider/model/effort suggestions, but they must not mutate task state, dispatcher settings, member config, or worktrees by themselves. Reject is audit-only. Accept selected items by calling the normal task-kernel mutation path so ordinary `task_ledger_event` rows are emitted; provider/model/effort suggestions stay inert unless their own selected items are accepted. Smoke target: `scripts/smoke/task-orchestration-assist.ts`.

### §10.7 — Board interaction overhaul (Phase 12, SHIPPED 2026-06-26)

- **`createCluster` defaults `taskBoardMode` to `'project'` — but `normalizeTaskBoardMode` is still the read-time source of truth.** New clusters surface the board attached; clusters persisted before P12.1 carry `taskBoardMode: 'none'` and MUST stay `'none'`. Read mode via `normalizeTaskBoardMode(state)`, never assume the create default applies to existing records. The `task-board.ts` smoke's first step asserts the new default; if you revert the default, flip that assertion back too.
- **The board's create/edit/delete operator surfaces send `wsSendRaw` task-ledger messages directly — they are NOT new server surface.** `createTask` / `editTask` / `deleteTask` in `ClusterTaskBoardTab` build `task_ledger_create` / `task_ledger_update` / `task_ledger_delete` with `workflowId = taskShowWorkflowId(members)` (first member, cwd-derived) and `boardKey = cluster.taskBoardKey`. Don't add parallel handlers — the existing `handleTaskLedger*` + `applyUpdate` / `removeTask` are the only path. `done` / `archived` are never set through these field edits; they stay on the dedicated status controls (the operator-acceptance boundary).
- **Create-on-board is a SINGLE board-level composer, deliberately not per-column.** `task_ledger_create` always yields `todo` (operator-confirmed-done invariant), so a per-column "Add a task…" field would mislead — every column's input would still produce a `todo` card. If a future change wants per-column create-into-status, it needs a create→update chain keyed on the server-assigned id (which arrives via snapshot, not a direct response), so it's non-trivial; don't "fix" the single composer into a misleading per-column one.
- **Drag-to-restatus routes through `onUpdateTaskStatus` (the operator path) with a done/archived confirm — dnd-kit, pointer-only.** `BoardCard` is `useDraggable`, `BoardColumn` is `useDroppable`, `handleDragEnd` → `moveTask(active.id, over.id)`. The 4px PointerSensor activation distance is load-bearing: it lets a plain click still open the drawer (the card is a `<button onClick>` with `{...listeners}`). Touch has no drag path — the drawer status dropdown is the fallback. Don't drop the 4px constraint (clicks would start drags) and don't move terminal-status drops off the confirm.
- **The editable drawer reuses DEBAG's *visual* edit pattern, not the `GateApprovalNotice` component.** §10.7 said "build against DEBAG's shared proposal component"; in practice the gate card carries approve/reject/remember + arg-parsing semantics that don't fit task authoring. The drawer uses inline `editLabelStyle` / `editInputStyle` (accent-on-dirty) matching the gate look. This was a deliberate, documented call (plan §13 2026-06-26) — not an oversight; don't force a shared component that would couple task editing to gate semantics.
- **The plan/peer→task bridges are board-attached-only and reuse `task_ledger_create`.** `PlanBodyRenderer.onPromoteTask` (→ TASK pill on unchecked checkboxes) and `MailboxModal.onMakeTask` (→ TASK per pending peer message) both fire `task_ledger_create` on the first member with `cluster.taskBoardKey`, gated on `cluster.taskBoardMode !== 'none'`. The peer bridge lives on the pending-mailbox surface (it has cluster context); a delivered-chat `PeerMessageBlock` bridge is deferred (needs per-workflow cluster context). Don't wire the bridges when the board is detached — the task would land on a board the operator can't see.
- **The automation header (DISPATCH / SWARM / ASSIST / DISPATCHER) is collapsed by default behind `▸ AUTOMATION`.** `automationOpen` defaults `false`; the task count + toggle stay visible. The `assistOpen` panel is now gated on `automationOpen && assistOpen`. This is a UX de-emphasis only — the controls and their fences are unchanged. Don't default it open "for discoverability"; the whole point is the board reads "groom tasks," not "run a swarm."

### §D3 — Director sign-off + per-card actions (SHIPPED 2026-06-27)

- **`LedgerTask.signoff` is ADVISORY and never a terminal-status path.** A director writes `{ state: 'met' | 'not_met' | 'partial', summary? }` via `cluster_task_update`; `peerTaskUpdate` validates the state, **server-stamps `byWorkflowId` + `at`** (the model can't spoof author/time), and threads it into `applyUpdate`. It does NOT move the task to `done`/`archived` — those stay operator-only (the §D1 / H48 fence is untouched; `scripts/smoke/task-peer-lifecycle.ts` step `(d3)` asserts a worker still can't reach `done` through this path). The sign-off is a recommendation the operator reads on the card; the operator click is still the acceptance boundary. Don't let a `met` verdict auto-apply `done`.
- **The verdict is a field ON the task, written through all three transports.** `cluster_task_update`'s `signoff` arg is wired in `peerTools.ts` (Native input_schema + dispatch), `clusterMcpServer.ts` (zod schema + `callTaskUpdate`), and the `task-update` HTTP op in `clusterRoutes.ts` (read by the MCP child for CLI/Codex). It lives on `LedgerTask` (not a comment) precisely because the board card only has the task list — comments aren't loaded until drill-in, so an at-a-glance chip needs the data on the task. Additive + projection-safe like `assignee`/`worktree`: `normalizeTask` sanitizes it (`sanitizeSignoff`), `rebuildFromEvents` folds it through the carried `ev.task`.
- **Per-card DONE / RECHECK are operator surfaces, not new server messages.** `✓ DONE` reuses `onUpdateTaskStatus` (the operator `task_ledger_update` path, same as MARK ALL DONE) — no confirm, since the labelled click is itself the acceptance. `↩ RECHECK` reuses the existing `operator_peer_send` rail to drop a templated recheck note into the sign-off author's mailbox. Both render only on REVIEW cards; the buttons are `role="button"` spans (NOT `<button>`, because the card is itself a draggable `<button>` — nested buttons are invalid HTML) with `onPointerDown`/`onClick` `stopPropagation` so they neither start a drag nor open the drawer.
- **RECHECK's sender must differ from the recipient — `operator_peer_send` rejects `from === to`.** `recheckSenderFor` picks the task's assignee (if a member and ≠ the sign-off author), else any other member; if the only member is the author, RECHECK is unavailable (the call site gates on `cluster.members.length >= 2`). Don't send the recheck from the director to itself — the server (`ws/clusterHandlers.ts`, "cannot send to self") will reject it.
- **LATENT (not fixed, by decision): `rebuildFromEvents` fold order is undefined on same-millisecond cross-workflow edits to the SAME task.** Events sort by `(at, seq)`, but `at` is wall-clock ms and `seq` is *per-workflow* monotonic — non-comparable across workflows on an `at` tie, so last-writer-wins is non-deterministic. This affects **only the rebuild-from-events fallback** (snapshot missing/corrupt), never the live board (mutations serialize under `withCwdLock` and broadcast in real order), and it's **field-agnostic + pre-existing** (predates sign-off; applies to `status`/`assignee` too). Real director review lands seconds after worker completion, so `at` never ties in production. The `task-peer-lifecycle` smoke forces the tie because it runs sub-ms; step `(d2)` inserts a few-ms gap modeling real review latency. A correct fix would need a per-cwd monotonic emit sequence folded on instead of `at` — its own change, weighed against the Phase-9 **byte-identical replay** invariant, with a dedicated same-ms smoke. Deferred until observed in practice; if a verdict/status silently reverts after a snapshot rebuild, this is the cause.

### Phase 2 client-side gotchas (CLUSTER_MODE_PLAN.md §7)

- **Operator NUDGE uses each member's normal chat send path.** Idle members get the existing wake-up prompt; active members get a gentler reminder to check mail at a convenient point, sent as `steer`. Providers without mid-turn steering fall back to delivery after the current turn. Choose wording from current busy/worker/streaming state at submit, keep recipients explicit, and leave `peer_read` responsible for draining mail. Typed BROADCAST still uses `cluster_broadcast` and queues busy recipients as follow-up. The NUDGE picker must remain usable even when the broadcast recipient picker has no selection.
- **`activeJobId` and `activeClusterId` are mutually exclusive in the store.** `setActiveJob` clears `activeClusterId` and vice-versa. App.tsx routes via `if (activeClusterId) <ClusterTab>` else `if (showCreator || !activeJob) <WorkflowCreator>` else `<WorkflowView>`. Adding a new top-level view that should also be exclusive needs the same pattern — add a clear-action on every `setActive*` reducer and add the routing branch in App.tsx in the same priority order. Don't try to express exclusivity through a single discriminator field; the cluster mirror lives in a separate map and the active key is the cleanest signal.
- **`MemberPane` threads REVIEW / HANDOFF / SPAWN / RECALIBRATE / UPDATE-PRIMARY / PLAN-MODE primitive callbacks (slice 6a).** TerminalInput's primitive buttons render conditionally on the `on*` props being set; MemberPane now passes them through from App → ClusterTab. Behavior is unchanged from standalone-workflow primitives — cluster-aware behavior (HANDOFF preserves cluster membership, SPAWN-into-this-cluster as a default option, REVIEW understands peer rows) ships in Phase 2.5 (master board row 6b; `docs/CLUSTER_MODE_PLAN.md §7.5`). The Phase 2 comment in `MemberPane.tsx` that said "primitives stay per-tab" was correct under Phase 2's flat tab structure but is no longer true after slice 6a hides member tabs from the top row. If you add a new primitive callback to App-level workflow dispatch, mirror the App → ClusterTab → MemberPane threading or it'll be unreachable for cluster members.
- **`PeerMessageBlock` early-return order in `ChatMessage.tsx` is load-bearing.** Peer rows must short-circuit before the system-notice / handoff-seed / spawn-seed branches because the existing branches assume `role === 'user' | 'assistant' | 'system'`. Reordering the branches breaks peer rendering silently — the role union widens but the existing branches don't widen with it. The peer branch sits right after the toolUse early return; keep it there.
- **Sender color is hashed from `senderWorkflowId`, not the displayed name.** djb2-style hash + 8-color palette in `ChatMessage.tsx`'s `hashSenderColor`. Same workflow always renders the same color across reloads, across sender/recipient panes, across cluster compositions. Don't switch to hashing displayName — that breaks the "color tracks identity, not display preference" invariant when Phase 5 lands operator-editable names.
- **`cluster_state` push gate (`CLUSTER_STATE_TRIGGERS` in `server/src/server/broadcast.ts`) is the single source of truth.** Adding a new workflow-scoped event that should refresh cluster_state means editing that set; don't sprinkle `notifyClusterStateForWorkflow` calls at random emit sites. Today's set is `{ primary_turn_started, primary_turn_ended, peer_message_sent, peer_message_received }` — busy flips on the first pair, mailbox depth flips on the second pair. Streaming `primary_text` chunks deliberately do NOT trigger.
- **Broadcast's idle-vs-queued split is a local snapshot, not the server's reply.** `ClusterTab`'s composer reads `cluster.members[i].status` at submit time to render the toast. The authoritative split lands in `cluster_broadcast_dispatched` but we don't currently mirror it to the store. The race window is tiny (member flips busy between snapshot and server fan-out) and the toast is informational. If a future surface needs accuracy (e.g. "couldn't reach member X" rendered visually), wire the dispatched event into the store there.
- **Layout-prop-leak invariant.** `MemberPane` props that are passed to ChatPanel + TerminalInput need to be passed in *every* density/layout variant of MemberPane — if you add a "compact MemberPane" mode, every prop the dense variant threads must also be threaded by the compact variant. Pokegents' AgentCard regression (referenced in plan §7e) was a missing prop in one variant; the bug only surfaced for users who hit that variant. Pattern fix: make the prop list explicit, never spread `{...props}` across variants.
- **Right-click context menu is the only such surface in the app.** `TabContextMenu` is scoped to "create cluster from this tab + others." If a future feature wants a tab right-click action (rename, duplicate, move-to-cluster), plug into the same component rather than spawning a parallel context-menu pattern. Resist the urge to make `TabContextMenu` generic until there are actually two consumers.
- **Cluster tear-down must go through `cluster_delete` (or REST `DELETE /api/clusters/:id`).** Don't add a UI-only "remove cluster from store" path. Removing the local entry without the server-side teardown leaves the mailbox directory + meta.json on disk and the per-member `clusterId` lookup intact — the next peer_send call would still resolve. The autonomy four-question test answer #4 (reversibility) requires a single teardown path; both REST and WS delegate to `clusterStore.deleteCluster`.
- **`cluster_state` is NOT persisted to events.jsonl — it's a derived snapshot.** Every member has its own events.jsonl with `peer_message_*` events; the cluster meta.json lives on disk; mailbox files live on disk. `cluster_state` is built fresh from those three sources every push. If you find yourself wanting to persist cluster_state for offline replay, you're recreating state that's already on disk in three places. Use those instead.

### Slice 6a gotchas (TabBar restructure)

- **`@dnd-kit` PointerSensor + button-that-covers-entire-tab requires NO `onPointerDown` stopPropagation on the activate button.** The activate `<button>` in `SortableWorkflowTab` / `SortableClusterTab` covers the whole visible tab face; if it stops `pointerdown`, the parent `<div>`'s `{...listeners}` drag handle never sees the event and drag-reorder silently dies. The `PointerSensor` is configured with `activationConstraint: { distance: 4 }`, which already separates click from drag — a stationary press fires `onClick` and never starts a drag; movement >4 px arms the drag. *Keep* `stopPropagation` on the small close × button (it's a tiny target and benefits from event isolation; losing 4 px of drag start area at the × corner is fine — that's how Chrome tabs behave). Rule of thumb: stopPropagation on small isolated buttons, NOT on buttons that span the entire drag-handle surface. Regressing this is silent and hard to diagnose because the tab still highlights on click — only drag is broken.
- **Bottom-row activation rule: render only when a cluster tab is the active top-row tab.** Standalone tab active → no bottom row. Cluster tab active → bottom row lists that cluster's members. Don't try to "always show the last viewed cluster's members" — that breaks the visual exclusivity the operator relies on for "where am I?" Bottom row collapses to nothing when activeClusterId is null.
- **Focused-member persistence lives in `appStore.clusterFocusedMember[clusterId]`, kept in sync by `setClusterState`'s reducer.** When a member is removed (or never selected), the reducer re-pins focus to the first remaining member — but only if the previous focus is now stale. The no-op-on-unchanged-state guard sits *after* the focus check; future contributors extending the reducer must not move the guard before the focus fix-up or focus will dangle on a removed member.
- **`settings.tabOrder` mixes workflowIds AND clusterIds in one list.** They share the top row; operator can interleave them. A stale entry (workflow closed, cluster deleted) lingers until the operator next drag-reorders. `applyTabOrder` ignores stale entries (they're filtered out before sorting) so the UI renders correctly, but `settings.json` accumulates them. Acceptable today; a periodic cleanup pass is deferred. Don't add server-side cleanup that fires on every workflow/cluster deletion — that'd race with optimistic local updates.
- **Bottom-row member × is "remove from cluster AND close workflow" — single click, single confirm.** If a future feature wants "detach but keep workflow alive" (the considered alternative), introduce a second mode (shift-× was sketched). Don't change the single-× semantics silently — it's a destructive operation gated on a confirm dialog, and the operator's mental model is "× ends this thing."
- **`cluster_member_remove` collapses the cluster if remaining members <2.** `clusterStore.removeMember` calls `deleteCluster` internally; subscribers get `cluster_deleted`. The leaving member's mailbox dir is wiped (peer messages destined for it lose recipient context). Don't try to leave a 1-member cluster alive — `createCluster`'s ≥2 invariant means it'd be inconsistent and `peer_send` from the lone member would fail with `unknown_peer` anyway. The collapse behavior is the right shape.
- **Cluster rename does NOT emit an events.jsonl event.** The display name is a label on the cluster, not a workflow-scoped action. Server pushes a fresh `cluster_state` so subscribers see the new name; that's it. If a future feature wants a "renamed by operator at <ts>" audit trail, that's an opt-in surface (e.g. a separate `cluster_renamed` push that lands in some cluster-scoped log file), not a workflow-event hijack.
- **Default cluster name (`<basename(cwd)> · <short-id>`) is computed at render time, never persisted.** `clusterDisplayName` derives it from `cluster.cwd` + `cluster.clusterId`. Operator-set name lives on `cluster.name`. Empty/whitespace `name` clears the override (the client falls back to the derived default). Don't try to "snapshot" the derived default into `cluster.name` on cluster create — that defeats the "renamed if op renamed, otherwise basename" mental model; the operator never typed that name.
- **Drag-reorder persistence is `setTabOrder` → POST `/api/settings`, not events.jsonl.** Tab order is operator preference, not a workflow-scoped action. Keep it that way — flooding events.jsonl with operator-preference noise breaks the autonomy fence #3 ("events.jsonl carries actions, not preferences").

### Phase 2.5 gotchas (primitives + cluster management UX)

- **HANDOFF preserves cluster membership for free — DO NOT add explicit rebind logic.** `clusterStore` keys membership on `workflowId`; HANDOFF rotates the session inside the same `workflowId`. The `clusterId` lookup at `handleUserMessage` turn start re-resolves on every turn, so the post-handoff session sees `peer_*` tools register naturally. Adding "preserve cluster" code in `handleHandoffRequest` would be no-op but would create the impression that membership is tracked elsewhere — and a future contributor might "fix" the no-op by introducing an actual rebind, which races against `clusterStore.memberIndex` and creates duplicate-member bugs.
- **SPAWN-into-cluster validates Native transport BEFORE creating the new workflow.** Up-front rejection means no orphan tabs on `member_not_native`. A post-create rejection (workflow exists but cluster join refused) is the backstop for genuine race conditions (cluster deleted between validate and join); it surfaces a system_notice rather than tearing down the new tab. Don't reorder — operator getting a created tab they didn't want is worse than a clear up-front error.
- **REVIEW peer-row serialization is in `extractTurns`, not a separate pass.** Don't try to inject peer rows in `formatTurns` by re-walking events — `extractTurns` is the only place that knows the turn-boundary positions (between `user_message_echo` and `primary_complete`). The `pendingPeerReceived` running buffer is the correct shape; resetting it on every echoed turn (whether or not the turn made it into the output) prevents stale received-rows from leaking forward.
- **`peer_message_sent` carries only a `contentPreview` (≤200 chars), not the full content.** The recipient's `peer_message_received` event has the full body — that's the durable record. Reviewer sees the preview on the sender side and the full body on the recipient side. Don't try to "rejoin" by msgId in the serializer; the reviewer's window into the conversation is per-workflow.
- **`createBlankNativeWorkflow` is a minimal pre-replay helper for B3 — don't extend it for resume / rehydrate cases.** It deliberately skips the savedMeta restoration logic (autoHandoff / efficiency toggles / outputStyle / planId / calibration / preview) that `handleCreateWorkflow` performs because B3 only creates *fresh* workflows. If a future surface wants to "fork N workers from a template that has prior state," it should call `handleCreateWorkflow` (so resume + rehydrate hooks fire) and chain the cluster create separately. Mixing the two paths in one helper would silently drop state on the fresh path.
- **Cluster provider fence follows peer-tool wiring, not provider brand.** Claude and Codex get peer tools through Native/CLI/MCP wiring; Ollama now gets them through the local tool registry when `WorkerConfig.clusterId` + `workflowId` are present. Gemini / Antigravity remain fenced out until they have an equivalent peer-tool path. Do not loosen a provider by UI-only changes; the worker must actually receive `peer_list` / `peer_send` / `peer_read` / plan tools.
- **Cluster Reviews sub-tab reads `job.reviews[]` per member, not a cluster-scoped review list.** Reviews dispatched from MemberPane fire through the existing per-workflow `requestReview` path; the per-member `job.reviews[]` is populated by the same `review_started` / `review_artifact_ready` WS handlers as the standalone Review tab. The Reviews sub-tab is purely a read-side aggregation. If cluster-wide review (one reviewer call across all members) lands later, that's a separate `cluster_reviews[]` list — don't write cluster-wide review state into per-member arrays.
- **TabContextMenu's three modes share one modal; mode preconditions short-circuit submit.** `ADD TO EXISTING` requires at least one same-cwd cluster AND anchor-not-already-a-member; `CREATE WITH EXISTING` requires at least one selected; `CREATE WITH FRESH` always allowed for Native anchors. Switching modes mid-flow keeps the picker open (no re-trigger needed). If a fourth mode lands (e.g. "fork cluster"), make it a sibling component rather than a fourth conditional — the current single-component conditional is at its readable limit.

### Phase 2.6 gotchas (cluster-attached plan + role hints)

- **System-block ordering invariant: FORCED_FIRST_BLOCK → north-star → cluster plan → cluster role → appendSystemPrompt → systemPrompt → output style.** The Native worker's `buildSystemBlocks()` is the single source of truth. The plan + role blocks land AFTER `FORCED_FIRST_BLOCK` (which keeps its `cache_control: ephemeral, ttl: 1h`) and BEFORE the operator's prompts so the model reads them as load-bearing framing. Reordering breaks the prefix-cache strategy AND moves the operator's prompt before the cluster context — that flip would cause the model to read cluster guidance as a mid-prompt aside instead of a frame. Plan + role both carry `cache_control: ephemeral` (no ttl, normal cache shape).
- **North-star (GOAL_NORTHSTAR_PLAN.md) sits immediately after `FORCED_FIRST_BLOCK`, before the cluster plan, and carries NO cache breakpoint of its own.** `goalBlock(this.config.goal)` is pushed at index 1 in `buildSystemBlocks()` (the most stable per-session block after the identity block, so it rides the plan-boundary / last-block breakpoint's cached prefix). It is null-when-empty (`goalBlock` returns null for blank ⇒ no block, zero extra input tokens). The trailer ("It is context, not an instruction to act autonomously… not trigger unrequested work") is load-bearing — it forecloses the model reading the north-star as license to self-drive; do not soften it. **Injection is per-provider, NOT via the turnEngine `finalAppend` fold:** Native pushes its own block; every other transport folds `goalBlock(config.goal)` inside its own provider (CLI Claude `--append-system-prompt`, both Codex prefixes, Z.ai system array, Ollama/llama.cpp/LM Studio `systemPrompt`, Cursor ACP worker + print path). This is deliberate — reviewers spawn via `manager.spawn` and handoff seeds via `runPrimaryTurn`, both of which bypass the operator-turn `finalAppend` assembly but still carry `config.goal`, so the provider fold is the one place that covers operator turns AND primitive seeds without double-injecting. Don't add a turnEngine `finalAppend`/`finalSystemPrompt` fold "for parity" — it would double-inject on non-Native operator turns.
- **Cluster plan + role are resolved per-turn in `handleUserMessage`, not at worker construction.** They live on `WorkerConfig.clusterPlan` / `clusterRole` and the Native worker reads `this.config.clusterPlan` from `buildSystemBlocks()`. `ClaudeNativeTurn.spawn` calls `worker.updateConfig(this.config)` on every turn, so plan / role changes between turns surface automatically — no callback, no re-construction. The "callback" alternative (worker invokes `clusterContext()` during build) was rejected in §13; don't reintroduce it without a §13 entry explaining why per-turn resolution stopped working.
- **Editing the plan mid-turn does NOT tear down the running turn.** The worker reads `this.config.clusterPlan` once at `buildSystemBlocks` per `messages.create`; the new body lands on the NEXT `runPrimaryTurn` call. This is the correct shape — in-flight tool dispatch carries on uninterrupted; the next operator prompt picks up the new plan. If you find yourself wanting to push the new plan into an in-flight turn, stop: that path leads to mid-turn system-prompt invalidation, which Anthropic's API doesn't support cleanly.
- **Plan body cap = 32768 chars (`PLAN_MAX_BYTES`), role cap = 120 chars (`MEMBER_ROLE_MAX`).** Both are server-enforced; the UI soft-warns at 28672 chars. Server returns explicit `{type:'error', message:'cluster_plan_set rejected: body_too_large:N'}` which rides through `NotificationToasts`. Don't loosen these without a §13 entry — the caps exist to bound steady-state per-turn context size.
- **Model-driven cluster plan writes are additive.** `cluster_plan_write` reads the existing `plan.md` and rejects writes that remove any existing checkbox task text or change its `[ ]` / `[x]` status. New sections, prose, and new checkbox items are fine. Status changes go through `cluster_plan_tick`; destructive replace/clear is operator-only through the PLAN tab. This guard applies to existing clusters because it reads the current persisted plan on every model write.
- **`cluster_state` push now carries the full plan body inline (up to 32 KB), not a hash + lazy fetch.** Acceptable today (32 KB × N members × push frequency is well under any practical bandwidth concern), but worth noting before adding additional `CLUSTER_STATE_TRIGGERS`. If push frequency grows, switch `state.plan` to a hash + `GET /api/clusters/:id/plan` fetch rather than dropping the field.
- **Plan block's `cache_control: ephemeral` means editing the plan invalidates the cache for the suffix.** `FORCED_FIRST_BLOCK` keeps its 1h-ttl cache regardless, so at minimum the front-of-prompt cache survives — but `appendSystemPrompt`, operator `systemPrompt`, and the output-style block all lose their hits on the next turn after a plan edit. This is the right trade (plan changes are operator-driven and rare; cache-miss-on-suffix is cheap), but don't add a second `cache_control` after the plan block unless you mean to fragment the cache further.
- **Role overlay is operator-chosen — presets are editor suggestions, never silent defaults.** Lite §12 open question 4 originally shipped no defaults; `shared/src/clusterRolePresets.ts` now offers presets the operator picks in the editor, and `normalizeClusterRoleText` only upgrades exact older preset strings (never operator-edited text). Defaults imply an opinion about workflow shape, which is a non-goal. If a future surface wants to suggest roles (e.g. "this looks like a director — set role?"), it must surface as an operator-confirmed suggestion, not a silent default. Read `CLAUDE.md`'s "operator-armed surfaces" framing before adding any seeded value.
- **`getMemberRole(clusterId, workflowId)` is synchronous (in-memory cache only).** Called per-turn in `handleUserMessage`. The cache is populated by `readCluster` on first access; if you ever add a path that calls `getMemberRole` without `readCluster` first (e.g. a fresh-server-process read for a clusterId that hasn't been touched yet), it returns `undefined` silently. The handleUserMessage path is safe because `getClusterIdForWorkflow` already populated the cache, but a different call site might surprise you.
- **PROMOTE TO CLUSTER copies the plan body into `cluster_plan_set`; it does NOT detach the source plan from the workflow.** The per-workflow plan stays attached to the member that promoted it. Operator can clear the cluster plan independently. This is intentional — the cluster plan is a separate concept from the per-workflow plan, not a "view" of it; treating them as linked would force a re-architecture of `planStore` keying.

### Phase 3 gotchas (CLI transport — MCP wiring)

- **MCP tool names are namespaced on the wire: `mcp__arco-cluster__peer_send`, NOT bare `peer_send`.** Claude CLI surfaces MCP tools with the `mcp__<server-name>__` prefix where `<server-name>` is the top-level key in `mcp-config.json`'s `mcpServers` map. Native registers bare names. Function shapes match so the model treats them identically, but any code that pattern-matches on the wire-level tool name (transcript renderers, hook filters, reviewer serializers) needs to handle both forms. Today's match site is just `isPeerTool` in `peerTools.ts`, which only runs on Native; CLI dispatches through MCP's own routing. If you add a CLI-side hook that fires on peer_* tool calls, match both prefixes. **Codex code-mode adds a third form**: inside the exec code cell the same tools appear as `tools.mcp__arco_cluster__peer_send` — dash → **underscore** in the server-name segment. Three shapes for one tool; never assume one.
- **Codex code-mode cluster members need the code-mode addendum — don't remove it.** Catalog-marked models including gpt-5.6 sol/terra/luna and gpt-6 astra/sol/luna run OpenAI's own multi-agent harness ("you are `/root`", native `spawn_agent`/`send_message`, tool surface = a JS `exec` code cell). The arco-cluster tools survive but only as `tools.mcp__arco_cluster__<tool>` inside the exec cell — a member told "use peer_send" finds nothing and coordinates through the harness's own (dead-end) tools instead, reporting itself as the director (2026-07-09 incident: 236 tool calls, 0 peer/cluster). `turnEngine` folds `codexCodeModeClusterAddendum()` (`server/src/providers/codexCodeMode.ts`) after the role hint for Codex members whose model the Codex `models_cache.json` catalog marks code-mode (`tool_mode`/`multi_agent_version` — the fields that gate the harness client-side). Detection is catalog-driven and fail-open on unknown; the addendum text is conditionally phrased so it's inert on plain function-calling models. App-server also sends the stable role, tool mapping, and Director contract through `developerInstructions`. Verified live on both Codex transports for the original gpt-5.6 fix; the Astra prompt contract is pinned by `npm run smoke:astra-cluster-director`. Full write-up: `docs/CODEX_GPT56_CLUSTER_INCOMPAT.md`.
- **The string-form cluster fold shrinks ONLY the plan — never the small blocks.** CLI transports retain the 16 KB `APPEND_CLUSTER_MAX`; Codex app-server uses a bounded 48 KB `CODEX_APP_SERVER_CLUSTER_MAX` because JSON-RPC stdin has no argv ceiling and the larger bound admits a maximum-size plan plus role/doctrines. Do not make app-server unlimited: its prefix repeats per turn and consumes thread context. `composeClusterAppend` reserves small blocks plus already-folded bytes first. An over-budget plan folds as a deterministic OUTLINE; `clusterPlanOutline.ts` preserves headings, checkboxes, and sections labelled standing/rules/gates/contract/authority/cadence, with the full body one `cluster_plan_read` call away. The outline header and truncation logging are load-bearing. Regression tests: `npm run test:cluster-fold -w server` and `npm run smoke:astra-cluster-director`.
- **The MCP child is per-spawn, NOT per-cluster.** Each `claude` CLI invocation launches its own short-lived stdio MCP child via `mcp-config.json`. N CLI members of one cluster CANNOT share one MCP child — stdio is point-to-point and Claude CLI owns the child lifecycle. The shared backing store is Arco's in-memory state, reached by every MCP child over loopback HTTP. Plan §13 (2026-05-10) records the decision. Don't try to refactor to a "per-cluster long-lived MCP server"; that path needs SSE transport and an internal IPC bridge that doesn't pay back its complexity.
- **Arco's HTTP port flows to the MCP child via positional argv, NOT env-inherited.** `mcpConfigWriter.writeFor` writes the third argv as `process.env.ARCO_PORT ?? '3001'`. The child does NOT read `process.env.ARCO_PORT` — claude may scrub or mutate env on the way down. If a future surface needs another runtime parameter passed to the child (auth token, cluster-scoped flag), add a fourth argv rather than relying on env.
- **The MCP child's stdout is the JSON-RPC wire — all logging goes to stderr.** `clusterMcpServer.ts` uses `console.error` exclusively (and the SDK's own logging also routes to stderr). A `console.log` slipping into the child would corrupt the MCP protocol stream and break tool dispatch silently. If you extend the child with new tools, hold this invariant. Operator-visible diagnostics surface as `[mcp:arco-cluster:stderr]` in the claude CLI's transcript.
- **`mcp-config.json` cleanup is best-effort, but mandatory on member detach.** `handleClusterDelete` and `handleClusterMemberRemove` call `mcpConfigWriter.cleanupFor` for every leaving member. A stale config on a downgraded-to-plain workflow would launch an MCP child on the next CLI spawn — the child would 404 on every tool call (REST surface verifies cluster exists) but the wasted spawn cycle is operator-visible churn. The cleanup uses `fs.rm({ force: true })` so missing files don't throw.
- **REST `/api/clusters/:id/peer/*` trusts the body's `from`/`workflowId` field — loopback-only assumption.** The MCP child has the workflowId baked into argv at spawn time, and the server binds 127.0.0.1 by default. Any future remote-access mode (tunnel, container exposure on 0.0.0.0) MUST add authentication to these endpoints first — otherwise a remote client could impersonate any cluster member. Memory: Arco remote access architecture says tunneling exists; Phase 3 makes the trust assumption explicit but doesn't gate on it.
- **CLI cluster member `--append-system-prompt` is soft-capped at 16 KB.** The peer-mailbox prologue + plan + role get combined into `finalAppend` in `handleUserMessage`. The hard kernel limit for argv is ~128 KB (ARG_MAX), and the existing tmpfile fallback (`--append-system-prompt-file`) handles arbitrary sizes — but the 16 KB soft cap surfaces pathological mailbox growth (worst case 50 × 16 KB = 800 KB) early instead of writing that to tmpfs every turn. The cap truncates with a `[... truncated by cluster appendSystemPrompt cap]` sentinel; the truncated content is always at the *end* (peer prologue + plan + role sit at the head).
- **`assertAllMembersClaude` was renamed to `assertAllMembersClaudeOrCodex` in Phase 4 — but the fence has since widened past its name.** It now gates on `isClusterMemberProvider(provider)` (`shared/src/protocol/clusters.ts` `CLUSTER_MEMBER_PROVIDERS = ['claude', 'codex', 'zai', 'ollama', 'llamacpp', 'cursor']`), so it accepts Claude (Native + CLI), Codex (`exec` + `appserver`), Z.ai GLM (native local-registry or the app-server path), Ollama, llama.cpp, and Cursor (ACP, peer tools via the loopback HTTP MCP); it still rejects Gemini / Antigravity. The function name is now misleading — don't infer the accepted set from it. A separate cap (`cluster_create_from_scratch` in `server/src/ws/clusterHandlers.ts`) rejects more than one local (`ollama`/`llamacpp`) member (`multiple_local_members`, default max 1). Every cluster-join path goes through `assertAllMembersClaudeOrCodex` — loosening it further (e.g. for Gemini once it grows MCP wiring) requires a §13 decision-log entry.
- **Shared `clusterPeerOps.ts` is the single source of truth for peer-op behaviour.** Native `peerTools.ts` and the REST handlers in `http/clusterRoutes.ts` (the CLI/Codex/Cursor MCP children's backend) both delegate to this module. The events emitted (`peer_message_sent` / `peer_send_rejected` / `peer_message_received`) are identical across transports. If you add a new rejection class, do it in `clusterPeerOps.peerSend`'s return shape (extend `PeerSendRejection.code`) so both transports get it for free. Don't add transport-specific event variants — the peer wire surface stays unified.
- **MCP SDK version (`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`) is loosely pinned.** Package.json says `^1.18.0`; npm resolved 1.29.0 at install. The API surface used (`McpServer.registerTool` + `StdioServerTransport`) is stable across this range. If Anthropic's CLI ever returns a protocol-version mismatch error, switch `^` to `=` and pin to the version that matches the CLI's `--version` output.

### Phase 4 gotchas (Codex transport)

- **Codex registers MCP via `-c key=value` argv overrides — there is NO `--mcp-config <path>` flag.** `codex --help` lists `-c <key=value>` (TOML dotted-path override) as the canonical mechanism for layering on top of `~/.codex/config.toml`. `mcpConfigWriter.buildCodexMcpOverrides` returns the two `-c` argv pairs; both `codex.ts` and `codex-appserver/worker.ts` consume that helper. If a future Codex CLI version adds `--mcp-config <path>`, switching is a one-edit change in those two providers — but until then, do NOT introduce a temp config.toml file or a `~/.codex/config.toml` merge layer; the overrides preserve the operator's existing MCP entries without any merge logic.
- **Codex `app-server`'s MCP child and cluster developer instructions live for the appserver thread bootstrap, not per-turn.** Asymmetric with `codex exec` and Claude CLI (both per-spawn). `currentMcpSignature` includes cluster wiring, account, role, and model. A change recycles the cached child and resumes the same Codex thread; `thread/start` / `thread/resume` then receive the current operator role, code-mode mapping, and derived Director contract through `developerInstructions`. Mutable plan content still rides per-turn input.
- **No `mcp-config.json` file is written for Codex cluster members.** `mcpConfigWriter.writeFor` is Claude-CLI-only. Phase 4's smoke explicitly asserts the file's absence for Codex members — if a regression starts emitting it, the cleanup-on-detach path won't be wired for the Codex side and stale configs would accumulate. (And it'd be wasted work — Codex doesn't read the file anyway.)
- **The Director contract delegates and lands; it never tells the director to keep working until the plan is done.** `clusterDirector.ts` gives a role starting with Director/Coordinator: delegate implementation/builds/tests via peer_send, don't write code, read the full diff + peer gate evidence, then commit/push; edit plan tasks but keep status on the board. 2026-09-26 incident: "integrate accepted work" + "continue the next plan step in the same turn" turned an Astra director into a doer that rewrote the plan into a self-fed status ledger. Don't reintroduce continue-until-done or evidence-into-plan wording (CLAUDE.md fence: model proposes/delegates, operator owns "is this done?"). Pinned by `smoke:astra-cluster-director` + `test:codex-code-mode`.
- **Cluster plan + role + mailbox prologue ride through `WorkerConfig.appendSystemPrompt` for both Codex transports.** Native still injects forced system blocks. Codex app-server also duplicates the stable role/tool/Director contract at developer level because its built-in `/root` framing otherwise outranks the user-text prefix. The app-server fold cap is 48 KB; CLI stays 16 KB.
- **Codex CLI's `-c` value is parsed as TOML.** Strings need quoting, arrays use TOML array syntax. We emit via `JSON.stringify` because TOML strings and arrays are a strict superset of JSON's quoting rules for `string | string[]` shapes — handy. If you ever extend `buildCodexMcpOverrides` to emit non-string-non-array values (booleans, integers), JSON.stringify still works for those primitives because TOML accepts the JSON literal forms.
- **`SpawnDialog` / `TabContextMenu` / `ClusterRightPanel` candidate filters now accept `provider === 'codex'` alongside `'claude'`.** A Phase 5 polish pass should rename the still-misleadingly-named `anchorIsNative` local in `TabContextMenu.tsx` to something provider-agnostic; left as-is to keep the Phase 4 diff scoped.

### Phase 5 gotchas (Polish — rehydrate · detach · activity · idle nudger)

- **History rehydrate fires on first `cluster_subscribe` per server lifetime — NOT on server boot.** `server/src/clusterRehydrate.ts` holds an in-memory `rehydratedClusters: Set<string>`; the first WS to subscribe triggers a `peek` over the recipient mailbox dirs and re-broadcasts `peer_message_sent` for each undelivered file. Subsequent subscribes (same server, additional WS clients) skip the scan. Decision in `CLUSTER_MODE_PLAN.md` §13 (2026-05-10). Don't add a boot-time eager rehydrate — it scans clusters the operator may never reopen and emits events on channels with zero subscribers.
- **Re-emitted `peer_message_sent` from rehydrate persists to the SENDER's events.jsonl.** The re-emit rides through `broadcastWorkflow` only — NOT `broadcastCluster`. Cluster panels always subscribe to every member workflow on tab open (the same pattern the original peer_message_sent emit uses), so broadcastWorkflow reaches both workflow observers and the cluster panel through that one channel. DOUBLE-BROADCASTING (via both `broadcastWorkflow` and `broadcastCluster`) would deliver every event twice to a WS subscribed to both, which is what an early Phase 5 draft did — the smoke caught it as a "cap appears broken" false positive (server-side cap held; client saw 2× events). Same rule applies to `cluster_member_nudged`: broadcastWorkflow only. Across a server restart, rehydrate may write a duplicate row to events.jsonl (the original send already persisted in the previous lifetime; rehydrate writes another). Accepted today as audit-trail bloat, not a correctness issue. If a future contributor wants to dedupe, scan the recipient's events.jsonl for a matching `msgId` before re-emit — DON'T silently skip re-emit, because the smoke verifies the event lands.
- **`clusterMailbox.drain` destroys the file — anything left on disk is by definition undelivered.** Rehydrate's `peek(wfid)` returns exactly the undelivered messages because there are no delivered messages on disk to skip. If you ever change `drain` to keep the file with a `delivered: true` stamp, you MUST also teach rehydrate to filter on `msg.delivered === false` (today's `peek` already does this, but the simplification "everything on disk is pending" is load-bearing for the audit trail).
- **No cancel path drains the mailbox — verified by audit, documented as a guarded invariant.** `handleCancel` / `handleCancelQueuedMessage` / `handleCancelReview` / `handleRemoveWorkflow` and every primitive teardown path are clean. The only `clusterMailbox.drain` callers are `seedWorker.drainPeerMailbox` (turn-start in `handleUserMessage`) and `clusterPeerOps.peerRead` (model-issued `peer_read` or the matching REST endpoint). The autonomy four-question test answer #4 (reversibility) requires this: cancelling a turn must preserve the recipient's pending peer messages, otherwise an operator STOP would silently destroy peer mail without notifying the sender. If a future feature adds a "cancel + drop pending mail" path (e.g. "reassign this member"), it must NOT live inside the existing cancel handlers — new surface = new handler.
- **HANDOFF preserves cluster membership for free — verified by audit (Phase 5 re-confirm).** `handleHandoffRequest`, `runExportContext`, `disposeProviderResources` do not mutate `clusterStore`. Cluster membership keys on `workflowId`; handoff rotates the worker session inside the same `workflowId`. `getClusterIdForWorkflow` re-resolves on every operator turn at `handleUserMessage`, so the post-handoff session sees `peer_*` tools register naturally. DO NOT add explicit "preserve cluster" code in `handleHandoffRequest` — it's a no-op but creates the impression that membership is tracked elsewhere, and a future contributor might "fix" the no-op with an actual rebind that races against `clusterStore.memberIndex`. (Mirrors the Phase 2.5 entry; restated here so the Phase 5 audit anchor is co-located.)
- **Detach button wires to the SAME `cluster_member_remove` message as the bottom-row ×.** Single wire shape across both UI surfaces means cleanup paths (mcp-config.json on Claude CLI, Codex `-c` overrides on next spawn, member-index drop, idle-nudger state drop) stay trivially symmetric. The workflow tears down — events.jsonl persists on disk for Resume; that's the "keeps its history" interpretation. Don't introduce a separate "soft detach that keeps the workflow alive" wire shape without concrete operator data motivating it — the UI question "where does the detached-but-alive workflow live in the bottom-row tab list?" is the trap.
- **Idle nudger predicate is a 5-AND gate; flipping any one prevents fire.** `idle ≥ floor` AND `cooldown ≥ floor since last nudge` AND `pending ≥ 1` AND `nudgesThisTurn < cap` AND `no cluster member is busy`. Per-member `nudgesThisTurn` resets on the member's `primary_turn_started` (caught via the `broadcastWorkflow` hook on `primary_turn_*`). Cluster-wide busy-suppression means if ANY member is busy, no nudges fire anywhere in the cluster — the simplification chosen over "only if this member's peer is busy" because the "peer" concept is fuzzy in N>2 clusters. Document any future predicate change in a §13 decision log entry.
- **Idle nudger NEVER sends prompts to workers.** The event is `cluster_member_nudged { reason, idleSeconds, pendingCount }` — toast-only operator alert. This is the autonomy-test-passing version of the rejected "auto-advance" pattern from `arco-autonomous`. If you find yourself wanting to "auto-issue a peer_read on the operator's behalf" or "kick a turn" from the nudger, STOP — that's the archived pattern. The whole point of the toast is to inform the operator who can then issue the prompt manually.
- **`ARCO_CLUSTER_IDLE_FLOOR_SECONDS` is the env knob for the idle floor.** Production default 10s; smoke uses 1s. Read at module load in `clusterIdleNudger.ts` (`process.env.ARCO_CLUSTER_IDLE_FLOOR_SECONDS`). `NUDGES_PER_TURN` stays a hardcoded constant (3) because hitting that cap means "the operator is genuinely ignoring this member" — a per-cluster override would just delay that conclusion.
- **`ClusterStatePayload.idleNudgerEnabled` is optional; absent ≡ false.** The nudger toggle persists in the cluster's meta.json; the client reads it from the `cluster_state` push. UI chip renders OFF when absent or false; ENABLED only when explicitly `true`. Don't render a third state — the toggle is binary at every layer.
- **Efficiency level is a deterministic 3-effect bundle; `clusterEfficiency.ts` is the SINGLE source of truth.** `level → { doctrineText, sendBudget, mailboxNoticeVariant }`. Never split these across files — if you tune the doctrine, the budget, or the notice independently they drift apart and contradict. The known trap: at `balanced`/`lean` the doctrine says "don't re-review unless signalled", so the pre-turn mailbox notice MUST swap to its softened/minimal variant — otherwise the notice's "re-engage on every inbound" language contradicts the doctrine and the over-review loop comes right back. `turnEngine` resolves the level once at turn start and uses it for BOTH the doctrine block AND the notice; keep them tied to the same level read.
- **Efficiency presets are STATIC operator-selected text — not LLM-mutated instructions.** This is the load-bearing distinction from the `headroom learn` hard fence (CLAUDE.md). The doctrine strings live in code; the operator picks a level + optionally appends a note. Never derive the doctrine from session traces, never let a model rewrite it. `thorough` (the default) injects NO doctrine, keeps the historical 15-send budget, and uses the byte-identical historical mailbox notice — so `efficiencyLevel`/`efficiencyAddendum` are emitted in `cluster_state` only when non-default (absent ≡ `thorough`), and pre-feature meta.json loads unchanged.
- **Peer-send budget cap is now per-call, not a global constant.** `clusterBudget.recordSend(senderWfId, cap)` takes the cap; `clusterPeerOps.peerSend` resolves it via `clusterEfficiency.sendBudgetFor(clusterStore.getEfficiencyLevel(clusterId))` per send (so an operator level-change bites immediately, mid-turn). The default arg stays 15 for any caller that omits it. The peer-send tool descriptions were de-hardcoded from "15 sends" to "tightens at higher efficiency levels" — the real cap rides back on the `budget_exhausted` rejection (`count` + `cap`), so the model learns it on refusal regardless of level.
- **Progress-tracking mode is a per-cluster deterministic doctrine knob whose default (`'both'`) is intentionally NON-empty — unlike efficiency's no-op `'thorough'`.** `clusterProgress.ts` `buildProgressDoctrine(mode, hasPlan, hasBoard)` returns a canned system-prompt clause bundle (`PLAN_CLAUSE`/`BOARD_CLAUSE`/`RECONCILE_CLAUSE`), gated on which surfaces actually exist — `undefined` only when neither exists. Modes `'plan' | 'board' | 'both'`, default `CLUSTER_PROGRESS_DEFAULT = 'both'`. turnEngine resolves it once and threads it as `WorkerConfig.clusterProgressDoctrine`; every transport that folds the cluster plan folds this too (Native pushes it as its own block after the efficiency doctrine, before the delegation contract). Operator entry is `cluster_progress_set { clusterId, mode }`; `ClusterState.progressTracking` persists to cluster meta.json ONLY when non-default (so pre-feature cluster meta stays byte-identical), echoed in `ClusterStatePayload.progressTracking` only when non-default.
