Because the default is non-empty, a brand-new cluster gets the doctrine — don't "optimize" it to inject nothing by default.
- **`ClusterActivityTab` derives membership/role/plan events from `cluster_state` diffs, not a server-side audit log.** The diff happens in a `useEffect` snapshot inside the component — if the operator opens the ACTIVITY tab AFTER a membership change, the change won't appear (no diff baseline). This is intentional: a server-side audit log would duplicate state that already lives in `cluster_state` + per-member events.jsonl. If a future surface needs full membership history, it scans events.jsonl, NOT a new audit file.
- **`ClusterRightPanel` uses the same two-level grouping pattern as `RightPanel`.** Groups are `WORKSPACE` (`members`, `files`), `AGENT` (`activity`, `reviews`, `handoffs`, `plan`, `board`, `mailbox`), `MEMORY` (`history`), and `RUNTIME` (`preview`, `config`, `github`). CONFIG is member-scoped: it renders the existing `ConfigTab` for the selected member and dispatches ordinary per-workflow update wires (`update_primary_config`, codebase-memory, MCP registry). Do not add an implicit "apply to every member" path without an explicit operator confirmation surface and event trail.

### Cluster-ops push gotchas (2026-05-11 — AUTO-PICKUP, plan-tick, observer-attach, cache cap, status-indicator race)

- **`buildSystemBlocks` cache_control stamping is breakpoint-based, capped at 4.** Anthropic returns 400 `invalid_request_error` if a request carries more than 4 system blocks with `cache_control` set. The Native worker (`buildSystemBlocks` in `server/src/providers/claude-native/worker.ts`) now pushes every block plain, then assigns cache breakpoints AFTER in priority order: (1) FORCED_FIRST_BLOCK with `ttl: '1h'`, (2) the final block (5min), (3) cluster-plan boundary, (4) role boundary. Block ORDER itself is unchanged (see the Cache-discipline entry above). Pre-fix path was "stamp every stable block as cached on push" — that blew past 4 the moment cluster plan + role + sandbox nudge + an operator system prompt were all present. If you add a new system block, decide whether it deserves a cache breakpoint (rule of thumb: stable across turns + ≥1 KB) and place it in priority order; don't unconditionally add `cache_control` on push.
- **`notifyClusterStateForWorkflow(workflowId)` MUST fire after every `wf.busy = false` write.** Pre-fix: the busy-flip-to-idle wasn't pushing `cluster_state`, so the cluster status indicator could stay solid after a turn settled. Three call sites: `runPrimaryTurn` finally block, the `user_message` crash handler, and `handleCancel`. If a future feature adds another path that flips `wf.busy = false`, add the notify call. `CLUSTER_STATE_TRIGGERS` covers `primary_turn_started / primary_turn_ended / peer_message_sent / peer_message_received` for the normal hot path, but ad-hoc busy resets (crash recovery, cancel) bypass the trigger and need the explicit call.
- **`cluster_plan_tick` schema cannot use top-level `oneOf`.** Anthropic input_schema accepts `properties` / `required` but rejects top-level `oneOf` / `allOf` / `anyOf` with 400 `invalid_request_error`. Shape is "both `lineIndex?` and `taskText?` optional in the schema; the dispatcher in `peerTools.ts` checks types and falls through to `peerPlanTick` which returns `invalid_matcher` when neither / both are supplied." The description text encodes the either/or contract verbatim so the model still picks one. Don't try to express the constraint at the schema level — the API rejects the request before the model ever sees it.
- **PROMOTE TO CLUSTER detaches the worker plan.** After `setClusterPlan` writes through, the worker's local plan slot clears via `detachPlan`. Single source of truth invariant: a cluster member should never inject BOTH the cluster plan (forced system block via `WorkerConfig.clusterPlan`) AND its own attached plan (`appendSystemPrompt` fold). Pre-fix path left both attached, doubling the plan footprint and surfacing two conflicting "the plan" framings. If you add another PROMOTE-shaped operation, mirror the detach.
- **`cluster_subscribe` attaches the WS as observer on every member workflow.** `handleClusterSubscribe` adds the subscribing socket to each member's `wf.observers` set; `Session.clusterObserved: Map<clusterId, Set<workflowId>>` tracks which adds came from a cluster-subscribe (so detach doesn't strip observer entries from other sources). Symmetric detach on `cluster_unsubscribe`, `cluster_member_remove`, and `cluster_delete`. This is what lets the cluster ACTIVITY tab surface per-member tool activity without the operator pre-opening each member tab. If you add a new "subscribe to a multi-workflow surface" wire shape, mirror the observer-attach pattern OR the new surface won't see per-member events. The detach is "remove ONLY when the cluster-subscribe was the source of the add" — don't blanket-remove from `wf.observers`, that'd kick out explicit tab subscribers and parallel cluster subscribers.
- **`peer_send` resolves by wfid prefix → exact displayName → displayName prefix (case-insensitive).** Ambiguous (>1 match) folds into `unknown_peer` with a roster hint (`available peers: <name> (<8-char>), …`). Don't add a fourth resolution mode without auditing the ambiguity rule — the priority order is intentional (wfid is unforgeable; exact name beats prefix). The bug this fixed: models commonly type the friendly name (`Seedbank #2`) instead of a wfid prefix, and pre-fix that just returned `unknown_peer` even though the cluster had exactly one member with that name.
- **`peer_message_sent.chainDepth` is stamped server-side from `currentAutoTurnDepth(senderWfid)`, never sent by clients.** `broadcastWorkflow` intercepts the event and reads the in-flight auto-turn depth marker for the sender. Operator-initiated peer-sends have no marker → `chainDepth = 0`. Auto-turn fires call `markAutoTurnDepth(R, parentDepth + 1)` BEFORE dispatching; `primary_turn_ended` clears the marker. The depth cap (default 3) is checked against the INCOMING message's chainDepth — i.e., if a peer_send rides at depth 3, no auto-turn fires for the recipient. Three knobs together prevent runaway: `ARCO_CLUSTER_AUTO_TURN_COOLDOWN_MS` (per-recipient cooldown, default 20000), `ARCO_CLUSTER_AUTO_TURN_RATE_CAP_PER_MINUTE` (per-cluster rolling 60s cap, default 10), `ARCO_CLUSTER_AUTO_TURN_MAX_CHAIN_DEPTH` (default 3). Smoke harnesses dial all three down to test guard behaviour without burning real API spend.
- **AUTO-PICKUP DOES dispatch a synthetic primary turn — distinct from the idle nudger.** The idle nudger is toast-only (operator alert, no worker prompt). AUTO-PICKUP dispatches a real `user_message`-equivalent on the recipient with the synthetic prompt `"(auto-pickup) New peer message arrived while you were idle — process your mailbox."` Both are operator-armed default-OFF; AUTO-PICKUP's safety is the three guard caps + reversible toggle. Don't fold the two into one toggle: the nudger's "toast only" semantics are the autonomy-test-passing escape hatch; AUTO-PICKUP picks a different point on the spectrum (operator-armed automation with deterministic caps).
- **`ClusterActivityTab` is sorted oldest-first / newest-at-bottom.** Chat-convention; pre-fix was newest-first. Auto-scroll is sticky-bottom, not unconditional: `onScroll` records whether the operator was near the bottom before new rows render, and `useLayoutEffect` snaps to `scrollHeight` only when that sticky flag is true (or on first fill / member-lane switch). Do not recompute "near bottom" only after rendering new rows — bursty tool activity increases `scrollHeight` and can make a previously pinned view look far from bottom. If you mount a sort toggle later, the persisted preference key should be cluster-scoped (one orientation per cluster), not global — operators with multiple clusters sometimes want different views per cluster.
- **Cluster thinking chunks coalesce AFTER the global time sort, with raw whitespace intact.** Cursor ACP chunk size is model-defined; Kimi K3 was observed emitting one word/punctuation token per `agent_thought_chunk`, which produced a full bordered ACTIVITY row per word even though the per-workflow Activity tab already coalesced its deltas. `clusterActivity.helpers.ts:coalesceThinkingRuns` now joins only adjacent same-member thinking rows; tools, cluster events, and other members remain boundaries. Do not `.trim()` each source chunk before this pass — a standalone whitespace chunk is often the only separator between two word chunks. Trim the completed display run only. The derived row is capped at 64K characters so coalescing cannot multiply the store's bounded memory footprint. Auto-scroll must key on the row-array update, not only row count, because a coalesced row grows while its count remains one.
- **`cluster_plan_tick` REST endpoint validates `workflowId ∈ cluster.members` before dispatch.** The MCP child posts as a member's worker, so the endpoint's auth is "membership in the named cluster." Don't drop the membership check thinking "the MCP child only spawns for cluster members anyway" — a stale config file could re-launch a child after detach, and the REST endpoint is the catch-all.

### Cluster-polish push gotchas (2026-05-11 afternoon — tool-driven mailbox, operator_peer_send, mailbox CANCEL, chat-row dedupe, peer-row role display)

- **Peer mailbox no longer auto-drains pre-turn — workers MUST call `peer_read` to receive content.** `handleUserMessage` used to call `drainPeerMailbox` before `user_message_echo`, injecting peer content into the system prompt and emitting `peer_message_received` events at turn-start. That created a false-negative: `peer_read` then returned empty (the drain already consumed the files) and the model conflated "I have nothing to read" with "I have no work to do." Pre-turn now only reads `clusterMailbox.depthFor(workflowId)` and folds a short notice into `appendSystemPrompt` (`clusterEfficiency.buildPeerMailboxNotice(level, depth)`, called from `server/src/ws/turnEngine.ts`; restricted-access members get a one-line variant). The `peer_message_received` events fire on the worker's actual `peer_read` call (where they belong). The OLD invariant about "drain destroys files; events.jsonl is the durable record" still holds — `peer_read` is just where the drain happens now. Do NOT re-introduce pre-turn auto-drain without rethinking the false-negative case.
- **The pre-turn notice is per-turn-variable and cache-busts.** It carries `N` (the pending depth at turn start), so the system block hash changes per turn — same trade-off as the old prologue. Don't try to "stabilize" by omitting N; the count is the load-bearing signal for the model.
- **`peer_send` dedupe is process-local; restarts clear the cache.** `(from, to, sha256(content))` cache in `clusterPeerOps.ts` (`PEER_SEND_DEDUPE_MS`), 60s window (`ARCO_PEER_SEND_DEDUPE_MS`). This is correct, not a bug: a server restart also wipes the orphan-tool-use state that caused the rapid-fire retry pattern in the first place, so cache survival across restarts would refuse legitimate sends that the model now has no memory of having issued. The cache is lazily swept on lookup (>256 entries triggers an inline sweep); no scheduled timer.
- **`operator_peer_send` bypasses budget + dedupe.** `clusterPeerOps.peerSend(ctx, args, opts)` accepts `{ bypassBudget?, bypassDedupe?, maxContentBytes? }`. The operator path (`handleOperatorPeerSend` in `server/src/ws/clusterHandlers.ts`) passes all three; model-driven sends never set them. Do NOT route model-driven sends through the operator path "for simplicity" — the gates exist to constrain model behaviour (per-turn budget, retry-loop dedupe), and bypassing them via the substrate would silently disable those constraints. Operator-driven raises the mailbox cap to 32 KB (matches the cluster-plan body cap); model-driven stays at 16 KB (`MAX_CONTENT_BYTES`).
- **Chat-row dedupe by deterministic id is load-bearing.** `peer_message_sent` → row id `peer-sent-<msgId>`; `peer_message_received` → row id `peer-recv-<msgId>`. Three layers of defense: (1) the WS handlers in `client/src/ws/handleServerMessage.ts` check for an existing row before `addMessage`; (2) `appStore.addMessage` does defense-in-depth id dedupe; (3) `onRehydrateStorage` strips persisted duplicates on load (one-time historical cleanup for pre-fix chats that piled up rows across tsx-watch restarts). Do NOT re-introduce non-deterministic ids (`peer-sent-${randomUUID()}` etc.) for peer rows — rehydrate-on-subscribe + every tsx-watch restart re-fires the same `peer_message_sent` and the chat grows unboundedly without the deterministic-id check. The random-id form is fine for genuinely unique rows (peer-rejected pills, user turns) because they never re-fire.
- **`ChatMessage.targetWorkflowId` is the full recipient wfid on `peer-sent` rows.** Set in the `peer_message_sent` WS handler (`client/src/ws/handleServerMessage.ts`); consumed by `PeerMessageBlock` in `ChatMessage.tsx`. The block looks up the relevant peer (recipient for sent rows, sender for received rows) in the cluster store and renders `displayName (role)`. Reactive on rename / re-role. The lookup memoizes on `clusters` (the referentially-stable map) — selecting via `s => ({...})` literal returns a fresh object each render and re-running through `useMemo` triggers an infinite update loop. Don't switch the selector shape.
- **Mailbox CANCEL is operator-only and only mutates pending state.** `DELETE /api/clusters/:id/mailbox/:wfid/:msgId` unlinks a pending file; delivered messages live in `events.jsonl` and are out of reach. The endpoint fires `notifyClusterStateForWorkflow(wfid)` so the depth chip drops without a refetch. No event is emitted to events.jsonl — the message was never delivered, so there's nothing to record. If a future surface wants an audit trail for cancellations, add an explicit `cluster_peer_message_cancelled` event; do NOT fake one through `peer_send_rejected` (that event's contract is "the substrate refused this send," not "the operator cancelled a queued send").
- **Review body inlines via `/api/workflows/artifact` are best-effort.** The `review_artifact_ready` handler in `client/src/ws/handleServerMessage.ts` seeds a header row immediately, then fetches the artifact body and patches the row. If the fetch fails, the header stays visible (REVIEWS tab still surfaces the artifact independently). Don't replace the header-then-patch with a single addMessage-after-fetch — the operator scrolling chat needs immediate verdict feedback even if the body fetch is slow.

### Cluster snapshots (2026-06-12 — save/restore full cluster state + member history)

- **Portable does not mean provider-session-portable.** `.arcocluster` carries Arco's config/events, not Claude/Codex/Cursor session stores or credentials. Native transports can rebuild from captured events where supported; CLI/app-server transports may start a fresh provider session on another machine. `portableWorkflowMeta()` clears `cliSessions` at BOTH save and restore: save keeps new artifacts honest; restore makes old artifacts safe. Do not remove the restore-side fence — otherwise a pre-fix Codex snapshot fails `thread/resume` with “no rollout found” on its destination. Never claim byte-identical proprietary provider context across computers.
- **Cloud blobs are encrypted BEFORE upload; the hub never receives the key.** `clusterVaultCrypto.ts` owns `ARCOCV01` AES-256-GCM envelopes and `~/.arco/cluster-vault.key` (0600). The hub-host hub stores opaque bytes + bounded metadata only. Do not move encryption into the hub or include the key in the portable bundle.
- **Imported/restored clusters are forks and runtime arming stays OFF.** Fresh cluster/member IDs prevent collisions. REMOTE, remote gates, idle nudges, auto-turn, and dispatcher execution are deliberately not re-armed by restore; a portable artifact must not start work or expose a cluster merely because it was imported. New snapshots carry the live member's full durable `WorkflowConfig` so client-only settings survive, but strip transient creation tokens and machine-local resume pointers; old snapshots fall back to canonical `workflowConfigFromMeta`. Passive cluster settings go through cluster setters.
- **Bundle verification is all-or-nothing.** `ARCOCL01` declares every regular file's path, byte length, and SHA-256. Traversal, duplicates, missing/extra files, truncation, checksum mismatch, and over-cap bodies fail before `importSnapshotFiles` creates the final snapshot directory.
- **Same-name snapshot replacement is ID-pinned and staged, never delete-then-save.** The first save returns `cluster_snapshot_conflict` with the existing summary; only the operator-confirmed retry carrying that exact `replaceSnapshotId` may replace it. `saveSnapshot` writes every member + manifest into a hidden sibling, renames the old directory to a hidden backup, then swaps the complete replacement at the stable id. Cloud follows the same ask/retry contract and pins `X-Arco-Vault-Replace-Id`; never weaken either path to a bare `replace: true` or delete the recoverable old copy before the new bytes exist.
- **A cluster tab is only a browser mirror; the live record is server-durable.** A lost localStorage entry must be recovered by listing `GET /api/clusters` and sending the normal `cluster_subscribe` for the original id — never by restoring an older snapshot (which forks new ids). Conversely, a genuinely missing id returns structured `cluster_not_found`; the client prunes that one stale mirror so it cannot emit “Unknown cluster” forever.
- **Restore replay MUST stay compacted — never replay raw streaming granularity.** `restoreSnapshot` pipes each member's captured `events.jsonl` through `truncateToLastNTurns` then `compactRestoredEvents` (`server/src/clusterSnapshots.ts`) before writing the target-cwd copy the WS handler replays. A live turn records token-ish `primary_text` deltas (~89% of a real log — 41,922 of 46,936 events in the incident log), started/completed `primary_tool_use` pairs, and per-call `primary_tool_counter` / per-file `diagnostics` state events. Replaying that raw flooded the client one WS message + one store update at a time: the tab stalled for minutes, missed heartbeats, and reconnect-looped under the SYNCING overlay (`snapshotReceived` never settling). Compaction merges adjacent same-phase text runs (the client coalesces replayed text into one bubble anyway), drops transient tool-status rows whose call settles later in the log, and keeps only the last counter / last diagnostics-per-file (both client handlers are last-write-wins). Post-compaction a 4-member 60k-event restore streams ~3.2k events in ~3s. Do NOT "restore full fidelity" — delta boundaries are invisible once the turn is history.
- **Codex call identity is `itemId`, not `toolUseId`.** Codex mints a fresh `toolUseId` per status event (started and completed carry different ones); only `itemId` (`call_…`) is stable across a call's lifecycle. `compactRestoredEvents` keys settled-call detection on `itemId ?? toolUseId`. Keying on `toolUseId` alone silently stops compacting Codex members (the 2026-06-12 first-pass bug).
- **Snapshot-shaped event types are latest-wins in compaction — `task_ledger_snapshot` especially.** Each one is a FULL copy of the cwd's task ledger and a busy cluster re-broadcasts it on every mutation: the real odytty snapshot carried 163 of them = 31.4 MB of its 58.9 MB total (one member: 110 copies, 22.7 MB of a 23.5 MB log). Text-delta coalescing alone left that whale untouched — the second 2026-06-12 incident (UI froze per batch, WS backpressure stalled the socket, the server's 15s zombie-socket heartbeat terminated the client mid-delivery, reconnect into a half-ingested cluster). Compaction now also: keeps only the last `task_ledger_snapshot` per workflowId (rule 5), truncates `primary_tool_use` input/output at 16 KB (rule 6 — the client's display cap; Native provider-context replay skips tool events so the model loses nothing), and keeps only the newest 300 tool rows per workflowId (rule 7 — the client trims chat at 500 messages / activity at 500 entries, so older rows were ingested and immediately discarded). Result: the 58.9 MB snapshot restores as 6.2 MB over the wire. If you add a new broadcast type that re-sends whole-state snapshots into `events.jsonl`, add it to the latest-wins set here.
- **The client persist layer must stringify LAZILY — never per `set()`.** `client/src/stores/persistence.ts` (`debouncedJSONStorage`) holds the partialized state object and JSON.stringifies once per debounced flush. Wrapping `createJSONStorage(...)` instead serializes the entire multi-MB jobs slice on EVERY store update — the debounce only delayed the disk write, not the serialization — which is what compounded a replay_batch ingest (thousands of `set()` calls in one task) into a minutes-long main-thread freeze. The freeze is what stops the browser draining the socket and gets the client heartbeat-terminated. Quota overflows at flush are logged (`persist flush failed`), not silent.
- **`cluster_created` is sent BEFORE any member history streams.** The restore handler (`handleClusterSnapshotRestore` in `server/src/ws/clusterHandlers.ts`) registers all members, sends `cluster_created`, and only then sends per-member `workflow_created` + event replay. Members-first ordering left the restored workers rendering as standalone tabs for the whole replay window (and permanently, if the operator closed the tab mid-flood). The cluster must exist client-side before any member does.
- **Each member's history is delivered as ONE `replay_batch` message — never a stream of N live events.** The client persists `lastSeq` + the last 200 chat messages on a debounce and reconnect asks for "events since lastSeq". Streaming history as live events races that model: an interrupt mid-flood (reload, heartbeat-miss disconnect) persisted `lastSeq≈head` with no chat, and replay-since-seq then had nothing left to resend — the member rendered as a **permanently blank worker** (2026-06-12). The batch is ingested by the client in one synchronous task (`case 'replay_batch'` in `handleServerMessage.ts` re-dispatches each inner event through the same reducer), so run-to-completion makes it all-or-nothing, React batches one render, and the debounced persist fires once. Do NOT revert to per-event `ws.send` loops for restore delivery. The reconnect path got the same treatment: `rehydrateOneWorkflow` (`handoffHandlers.ts`) ships a gap larger than 200 events as one `replay_batch` too (small gaps keep the per-event path), so a client that disconnects mid-restore can't be re-flooded by its own catch-up replay.
- **Provider-side context after restore (what the model actually sees on the next turn).** The restored chat window is only honest if the worker's next turn carries the conversation. Per transport: **Claude Native** rebuilds its API message history from the restored events.jsonl itself (`replayMessagesFromEvents` — pair-flush of `user_message_echo`/`primary_text`/`primary_complete`; tool events skipped), so the operator's `turnsPerMember` choice IS the model's restored context depth, and the compacted log is the ideal input (text already merged into one event per run). **Claude CLI** resumes the snapshot's last `cliSessions` entry — safe to wire even while the original member is open because `claude -p --resume <id>` FORKS into a fresh session file — but only on same-cwd restores (session files are keyed by cwd on disk; cross-cwd resume errors). **Codex** (`exec resume <uuid>`) APPENDS to the one thread with no fork, so the restore handler wires resume only when the original member workflow is no longer live; two live workflows pinned to one thread would interleave their turns. A codex member restored while its original is open starts its next turn with fresh provider context (chat window is history-only) — known, deliberate limitation.
- **Restore is a fork.** New clusterId + new member workflowIds are minted up front and folded into one idMap; `rewriteEventsLine` remaps every embedded id (peer `to`/`from`, the old clusterId in `cluster_member_nudged`, …) in the replayed lines. The snapshot's `planId` is deliberately NOT reattached — plans are keyed `(cwd, planId)`, so reuse would make the fork share one mutable plan file with the original; restore mints a fresh plan from `manifest.planBody`.
- **Save requires a quiescent cluster.** The save handler refuses while any member is `busy` — the event buffer flush + whole-file read could otherwise capture a torn final line. Snapshots store FULL member logs (no truncation at save time); truncation + compaction are restore-time so the operator chooses depth (`turnsPerMember`, default 25, "All" = omit) per restore from one full-fidelity capture.
- **`done`/`archived` stay operator-gated; smoke is `npm run smoke:cluster-snapshot`** — it asserts over-the-wire delivery (workflow_created + replayed history reach the client), truncation count, clusterId remap, and delta coalescing. Keep those assertions; each one guards a shipped regression.

### No-turn member rehydrate after a restart (2026-07-10 — roster/job decoupling)

- **A cluster member's roster card and its transcript job come from two DIFFERENT messages, and they can drift.** `cluster_state` carries the roster (member cards render from `cluster.members`); `workflow_created` carries the per-member job that the transcript + Activity panes read from (`ClusterTab` renders the pane only when `jobs[wfid]` exists, else the **"AWAITING REHYDRATE…"** placeholder). Member jobs are materialized LAZILY — a member that never takes a turn never gets a `workflow_created`, so it's never persisted to the client's `arco-jobs` localStorage and can't ride the reconnect `rehydrate_workflows` (which is built from `Object.values(store.jobs)` and needs the config the client never got). Result before the fix: a no-turn member (or one shadowed by a stale-localStorage twin — same display name, different id) rendered a card but its pane was stuck on "AWAITING REHYDRATE…" **forever**, and any turns it ran post-restart were invisible (their events fanned out to the cluster observer but landed on no job). It is NOT a blank conversation — there is no job object at all.
- **The server's `workflows` map does NOT register cluster members on boot — it fills lazily from client `rehydrate_workflows`.** So `workflows.get(memberWfid)` returns `undefined` after a restart for any member no connected client rehydrated. The first-pass fix (a `cluster_member_rehydrate` message that only sent `workflow_created` for a member already in `workflows`) therefore bailed silently on exactly the stuck case. The shipped handler (`handleClusterMemberRehydrate`) now reconstructs the member's `WorkflowConfig` from its persisted `meta.json` (config is stored field-by-field there — `primaryProvider`/`primaryModel`/`primaryEffort`/`cwd`/`name`/`providerAccountRef`/`efficiencyToolFilter` — NOT under a `config` key; `skipPermissions` defaults `true`) and registers the `WorkflowState` (WorkerManager + `derivePrimaryConfig` + `deriveAutoHandoff` + restored `cliSessions` primarySessionId) before sending `workflow_created` + `rehydrateOneWorkflow(…, 0)`. Per-branch `console.log` on every early return — the silent bail cost a debug round-trip; keep the logging.
- **The client request is deduped by `workflowId` and cleared ONLY on `workflow_created` (`requestedMemberRehydrate` in `handleServerMessage.ts`).** A failed attempt (server bailed, no `workflow_created`) leaves the guard set, so a plain WS auto-reconnect won't retry — a **page reload** is required to reset the module-level Set. This is why the recovery needs a hard refresh after a fix-deploy, not just a reconnect. If you make this retry on reconnect, clear the guard on a timeout or on `cluster_state` instead.
- **Eager boot-time member registration is now the primary fix (2026-07-10 followup).** `registerAllClusterMembersOnBoot` (`handoffHandlers.ts`, fired failure-isolated in the `index.ts` listen() epilogue alongside the recall backfill) walks `clusterStore.loadAllClustersFromDisk()` and registers every member's `WorkflowState` from `meta.json` so the `workflows` map never drifts across a restart — no more lazy-fill gap. It also warms `clusterStore`'s `memberIndex` (via `readCluster` per cluster) so a member's first post-restart turn resolves its `clusterId` for peer-tool wiring. `cluster_member_rehydrate` stays as the client-driven job-materialization channel (boot registration makes the server side complete; the client still needs `workflow_created` to build its job) AND the dynamic-add path (a member added mid-session that this browser has no job for).
- **Config reconstruction lives in ONE mapper — `workflowConfigFromMeta` (`workflowMeta.ts`).** Shared by the boot path and the on-demand handler (via `materializeMemberFromMeta`) so they can't drift. It carries EVERY turn-relevant persisted field, NOT just provider/model/effort — dropping `claudeTransport`/`codexTransport` alone silently runs a Native member on CLI / an appserver Codex member on exec (the latent bug the first pass shipped). Falsy-but-valid values must use type checks, not truthiness: `webSearchMaxUses: 0` (`typeof === 'number'`), `claudeNativeCompact: false` (`!== undefined`), efficiency `'off'` (truthy string). `caveman` outputStyle is aliased to `terse` in the mapper (the config type dropped the legacy value).
- **`meta.json` cannot carry client-only config fields — `advanced` (fallbackModel / additionalDirectories), `fastMode`, `allowedProviders`, `skipPermissions`.** A boot/on-demand materialization forces `skipPermissions: true` (the cluster-member default) and resets the rest. Two load-bearing consequences: (1) a member materialized from meta is marked `wf.bootMaterialized = true`, and the `existing` branch of `handleRehydrateWorkflows` REPLACES that placeholder config with the client's full one + re-derives `primary` when the client reconnects (`!busy` guarded) — this is what restores a SAFE-mode member's `skipPermissions: false` and a `fallbackModel`. Without it, boot registration wins the race against the client reconnect and the client's authoritative config is silently discarded. (2) A **boot-only member** (no client ever reconnects with its config — e.g. an armed dispatcher fires a turn on a browserless member) still runs with `skipPermissions: true`; the durable fix is to persist `skipPermissions`/`advanced` into `WorkflowMeta` (deferred follow-up).
- **`materializeMemberFromMeta` re-checks `workflows.get()` AFTER its `await readMeta` (TOCTOU).** The boot scan runs fire-and-forget concurrently with client reconnects; the top-of-function guard fires before the await, so a concurrent `rehydrate_workflows` could register the member in the window and the unconditional `workflows.set` would clobber it — dropping the client's observer (→ the exact frozen-pane symptom) and resetting an in-flight `WorkerManager` (→ defeated busy guard, double-turn). The post-await re-check closes it. Never remove it.
- **Dynamic membership (frequent add/remove) is handled by the existing paths, not by this fix.** Every add/remove path (add-existing, spawn-join, fresh-workers, detach/remove) already registers/deregisters in the `workflows` map at the moment of the change AND pushes a fresh `cluster_state`, so `requestMissingMemberJobs` materializes a runtime-added member and a removed member drops from the roster. Boot registration + the on-demand message only close the RESTART gap; both key off the atomically-maintained `members[]`, so add/remove between restarts is reflected automatically. Known minor edges (follow-ups): a client job for a removed member lingers (no `workflow_removed`; `cluster_member_removed` is a client no-op), and the `requestedMemberRehydrate` dedup guard clears only on `workflow_created` (a genuinely-unreadable-meta member stays stuck for the session).
- **Smoke: `npm run smoke:cluster-member-rehydrate`.** Drives the real reconstruction branch WITHOUT a restart via `remove_workflow` (evicts the map entry, keeps roster + meta). Two load-bearing traps it was written to avoid: (1) it must DRAIN the member's creation-time `workflow_created` before asserting the rehydrate one, else the buffer-first `waitFor` matches the stale message and passes even if the fix is broken; (2) the membership-guard test must use a workflow that HAS `meta.json` but isn't in the roster (else `readMeta`→null makes it pass guard-independently).

## Integrated terminal (Workbench Expansion §K1 / §K2 / §K3 / §K4)

- **Operator-only — NEVER wire the model into the PTY.** `ptyManager` is not a tool; nothing in `server/src/providers/*Tools.ts` or any tool registry references it. Agents have their own per-turn Bash within tool-use. Exposing this PTY as a callable tool is the autonomous-shell fence — the exact thing the four-question test exists to reject. If you add a "run a command in the terminal" surface, it must be operator-pre-filled-never-auto-executed (the K3 chip pattern), not a model tool.
- **Session ≠ connection — closing the window must DETACH, not kill.** `terminalChannel.ts`'s `ws.on('close')` calls `ptyManager.detach`, never `kill`. The shell (and any `npm run dev` / `sleep 30` in it) keeps running; the scrollback ring keeps filling so the next attach repaints. Only `terminal_kill` (operator KILL), `killForWorkflow` (tab close), or `killAll` (shutdown) reap a session. If you ever make window-close kill the shell, you've broken THE acceptance criterion. The smoke (`npm run smoke:terminal`) asserts detach-keeps-alive explicitly.
- **The raw byte stream is OFF the seq'd `/ws` protocol and OUT of `events.jsonl`.** It rides the dedicated `/terminal` WS path (`terminalChannel.ts`), which never touches `persistAndStampSeq` or `broadcastWorkflow`. Only lifecycle (`terminal_opened` / `terminal_killed`) and command lines (`terminal_command_logged`) are persisted, via `broadcastWorkflow` from `index.ts`. Don't "unify" the channels to simplify — streaming every PTY frame through `events.jsonl` would bloat the log by orders of magnitude (the deliberate granularity choice).
- **`/terminal` inherits `/ws`'s access posture; it does not add its own.** Both ride the same loopback-bound HTTP server (`HOST=127.0.0.1` by default — `server/src/server/constants.ts`). There is NO per-connection token/origin check on `/ws`; the loopback bind IS the gate. `terminalChannel.handleTerminalUpgrade` is fed from the same `httpServer.on('upgrade')` fan-out, so it's exactly as exposed as `/ws` — no more, no less. If you ever add auth to `/ws`, add the same to `/terminal` in the same change; don't let them drift.
- **The HTTP server must have exactly ONE `upgrade` listener — the manual fan-out in `index.ts` (2026-06-01 incident).** Do NOT build the `/ws` server as `new WebSocketServer({ server: httpServer, path: '/ws' })`: that makes ws attach its OWN `upgrade` listener IN ADDITION to the manual fan-out. Both fire for every upgrade, and for any non-`/ws` upgrade (`/terminal`, preview) the fan-out upgrades the socket first, then ws's listener (path mismatch → `shouldHandle` false) calls `abortHandshake(socket, 400)` on the now-LIVE socket, writing `HTTP/1.1 400 Bad Request` into the raw WS stream. The browser reads the leading `H` (0x48) as a frame header with RSV1 set → "Invalid frame header" and a permanently dead channel (the Vite proxy is innocent — this reproduces on a bare `:3001` connection). Fix/shape: `wss` is `noServer:true`; the single fan-out routes `/ws` via `wss.handleUpgrade(req, socket, head, (c, r) => wss.emit('connection', c, r))`. If you add another WS endpoint, route it in that SAME fan-out — never give a second `WebSocketServer` the `{ server }` option.
- **xterm needs LITERAL colours/fonts — CSS custom properties don't resolve in a canvas (2026-06-01).** `XtermView` renders to `<canvas>`, so `theme.background` / `fontFamily` must be concrete strings; `fontFamily: 'var(--font-code)'` silently fell back to monospace and the bg was hardcoded. Read the computed tokens off `:root` — `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue('--color-bg' | '--color-fg' | '--color-accent-primary' | '--font-code')` — pass those to the `Terminal` ctor, and re-apply on `settings.themeId` change (apply.ts has written the new vars by then). Never hand `var(--…)` to xterm options. ANSI 16-colour palette is left at xterm's default on purpose so coloured shell output stays legible on every theme.
- **The `/terminal` WS must be proxied in `client/vite.config.ts` — and the smokes can't catch a regression (§K4).** XtermView connects to `ws(s)://${location.host}/terminal`; in the browser / over a 5173 tunnel that host is Vite, so the `configureServer` upgrade handler must forward `/terminal` upgrades to the Arco server exactly like `/ws`. K1 shipped proxying only `/ws`, so the in-app terminal and the K4 pop-out were both dead in an actual browser — undetected because `scripts/smoke/terminal*.ts` connect to `:3001` directly, bypassing Vite. K4 added `/terminal` to the handler (`wsPath === '/ws' || wsPath === '/terminal' || previewWfid`). It's collision-free: Vite's own HMR upgrade keys off the `vite-hmr` subprotocol, which a bare `new WebSocket('/terminal')` never sends. If you touch that upgrade handler, keep `/terminal` in it; there is no automated test guarding it (browser-only path).
- **Remote-shell caveat (Tailscale).** When Arco is tunneled, `/terminal` is a *remote shell on the workstation*. This is by design (the operator opted into the tunnel) but is the single highest-blast-radius surface Arco exposes. Keep the docs warning prominent; if a future "expose remotely" toggle lands, gating the terminal behind an explicit opt-in is reasonable. Arco does not sandbox the operator's own commands — same as any terminal.
- **cwd never escapes the workflow root.** `ptyManager.spawn` runs `resolveWithinCwd(workflowRoot, requestedCwd)` on every spawn; an escape throws and NO shell is spawned (the `index.ts` handler errors back). The Files-tab "open terminal here" entry passes an absolute dir path that's already inside the root, but the guard is the backstop. Don't bypass it by passing a pre-resolved cwd straight to `pty.spawn`.
- **Command-line capture is best-effort and client-side.** `XtermView` accumulates printable input and flushes on Enter (`\r`). It's a visibility log, not a shell parser — it doesn't expand aliases/history, handle heredocs, or know about `&&` pipelines. The *guarantee* is robust lifecycle logging (`terminal_opened`/`terminal_killed`); exact command capture is documented best-effort. Don't build server-side parsing of the raw stream to "improve" it — that re-introduces the chattiness the channel split exists to avoid.
- **§K2 multi-listener: `attach` ADDS a listener, it no longer REPLACES.** K1's `PtySession.onData`/`onExit` was a single callback; a second attach silently stole the stream from the first window. §K2 widened this to a `Set<Listener>` so two windows can view one session and both render live output, and `proc.onData` fans out to all. `attach` returns a `dispose` that removes EXACTLY that listener; the channel calls it on socket close. Don't revert to a single callback or use `detach(sessionId)` (clears ALL) on a single-window close — closing one of two co-viewing windows would kill the other's stream. The smoke asserts two listeners on one session both receive, and that disposing one leaves the other live.
- **§K2 session list comes from the LIVE `ptyManager`, not events.jsonl.** `terminal_opened` is only broadcast at spawn time and PTYs don't survive a server restart, so replaying the events.jsonl lifecycle history would list DEAD sessions. The switcher is populated by `terminal_list`→`terminal_sessions` (a live `ptyManager.infoForWorkflow` snapshot), and the server re-emits `terminal_sessions` on rehydrate (per-socket, in `rehydrateOneWorkflow`). Don't try to reconstruct the session list from replayed `terminal_opened`/`terminal_killed` events — it'll resurrect sessions that died with the last restart.
- **§K2 PTY orphan reaping mirrors workers, with NO RSS auto-kill.** See `§ Worker safety` above for the full rule. Short version: `ptyPidRegistry.ts` + `reapOrphanTerminals()` (wired after `reapOrphanWorkers()` before `listen()`) reap shells leaked by a hard kill; a clean `kill`/exit/shutdown unregisters the pid so the next boot sweep won't SIGKILL a recycled pid. **Never RSS-auto-kill an operator shell** — it runs processes the operator started on purpose; that's the operator-in-control fence, distinct from the autonomous-worker RSS guard.
- **§K4 pop-out is a query-param-on-root branch, NOT a new route/router.** There is no router in the client. `main.tsx` reads `?popoutTerminal=<sessionId>` and renders `<TerminalPopout/>` instead of `<App/>` (after `bootstrapCachedTheme()`). The query-param-on-root shape is deliberate: root `/` is always served `index.html` (Vite dev SPA fallback; the `^/.*` proxy bypass returns `req.url` for any non-preview request), so it can't collide with the `/terminal` **WS upgrade** path (an upgrade is not a GET; the fan-out matches the exact `/terminal` path post-query-strip), `/ws`, or `/api/*`. Don't "promote" this to a `/terminal/<id>` path segment — it would visually collide with the WS path and any future `/terminal` proxy rule, and Arco ships only as a Vite dev server (the :3001 server serves no client HTML), so there's no prod static handler to lean on. Don't add react-router for this.
- **§K4 pop-out ATTACHES, never spawns — and offers no Kill.** `TerminalPopout` mounts `<XtermView sessionId={id}/>` only; it boots no app stores and no `/ws`. The `/terminal` `attach` needs only the sessionId (the session knows its workflowId server-side). The pop-out coexists with the in-app floating window viewing the same session (§K2 multi-listener fans output to both; typing in either drives the same shell) — popping out does NOT close the floating window, and that coexistence is the K4 acceptance point. Closing the pop-out fires `ws.on('close', detach)` → the per-socket `disposeListener` removes ONLY that socket's listener, leaving the floating window attached and the shell alive (the §K1/§K2 invariants, unchanged — K4 needed no server change). The pop-out has no KILL (it can't resolve the label without `/ws` either — it shows the 8-char id); Kill stays on windows that have the `/ws` control plane. K4 added no protocol/server surface, so it has no smoke of its own — acceptance is manual (open a pop-out, type in both windows, close the pop-out, confirm the shell survives); the existing `smoke:terminal` multi-listener step already covers the two-sockets-one-session mechanism it relies on.
- **§K3 pre-fill MUST NOT submit a line — single OR multi-line.** The "Run in terminal" chat chip writes an agent-suggested command to the PTY input line via the `/terminal` channel's `prefill { data }` message; the handler calls `ptyManager.write(sessionId, wrapPrefillForPty(data))`. `wrapPrefillForPty` strips ANY trailing `\r`/`\n` (via `stripLineTerminator`) AND wraps the body in **bracketed-paste markers** (`\x1b[200~` … `\x1b[201~`). The wrap is load-bearing for **multi-line** blocks: `stripLineTerminator` only removes the *trailing* terminator, so a multi-line ```` ```bash ```` block (e.g. `npm install\nnpm test`) still carries an INTERIOR `\n` — written bare, the PTY line discipline executes the first line immediately, leaving the rest on the prompt. Bracketed paste makes a paste-aware shell hold the whole block on the edit line until the **operator** presses Enter — nothing executes without an operator keystroke. This is the operator-in-the-loop guarantee that makes K3 not "the agent runs a command". If you drop the bracketed-paste wrap, or append `\n`/`\r` on the prefill path (client or server), you've broken the fence and turned a suggestion into an auto-run. **Degradation caveat:** a shell with bracketed paste *disabled* shows the `\x1b[200~`/`\x1b[201~` markers as literal text rather than executing — strictly safer than the old bare-newline path, just cosmetically odd (matches a real paste into such a shell); modern shells (bash ≥4.4, zsh, fish) enable it by default. There is NO server path that submits a command line on its own. The "run immediately" toggle from the plan was deliberately not built. The smoke (`npm run smoke:terminal-prefill`) asserts the wrapped transform (single + multi-line) + spawns a real PTY to confirm neither a single-line command nor a multi-line block's first line executes until Enter.

## Worktree bake-offs

- **A bake-off is an implementation race, not COMPARE with write access.**
  COMPARE stays a read-only, no-tools answer primitive. BAKE-OFF creates
  ordinary visible workflow tabs and isolated linked worktrees from one exact
  source commit. Keep the surfaces and protocol domains distinct.
- **The source must be project-clean at start and promotion, and its `HEAD`
  must still equal `baseRef`.** `.arco` state is intentionally ignored by
  the cleanliness check. Never weaken the exact-base guard into "merge whatever
  is current" — a stale candidate then stops being a fair/reproducible race.
- **No model picks the winner.** Attempt agents may implement and report; only
  `bakeoff_promote`, sent by an explicit operator click, may merge. There is no
  automatic rank, merge, push, or cleanup path.
- **`.arco` must never reach a candidate merge.** Candidate diffs exclude it;
  promotion stages normally then unstages `.arco`; and pre-existing attempt
  commits are scanned and refused if they contain `.arco` paths. This matters
  because each attempt is itself an Arco workflow and writes journals into
  its worktree.
- **Force-removal is fenced twice.** `removeBakeoffAttempt` accepts only a path
  beneath `~/.arco/bakeoffs/<cwd-hash>/worktrees`, then checks git's registered
  worktree list. Cleanup removes the checkout, not its branch or manifest.
- **Project trust inheritance requires proof, not the config field.**
  `managedWorktreeSourceCwd` is persisted so attempt tabs can reuse the source
  repo's DD7 decision, but `turnEngine` first proves the target is beneath the
  source's managed bake-off root and registered by git. Never trust the
  client-supplied source path on its own or write a duplicate trust decision
  for the generated worktree path.
- **Disable fan-out multipliers on attempt tabs.** Attempt config forces
  Ultracode off and clears Goal Run; the bake-off's 2–5 legs are the entire
  operator-authorized budget. `workflow_created.backgroundCreated` also keeps a
  batch from stealing source-tab focus once per leg.

Smoke: `npm run smoke:bakeoff`.

## ARCHIVE explorer (`?app=explorer`)

- **Every `/api/explorer/*` listing/scan endpoint is $HOME-jailed and fail-soft — always 200, never 5xx.** `server/src/http/explorerRoutes.ts` applies a post-resolution `realpath` check ($HOME jail, the same discipline as `/api/docs/source`) on every path it touches; `artifacts` / `docs` / `recents` / `changed` degrade to an empty collection (`[]`) on any error rather than throwing. `POST /api/explorer/open` also returns 200 `{ ok:false, code }` (`no-browser` / `spawn-failed`) on launch failure so the client can fall back. Don't add a path input that skips the realpath jail, and don't let a scan throw a 5xx — the window degrades to empty, never an error page.
- **The window opens via a same-browser `window.open` popup FIRST — that's what gives it correct per-monitor DPI.** `client/src/explorer/launch.ts` `openArchive()` tries a named `window.open(..., 'arco-explorer', 'popup,...')` popup because it inherits the workspace window's per-monitor DPI (a server-spawned `--app=` browser guesses the primary monitor's DPI → renders huge on 1080p, tiny on 4K) and opens on the operator's machine (correct over a tunnel). Only if the popup is refused does it fall back to `POST /api/explorer/open` (server-spawned window), then a plain tab. Don't reorder the server-spawn ahead of the popup.
- **The window is a query-param-on-root branch, not a route.** `client/src/main.tsx` renders `<ExplorerApp/>` when `?app=explorer` (parallel to the terminal pop-out branch). Read-only, zero model authority.
- **The recent-files ledger is advisory/best-effort — `appendRecord` swallows its own errors so it can NEVER break a broadcast.** `server/src/recentFilesLedger.ts` (`~/.arco/recent-files.jsonl`, append-only, compacted `MAX_LINES=1000` → `KEEP_LINES=500`) is fed fire-and-forget from the persisting broadcast chokepoints (`server/src/server/broadcast.ts`) and the manual `POST /api/files/write`. A ledger write failure is caught and dropped; don't `await` it into a broadcast path or let it reject a mutation.

## Backup / restore archive safety (ARCO_OPS_PLAN §F)

- **The FORMAT eliminates dangerous archive ENTRIES; restore guards the TARGET tree.** `scripts/backup/archive.ts` can ONLY represent regular files (`{relPath, bytes}`) — there is nowhere on the wire to encode a symlink target, a hardlink, or a special-file type, and `readArchive` re-rejects an unsafe `relPath` (leading `/` or any `..` segment) on read. So a malicious/corrupt archive can't smuggle a symlink/device/fifo through. **Do not add symlink/hardlink/special-entry "support" to the format** — that re-introduces the exact dangerous-entry class the format exists to delete. Restore (`scripts/backup/restore.ts`) therefore defends the **target directory** and the **extraction act**, not the entries: traversal, target-tree symlinks, overwrite, and exhaustion.
- **Restore is verify-FIRST and aborts the WHOLE run on any problem.** `planRestore` runs `verifyArchive` before anything else; on any problem it returns `aborted` and `applyRestore` writes NOTHING. There is no "restore the good parts of a bad archive" path — a tampered/truncated/incomplete archive never touches the fs. Don't add a `--skip-verify` flag.
- **`applyRestore` lstat()s EVERY intermediate path component and refuses to descend a symlink.** The archive can't carry a symlink, but the **target** dir might already contain one (`linkdir -> /etc`), and writing `linkdir/x` would escape the root. Per planned file, walk each directory component under `targetRoot` with **`lstatSync`** (NOT `statSync` — we must SEE the symlink, not follow it); a symlinked (or non-directory) component → the entry is recorded `rejected` and skipped, never followed. Don't "optimize" this to a single `mkdir -p` + write: `mkdir` follows existing symlinks and the write would land outside the root.
- **Never overwrite — writes use the `wx` flag (`O_CREAT | O_EXCL`).** A pre-existing target makes the write fail `EEXIST` → recorded as `skippedExisting`, never clobbered. This is why restore can't destroy an operator's local edits even if pointed at a populated dir. Don't switch to `writeFile` default flag or a `force`/overwrite mode without re-deriving this fence — the smoke asserts original bytes survive.
- **A cap breach aborts the WHOLE run (no partial extraction).** `MAX_FILES` (50 000) / `MAX_TOTAL_BYTES` (2 GiB) are checked in `planRestore` against the declared entry set; exceeding either returns `aborted` so `applyRestore` writes nothing. The point is to bound a maliciously-inflated manifest BEFORE any bytes hit disk — keep the cap check in the pure plan, not mid-write.
- **Restore NEVER writes into the live `~/.arco` by default.** `--into <DIR>` is required and must NOT already exist (a fresh dir can't merge into a live tree). The CLI refuses a target that is or is inside the live Arco home (`ARCO_HOME`/`~/.arco`) unless `--force-into-live` is given. `planRestore`/`applyRestore` are home-agnostic (operate only on the `targetRoot` handed in); the live-home refusal lives in the CLI (`doRestore`), which is the only layer that knows the home. Any rejection surfaces as a non-zero exit so a scripting operator notices a partial result. Smoke: `npm run smoke:backup-restore`.

## Genesis (ARCO_OPS_PLAN §M)

- **A from-nothing genesis workflow is NOT cwd-less at runtime — it runs in a scratch dir.** The turn engine hard-guards on a working directory (`turnEngine.ts` — "Workflow has no working directory configured"), so `handleCreateWorkflow` assigns `~/.arco/genesis/<wfid>` (`genesisScratchDir`) when `config.genesisSeed && !config.workingDirectory`. "No cwd" is a UI/product framing; on disk there's always a real cwd. Don't try to relax the turn guard for genesis — the scratch dir is the sanctioned answer.
- **The genesis system block injects while in the scratch dir OR while Creative mode is armed.** `turnEngine.ts` gates `buildGenesisSystemBlock` on `inGenesisScratch || inGenesisCreative` — `inGenesisScratch = genesisSeed && /[\\/]\.arco[\\/]genesis[\\/]/.test(cwd)` (M2 from-nothing) and `inGenesisCreative = genesisSeed.creative === true` (M3 in-workflow). A from-nothing genesis stops injecting after scaffold (cwd becomes real, `creative` unset); a Creative-mode workflow keeps injecting until disarmed. **Don't gate on `genesisSeed` alone** (it's mirrored into meta after a from-nothing scaffold, so it stays set — you'd inject forever on a real project that isn't in Creative mode).
- **Scaffold = move-the-store rebind; seq + transcript continuity depend on the exact order.** `handleGenesisScaffold` MUST: `flushWorkflowBuffer(scratch)` → move `.arco/workflows/<wfid>` scratch→real (rename, EXDEV-fallback to cp+rm) → `forgetWorkflowBuffer(scratch)` → `initWorkflowStore(real)` (which recovers the seq from the MOVED `events.jsonl` tail). Re-init AFTER the move, not before, or the seq resets to 0 and the client sees a gap. Guard on `!wf.busy` so no turn is writing the events file mid-move. Smoke: `npm run smoke:genesis-rebind`.
- **From-nothing scaffold reuses the current workflow tab, then renames it.** It does NOT spawn a new tab by default; it rebinds the existing scratch workflow to the real project cwd and broadcasts `workflow_renamed` with the final seed title. Otherwise the tab keeps the throwaway Genesis working name even though files/cwd are correct.
- **The scaffold writer is the M2/M3 security boundary (Appendix D).** `writeScaffold` rejects absolute / `..` / backslash / NUL / hidden-dotfile paths except the deterministic root `.gitignore`, refuses to write through a **symlinked ancestor** (lstat the chain), never overwrites (`wx` flag), and caps the file count. Seed docs are generated deterministically from the seed — **no model proposes paths**. Don't reroute scaffold writes through `writeFileWithinCwd` (it allows overwrite + doesn't reject symlink ancestors). Smoke: `npm run smoke:genesis-scaffold`.
- **Genesis `.gitignore` must protect local workflow/agent state before git init.** From-nothing scaffold moves `.arco/workflows/<wfid>` into the project before `git init`, so `buildSeedDocs` must generate a root `.gitignore` that excludes `.arco/` and local agent notes (`AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `GEMINI.md`) before `git add -A` runs. Public-facing project brief belongs in README/SPEC/TODO; local agent notes stay untracked. Smoke: `npm run smoke:genesis-scaffold`.
- **Genesis field assist is proposal-only.** `genesis_assist_request` may call the workflow's current primary agent, but it must run as a read-only, tool-less worker and emit only `genesis_assist_started` / `genesis_assist_proposed`. It must NOT mutate `wf.config.genesisSeed`, write files, generate scaffold paths, or bypass the normal `genesis_seed_update` apply path. Smoke: `npm run smoke:genesis-assist`.
- **Genesis name history lives on the seed, not transient UI.** Working-name assists can return `options[]`, but shortlisted/rejected names must persist through `GenesisSeed.nameWorkbench` via `genesis_seed_update`; otherwise a refresh/restart makes the model loop through rejected names again. Smoke: `npm run smoke:genesis-assist`.
- **The seed's durable home while cwd-less is `WorkflowConfig.genesisSeed`, not meta.json.** Before scaffold there's a scratch meta.json, but the authoritative copy rides the client-persisted config (re-sent on `rehydrate_workflows`). After scaffold it's mirrored into `meta.json`. The `genesis_seed_update` handler writes both (config always; meta only if a cwd exists).
- **Creative-mode scaffold NEVER touches the existing project (M3).** `handleGenesisScaffold` branches on `seed.creative` to `handleCreativeScaffold`, which writes into a sibling `<cwd>/ALTERNATIVE/<slug>/` dir and seeds a **separate** plan (fresh id). It must NOT move the store, NOT rebind the cwd, NOT `git init` (it's inside the existing repo), and NOT set `wf.planId` (the existing active plan stays the workflow's plan; the alternative is attached so it's discoverable, not imposed). Confinement to `ALTERNATIVE/` + the writer's no-overwrite guard are the safety boundary. Don't reuse the from-nothing move/rebind path here.
- **Creative-mode arm reads are deterministic and model-free.** `gatherProjectContext` / `assessCodebase` (`server/src/genesis/creative.ts`) only read disk (`package.json`, `README.md`, `git ls-files`, a capped walk) — never a model, never a write. The walk skips `.git`/`node_modules`/build dirs and caps at 400 so a giant tree can't stall arming. `assessCodebase`'s `established`/warning is **advisory** — it gates nothing. `gatherProjectContext`'s output rides `seed.projectContext` and is injected *before* the seed so the model anchors to it; the grounding rule forbids inventing past it. Smoke: `npm run smoke:genesis-scaffold` (creative section).


## Hands-free voice

- **Do not enable browser microphone AGC.** Both production capture and the
  standalone microphone preview request `autoGainControl:false`: browser AGC
  can lower the system input gain behind the operator's back (StreamCam was
  found at 43%, 2026-10-17; the actor was not conclusively identified). Retain
  echo cancellation and noise suppression. New constraints apply on mic start.
- **Speech endpointing is only transcription scheduling.** Use 350 ms silence
  segments while retaining the two-minute command buffer and explicit closing
  phrase. Parakeet v3 INT8 is the preferred CPU recognizer on Linux; retain
  Moonshine on Windows (known empty Parakeet finals there). Prepare both ASR
  and TTS before listening; no speech model stays alive after session deletion.
  Recognition failure discards the pending command rather than allowing a
  later End capture to submit the incomplete remainder. Timing diagnostics
  stay in the non-persisted voice store.
- **Project updates are one bounded operator request.** Resolve the named
  workflow or one unique cluster director; preserve drafts/attachments and
  send through normal chat as follow-up. Never guess a director. Only an idle
  recipient gets one new-answer readback subscription, cancelled on off,
  mode/selection change or the next command. Never read an older busy turn
  as the requested status. This adds no new worker/orchestration authority.
- **Fast wake is provisional; full transcription authorizes capture.** Streaming
  local Vosk phrase detection can play the acknowledgement/show capture before the
  silence endpoint. It cannot invoke Luna or execute a command. Keep the full
  overlapping PCM segment, validate Computer as its first whole word, and retain
  immediate follow-on words. "First whole word" is tolerant, not literal
  (`repairWakeWord`, 2026-10-17: the operator said Computer three times before one
  was accepted): a first token one edit from "computer" (s ignored —
  Computers, Commuter, Computed) wakes alone; with the phrase grammar or the
  fast wake agreeing, up to three edits or two fused tokens ("Come here").
  Never loosen it to a prepend: "Hey Computer …" and an unrecognizable first
  word must still fail, or a speculative fast wake could authorize an
  utterance whose first word was something else (the runtime test pins it).
  Reject later/prefixed mentions, canceled/late candidates and responses after
  disable. The fast-wake partial is HELD until 0.6 s of speech has been heard
  (`recognizer.py`, 2026-10-17): the forced grammar turned "Can you…" into a
  partial "computer" 250 ms in and chimed; "Can you bring up the plan" now
  stays silent in `scripts/voice/check-phrases.py` while "Computer" still
  chimes as the word ends in that synthetic check — but live it still fired on
  "Thank you", so **the chime no longer plays on the fast wake at all**: it
  plays when the full transcript verifies Computer as the first word
  (`receive`, `input.started`). The fast wake only shows the early capture
  so follow-on speech is kept. Never move the chime back to the grammar.
- **Computer does the literal thing; dialogs open, they don't act.** "Bring
  up the nudge dialog" once sent a nudge because the only nudge-shaped tool
  sent one. `arco_open_dialog` opens a picker via `voice/dialogs.ts` (a
  synchronous DOM event the owning ClusterTab / TerminalInput answers) and
  reports "not available" when nothing answered; `arco_nudge` sends. Keep
  the prompt's open-means-open rule and never let a tool description invite
  the model to substitute a nearby action.
- **`arco_screen` / `arco_act` are the long tail, not a shortcut.** They
  drive the real DOM (`.click()`, native value setter + input/change,
  KeyboardEvent on the focused element or document), so a control is only
  reachable if it is visible and has a name: aria-label, label, text, help
  title or title. New icon-only buttons need a `data-help` key (with a title)
  or an aria-label or Computer cannot see them. Dialogs must keep
  `role="dialog"` + `aria-label` (or a heading) — that is how the listing
  groups them and how act reports "Dialog X closed". Destructive labels
  (`DANGER` in screen.ts) refuse without `confirm`; keep dialog Cancel/Close
  off that list. The numbered snapshot is only valid until the next
  `arco_screen`; stale numbers are rejected rather than clicking whatever
  moved into the slot. A manual End during verification
  waits for that verification; Cancel prevents late rearming. Wake failure
  leaves full recognition available. Never restart wake scanning mid-utterance.
- **Reserved-phrase repairs need independent final acoustic agreement.** Keep
  raw general transcription separate from the constrained grammar. Repair a
  hallucinated A/The Computer prefix only at a new capture, and And capture
  only as a verified terminal closing phrase in a command. Ordinary dictation
  and later Computer stay literal. Show raw → corrected diagnostics in memory;
  no control history is created. Never replace/drop the directive body just
  because the phrase grammar only recognized Computer.
- **Voice status reserves layout space.** The compact row is beneath the tabs;
  details expand inside it and collapse after enabling. No fixed/absolute
  overlay over workflow content. Disable and capture controls stay reachable
  while collapsed; ambiguity choices must remain visible.
- **Computer is the required first word for commands.** Use the shared client
  `voiceCommandBody` gate before deterministic parsing or Luna interpretation,
  also in the standalone recognition test. No legacy Arco trigger or "hey"
  preamble. Computer alone arms an empty directive. Unprefixed speech continues
  an armed capture or explicit dictation; otherwise it is ignored.
- **Pause is never a command terminator.** `VoiceCommandBuffer` accumulates
  segments until the exact “End capture” directive. No parsing, model
  call, or action before submission. No timer executes a command. Pending text
  stays visible and memory-only; cancel/disable discard it. Bind selection at
  command start and reject changes before spending quota/executing. Quiet/off
  are the immediate controls; playback cannot arm/append/commit commands.
  End capture and Cancel capture are whole reserved closing phrases, including
  across ASR segments. A two-minute idle expiry discards the buffer without
  action; each recognized segment renews it. Wake acknowledgement uses a short
  local chime without blocking the next spoken directive behind TTS playback.
- **OFF owns no microphone or recognition work.** Voice never auto-arms on
  mount/reload. Stop tracks, close audio, abort fetches, delete the server lease
  and invalidate the epoch before accepting any more callbacks. Late permission
  grants must stop their newly returned tracks; late transcripts/model/speech
  responses must not execute. Disconnect/pagehide also disarm.
- **Voice control history is deliberately absent.** Audio, utterances, rosters,
  model responses and usage stay in memory. No voice audit event, transcript,
  console tee or persistence cwd. Only ordinary effects use existing history.
  Incognito names/content never enter Luna; direct/local commands remain usable.
- **Recipient pinning and explicit send are load-bearing.** Silence only ends an
  audio segment. Drafts stay on the original workflow across navigation. Reject
  a command if selection changed while it was recognized/interpreted. Ambiguity
  requires a numbered choice; removed recipients/disconnect/compaction preserve
  the draft. Playback-captured audio can only quiet or disable, never send —
  unless it starts with the name: "Computer …" heard during playback cuts
  the speech and is an ordinary directive (barge-in, 2026-10-17).
- **Barge-in holds speech, it does not kill it.** `receiveWake` during
  playback calls `holdSpeech()` (the fast wake is only the phrase grammar's
  guess); the verified transcript ends playback via `quiet()`, an unverified
  one (`wake-unverified`), a discarded utterance or the wake expiry calls
  `releaseSpeech()` / `quiet()`. Never drop the `speech.held` replay path: a
  false fast wake would otherwise silently swallow the rest of an answer.
  `speakingItsOwnName()` ignores a wake while the sentence being spoken
  contains "Computer" — keep it; the manual's voice section says the word in
  most sentences and echo cancellation is not perfect. "stop" / "quiet" are
  closings ONLY for an empty capture (`STANDALONE_STOP`); inside a directive
  they are literal (the operator: "I might use those words in a directive").
- **Luna is a discrete subscription helper, not a standing director.** Direct
  commands/dictation bypass it. Fresh ephemeral Codex turns use low effort,
  fixed instruction/schema files, no project docs/user config/rules/MCP, disabled
  shell/agent/web/app features, read-only scratch cwd, timeout/output limits and
  no retries. Keep `OPENAI_API_KEY` out of the child environment. Token receipts
  are live-only, not a claimed percentage of subscription quota.
- **Local speech processes are leased to an enabled window.** Heartbeat every
  15s, expiry at 45s, at most two windows. Delete aborts interpretation and kills
  recognition/output processes; Linux speech children also set parent-death
  signalling. Setup installs assets only and never starts microphone capture.

- **A tab on a stale bundle answers "Unknown tool." to every tool it predates** (2026-10-17: "close the nudge window" → Computer said "the screen tool returned an error, can you close it manually?"). Prod serves `client/dist` from disk, so every `npm run build` swaps the bundle under open tabs silently. `client/src/buildCheck.ts` re-fetches `index.html` on every WebSocket open and raises the yellow RELOAD banner (`StaleBuildBanner`) when the hashed bundle differs; the executor's unknown-tool error now says the page needs a reload. Keep both: the banner is the only signal an operator gets, and Computer relays the error text verbatim. Every `/api/voice/tool` call and its answer is logged as `[voice tool] name args → ok|ERROR ms` so the next one is diagnosable from the journal.
- **A question *about* a project is answered by Computer; a question *to* a worker is typed to it.** The operator (2026-10-17): "it's like a layer between me and Arco… it knows WHERE it needs to go to get information." The prompt's third case (CONVERSATION) routes: how Arco works → manual (`arco_open manual` + `arco_read_text` filter); project/member state → `arco_read_recent` / `arco_status` on the project or its Director; on-screen → `arco_read_text`; otherwise just answer. Only an utterance that addresses the worker ("ask the director…") becomes `arco_send_message`. The smoke's status-question steps pin this; earlier they expected the opposite (typed to the worker), which was the 2026-10-17 morning design and is superseded. Memory is the Codex thread itself (one persistent chat, resumed every turn); the prompt tells Computer to use it for follow-ups and to drop context on a subject change.
- **Dictation is a prompt rule, not a client keyword.** "dictate" / "verbatim" / "word for word" / "exactly this" make the rest of the utterance (or, after "I want to dictate a message to X" → "Go ahead.", the whole next utterance) go to the worker character for character; otherwise messages are typed in the operator's words and intent. The smoke asserts byte equality on two dictations with fillers and a stutter. Don't add a client-side "dictate" parser — that would be exact matching, and Luna already keeps the text intact at low effort.
- **`arco_read_text` is Computer's eyes for words; `arco_screen` is for controls.** 2026-10-17: asked to read the Strata section of the manual, Computer opened the manual, clicked the right entry, then said "the text isn't exposed to me" because nothing returned text. `readText` returns the open dialog's text (topmost = last in DOM order), or one section by heading match (`section`/`article`/`details`/… ancestor of a matching `h1–h6`), else passages mentioning the word, capped at 12 000 chars. Real browsers use `innerText`; jsdom gets a block-aware walker. The prompt says reading aloud is the one case where a long reply is right.
- **Role-less modals are dialogs too.** Most Arco modals are a `position: fixed; inset: 0` backdrop with no `role="dialog"` (DOCS, GIT, SPAWN, REVIEW, HANDOFF, SYSTEM, the nudge picker). `screen.ts` treats any fixed ancestor covering ≥80 % of the viewport that takes pointer events and holds a control as a dialog, named by its aria-label, heading, or first short text run; the phosphor backdrops are `pointer-events: none` and never qualify. Don't add a per-dialog list. The nudge picker also got `role="dialog" aria-label="Nudge — pick recipients"`.
- **Labels exclude nested controls and join text nodes with spaces.** A tab wrapper is "SIGNALQUEUE", not "SIGNALQUEUE×"; a member row is "Builder BUILDER BUSY", not "BuilderBUILDERBUSY". When several controls match exactly and one contains the others, the innermost wins (the name button inside the tab wrapper); `kind + label` ("button SEND") also counts as an exact match.


## Hands-free Computer voice operator (VOICE_AGENT_PLAN.md)

- **Resolve conversational scope before testing ambiguity.** The roster line
  describes the UI, not a replacement for the retained conversation. Explicit
  current targets win, then clear references in recent questions/answers/tool
  results, then the selected cluster when no topic exists. A SignalQueue mail
  answer naming its unique builder establishes “the builder” for a nudge even
  if ORBIT is selected. Two builders inside the relevant cluster still require
  a scoped question; never choose by mail count/activity/list order. Do not
  restore the old global-roster-count rule or require a previous send before
  conversational references count. Keep this in the shared model prompt, not
  client phrase/name heuristics. `smoke:voice-luna` exercises the real Luna LOW
  over multiple turns with actual target resolution and canned worker actions.

- **Completion watches bind IDs AND busy generations.** Only release of the
  owning user-turn drain claim reports finished, after queued messages. Never
  use provider `primary_turn_ended`, selection changes, matching display names
  or model judgments. Other busy releases default to interrupted. STOP/removal
  revokes the claim before a resend can replace it. Saved watches become
  interrupted on restart; private watches never persist or enter notification
  transports and are discarded on private clear/removal. No polling AI or
  worker prompts. Announcement claims deduplicate across windows; microphone
  and category mute still gate speech. Unit state-machine + real fake-provider
  HTTP lifecycle coverage: `test:voice`, `smoke:computer-tools`.
- **Quota is separate from tokens and throttling.** `arco_usage` preserves
  source/fetchedAt/unavailable state. Only explicit used/remaining percentages
  yield a numeric quota value. Never leak Codex profile paths/raw account text.
- **Voice sensitivity changes the worklet gate, not ASR text.** Preserve the
  adaptive room floor. A Vosk “over” must not overrule a longer full transcript
  such as “over it”; that can be legitimate dictation. Use diagnostics to
  distinguish an unheard quiet word from a wrong transcript.


- **Tool strings must contain well-formed Unicode.** `String.slice` can leave
  half an emoji at a label/result cap. JS JSON accepts an escaped lone surrogate;
  Codex's Rust MCP path can then wait 300 seconds for a result already returned by
  Arco (2026-10-17, nudge picker). Use shared `voiceText` for screen text and
  central relay result/error caps. Test with a deliberately malformed label,
  not just large ASCII. The real Luna smoke includes one on every screen read.
- **Computer liveness measures useful progress, not process activity.** The
  Codex voice-only watchdog ignores token/CPU ticks and repeated code-mode
  waits. It fences late tools before disposing, retires the process before any
  new resume, surfaces a spoken error, and never retries actions. Timeout
  cancellation removes queued optimistic rows too. A private thread lost with
  the worker must clear memory/browser context visibly; saved threads resume.
  Do not extend this to coding agents or local model loads by accident.
- **The capture acknowledgement is intentionally quiet.** Use the bundled
  230 ms Soft double pulse at its authored peak 0.065, with unity playback.
  Never normalize it toward the old TNG alert level. Include `capture-ready.wav`
  in the server build. A fast grammar wake stays silent; verified Computer
  plays the cue once, without awaiting completion before accepting more speech.
- **Computer switches wait for the SERVER clean boundary.** Provider
  `primary_turn_ended` is emitted before the queue drains and the server busy
  claim releases. Client `computerTurnActive` stays set until transient
  `computer_context_updated {settled:true}`; reconnect snapshots rebase it.
  Do not switch on a per-provider end or an empty queue update alone.
- **Computer no-history is strict Incognito, not hidden saved history.** Use a
  separate private workflow; never toggle an existing saved workflow in place
  or copy its messages. Clear also discards memory events + browser messages;
  leaving removes the private workflow. Only non-content preferences persist.
  Voice requests carry a capture-bound private header; suppress recognition,
  diagnostics and tool-relay logs. Drop late recognition across selection
  boundaries. Only Computer gets narrow private `voice_tool_result` and
  `computer_context_control` protocol exceptions. Strata stays saved-only.
- **Local Computer history must include actual tool calls/results.** Text-only
  USER/AGENT replay teaches Qwen that a bare Done executes an instruction.
  `voice/history.ts` retains structured assistant tool calls and matching tool
  results; replay only completed turns since the last full reset. No heuristic
  tool subsetting, phrase routing or automatic compaction in Computer's local
  path. Native/Codex paths retain the same bounded tools and no project access.
  Private Codex keeps live token receipts in memory: there is no rollout to
  read for context fullness. Never silently downgrade its ephemeral handshake.
- **Reply voice is independent of microphone/conversation state.** Persist only
  the validated output ID, never enabled/capture state. A change applies to the
  next reply; freeze one ID for every chunk/prefetch of the current reply.
  Retain the session/recognizer/waker when rotating only its speech worker.
  An old cancelled request may demote only its own worker, never a replacement.
  Off-mic previews create no lease or recognizer and stop before enabling;
  live previews use runtime playback so echo/barge-in guards remain intact.
  Test: `npm run test:voice`; browser: `npm run smoke:computer-panel`.
- **Computer fresh-context thresholds run only after the FULL turn/queue drain.**
  Default 50% subscription / 75% local of the provider-reported/configured window; KEEP CONTEXT/OFF pause automatic
  resets. Never use model output, topic heuristics or a timer to decide that
  work is done. A manual busy request is persisted as `pendingFresh`. Retire the
  idle app-server and persist the archive + cleared active CLI ledger before
  announcing the boundary; otherwise a restart can silently resume old context.
  `computerResetting` must keep new prompts queued even if STOP revokes busy
  during the disk write. The transcript/lifetime usage stay; only model context
  is reset. `smoke:computer-context` pins queue continuity and restart behavior.
- **`voice_tool_call` is sent straight to the chat's sockets, never through
  `broadcastWorkflow`.** A persisted call would be replayed on reconnect and
  could re-run an action. The model's own tool-use item in the Computer chat
  transcript is the durable record; the relay is ephemeral (`operatorTools.ts`).
- **Only the window that opened the Computer chat answers, and only while
  hands-free is enabled** (`toolBridge.ts`). Other observers ignore the call;
  an unanswered call times out after 20 s so the model gets an error, not a
  hang. A call arriving after DISABLE is refused (browser smoke pins this).
- **Computer has a header panel, never a project tab.** `voiceOperator` jobs
  stay in the store and persistence for their history/relay, but creation
  must preserve the workbench selection, and TabBar / tab search / PAST CHATS
  exclude them by flag, never name. `setActiveJob` routes history links to
  `ComputerPanel` without changing the tool target; hydration migrates old
  active Computer tabs back to the workbench and keeps their saved thread.
  Opening/closing the panel must never enable/disable voice or send a turn.
- **The arco-voice MCP child does not import `@arco/shared`.** It is
  spawned without `--conditions development`, so a shared import would resolve
  to a possibly stale `shared/dist`. Constants are mirrored literally.
- **`/api/voice/tool` is cookie-exempt but loopback-only**, like the cluster
  peer routes; it also requires the target workflow to be a `kind:'chat'` with
  `voiceOperator:true`.
- **Edge TTS is one failure away from Kokoro.** `session.edgeSpeaker` is set to
  null on the first error and the WAV path takes over without a client change;
  the client decodes MP3 and WAV alike. `X-Voice-Engine` says which spoke.
- **Capture cancellation is not script cancellation.** Standalone cancel/
  belay that or Cancel capture discards only the browser's unsent capture,
  without a model call. Fillers such as “oh cancel” can reach the model as
  ordinary text; the operator prompt requires clarification, not cancelling
  a recently discussed script/watch/worker. Specific “cancel that script”
  remains an ordinary authorized action. Keep this semantic distinction in
  the prompt; do not add fuzzy cancellation routing in the client.
- **There is no deterministic command parser.** Every captured directive goes
  to Computer (`runtime.ts`); the only instant words are the microphone
