  controls in `immediateVoiceControl`. The operator, 2026-10-17: "there should be no
  exact matching going on with anything" after "switch to workstationus hyphen
  maint" failed on a string match. Do not add phrase patterns back — add tools,
  and let the prompt teach Luna how names sound.
- **The arco-voice entry must carry `default_tools_approval_mode="approve"`.**
  Codex 0.163 refuses every MCP call in a chat-mode thread (approvalPolicy
  'never', read-only profile) with "MCP tool call requires approval, but
  approval policy is never"; the model then reports it "can't see" the
  project. Agent-tab MCP children (arco-cluster) are not affected.
  `npm run smoke:voice-luna` (opt-in, spends Codex quota) boots an isolated
  server against the real Luna and pins tool selection for four utterances.
- **The generic Chat preamble is skipped for the Computer chat** (`turnEngine.ts`,
  `wf.config.voiceOperator`). Folded, its "do not use backend tools… continue
  in an Agent tab" made Luna refuse the arco-voice tools even with the
  operator prompt as base instructions.
- **A bare `Done.` from Computer is swallowed, not spoken** (`COMPUTER_SILENT`,
  `conversation.ts`). The prompt asks for exactly that after acting; the
  operator reads the result in Arco. Do not "improve" the prompt into
  confirming what it did — the operator: "I don't want constant feedback from the voice."
- **Computer names the selection the way the operator did.** Luna passes
  `target:"this project"`, `"it"`, `"the current tab"`; `resolveVoiceTarget`
  maps all of those to the selection. Keep that list in step with the prompt.

- **Computer request status must not depend on speech playback.** A settled
  clarification can wait behind capture or have spoken feedback disabled.
  `ComputerConversation` clears working status when the reply settles, and
  `computerVoiceStatus` repairs an old working label when the selected Computer
  is idle. Panel/strip Stop buttons share `computerRequestActive`, including
  queue and `computerTurnActive`; provider end alone is not full settlement.
- **Typed Computer requests work with the microphone off.** A deliberate send
  scopes `toolBridge.allowComputerTextTools` to that exact Computer until full
  settlement. STOP, disconnect and owner changes revoke it. Panel visibility
  alone is never a tool grant. Drafts use memory-only `draftStore`, including
  private Computer. Do not enable the mic or broaden project tools for typing.

### Computer conditional mailbox rules (2026-10-17)

- `arco_mail_rule` is operator-armed deterministic mailbox automation, not a
  model polling or judging completion. Resolve exact IDs in the shared prompt;
  no voice phrase/name heuristics. One-shot nudges/watches keep their semantics.
- All non-final pinned members must be idle with empty mailboxes and queues;
  final recipient idle WITH mail. Membership/AUTO-PICKUP changes stop the rule.
  Never silently toggle AUTO-PICKUP, auto-drain mail or interrupt a busy worker.
- Check strict mailbox IDs under `clusterMailboxRoot`’s existing lock through
  the actual turn busy claim, after async turn preflight. Conditional nudges
  never enter the ordinary busy queue. Cancellation during preflight must fence
  the claim. Corrupt mail is an error, never an empty-mailbox success.
- Same pending IDs never cause another nudge. Bounds: two hours, 30s cooldown,
  eight nudges per worker, ≤16 selected workers, one active rule per cluster.
  Monitoring is free; worker turns incur normal provider usage. Final success
  means nudge dispatched, not work completed. STOP/errors/removal stop rules;
  restart interrupts and requires re-arming, never resumes synthetic turns.
- Private rule metadata never reaches disk, recipient audit notices or external
  transports. Full private clear/removal fences pending dispatch and drops it.
  Only the requested generic nudge enters a normal worker’s history. Keep
  Native/local and MCP array serialization aligned via `computerToolWireArgs`.

### Computer event stream and optional agent updates (2026-10-17)

- **General scripts are declarations, never eval or canned phrase routing.**
  `arco_condition` composes typed worker/group/count facts with AND/OR/NOT,
  ordered actions and dependencies. Unknown stays unknown under NOT. A Director
  nudge is never implicit. Named tool compaction aliases normalize to the same
  server-side compact action; no microphone/browser is required for worker
  sends, nudges or compact. UI actions retain ordinary schema/confirmations,
  exact target IDs and stable control labels. Scripts cannot re-arm scripts
  or create more standing authority. Keep all four operator-arming safeguards.
- **Revalidate snapshots through claim.** Sorted mailbox locks across scope
  cover the last mailbox/busy/queue/input check and the real busy claim, even
  after provider preflight. Cancellation, source identity changes, STOP and
  private clear must fence late callbacks. Compaction's full internal operation
  must finish before compacting=false; Codex thread/compacted alone is too early.
  A manual compact's interrupted busy release is not an operator STOP.
- **Input holds do not answer questions.** Native questions/gates and Codex
  operator questions are explicit facts. For armed scripts, changed ordinary
  replies may use isolated ephemeral tool-free Luna LOW observation, capped
  at 24 batches/owner/hour and reported separately from optional agent updates.
  Unknown/failure/truncation holds; never regex-match prose or infer permission
  from idle. Identity/version fence the result. Human input starts a new check.
  An explicit needs-input notification can alert while other actions are held.
  Restore the latest reply with the streaming visitor in scanEventsOfTypes;
  a fixed byte tail can contain only later board/activity records and strand
  needs_input=null forever. Retain only one bounded reply, require a complete
  turn boundary, fence live changes, and keep true long/incomplete histories
  held. Explicit resume can retry unknown restored history; no automatic loop.
  Preserve detailed history/budget/failure hold reasons after predicate checks.
- **Actions in a clause are ordered.** A missing/busy/mail-free worker action
  cannot be skipped to emit a false “I nudged it” notification. A started once
  clause latches, retaining holds/idle/cancellation checks while completing its
  actions. Dependencies wait for all earlier action targets to dispatch;
  successful dispatch is not completed project work. Do not invent dependencies
  on optional recurring mail nudges for a state-only finishing condition: a
  mailbox can already be empty without that worker ever being nudged.
  Recurrence is bounded and
  mail IDs deduplicate independently of busy changes. Recurring compact watches
  completed runs; continuation after each in-place compact watches
  compactions_finished. Ollama only arms next-turn compaction: its requested
  continuation starts that turn; waiting on the in-place counter first deadlocks it.
- **Restore context facts after restart.** `conditionContext` reads saved
  final-request measurements, with the current account/session's Codex rollout
  fallback for older records. Never use summed billing input/cache tokens.
  Reads are coalesced once per uninitialized public worker and fenced against
  live measurements, compaction, removal and provider/session changes. Prior
  compaction/handoff/rewind readings stay unknown; private state is excluded.
  Absolute token counts do not require a known model window.
- **Wait on post-action facts, not the trigger.** A 40%-context trigger belongs
  before compact. Requiring context≥40 afterwards strands continuation after
  the summary shrinks it. Native context becomes unknown until measured again;
  no false zero or stale aggregate is invented. A finishing clause waits for
  every requested action. Default STOP/error behavior can be overridden only
  by an operator-directed declaration that handles those events explicitly.
- **Script budgets belong to the operator.** General scripts default to null
  expiry/action/worker-turn limits, not the legacy 2h/32-action/8-turn ceilings.
  Explicit positive limits still enforce stopping; cooldown defaults to 30s
  but accepts operator-requested zero. Saved numeric limits remain unchanged.
  Retain counters/receipts beyond the old maxima on save/load; lifting only the
  create schema strands valid scripts at restart. Null expiry schedules no
  expiry timer; cap long setTimeout delays to the supported timer range.
- Script edits retain spent counters, seen mail and matching completed
  actions. Read the full definition/current revision before update, preserve
  clause IDs, and fence old-revision preflights. Do not cancel/recreate merely
  to add a guard or remove a limit; that could resend work already handled.
  Unchanged TTL preserves the absolute deadline; explicitly changed numeric
  TTL starts from the update and null removes expiry. Validate the new deadline
  before mutating any clause state. Runtime idle/input/deduplication guards
  remain even without count/time limits.
- Saved script definitions/state/claims use locked durable atomic writes;
  persist before dispatch and fail closed. Restart interrupts, never retries.
  Private definitions/counters/notices stay memory-only and clear with their
  owner. Bounded worker text is volatile, not another persistent transcript.
  Legacy mailbox rules/AUTO-PICKUP cannot coexist with scripts in one cluster.

- **Restore the saved Computer owner before reading activity.** `ComputerPanel`
  can render a saved chat while `computerStore.activeId` is still null after
  reload. Bind the matching saved selection on mount and delayed job hydration,
  without requiring an utterance/USE AGENT, enabling the mic, changing project
  selection or auto-adopting a private context. Activity/watches/rules share
  that owner; otherwise HTTP results are discarded and controls stay disabled.
  Keep loading/disconnection/errors visible and offer an explicit retry.

- Publish metadata from real mutation sites, not provider text/tool deltas.
  Worker finished is after the full claim/queue release; STOP/failure stays
  distinct. Never put Computer’s own turns or private chats in the public bus.
- Mailbox rule checks subscribe to worker/queue/mailbox/cluster changes, latch
  changes arriving during an async evaluation, and validate under the mailbox
  lock through claim. Only real cooldown/expiry deadlines have timers. No
  recurring condition scan or browser projection poll. External manual edits
  of mailbox files are not supported event sources.
- Background agent updates are operator-opted-in per owner, OFF by default;
  source facts remain data. Enforce read-only at the central tool relay for ALL
  providers, not only in the prompt. Queue user directions as follow-ups, clear
  that restriction at the first actual settled update, and token-fence stale
  cleanup. Wait through `computerSettled`/the complete reset boundary before
  dispatching another update; do not steal an operator reply between turns.
- Bound spend and memory: 12 batch turns/rolling hour, ten facts/batch, 50
  waiting facts. Persist rate counters separately from capped delivery history;
  never evict in-flight state for overflow/history. Failures/restart never
  automatically replay an update. Switching Computer agents disables the old
  feed and cancels its waiting updates without copying private context.
- Transient `computer_activity_changed` invalidations drive coalesced HTTP
  refreshes. Reads do not invalidate themselves. Initial/reconnect snapshots
  close missed-push gaps; epoch guards reject stale private responses. Keep
  normal activity metadata, private matched facts and owner inboxes separated
  before every disk write/notification. No Redis or extra worker model call.
